# Large-field admission with insertions

**Full I-2 remains open for the designated H.** A native first-return fine-slot obstruction cancels in the matched small-record completion. For the actual first globally clean quantum file, the denominator belongs to the full conditional integral. Equations (4.139)–(4.142) identify the filed coefficient and its restricted polymer sum. Equation (4.145) gives a strict shrinking birth-radius comparison on a protected second return. The separated argument for (4.153)–(4.159) requires a varying axial–Landau input. Equations (4.165)–(4.173) bound the total prepared output, reconstructed current, two-index scalar and compensated axial frame on the explicit consecutive native charts, including the original reinsertion radius. Equations (4.175)–(4.188) treat both fine-current indices on short retained edges, including an actually normalized still-live file and the failure of an isolated transverse-current bound. Equations (4.189)–(4.192) control dense fractional walls and direct finite-stencil contacts under the stated native radius comparison, with a separate exact cancellation at flat baselines. Equations (4.193)–(4.194) cover dense walls on the entire prescribed contour on the early thin A/C corridor. Theorem 4.126 and Proposition 4.127 give the full dense contour at arbitrary age for fixed primitive scales satisfying (4.198), including one incident mixed frame. Their hypotheses specify the allowed preparation and external variables. **P** denotes a proof here, **L** a checked primary result, **C** a conditional application and **O** an open assertion about the actual Wilson construction.

## 1. Target and interfaces assumed

The only assumed program interface is **I-0c**: on the fixed reference torus T*, the complete small-/large-field representation exists at every intermediate step k≤K, with its stated uniform unmarked bounds and coupling recursion

\[
g_{k+1}^{-2}=g_k^{-2}-b_0\log L+r_k,\qquad |r_k|\le Cg_k^2.
\]

This does not assume a marked representation, an old-domain inclusion, a common holomorphic gauge, or a source-uniform zero-free region. I-1 and the standing hypotheses SF1–SF6 of the T1 paper ([Transport of insertions on a reference torus](insertion_transport.md)) are **not** imported. In particular an analytic member known only on a regular domain has not thereby acquired a compact-group extension.

The target I-2 is, for every admissible original history ω, retained coordinate ξ, outer variable z, and simultaneous preparation/localization/source wall s,

\[
W_{\omega,s}=P_{\rm old}(q_{\rm old}\Gamma^R_{\omega,s}(z,\xi)),\qquad
(W_{\omega,s},J_{\rm phys}(W_{\omega,s}))\in\mathcal C^{\rm spare}_{j,e},             \tag{1.1}
\]

with strict margins on the *entire original domain*, and the stored packet estimate

\[
\mathcal N_I[R_j(W_{\omega,s},J_{\rm phys}(W_{\omega,s}))]
 \le\sum_\gamma a_{I,\gamma;j,e}Q^{D_{I,\gamma}}.                              \tag{1.2}
\]

All coefficients retain their original birth mesh, source normalization, radius, coupling grade, age, and anchor weights. The ICR-2 extension includes first- and second-source activities and their marked carriers, not just an unmarked effective density. No result below silently resets the birth scale or defines a different minimum for different roots.

The constants in the elementary theorems below depend only on their explicitly listed norms and radii, not on the dimension of the integration space. Applications would be cutoff/depth uniform only after those norms and radii are verified uniformly. The fixed parameters N,L,M,ℓ₀,t*, the selected finite derivative order m, and the original packet carrier are allowed in constants. No volume-uniform conclusion is claimed.

## 2. Exact objects and original domains

### 2.1 Original stored state

The definitions are those of the [source estimates](source_estimates/README.md), printed pp.781–784, LM42–LM52, and of [Boundary traces and reconstruction at a changing base](block_and_transfer/conditional_estimates/boundary_traces_and_reconstruction_at_a_changing_base.tex), §§1–9. Γ^R is the actual nested preparation of LFR II (1.61)–(1.65), with all old determining maps and relative gauges retained. It is not an arbitrary field satisfying a plaquette test. q_old contains the original oriented averages, bare identity rows, meshes, full path prefixes, endpoint frames, exterior fields and boundary conditions. P_old is the original constrained Wilson minimum for that record. J_phys is the Wilson current for that same action and its original normalization. The kernel's independent current variable is not replaced by J_phys.

The simultaneous domain is denoted D_ω; this is the domain prescribed by the original outer-pair ball and all original wall radii, with ξ on the original selector support. It is specified *before* an admission estimate. The notation C^spare_{j,e} means the actual paired domain on which the saved age/grade estimate for R_j was proved; the source record supplies its numerical radii. Neither the LM47 statement nor the interface supplies a single numerical radius valid for every possible R_j. Consequently a proof has to compare to those stored radii, not choose new smaller outer walls after seeing the image.

The finite norm N_I consists of the specified current/source derivatives and weighted absolute anchor sums of that record. Scalar supremum bounds are only one of its rows. For the clean-born degree-two stable H packet, the saved scalar envelope is

\[
|F|\le A_j\rho^2\{r^{s_0}Q^2+u r^bQ^3+u^2r^2Q^4\},\quad
r=L^{j-e},\ \rho=g_e/g_j,\ u=g_e/\alpha.                         \tag{2.1}
\]

Here s₀ is the saved age exponent (distinguished from the wall tuple s), and Q includes pinned and exterior data. Squaring reaches degree eight. This envelope is recalled as a stored bound on its previously verified domain, not asserted on Γ^R or W.

### 2.2 Literal Balaban domains (L)

The following are taken from LFR I, printed pp.190–196. Equations (1.64)–(1.69) define mixed complex configuration spaces on localization domains, with separate layer-dependent bounds for curvature, the constrained reconstruction, independent current, its reconstruction, complex perturbation, its derivatives, and local real gauges. They are invariant under compact gauge transformations; the nesting requires β≤1/4 and L₀²≤L/3. Their native layer weights must not be dropped.

The selector (1.75), printed p.193, restricts only the field outside Λ: it requires an extension whose reconstructed curvature is less than 2ε_kη². Proposition 1, printed pp.194–195, supplies one critical **orbit**, a minimum, with boundary-data analytic extension and interior bound B₅M⁵ε. The background U₀=U_{k,Z}(V_Λ), (1.79), satisfies

\[
|U_0(\partial p)-1|<2\epsilon_k\eta^2+
C B_3B_5M^5e^{-\delta\operatorname{dist}(p,\Lambda)}\epsilon_k\eta^2,
\quad p\in\Omega_k.                                                    \tag{2.2}
\]

Equation (1.82), printed p.196, is χ′=1{|B′(b)|<δ′_k for b∈B₀}, V′=exp(iB′), with an explicitly constructed nested-shell tree gauge. It is not an independent Gaussian cutoff introduced by changing variables without proof.

These are four parts of the unmarked admission mechanism: outside extension selector, analytic constrained orbit, decay of its background, and preliminary integrations with enlarged member domains. A spectator insertion does not change those maps. It adds an obligation at **each composition**: its previously completed representative, with its physical current and all derivative rows, must be defined and bounded on that image. A positive answer for the effective-action members is not a positive answer for an arbitrary old marked member.

### 2.3 Distinct denominators

There are two distinct normalizations. LFR II (1.10)–(1.11) factor the small-field denominator after extracting the Wilson/Gaussian terms. LFR II (1.71), (1.73)–(1.75) concerns the final localized integration operator T′_k(X) and its *own mass*. The latter reference mass can be very small and cancels in its own normalized expectation. The absolute rare numerator divided by a different small-field denominator is not automatically such an expectation. All uses below name which normalization occurs.

## 3. Main theorems

### Theorem 3.1. Same-mass normalization and two marked source rows (P)

Let ν be a positive finite measure on the *actual* selector domain Ω with 0<M=ν(Ω)<∞. No lower bound on M is imposed. Let

\[
T_zF=\int_\Omega e^{\sigma(z,\xi)}F(z,\xi)\,d\nu(\xi),\qquad Z_z=T_z1.
\]

Assume the displayed integrands are holomorphic in z, locally dominated, and that, after subtracting a ξ-independent complex scalar c(z),

\[
|\operatorname{Im}(\sigma-c)|\le\theta<\pi/2,\qquad
\sup_\xi\operatorname{Re}(\sigma-c)-\inf_\xi\operatorname{Re}(\sigma-c)\le h.
                                                                    \tag{3.1}
\]

Then Z_z≠0 and

\[
|T_zF/Z_z|\le C_0\|F\|_\infty,\qquad C_0=e^h\sec\theta.              \tag{3.2}
\]

Thus the normalized functional is holomorphic. If A,B are bounded holomorphic carrying functions, set

\[
G_z(t)=\frac{T_z\exp(tA+t^2B)}{Z_z}.
\]

For η_J=r‖A‖∞+r²‖B‖∞, if θ+η_J<π/2, then G_z has no zeros on |t|<r and has a unique holomorphic logarithm with log G_z(0)=0 on this disc. With ⟨F⟩_z=T_zF/Z_z,

\[
(\log G_z)'(0)=\langle A\rangle_z,\qquad
(\log G_z)''(0)=2\langle B\rangle_z+
\langle A^2\rangle_z-\langle A\rangle_z^2.                           \tag{3.3}
\]

The absolute bounds are C₀‖A‖∞ and 2C₀‖B‖∞+(C₀+C₀²)‖A‖∞². For multiple sources, replace tA+t²B by the declared finite source polynomial; its cross derivatives give the corresponding joint cumulants and original contact coefficients. No Gaussian law replaces ν.

**Relation to the source (L/C).** LFR II printed pp.379–380 derives (1.73)–(1.75) by writing the complex operator as a positive real operator times exp σ, with σ uniformly small. Equation (3.2) gives a direct proof of the required cancellation. If |σ|≤η≤1, then C₀≤e^{3η}; this recovers its (1.75). Its application to an inherited mark remains conditional on that mark being bounded on exactly this integration domain. The source letter J in Balaban's pair (U,J) is the independent current, and is not being confused with the additional generating parameter t here.

### Theorem 3.2. Rare marked numerator and the exact domination needed (P)

Under Theorem 3.1, suppose the marked contribution is supported in E⊂Ω and ν(E)≤bM. Then for H(t)=tA+t²B,

\[
\left|\frac{T_z[1_E(e^{H(t)}-1)]}{Z_z}\right|
 \le C_0 b\,e^{\eta_J}\eta_J,\qquad |t|\le r.                        \tag{3.4}
\]

The first and second derivatives at zero have bounds C₀b‖A‖∞ and C₀b(‖A‖∞²+2‖B‖∞). In particular b=e^{-R} stays **once** in both marked rows. If ν_E is a different positive measure, the same conclusion holds with ν_E(Ω_E)≤bM and with its complex numerator's real and imaginary bounds compared to the denominator's reference scalar. An *absolute* estimate ν_E(Ω_E)≤B alone is insufficient; the bound then contains B/M.

This is the precise distinction needed by T1 R-3. The same-operator quotient needs no absolute denominator bound. A mismatched numerator requires a relative-mass estimate, or a separately verified factored denominator and cancellation of all extracted terms. Theorem 3.2 supplies neither by assumption I-0c alone.

### Theorem 3.3. Quantitative continuation of the complete original constrained problem (P)

Work in a fixed finite-dimensional holomorphic gauge-fixed coordinate chart, retaining the complete nonredundant old target quotient and all boundary rows. Write x=(A,λ), and let z denote all outer and wall variables. For the complex-bilinear transpose *, define

\[
\mathscr F(x,z)=
\begin{pmatrix}\nabla S_z(A)-Dq_z(A)^*\lambda\\q_z(A)-v(z)\end{pmatrix},
\quad v(z)=q_z(\Gamma^R(z)).                                      \tag{3.5}
\]

The action here is its holomorphic continuation; Re Tr on the real group is continued by the original trace-plus-inverse formula, not complex conjugation. At a known admitted base (x₀,z₀), let K₀=D_xF(x₀,z₀) be invertible. Choose any Banach norms, possibly weighted native norms, and a closed radius-R ball around x₀. Let U be the *unchanged proposed simultaneous z-domain*. Suppose F is holomorphic on a neighborhood of that ball ×U and

\[
\sup_{x,z}\|I-K_0^{-1}D_x\mathscr F(x,z)\|\le\vartheta<1,
\qquad
\sup_{z\in U}\|K_0^{-1}\mathscr F(x_0,z)\|< (1-\vartheta)R.          \tag{3.6}
\]

Then there is one holomorphic solution x(z) inside that ball, unique among solutions there, and

\[
\|x(z)-x_0\|\le
\frac{\|K_0^{-1}\mathscr F(x_0,z)\|}{1-\vartheta},\qquad
\|(D_x\mathscr F)^{-1}\|\le\frac{\|K_0^{-1}\|}{1-\vartheta}.          \tag{3.7}
\]

If χ_z(A) is the actual physical-coordinate recovery and the paired map
Ψ_z(A)=(χ_z(A),J_phys,z(χ_z(A))) obeys

\[
\operatorname{dist}(\Psi_z(A_0),\mathcal C_{j,e}\setminus
 \mathcal C^{\rm spare}_{j,e})\ge d_*>0,
\quad
\|\Psi_z(A)-\Psi_z(A_0)\|_{\rm saved}\le C_\Psi\|A-A_0\|,
                                                                    \tag{3.8}
\]

and C_Ψ sup_z‖K₀⁻¹F(x₀,z)‖/(1−ϑ)<d_*, then the solved branch is admitted to the saved domain with the displayed spare margin. Equivalently one may state (3.8) as an explicit ball inclusion of radius d_* about Ψ_z(A₀); this avoids any ambiguity if the saved domain is open. The inequality must hold for the actual current as well as the field.

For the real problem, this branch agrees with the original P_old wherever its established uniqueness theorem applies. Positive definiteness of the real constrained Hessian ensures it is a local constrained minimum. Identifying it with the specified global/unique admitted minimum on all real data is a separate uniqueness check, not a consequence of an invertible complex Hessian alone.

The criterion is a residual-and-Hessian test, not the required admission restated as a hypothesis: its first residual is explicitly

\[
\mathscr F(x_0,z)=
\begin{pmatrix}\nabla S_z(A_0)-Dq_z(A_0)^*\lambda_0\\
q_z(A_0)-q_z(\Gamma^R(z))\end{pmatrix}.                              \tag{3.9}
\]

It exposes changed base, changed boundary, and target defects in a form suitable for Green-function estimates. Verifying (3.6), (3.8) uniformly for Balaban's entire mixed history family remains **O**.

### Theorem 3.4. Full changed-base and frame response (P)

On the admitted branch of Theorem 3.3, let

\[
Q=Dq(W),\quad L=S''(W)-\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha D^2q_\alpha(W),\quad
K=\begin{pmatrix}L&-Q^*\\Q&0\end{pmatrix},\quad
K^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}G&P\\ *&*\end{pmatrix}.
\]

For fixed S,q and v=q(Γ), the exact first response and its off-base difference are

\[
W_i=P Dq(\Gamma)\Gamma_i,\qquad
W_i-\Gamma_i=-GL\Gamma_i+P(Dq(\Gamma)-Dq(W))\Gamma_i.                 \tag{3.10}
\]

No old-to-new row defect is dropped. The second response is

\[
\begin{aligned}
W_{ij}&=P\{v_{ij}-D^2q(W)[W_i,W_j]\}-G\mathcal B_{ij},\\
v_{ij}&=Dq(\Gamma)\Gamma_{ij}+D^2q(\Gamma)[\Gamma_i,\Gamma_j],\\
\mathcal B_{ij}&=S'''(W)[W_i,W_j]-\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha
D^3q_\alpha(W)[W_i,W_j]\nonumber\\
&\quad -(D^2q(W)[W_i])^*\lambda_j-(D^2q(W)[W_j])^*\lambda_i.
\end{aligned}                                                       \tag{3.11}
\]

For moving action and rows, put b_i=∂_i[∇S_z−Dq_z^*λ] at fixed W,λ. Then

\[
W_i=-Gb_i+P\{Dq_z(\Gamma)\Gamma_i+
(\partial_iq_z)(\Gamma)-(\partial_iq_z)(W)\}.                       \tag{3.12}
\]

All orders, including mixed derivatives of action, constraints, boundary and wall data, are specified without omissions by the following recursion. Set y(z)=(x(z),z). For a labeled derivative set I with |I|≥2,

\[
D_Ix=-K^{-1}\sum_{\substack{\pi\in\operatorname{Part}(I)\\|\pi|\ge2}}
D^{|\pi|}\mathscr F(y)[D_By:B\in\pi].                             \tag{3.13}
\]

Here D_By=(D_Bx,0) for |B|≥2, and D_iy=(D_ix,e_i) for singletons. The first-order equation is K D_ix=−∂_iF. Thus (3.13) contains all mixed partial derivatives of F, rather than only field derivatives.

For a moving endpoint gauge h, define θ_i(x)=h(x)⁻¹∂_ih(x). Exact matrix differentiation gives

\[
\partial_i U^h_{xy}=h(x)\{\partial_iU_{xy}+\theta_i(x)U_{xy}
-U_{xy}\theta_i(y)\}h(y)^{-1},                                    \tag{3.14}
\]

\[
\partial_iJ^h(x)=h(x)\{\partial_iJ(x)+[\theta_i(x),J(x)]\}h(x)^{-1}.
                                                                    \tag{3.15}
\]

Repeated application of ∇^h_i=∂_i+ad θ_i gives every higher current-frame row, in its original order. A moving coordinate recovery χ_z similarly contributes both Dχ_z(W)W_i and ∂_iχ_z(W). These terms vanish only under a separately verified invariant simultaneous transformation of the whole scalar and all its slots; they cannot be removed from an absolute current norm by gauge invariance alone.

### Theorem 3.5. Holomorphic path frames without a fine-link count (P)

Let γ have links e₁,…,e_n with physical lengths a_i and fixed compact reference links U_i^0. Write U_i(w)=exp(a_iH_i(w))U_i^0, with H_i holomorphic on a common original parameter domain. This includes inverse links by reversing their word and conjugating the corresponding Lie-algebra perturbation. For a multi-index derivative label set I define

\[
A=\sum_i a_i\sup\|H_i\|,\qquad
b_B=\sum_i a_i\sup\|D_BH_i\|\quad(\varnothing\ne B\subseteq I).
\]

Then the ordered path product U_γ and its inverse are holomorphic, obey ‖U_γ‖,‖U_γ⁻¹‖≤e^A, and

\[
\|D_IU_\gamma\|,\ \|D_IU_\gamma^{-1}\|
 \le e^A\sum_{\pi\in\operatorname{Part}(I)}\prod_{B\in\pi}b_B.
                                                                    \tag{3.16}
\]

In particular ‖U_γ(U_γ^0)⁻¹−1‖≤e^A−1. If a_i=a and n a≤ℓ, bounds on H and its derivatives of order ℓ⁻¹ give constants independent of a and n. Normalized finite averages and their logarithmic charts inherit these estimates *only when their images remain on the same specified logarithm branch with a uniform branch margin*. Every old prefix and endpoint frame must use this same complete word construction.

This theorem constructs holomorphic tree/path frames from already controlled links and retains all frame derivatives. It does not manufacture current-unit W^{1,p} control from plaquette regularity, nor assert compatibility with an arbitrarily prescribed exterior frame. Compatibility is checked by the actual endpoint words; a mismatch is boundary data, not a disposable gauge parameter.

### Theorem 3.6. A native-metric bound that is actually sufficient at δ₀/8 (P)

Let (V,d,μ) be a discrete metric measure space and assume, uniformly over all relevant histories,

\[
\sum_{y:d(x,y)\le R}\mu_y\le C_V(1+R)^d\quad (R\ge0).               \tag{3.17}
\]

For α>0 and any fixed moment t≥0,

\[
\sup_x\sum_y\mu_y(1+d(x,y))^t e^{-\alpha d(x,y)}
 \le C_V e^\alpha\sum_{n=0}^\infty(2+n)^{d+t}e^{-\alpha n}<\infty.
                                                                    \tag{3.18}
\]

The same bound holds with α=δ₀/8. To control kernel compositions, define

\[
\|A\|_\alpha=\max\left\{
\sup_x\sum_y\mu_y e^{\alpha d(x,y)}\|A(x,y)\|,
\sup_y\sum_x\mu_x e^{\alpha d(x,y)}\|A(x,y)\|\right\}.
\]

This is a submultiplicative norm under composition (AB)(x,y)=Σ_z μ_z A(x,z)B(z,y). If ‖G₀E‖_α<1, then

\[
\|(I+G_0E)^{-1}G_0\|_\alpha\le
\frac{\|G_0\|_\alpha}{1-\|G_0E\|_\alpha}.                         \tag{3.19}
\]

For example a pointwise bound Ce^{−δ₀d(x,y)/4}, together with (3.17), controls this norm at α=δ₀/8 using the remaining δ₀/8. If a forcing f is supported distance at least R from a readset S, the weighted sup version of the norm gives

\[
\sup_{x\in S}\|(Gf)(x)\|\le e^{-\alpha R}\|G\|_\alpha\|f\|_\infty.
                                                                    \tag{3.20}
\]

These statements apply to blocks of the augmented inverse, so the forcing includes all constraint adjoints and boundary rows. A prefactor arising from row scaling must be included in μ or the block norm before (3.17) is tested. The actual Balaban mixed native metric, its scale-dependent weights, and the geometry of all joined row stencils have not yet been proved to satisfy (3.17) with history-uniform constants. No estimate at rate δ₀/2 is substituted for that check.

### Proposition 3.7. AE8 for the specific selected background U₀ (P from checked L)

Use the units of LFR I (1.80): plaquettes have fine spacing η, and D is a fixed-size union of plaquettes contained in Ω_k. Suppose the real field U₀ is the *specific background* (1.79), on the selector (1.75) and with Proposition 1's geometric and weak-field hypotheses. Put C_*=2+CB₃B₅M⁵. For C_*ε_kη²≤1/2 the principal matrix logarithm defines F_η(U₀)_p=η⁻²log U₀(∂p), and

\[
\|F_\eta(U_0)\|_{L^p_\eta(D)}\le2C_*\epsilon_k|D|_\eta^{1/p}.
                                                                    \tag{3.21}
\]

Here |D|_η is the plaquette counting volume, including its fixed orientation multiplicity. For every disjoint assigned-cell partition of these same plaquettes, with lengths σ_Δ in the same units,

\[
\sum_{\Delta\subset D}\sigma_\Delta^{4-2p}
\|\sigma_\Delta^2F_\eta(U_0)(\sigma_\Delta\cdot)\|
 _{L^p_{\eta/\sigma_\Delta}(\sigma_\Delta^{-1}\Delta)}^p
\le (2C_*)^p\epsilon_k^p|D|_\eta.                                \tag{3.22}
\]

This is the required curvature sum for **U₀ on D⊂Ω_k**, for every finite p, including p>8. It is uniform in the fine mesh on such D and independent of the cell ages. It does not assert this estimate for Γ^R, for W=P_oldq_oldΓ^R, for plaquettes outside Ω_k, or at all complex simultaneous walls. No one of these fields has been equated with U₀. A complex version follows by the identical proof only on a domain where the *same* (2.2) holds in matrix norm with its actual complex radius. Analyticity without a magnitude estimate is not that hypothesis.

The real cutoff ceiling in (3.22) is τ=C_*ε_k. Where ε_k is g_k times the stated polylogarithmic cutoff, this is that actual ceiling, not a fixed complex source radius. This positive restricted result shows exactly which part of AE8 is already furnished by (1.80), and why an identification of (1.80) with the full mixed AE8 is too broad.

### Proposition 3.8. Exact cell accounting and the pinned obstruction (P)

For *any* specified field Ξ with a common curvature branch, disjoint cell partition, and p>0,

\[
c_\Delta(\Xi)^p=\sigma_\Delta^{2p-4}
 \sum_{P\in\Delta}a^4|F_a(\Xi)_P|^p,\qquad
\sum_\Delta\sigma_\Delta^{4-2p}c_\Delta(\Xi)^p
=\|F_a(\Xi)\|_{L^p_a(D)}^p.                                    \tag{3.23}
\]

If q_old contains an identity row at every link of a plaquette stencil, then q_oldP_old(v)=v implies W_p=Γ_p on that stencil; all parameter derivatives of the complete plaquette word agree. Hence the left side of (3.23) for W dominates the Γ contribution from all such pinned plaquettes. Reconstruction cannot improve that nonnegative portion of the curvature sum. The same retention statement applies to the physical current only when the *whole collection of incident plaquette stencils and the original action coefficients* is pinned.

If only c_Δ≤ε_Δ is known, the exact resulting upper bound is Σ_Δσ_Δ^{4−2p}ε_Δ^p. There is no legitimate step replacing it by Σ_Δε_Δ^p. In particular σ=L⁻ⁿ converts a one-cell norm by L^{(2−4/p)n}. This algebra is a test of a proposed estimate, not a counterexample asserting that a bad Γ occurs on the original selector support.

### Theorem 3.9. The nonlinear-rate native sum in Balaban's original geometry (L/P)

This gives a stronger, directly applicable alternative to the sufficient polynomial-volume hypothesis of Theorem 3.6. Use CMP96 (1984), printed p.224, (2.1)–(2.4): nested domains Ω₁⊃⋯⊃Ω_k, each made of blocks at scale L^jη, with

\[
(L^j\eta)^{-1}\operatorname{dist}(\Omega_j^c,\Omega_{j+1})>RM.       \tag{3.24}
\]

The block-center sets Λ_j of (2.3) give a partition B^j(Λ_j) of the underlying lattice, with Λ₀=Ω₁^c. Set B=⋃_{j=0}^kΛ_j. For admissible contours whose portion inside B^j(Λ_j) follows the j-scale lattice, define the *actual native metric* of (2.46), printed p.231,

\[
d(y,y')=\inf_{\Gamma:y\to y'}\sum_j(L^j\eta)^{-1}
 |\Gamma\cap B^j(\Lambda_j)|.                                    \tag{3.25}
\]

Let c₀(α)=Σ_{n∈Z}e^{−αδ₀|n|}; if necessary decrease the available propagator decay rate so δ₀≤1, and then c₀(α)<4/(αδ₀) for 0<α≤1. CMP96 Lemma 2.1, (2.59)–(2.63), is stated for **every** 0<α<1 satisfying

\[
\tfrac14\alpha\delta_0 RM>2d\log c_0(\alpha/2)+1.                 \tag{3.26}
\]

Consequently, at the nonlinear rate,

\[
\sup_{y\in\mathcal B}\sum_{y'\in\mathcal B}e^{-\delta_0d(y,y')/8}
 \le12c_0(1/16)^d\le12(64/\delta_0)^d,                            \tag{3.27}
\]

provided δ₀RM/32>2d log c₀(1/16)+1. The constants are independent of η,k and the admissible nested domains. This is not inferred from the example α=1/2; it is the substitution α=1/8 in a theorem quantified over α.

There is also a useful weighted version proved here. For fixed q,t≥0, suppose (3.26) holds at α=1/16 and

\[
q\log L/(RM)\le\delta_0/32.
\]

With j(y) the native layer and C_{t,δ}=sup_{r≥0}(1+r)^t e^{−δr/32}<∞,

\[
\sup_y\sum_{y'}L^{q|j(y)-j(y')|}(1+d(y,y'))^t
e^{-\delta_0d(y,y')/8}
\le L^q C_{t,\delta_0}\,12c_0(1/32)^d.                            \tag{3.28}
\]

Thus fixed polynomial native anchor moments and fixed relative-scale powers can be summed at δ₀/8 by an explicit once-chosen geometric enlargement RM. This does not permit changing RM midway through an already fixed construction. If its existing value fails these inequalities, the construction must be set up with the larger fixed value and all prior small-coupling thresholds checked with it.

### Proposition 3.10. Full path-row dictionary with weighted multiplicity (P)

An old path can meet arbitrarily many finer new cells after a join; an assertion of a uniformly bounded cell count is neither needed nor assumed. Let γ_r be an original row path of physical length at most C_rℓ_r, including every prefix used in that row and its endpoint frames. Relative to any disjoint native partition, set

\[
\lambda_r(y)=\ell_r^{-1}\sum_{e\in\gamma_r\cap B^{j(y)}(y)}a_e.
                                                                    \tag{3.29}
\]

Assign each link once, and count repeated occurrences in the original word with their multiplicity. Then Σ_yλ_r(y)≤C_r. A normalized average of such path words uses the convex combination of these measures and obeys the same bound with the averaged C_r. In physical perturbation coordinates ℓ_r H_e, Theorem 3.5 implies that its first derivative has absolute coefficient mass bounded by e^A C_r. Higher derivative tensors have absolute mass bounded by e^A times the partition polynomial in the corresponding derivative masses; derivatives of an admitted logarithmic row have, in addition, its fixed branch-margin constants. The transpose row has the identical absolute coefficient mass: this is the appropriate norm for its adjoint forcing.

For a response kernel obeying a bound C e^{−αd(x,y)} to block-supported forcing, the first row response satisfies

\[
|\text{response}_r(x)|\le C e^A
 \sum_y\lambda_r(y)e^{-\alpha d(x,y)}
\le C e^A C_r e^{-\alpha\operatorname{dist}(x,\operatorname{supp}\lambda_r)}.
                                                                    \tag{3.30}
\]

For a family of rows, define its actual weighted density ν_row(y)=Σ_r w_rλ_r(y). If sup_yν_row(y)≤C_row, (3.27) or (3.28) sums the entire family with that factor. Without this density bound, keep Σ_yν_row(y)e^{−αd(x,y)} explicitly. Nothing permits replacing it by the number of row labels near x.

This dictionary resolves the fine-prefix counting issue and applies regardless of how many new native blocks a path crosses. It leaves two genuinely geometric tests: (i) whether the joined native blocks satisfy (3.24); (ii) the actual ν_row bound and distance from the **whole adjoint support** to the protected readset. Neither follows from closeness of the row endpoint alone. A prefix entering the protected collar makes the last distance zero, even when its endpoint is far away.

**Native-unit conversion must still be retained (P).** The mass λ_r in (3.29) uses common row-scale perturbation coordinates ℓ_rH. If the propagator input on cell y is instead the native coordinate A_y=ℓ_yH, ℓ_y=L^{j(y)}η, the coefficient mass is

\[
\lambda_r^{\rm nat}(y)=\frac{\ell_r}{\ell_y}\lambda_r(y),\qquad
\nu_{\rm row}^{\rm nat}(y)=\sum_rw_r\frac{\ell_r}{\ell_y}\lambda_r(y).
                                                                    \tag{3.30a}
\]

Use λ_r^nat, not λ_r, in (3.30) when the response kernel is normalized in these native coordinates. Its unweighted total need not be bounded. For an old row of layer j_r, relative to a response anchor x this factor is exactly

\[
L^{j_r-j(y)}=L^{j_r-j(x)}L^{j(x)-j(y)}.                            \tag{3.30b}
\]

Theorem 3.9 pays the second factor with q=1 (or the appropriate fixed power for a tensor row), under its stated spacing condition. The first is an old-to-new normalization factor and remains unless the dictionary verifies j_r=j(x) or the saved norm already contains it. Thus the weighted path lemma removes a spurious *link count*; it does not remove the actual scale conversion. This distinction is essential for the saved age and coupling grades.

### Theorem 3.11. An actual mixed/live carrying sector: fully retained Wilson stencils (P/L)

Consider an original large-field history and one joint localized R integration T_z of LFR II (1.71). Select a finite family of Wilson words in links which are actual compact retained/integrated coordinates ξ of this **same joint record**, with their entire words and frames retained and with no dependence on the outer complex pair. The integration domain, selectors, and unmarked kernel are unchanged. Let F(ξ) be a finite polynomial in traces of these words and their inverses. Then:

1. F is a legitimate arbitrary carrying function in (1.73) on every complex pair in that operation's original analyticity domain. Its raw output satisfies |T_zF|≤e^η M‖F‖∞ when |σ|≤η; its normalized output satisfies Theorem 3.1. This conclusion is uniform over live histories for which the displayed kernel bound holds, without any reconstruction of a fine field or new source-wall extension.
2. If all old identity rows on the complete source stencil are among these retained coordinates, then wherever P_old exists, F(W)=F(ξ) exactly. Thus this carrying construction is a source extension with the correct original physical endpoint. It applies to a mixed history even if other components, other rows, and the outer field undergo nontrivial R preparation. It does not assert that P_old itself exists on the whole complex domain.
3. Finite source coefficients can be passed through the joint integral exactly: for exp(Σ_i t_iF_i), its coefficient of ∏_i t_i^{m_i} is T_z(∏_i F_i^{m_i})/∏_i m_i!. This is one joint integral and one unmarked mass, not a product of separately normalized masses. In particular first and second source activities, including contacts, are bounded on the **actual native pair domain**.

This class includes a concrete ordinary physical source. Take the complete incident-plaquette link stencils D(b) for every b in supp f, including all original frames, to be pinned retained rows. Set

\[
F(\xi)=r_f(\xi)=\sum_b f_b\left\{\beta\sum_{i,A}(X_b^AS_i)^2
-\tfrac13\sum_A(X_b^A)^2S\right\}.                              \tag{3.31}
\]

For the fixed original generators T_A, write d_G=N²−1 and t_G=max_A‖T_A‖op. Each matching has two plaquettes incident to b and there are six incident plaquettes in total. Direct differentiation of the four-link words gives

\[
|X_b^AS_i|\le2Nt_G,\quad
\left|\sum_A(X_b^A)^2S\right|\le6Nd_Gt_G^2,
\]

and therefore

\[
\|r_f\|_\infty\le
B_f:=d_Gt_G^2(12N^2\beta+2N)\|f\|_1.                            \tag{3.32}
\]

For every fixed number m of original link derivatives, the corresponding primitive derivative bounds are C_{N,m}(1+β)‖f‖₁, because the complete finite word stencils and all original force-product terms are differentiated. This does not delete a selector boundary distribution: it bounds the carrying function and its link derivatives; differentiating a selected kernel still differentiates the selector.

Consequently the two raw source coefficients obey

\[
|T_z r_f|\le e^\eta MB_f,\qquad
\tfrac12|T_z(r_f^2)|\le\tfrac12e^\eta MB_f^2.                    \tag{3.33}
\]

The normalized connected second row is (3.3), with B=0, and is bounded by (C₀+C₀²)B_f². This is a finite-cutoff source bound, **not** the susceptibility bound C M_f: ‖f‖₁² remains. There is no false conversion to an ℓ² estimate.

For bounded Wilson traces, B_f is independent of the cutoff. For ordinary r_f, it is polynomial in β. If a genuinely owned unused reserve has form exp[−c(log x)^p], x=g^{-2}, p>1, and β≤C x^q at the *same scale*, any fixed polynomial loss x^m can be paid once: for 0<ε<1,

\[
x^m e^{-c(\log x)^p}\le C_{m,c,p,\varepsilon}
e^{-(1-\varepsilon)c(\log x)^p}.                                \tag{3.34}
\]

This follows by taking the supremum of mt−εct^p for t=log x≥0. It works with Balaban's actual polylogarithmic rare exponent; it does not require exp(−c/g²). An old β_j is not bounded by a current x_e without proof, and no reserve is newly issued on each live step. Until the retained variables are integrated, the carrying function is unchanged, so its compact bound is used once for the joint record.

At integration/healing the function T_zF is holomorphic on the actual post-operation native pair domain, by dominated holomorphic integration. Cauchy gives each finite outer derivative on a smaller disc with its **actual** domain widths. That statement supplies a genuine family of marked mixed/live/healed coefficients. It does not identify those widths with the older physical/canonical radii or prove the old saved age for coefficients whose original variables were already integrated. Such a completed R_j is not replaced by the primitive (3.31).

### Theorem 3.12. Completion of the NM12.17 native curvature summation gate (L/P on the native domain, C for unmatched joined inputs)

The [source estimates](source_estimates/README.md), §159, printed pp.1215–1219, distinguish the nonlinear response rate δ₀/8 from an available sum at δ₀/2, and isolates (NM12.17): an unweighted native sum at some β′<δ₀/8. For the **same native geometry** (3.24)–(3.25), choose β′=δ₀/16 and impose the explicit fixed spacing condition (3.26) at α=1/16. Theorem 3.9 proves that missing geometric assertion, for all native anchors and hence for its prescribed top-scale anchors.

Here is its actual curvature-norm consequence, preserving cell volumes. Let Q_y be original assigned cells, σ_y their native lengths in current units, and |Q_y|≤νσ_y⁴, with each plaquette assigned once. Suppose an already admitted nonlinear response column, from a **top-scale reference coordinate** x, obeys the stated NM12.6 bound

\[
K(y,x)\le C |Q_y|^{1/2}\sigma_y^{-2}
 e^{-\delta_0d(y,x)/8}.                                         \tag{3.35}
\]

Then in four dimensions K(y,x)≤C√ν e^{−δ₀d(y,x)/8}. Let X_top be any subset of the top-scale input coordinates. With the fixed orientation/color multiplicities included in C, the absolute response matrix satisfies

\[
\|K\|_{\ell^2(X_{\rm top})\to\ell^2(\mathcal B)}
 \le C\sqrt\nu\,12c_0(1/32)^4,                                 \tag{3.36}
\]

and its matrix restricted to d(y,x)≥H satisfies

\[
\|1_{d\ge H}K\|_{2\to2}\le
C\sqrt\nu\,12c_0(1/32)^4 e^{-\delta_0H/16}.                     \tag{3.37}
\]

Thus the arbitrary input-count factor in NM12.10 is unnecessary for this kernel class. The geometry is **P from checked L**. This uses the original CMP102 Proposition9 and (190). On its original admitted native domain, its first covariant derivative row gives (3.35) for the relative potential curvature differential, with a fixed orientation multiplicity. For the derivative of the actual plaquette word, each tangent sum differs from that covariant curl by bounded transports and lower-order products with the admitted background. On a native regular chart the latter cost Cσ_y⁻¹ times the potential row in (190), yielding the same output weight. The local analytic exponential has bounded first differential there. Thus (3.36)–(3.37) apply to the original admitted response for top-scale target input, **L/P**. The application to an arbitrary changed joined target remains **C** on the original domain inclusion proved in §4.13 only for the stated subclasses.

For non-top-scale inputs, retain the literal derivative density σ_x^{-4} and its integration measure from the original response formula. A Hilbert-space renormalization may leave additional σ_x factors. No arbitrary fine-anchor version of the original weighted sum (162) is asserted. The source/current rows, which need more than a first curvature response, remain separate. The theorem closes a specific curvature-response summation gate, not the entire packet norm.

**Original-parameter condition.** In the CMP102 notation used in NM12.3, replace RM in (3.26) by the actual RM₁. If that originally fixed value has only been verified for α=1/2, (3.36)–(3.37) remain conditional until its stronger α=1/16 inequality is checked. One may choose a sufficiently large fixed M₁ at the beginning of a new construction and then choose the weak-field threshold afterwards, as in the usual parameter order; this does not change the proof for a previously fixed smaller M₁ retroactively. No per-history collar, selector or source-wall domain has been enlarged here.

**Proof.** Apply Lemma 2.1 with α=1/16 to bound both row and column sums of e^{−δ₀d/16}, using symmetry of d and that X_top is a subset of B. The cell-volume factor in (3.35) is at most √ν, exactly because the dimension is four. Schur's inequality then gives (3.36). For the tail, split e^{−δ₀d/8}=e^{−δ₀d/16}e^{−δ₀d/16}, bound one factor by e^{−δ₀H/16}, and apply the same two Schur sums. Every cell and its actual multiplicity remains in the computation. ∎

## 4. Proofs and substitution into the saved packet

### 4.1 Relative phase, marks, and normalization

**Proof of Theorems 3.1–3.2.** Multiply numerator and denominator by e^{-c}. If m₀=inf Re(σ−c) and m₁=sup Re(σ−c), then

\[
\operatorname{Re}(e^{-c}Z_z)=\int e^{\operatorname{Re}(\sigma-c)}
\cos(\operatorname{Im}(\sigma-c))d\nu\ge e^{m_0}\cos\theta\,M>0.
\]

The absolute numerator is at most e^{m₁}M‖F‖∞. Dividing proves (3.2), with M canceling exactly. Holomorphy follows from locally dominated integration and a nonvanishing holomorphic denominator. Adding H=tA+t²B changes the phase bound by at most η_J; the resulting integral remains in a strict half-plane after the same rotation. It has no zeros, and the disc is simply connected, proving existence of its normalized logarithm. Differentiate at t=0 to obtain (3.3). The phase argument is pointwise in z and does not require a holomorphic selection of a real reference configuration as z varies; holomorphy is a property of the original integrand.

On E, |e^H−1|≤e^{η_J}η_J. Restrict the numerator estimate to ν(E), use ν(E)≤bM, and divide by the same denominator. Differentiation at zero gives the stated first two bounds. For a different measure the identical argument requires its mass relative to M. To see necessity, take σ=0, ν=Mδ₀, a numerator measure δ₁, and F=1. Its absolute numerator bound is one but its quotient is 1/M, arbitrarily large. This is a normalization counterexample, not a Wilson-history counterexample. Finally cos η≥e^{-η} for 0≤η≤1: log cos η+η is concave and nonnegative at both endpoints. Hence e^{2η}secη≤e^{3η}. ∎

### 4.2 Constrained continuation and all response rows

**Proof of Theorem 3.3.** Define T_z(x)=x−K₀⁻¹F(x,z). The derivative bound makes it a contraction with constant ϑ on the ball. The residual bound gives

‖T_z(x)−x₀‖≤ϑ‖x−x₀‖+‖K₀⁻¹F(x₀,z)‖<R.

Iteration from x₀ converges uniformly on parameter compact sets to the unique solution. Each iterate is holomorphic, so the limit is holomorphic. The same estimate at the fixed point proves the first bound (3.7). The Neumann series for K⁻¹=[I−(I−K₀⁻¹K)]⁻¹K₀⁻¹ proves the second. The strict ball inclusion in (3.8) and the Lipschitz estimate give the announced spare margin. Only the established real uniqueness theorem identifies this local branch with the designated P_old; no compactness argument alone supplies that identification. ∎

**Proof of Theorem 3.4.** From K⁻¹K=I one has GL+PQ=I and GQ*=0; from KK⁻¹=I one has QP=I and QG=0. Differentiating F=0 with fixed q,S gives K(W_i,λ_i)=(0,v_i). This proves the first identity (3.10); subtract Γ_i and use GL+PQ=I for the second. Twice differentiating the stationarity equation yields

L W_{ij}−Q∗λ_{ij}=−B_{ij};

twice differentiating q(W)=v gives QW_{ij}=v_{ij}−D²q(W)[W_i,W_j]. Applying the upper row of K⁻¹ proves (3.11). In particular the nonlinear constraint contribution to L and both multiplier-derivative terms are necessary. With moving q,S, the corresponding first derivative includes the explicit fixed-(W,λ) derivative b_i and explicit ∂_iq; this gives (3.12). The labeled multivariate chain rule for F(y(z))=0 has one partition with a single block I, equal to KD_Ix; moving it to the other side proves (3.13). Finally differentiate h(x)U h(y)⁻¹ and hJh⁻¹ using ∂h=hθ and ∂h⁻¹=−θh⁻¹. This gives (3.14)–(3.15) and the ordered higher response. ∎

### 4.3 Full words, weighted rows, and native sums

**Proof of Theorem 3.5.** Move all fixed unitary reference factors to the right, conjugating each H_i by fixed unitary prefixes. The remaining product is the evolution of a piecewise constant matrix generator on consecutive intervals of lengths a_i. Its generator has integrated norm at most A. The usual integral equation and its inverse give ‖U‖,‖U⁻¹‖≤e^A and ‖U−1‖≤e^A−1. Differentiate the integral equation in labeled directions. Every term is indexed by an ordered partition of I into nonempty blocks, one differentiated generator for each block, between evolution propagators. Their propagator norms multiply to at most e^A. For each unordered partition, summing its possible time orders fills the product integration domain; its integral is bounded by ∏_B b_B. Summing the partitions proves (3.16). The same argument with the reversed signed generator proves the inverse bound. The analytic logarithm is only composed on its specified branch, where its finite derivatives are uniformly bounded by that branch's margin. ∎

**Proof of Proposition 3.10.** Each original link occurrence contributes its literal length a_e to (3.29). A disjoint partition changes only which cell receives that contribution, not its sum. Apply the preceding ordered-partition proof coefficientwise, with independent physical link perturbations: a derivative in a link carries a_e/ℓ_r. Taking absolute coefficient sums produces exactly the occupation masses. Weighted averages preserve them; the chain rule for an admitted logarithm introduces its bounded derivatives. Transposition does not change the sum of absolute entries of an individual row tensor. Convolving its cell occupation measure with the actual response kernel proves (3.30). Summing over r first gives precisely ν_row, so an overlap hypothesis is needed only in the stated weighted sense. ∎

**Proof of Theorem 3.6.** Split the y sum into shells n≤d(x,y)<n+1 and use (3.17), giving (3.18). The triangle inequality gives e^{αd(x,y)}≤e^{αd(x,z)}e^{αd(z,y)}; summing absolute norms proves submultiplicativity. Sum the norm-convergent Neumann series to prove (3.19). For x∈S and y in supp f, extract e^{-αR} before the weighted row sum to obtain (3.20). ∎

**Proof of Theorem 3.9.** The original geometry (3.24) ensures that each passage between successive distinct layer boundaries costs at least RM in native length. A minimal contour can be split into its first/last boundary hits as in CMP96 (2.47)–(2.48). For m transitions, split the decay evenly between transition cost and the within-layer path distances. Summing the latter over all unrestricted unit-lattice hit points gives the upper bound of its (2.58),

\[
\sum_{j'=0}^k\sum_{m\ge\max(|j-j'|-1,0)}
 e^{-\alpha\delta_0RMm/2}c_0(\alpha/2)^{d(2m+1)}.                 \tag{4.1}
\]

This is a sum over actual block representatives; a sum over all fine links without their proper native weights is a different expression. Under (3.26), the geometric series in (4.1) and the layer sum are bounded by 12c₀(α/2)^d, precisely Lemma 2.1. Substitute α=1/8 to obtain (3.27). The same contour argument gives

d(y,y′)≥RM max(|j(y)−j(y′)|−1,0).

Therefore L^{q|j−j′|}≤L^q exp[(q log L/RM)d]. Bound (1+d)^t by C_{t,δ₀}exp(δ₀d/32). The two extra weights together use at most δ₀d/16 of the δ₀d/8 available, leaving δ₀d/16. Apply Lemma 2.1 with α=1/16. This proves (3.28) with the unchanged native metric. ∎

### 4.4 Curvature and the genuinely retained mixed sector

**Proof of Propositions 3.7–3.8.** If ‖V−1‖≤1/2, the absolutely convergent series for log V gives ‖log V‖≤2‖V−1‖. Apply (2.2), including its tail, to every plaquette of D. Divide by η² and sum with weight η⁴ to obtain (3.21). A change of lattice units in each cell gives

c_Δ^p=(a/σ_Δ)^4Σ_{P∈Δ}|σ_Δ²F_P|^p=σ_Δ^{2p−4}Σ_{P∈Δ}a⁴|F_P|^p.

Summing over a disjoint partition proves (3.23), and hence (3.22). If a cover is used instead, its actual overlap constant multiplies the upper bound. Identity-row retention gives W_e=Γ_e on every pinned oriented link, including inverses. Substitution into complete plaquette words proves the pinned claim. Differentiating that identity gives all jets; it does not mean that one may apply a nonlinear determining map linearly to its tangent. The current reads more plaquettes, so its identity requires the larger stated stencil. ∎

**Proof of Theorem 3.11.** On SU(N), |Tr U_γ|≤N, and inverse words remain unitary. Every polynomial in finitely many such traces has a finite explicit compact bound. Its values are functions of ξ only on the chosen joint record; changing the outer complex pair changes the kernel, not those values. LFR II (1.73) consequently applies with its literal arbitrary F. The phase proof above yields the normalized bound. The original identity constraints prove endpoint equality with the physical readout wherever that readout is defined. Expanding the source exponential to a fixed finite degree commutes with the finite joint integral by boundedness. This proves the source and contact coefficient formula, with the original factorials and one mass.

For r_f, a first left derivative inserts T_A once into an incident plaquette word; absolute trace is ≤Nt_G. Two incident plaquettes per matching give the first bound (3.32). A second derivative on the same bond inserts T_A² into the word, giving ≤Nt_G² per incident plaquette. Sum over six plaquettes and d_G generators. The squares of the three matching forces supply 3d_G(2Nt_G)². These are exactly the two terms in (3.32). Any fixed ordered further derivative inserts a fixed finite number of generators into these finite words and force products; the number of terms depends on m,N and the fixed stencil, not the lattice volume. This proves the primitive derivative bound. The mass inequalities (3.33) follow immediately. For (3.34), the continuous function mt−εct^p tends to −∞ as t→∞ when p>1, so it has finite supremum. Finally a bounded carrying function times the original locally dominated holomorphic kernel has a holomorphic integral. No claim has been made about the domain of an older, already integrated coefficient. ∎

### 4.5 Exact substitution into LM47 and the saved packet

The following algebra gives a checkable route from the response estimates to (1.2); it does not replace their verification. Let Ψ(z)=(W(z),J_phys(W(z))) be one common admitted map for the entire completed packet, including lower/contact rows. On that domain, for every labeled finite derivative set I,

\[
D_I(R_j\circ\Psi)=
\sum_{\pi\in\operatorname{Part}(I)}
D^{|\pi|}R_j(\Psi)[D_B\Psi:B\in\pi].                            \tag{4.2}
\]

Every D_BΨ has both a field and a current component. Expand each slot accordingly; the resulting sum includes all current chain-rule terms. For I=∅ the expression is R_j(Ψ), so the changed base cannot disappear by estimating only derivatives.

**Absolute-anchor composition lemma (P).** Let kernels be indexed by native blocks with their original measures and scaling factors. Suppose the stored norm is an absolute kernel sum with weight w, and the weights for a substituted kernel obey

\[
w(x;\mathbf u)\le w_R(x;\mathbf y)
 \prod_{B\in\pi}w_H(y_B;\mathbf u_B).                            \tag{4.3}
\]

If the absolute outer-kernel sum is bounded by b_{j,|π|} and each response kernel by h_B, the π contribution to that norm is bounded by b_{j,|π|}∏_B h_B. To prove this, expand the contraction as the sum over intermediate y_B, take absolute values, use (4.3), and sum the input variables for each block first. Tonelli applies to the nonnegative sums, and the remaining sum is exactly the outer norm. Exponential tree weights satisfy (4.3), because a tree joining x to the intermediate anchors together with trees joining each intermediate anchor to its input variables is an admissible joining network. Fixed polynomial tree moments satisfy it too, up to their explicitly retained finite constants, using 1+Σ_i r_i≤∏_i(1+r_i). Native summation is supplied by Theorem 3.9 where its geometry applies. This is an absolute coefficient statement, not an inference from an unweighted multilinear operator norm.

Thus a concrete sufficient bound for the actual stored packet is

\[
\mathcal N_I[R_j\circ\Psi]\le
\sum_{\pi\in\operatorname{Part}(I)}
\sum_{\text{field/current slot types}}b_{j,\pi}\prod_{B\in\pi}h_{B,\text{type}}.
                                                                    \tag{4.4}
\]

To deliver (1.2), each term in (4.4) must be charged to its *existing* a_{I,γ;j,e}Q^{D_{I,γ}} with the original scale measures, lowered coupling grade, saved age, source factorial and anchor moment. Equations (3.10)–(3.13), (3.29)–(3.30), and (3.28) give the explicit response kernels and the sums to estimate. Missing a power of r or g_e cannot be repaired by renaming b or shrinking the prescribed wall domain.

For coupling grades, a useful precise criterion is holomorphic dependence of Ψ on the physical variable w=g_e z on an unchanged ball of physical radius δ, with a uniform first-derivative bound there. Cauchy on a fixed smaller ball then gives, for each fixed m,

\[
\|D_z^m\Psi(g_ez)\|\le C_m g_e^m\delta^{1-m}\sup\|D_w\Psi\|.       \tag{4.5}
\]

For a coordinate multi-derivative this follows by Cauchy on a polydisc inside the physical ball; for multilinear derivatives polarization changes only C_m. The original δ factors are retained. In contrast a bound D_zΨ=O(g_e) on a fixed canonical ball permits Ψ(z)=g_e(z+z²) and gives no O(g_e²) bound for D_z²Ψ. The saved physical ball, not mere native analyticity, supplies the full grade.

Theorems 3.3 and 3.4 therefore provide a constructive local LM47 gate: first estimate (3.9) and the inverse perturbation on the whole prescribed wall domain; then verify the paired saved-radius inequality including current and frames; then insert the resulting absolute response kernels into (4.4). The original geometry resolves (3.27) but has not yet resolved every joined-row ν_row and base residual. Consequently (4.4) is a proved algebraic reduction, while its all-history Wilson bound is **O**.

### 4.6 Constructive old-minimum correction at the first actual join

The source for this paragraph is LFR II printed pp.373–375, (1.61)–(1.65). Its first determining set is B″_k(Y₁)=B(Y₁)∪B″_k; Y₁ is the smallest localization domain containing X and the Z-components meeting X. Old and new sets coincide on Y₁⁰, and Y₁∖Y₁⁰ is a boundary layer of width at most 2M₁ in the corresponding native scales. The relative gauge u_k is expressly retained because two different representations meet at that boundary. Thus on genuinely coincident interior rows the anchor-scale mismatch in (3.30b) is absent. It is the *artificial boundary rows and the old prefixes reaching them* that need the remaining comparison. Minimality of Y₁ does not imply positive distance between X and that layer.

**Proposition 4.1 (P; changed-base residual with exact shared-row cancellation).** In one actual common gauge-fixed holomorphic chart, let Γ be a known new constrained critical point,

∇S_n(Γ)=Dq_n(Γ)*λ_n.

Split old and new constraints into literally identical shared rows q_s and remaining rows q_o′,q_n′, retaining their physical weights by absorbing them into the dual multipliers. Put λ_o=(λ_{n,s},0) in the old dual space, and v_o=q_o(Γ). Then the residual of the old KKT equations at (Γ,λ_o) is exactly

\[
\mathscr F_o(\Gamma,\lambda_o;v_o)=
\begin{pmatrix}\nabla(S_o-S_n)(\Gamma)+Dq_n'(\Gamma)^*\lambda_{n}'\\0\end{pmatrix}.
                                                                    \tag{4.6}
\]

If instead the apparent shared rows are related by a field-dependent frame matrix T(A), so q_n(A)=T(A)q_o(A)+e(A), choose λ_o=T(Γ)*λ_n. The first residual is

\[
r_o=\nabla(S_o-S_n)(\Gamma)+De(\Gamma)^*\lambda_n+
\big(DT(\Gamma)[\cdot]\,q_o(\Gamma)\big)^*\lambda_n.              \tag{4.7}
\]

In particular a frame derivative is not absorbed into the phrase “shared row.” If all row weights are physical, q_c=r_c⁻¹log(target word), and the pairing is Σ_c r_c⁴⟨λ_c,q_c⟩ as in the convention of the stored-record construction, the algebraic multiplier in (4.6) is r_c⁴λ_c. It may not be replaced by λ_c; the derivative of q_c contributes its additional r_c⁻¹. The naturally scaled multiplier is r_c³λ_c.

Let K_o be the derivative of the old KKT map at this approximate solution. If its inverse exists and the residual/Hessian bounds (3.6) hold with this center and old target v_o, the contraction proof constructs the old holomorphic solution. The center need not already solve the old problem: inspection of the proof of Theorem 3.3 shows that no such property is used. The same proof works for a holomorphic moving center Γ(z) and holomorphic K_o(z)⁻¹ after writing x=Γ(z)+y and using uniform bounds. Hence one can construct the old branch from the known joined new branch, rather than presuppose old admission before using a response formula.

If (4.6)'s full adjoint forcing is supported in E, and the inverse and nonlinear Lipschitz estimates hold in the norm weighted by e^{α dist(·,E)}, the solved correction at a readset S is bounded by

\[
\|W-\Gamma\|_S\le
\frac{\|K_o^{-1}(r_o,0)\|_{\alpha,E}}{1-\vartheta}
e^{-\alpha\operatorname{dist}(S,E)}.                            \tag{4.8}
\]

Here E is the union of full path, prefix, endpoint-frame, scalar and identity-row adjoint supports, together with any changed action stencil. It is not merely the set of boundary row anchors. The native estimates of §§3.9–3.10 provide an explicit way to estimate the weighted numerator when those supports have the original admissible geometry.

**Proof.** Subtract the old and new stationary equations with the stated multiplier choices. Identical shared rows cancel exactly. For (4.7), differentiate T(A)q_o(A) as a product; the DT term remains. The lower residual vanishes because the old target is q_o(Γ). Apply the contraction proof to this residual. The weighted conclusion follows by proving the contraction in that weighted norm and then removing the weight on S. ∎

**What this does and does not settle.** Equation (4.8) controls W−Γ, including the changed-base forcing, provided its quantitative tests are verified. To compare to the last admitted old field W_before one must also bound Γ−W_before. The latter contains the zero-fluctuation joined base and the re-averaged boundary trace. One may apply the same criterion to the new KKT problem centered at W_before; its lower residual is now the nonzero actual target defect q_n(W_before)−v_n. It is not erased by setting the internal fluctuation to zero. Therefore a small H_k correction in (1.61) alone cannot establish the saved-radius comparison.

### 4.7 A verified row subcase and the remaining anchor mismatch

**Lemma 4.2 (P).** Suppose all paths and prefix words of a family of rows lie in one regular native layer of length h; their anchors lie on its h-lattice with at most n₀ row types per site; every complete word stays within distance C h of its anchor and has total length at most C h. Allow normalized averages over arbitrarily many such paths. Then λ_r^nat=λ_r and

\[
\sup_y\sum_r\lambda_r^{\rm nat}(y)
\le n_0(2\lceil C\rceil+3)^4 C.                                \tag{4.9}
\]

Indeed only anchors within the indicated finite h-lattice neighborhood can contribute to cell y, and each row has total occupation mass ≤C. Normalized averaging preserves the bound. This covers ordinary block rows and their complete finite within-block frames when they remain in a single coincident layer of the first join. It proves an actual joined-interior dictionary; it does not cover an artificial row connecting a coarse old block to arbitrarily finer new layers.

The remaining scale question can now be stated without an artificial link-count obstruction. For the actual artificial boundary rows in (1.61)–(1.63), estimate

\[
\sup_{x\in S_{\rm old}}\sum_{r,y}w_r
L^{j_r-j(y)}\lambda_r(y)e^{-\delta_0d(x,y)/8},                    \tag{4.10}
\]

where w_r is the **actual** multiplier/source/anchor weight of the stored row, including its physical r_c⁴ pairing and r_c⁻¹ logarithmic normalization where present. The factor L^{j(x)−j(y)} can be summed by (3.28); the residual L^{j_r−j(x)} is harmless when j_r≤j(x), and absent for coincident rows. If j_r>j(x), either the actual w_r cancels it, or the full support must have a native separation which pays it, or a new boundary estimate is required. The saved scalar envelope (2.1) alone contains no permission to discard it. This is the precise old/native anchor mismatch left after the recovered counting lemma.

### 4.8 A quantitative first-join case with one unmatched row

The following settles the multiplier and readmission problem for a restricted but concrete class, rather than assuming a multiplier estimate. Its constants depend on a fixed bound n₀ on native depth; it is not a depth-uniform solution of I-2.

**Geometric class.** Work in a contractible rectangular record of current diameter at most C_D, with actual underlying microscopic spacing in current units η≥L⁻ⁿ⁰. Thus the underlying fine-grid depth, not merely the number of retained native layers, is bounded by n₀. Fix its literal exterior links to the identity, or to a sufficiently small specified perturbation in a boundary-fixed frame. All determining rows are the original finite identity/averaging/path rows, with their complete prefixes. Old and new records have the same fixed exterior and a declared nonredundant target quotient; their common rows are Q, and there is one additional or replaced independent scalar/color row r after separating the common rows. There is no periodic toron. The complete record, including its original holomorphic wall formulas, lies in the LFR native regularity range; this last condition refers to the actual field on every cell in this record, not only the exterior of a rough hole. The original real cutoff and fixed localization-wall radius are unchanged. Such a record can be adjacent to, or carry, a live component; the restriction is on the complete prepared record being estimated.

The bound on native depth makes the number n of gauge-fixed algebra coordinates, the number of row factors, and the number of original word occurrences bounded by constants depending only on N,L,M,C_D,n₀. A rooted forest fixing only interior gauges leaves the exterior fixed. At the flat field, zero curl plus the pinned exterior implies a pure gradient, and the forest gauge removes that gradient. Thus the Wilson Hessian H is strictly positive in these coordinates. Write H_max for an explicit row-sum upper bound on H; it is bounded by a fixed multiple of the number of plaquettes meeting a link and the trace metric. No continuum spectral-gap assumption enters this finite-block fact.

**Lemma 4.3 (P; exact unmatched-row multiplier).** In the quadratic problem with shared targets zero, let V=ker Q and let G be the inverse of H restricted to V, extended by zero on its orthogonal complement. For the additional target rA=θ, with r independent modulo Q,

\[
s_r=rGr^*>0,\qquad
A_\theta=Gr^*\theta/s_r,\qquad \lambda_r=\theta/s_r.              \tag{4.11}
\]

Let the flat row coefficients have common denominator D and absolute value at most B, after the fixed physical row factors have been included. This is the case for the original flat identity/normalized block-average rows on the finite rational lattice; color indices factor as identities. If Q has m independent scalar rows, then

\[
s_r\ge H_{\max}^{-1}
D^{-2(m+1)}(nB^2)^{-m}.                                         \tag{4.12}
\]

For m=0 the last factor is one. The estimate is deliberately crude, but positive and independent of the original ultraviolet cutoff inside this fixed-depth class.

**Proof.** Minimize the positive quadratic form on V with one constraint. Lagrange multiplication gives A=Gr*λ, so rA=s_rλ=θ. Positivity and independence imply s_r>0. Let Π_V denote orthogonal projection. Since H≤H_max I, s_r≥H_max⁻¹‖Π_Vr*‖². The squared distance of r from the row space of Q is the Gram determinant quotient

det([Q;r][Q;r]*)/det(QQ*).

At least one full-rank minor of the integer matrix D[Q;r] has nonzero integer determinant, so Cauchy–Binet bounds the numerator below by D^{-2(m+1)}. Hadamard bounds the denominator by (nB²)^m. This proves (4.12). The entire force is r∗λ, on the literal complete adjoint support of r. No distance to the source has been inserted. ∎

For nonzero shared target v, first take its shared constrained minimum A_v. Replace θ by θ−rA_v in (4.11) and add A_v. This explicitly retains the nonzero joined base. If a row is replaced, compute both old and new minima relative to the same shared problem and use their two Gram factors. The old reconstruction is determined by its actual read target r_oldΓ, not by setting that target to zero.

**Proposition 4.4 (P; nonlinear Wilson continuation for this class).** There are constants ε_*>0 and C_*<∞ depending only on the displayed fixed geometry, N,L,M,n₀ and the original row conventions such that the old and new Wilson minima are holomorphic for all their actual boundary/target coordinates of magnitude <ε_*. Their unmatched multipliers and fields satisfy

\[
\|\lambda_{\rm unmatched}\|+\|A\|+
\|J_{\rm phys}(A)\|\le C_*\|\text{complete boundary/target data}\|.
                                                                    \tag{4.13}
\]

In particular the extra multiplier generated by the additional nonlinear row q_r is bounded by C_*|θ−q_r(A_v)| with all shared data held fixed, plus its explicit shared-data derivative terms for mixed variations. The zero-data value is zero. All fixed-order derivatives obey the corresponding Cauchy bounds on a fixed smaller *physical* ball. The same is true for complete path/frame derivatives and absolute kernel sums, because there are only boundedly many coordinates and original word occurrences.

**Proof.** The flat augmented Hessians for the old, new and shared problems are invertible: H is positive and each declared row quotient is nonredundant. Equation (4.12) controls the new Schur factor explicitly; the remaining finite blocks are controlled by their positive smallest eigenvalues. There are only finitely many original determining geometries at the prescribed sizes and depth, so their maximum inverse norm K_* is finite. All holomorphic Wilson and original logarithmic averaging derivatives on a fixed near-identity link ball are bounded by a finite C₂; this follows directly from their finite words, normalized finite sums and logarithms on their principal branches. Choose a ball with K_* C₂ε_*<1/4, and decrease ε_* to keep every original log branch valid. Theorem3.3's contraction now applies simultaneously to both problems with their full target data. Its first-order residual is bounded by C times those data, and gives (4.13). The implicit solution at zero data is the flat field with zero multiplier. Differentiating on the fixed physical ball gives all finite derivative bounds, including the native scaling factors already incorporated in the rows. No source-locality hypothesis is needed to bound the multiplier even when its support touches the packet. ∎

**Application to the literal cutoff and saved radius in this bounded-depth case (P/C).** The finite native scale range converts LFR's own-scale regularity bounds on this entire record into a common finite-lattice bound C_*ε_e. The rooted forest gauge is holomorphic (finite products of link matrices); with the boundary fixed, its non-tree links are products of finitely many plaquette words and prescribed small boundary words. Their logarithms exist for ε_e small. Their inverse and all required derivatives are bounded by Theorem3.5. Finite lattice differences on this record cost at most fixed powers of L^{n₀}; hence its common W^{1,p}, curvature and current bounds are C_*ε_e. This is a genuine boundary-compatible gauge construction in this class, rather than curvature alone being called a gauge theorem.

For the original real ceiling ε_e≤C g_e(log x_e)^{p₀}, x_e=g_e⁻², this gives t≤C_*g_e(log x_e)^{p₀} on the entire real cutoff with all fixed-radius localization walls included; their finite analytic amplification is part of C_*. The saved completed-R radius recorded in M8.16 is ρ_j=c_jg_j(log x_j)^q, q>p₀, c_j bounded above and below. For birth/entry gaps bounded by n₀, the flow bound of I-0c gives x_j/x_e=1+O(n₀/x_e), so

\[
\frac{C_*t}{\rho_j}\le C_{*,n_0}(\log x_e)^{p_0-q}\longrightarrow0. \tag{4.14}
\]

Thus all stored finite paired-radius conditions in this fixed-depth class fit with a strict spare margin for one sufficiently weak coupling threshold. The larger original complex ball cρ_e fits by the same finite-dimensional bound provided its *once-chosen original coefficient* c obeys c<C_*⁻¹ times the specified spare fraction. This is an explicit original-domain parameter check; it does not replace a larger already specified c by a smaller one after the fact. General saved radii other than M8.16 must be compared to their actual values instead.

On this admitted finite record, (4.2)–(4.4) prove the stored finite source/jet/absolute-anchor bounds from the stored R_j bounds, with constants depending on n₀ and the fixed derivative order. In physical canonical coordinates every mth response has g_e^m, by (4.5); all old source coefficients and radius factors remain explicit. Since the birth/entry gap is bounded, an originally saved geometric age can be kept with a fixed n₀-dependent comparison constant. This is a **bounded-depth marked admission result**, not the cutoff/depth-uniform I-2 required for arbitrary histories.

The obstruction to extending this proof is now quantitative. The crude D,n,K_* in (4.12) grow with n₀; taking n₀ equal to the entire ultraviolet history would destroy uniformity. The recovered native Schur sums control long-scale propagation only after the actual joined row normalization and physical multiplier norm have been matched. The remaining job is to replace this finite-matrix lower bound by the original mixed typed inverse on the artificial boundary rows while proving (4.10). A speculative rough-field counterexample is neither used nor needed.

### 4.9 The actual one-row defect cancels its small Schur denominator

The preceding finite-matrix bound is not the right estimate when rows nearly coincide. The actual target is a readout of a retained field, so its numerator is constrained by the same row as its denominator. This gives a depth-uniform result in the original energy norm.

**Theorem 4.5 (P; actual-target one-row energy bound).** Let H be any positive gauge-fixed quadratic Wilson/Maxwell Hessian on any finite lattice. Let Q contain the exact shared original rows, A_v its constrained minimum at target v, and let Γ be the actual retained/prepared field with QΓ=v. Set

\[
u=\Gamma-A_v,\quad \theta=r\Gamma-rA_v=ru,\quad
s_r=rGr^*,\quad \delta A=Gr^*\theta/s_r.                         \tag{4.15}
\]

Then, independently of the dimension, depth and size of s_r,

\[
\frac{|\theta|^2}{s_r}\le\|u\|_H^2,\qquad
\|\delta A\|_H\le\|u\|_H,\qquad
\|\Pi_{\ker Q}r^*\lambda_r\|_{H|_{\ker Q}^{-1}}
=|\lambda_r|\sqrt{s_r}=\frac{|\theta|}{\sqrt{s_r}}
\le\|u\|_H.                                                     \tag{4.16}
\]

The same bounds hold for complex u,θ with the Hermitian energy norm and the real original Q,r,H. No separation of the full adjoint support from the packet is needed for these energy inequalities. If s_r tends to zero because r approaches an old shared row, neither the actual correction energy nor the physical projected adjoint force is forced to blow up. The raw coordinate multiplier λ_r may grow; it is the wrong standalone norm for this cancellation.

**Proof.** Since Qu=0, the Riesz representative in the H inner product of u↦ru is Gr*. Cauchy–Schwarz gives |ru|≤√(rGr*)‖u‖H. Substitution in (4.11) gives (4.16). Equivalently u itself is a compatible trial correction, and δA is its H-orthogonal projection onto the one-dimensional representer. This uses the actual θ=ru, not an arbitrary independent target. ∎

For the same homogeneous action, any old reconstruction P_oq_o is the H-orthogonal projection onto (ker q_o)^{⊥H}. Consequently every composition of these *physical quadratic reconstruction maps* has energy operator norm at most one, even if the determining records differ. This statement keeps each original row map and its full adjoints. It does not apply to a replacement action, a changed prescribed exterior without its affine shift, or an independently weakened kernel that is not that projector.

**Corollary 4.6 (P; complete boundary shifts retained).** If Γ is the minimizer for the new record, while W=P_oq_oΓ and A_v is the shared-row minimum, then

‖W−A_v‖H≤‖Γ−A_v‖H.

Indeed on the affine shared fiber the old reconstruction is the corresponding affine energy projection. This includes a replaced row by taking shared Q first. If the exterior/action is changed, its actual affine lift and action-defect forcing must be added before applying the projection inequality; they are not included in θ by fiat.

**Proposition 4.7 (P; commuting Wilson extension on an admitted convex chart).** In the commuting Cartan sector, with fixed original logarithmic branches, the original logarithmic path averages are linear in the link angles. On a real regular branch where all root plaquette angles have modulus at most ε<π/2, the Wilson Hessian on the shared fiber obeys

cos ε·H₀≤S″(A)≤H₀,

where H₀ is its flat Maxwell Hessian. Let A_v and A_θ be the actual interior constrained minima at the shared and enlarged targets, and suppose the straight segments used below stay in that same convex chart. For the actual trial u=Γ−A_v, with Qu=0 and ru=θ,

\[
\|A_\theta-A_v\|_{H_0}^2\le(\cos\epsilon)^{-1}\|u\|_{H_0}^2.
                                                                    \tag{4.17}
\]

This is again depth uniform and contains no reciprocal small row Gram factor. It follows because A_θ minimizes over a fiber containing Γ, while the Bregman action difference from A_v is between (cos ε)/2 and 1/2 times the H₀ norm squared. The linear term vanishes on the shared fiber by its exact KKT equation.

There is an analogous complex comparison on any *already existing* critical branch for which the integrated Hessian K between A_v and A_θ satisfies Re⟨w,Kw⟩≥μ‖w‖H₀² and |⟨v,Kw⟩|≤M‖v‖H₀‖w‖H₀. Put δ=A_θ−A_v. Stationarity gives Kδ in the span of the original shared and added adjoints; hence ⟨δ−u,Kδ⟩=0. Therefore

\[
\|\delta\|_{H_0}\le(M/\mu)\|u\|_{H_0}.                        \tag{4.18}
\]

The comparison is proved, but it does not construct that complex branch or give an L^p curvature bound with p>8. In particular energy control cannot be substituted for AE8. The actual unresolved estimate is now the stronger norm of the compatible trial u (including its joined base and exterior data), or a smoothing estimate for its projected correction. No lower bound on s_r alone is needed for the energy part.

**Why finite depth is still only a subcase.** The first R-operation in LFR I uses N preliminary steps with N growing as a positive power of log g⁻². Thus a fixed microscopic-depth restriction cannot automatically describe a continuum-uniform sequence of completed R joins. The fixed-depth result in §4.8 controls its declared early/local geometries and finite-cutoff joins; it is not claimed to have a coupling range compatible with N≤n₀ for arbitrarily small g. The depth-uniform cancellation (4.16), rather than enlarging the finite Gram-determinant constants, is the useful step toward the true all-history problem.

### 4.10 Native multiplier scales and the actual flat current

The source supplies more than an abstract energy estimate, but less than a general second-derivative estimate. Here is the precise usable statement. Put σ_y=L^jη for y in the jth native layer. Kernel conventions include the native integration weight σ_{y'}^d. CMP99 (3.132), printed p.422, gives

\[
 |(QGQ^*)^{-1}(y,y')|\le C\sigma_y^{-2}\sigma_{y'}^{-d}
 e^{-\delta_0d(y,y')}.                                      \tag{4.19}
\]

The same bound holds with G₁. The accompanying (3.133) bounds H=GQ*(QGQ*)⁻¹ and its first covariant derivative. Theorem3.12, p.423, explicitly **excludes** the covariant Laplacian bound for G and G₁. It must not be cited as a physical-current estimate.

**Proposition 4.8 (P from L; one original native target).** On the precise regular hierarchy and background domain of CMP99 Theorem3.12, give one original row r the dimensionless target θ and all other linearized targets zero. In the source convention B_r=θ/σ_r. With ω=(QGQ*)⁻¹B and A=HB,

\[
 \sigma_y^3|\omega_y|\le C(\sigma_y/\sigma_r)|\theta|
 e^{-\delta_0d(y,r)},\qquad
 \sigma_y|A|_{\Delta(y)}+\sigma_y^2|\nabla A|_{\Delta(y)}
 \le C(\sigma_y/\sigma_r)|\theta|e^{-\delta_0d(y,r)/2}.
                                                               \tag{4.20}
\]

The physical constrained multiplier is λ=ω−aB, not ω: the added quadratic term Q∗aQ in the source inverse is subtracted from the constrained action. Here a_y is the source coefficient of order σ_y⁻². Its diagonal contribution obeys the same scaled bound. Under the original spacing test of Theorem3.9, the scale ratio in (4.20) is absorbed by a fixed part of the exponential. Arbitrary simultaneous dimensionless row data bounded by ε therefore give the uniform native bounds Cε, without a row count or a smallest-Gram-eigenvalue factor.

**Proof.** The integration weight σ_r^d cancels the factor σ_r⁻ᵈ in (4.19); B_r supplies σ_r⁻¹. Multiplication by σ_y³ leaves exactly σ_y/σ_r. The same cancellation in (3.133) gives the other two estimates. The subtraction follows directly from the source Euler equation (3.122). Native scale separation and Theorem3.9, with a fixed decay reserve, control both the ratio and the sum over r. This proves the claim on the source's stated domain; it is not a theorem about an extra overlapping row appended to that hierarchy. ∎

**Proposition 4.9 (P/L; depth-uniform flat current on an original hierarchy).** Take the flat background, the complete original mixed determining hierarchy of CMP96 (2.1)–(2.4), and its original linear averaging rows. Assume RM>4L, in addition to the fixed summability test already displayed. The source minimal linear reconstruction satisfies

\[
 \sup_y\left(\sigma_y|A|_{\Delta(y)}+
 \sigma_y^2|\nabla A|_{\Delta(y)}+
 \sigma_y^3|d^*dA|_{\Delta(y)}\right)
 \le C\sup_r|\theta_r|.                                    \tag{4.21}
\]

For the current term, the supremum over Δ(y) means fine bonds in the native cell, with boundary bonds assigned to an incident native cell; a fixed enlargement or another incident assignment changes C only. The constant depends on d,L and the once-fixed separation and source constants, not on η, the number of layers or the number of targets. A one-row target retains an exponential native tail with any fixed smaller decay rate allowed by the scale comparison.

**Proof of the additional current assertion.** At the flat background the source gauge-invariant extension Δ_π is exactly d∗d. The gauge condition RD*A=0 removes the DRD* term. Hence the exact Euler equation, with no weakened factors, is

\[
                         d^*dA=Q^*\lambda.                  \tag{4.22}
\]

The flat averaging formula is CMP95 (1.18), obtained by composing its (1.11). If n=σ_r/η, its coefficient is n⁻ᵈ⁻¹ times the sum along each of nᵈ parallel translated paths of length n. For a given fine bond and a fixed coarse row, at most n translated paths contain that bond. Passing to the adjoint multiplies by the measure ratio nᵈ. The resulting nonnegative incidence coefficient is therefore at most one. At a fixed scale only a bounded number, depending on d, of coarse rows have paths containing that fine bond. This calculation uses the original full straight paths; no path segment has been discarded.

It remains to check mixed scales. A row of scale σ_r has its support within 2σ_r of its native cell. If this support meets a fine bond assigned native scale σ_x, the nested-buffer condition implies

\[
                 L^{-1}\sigma_r\le\sigma_x\le L\sigma_r.
                                                               \tag{4.23}
\]

Indeed, reaching a cell at least two layers finer would cross the buffer between Ω_r and Ω_{r−1}^c, of width greater than RMσ_r/L>4σ_r; reaching at least two layers coarser would cross the buffer inside Ω_{r+1}, of width greater than RMσ_{r+1}. Both contradict the support radius. The same argument applies to boundary bonds using their incident cells, allowing one fine bond of slack, covered by the strict displayed margin. There are consequently at most three candidate scales and a bounded number of rows at each. Equations (4.20), (4.22) and the adjoint incidence bound prove the current estimate. For a single input row, the intermediate row centres are a bounded native distance from x, so the exponential tail follows by the triangle inequality and the scale-ratio bound. ∎

This settles the complete physical current of the linear reconstruction at the flat background, at arbitrary native depth. It also shows why a small Schur denominator for an arbitrarily appended row is not the correct native obstruction: the complete hierarchy inverse already controls the physical multiplier. It does **not** supply the missing nonlinear current response on the full joined complex-wall domain. At a nonflat background the extension Δ_π contains the gauge-restoring terms in CMP99 (3.119)–(3.120), and the averaging-Hessian correction appears in (3.127)–(3.138). Their physical current must be reconstructed from the exact original KKT identities before localization; separately weakening those factors does not preserve (4.22).

### 4.11 The literal first joined hierarchy

**Source construction (L).** CMP119, printed pp.255–257, (2.2), (2.10)–(2.15), give the missing construction. Let B be the record with native layers Γ₀=Ω₁ᶜ, Γ_h=Ω_h⁽ʰ⁾∖Ω_{h+1}⁽ʰ⁾ (0<h<k), and Γ_k=Ω_k⁽ᵏ⁾. The averaging row on Γ_h is Mʰ, including the identity M⁰. For a localization domain Y whose boundary is in the native layer j, form its minimal record B_j(Y) as follows. In units of σ_j use microscopic mesh ξ=L⁻ʲ. Set D₀=Y. Successively take the maximal D_h inside D_{h−1} formed from LʰξM₁ cubes and separated from D_{h−1}ᶜ by at least LʰξM₁, for 1≤h≤j. The source states Y^{~−2}⊂D_j. Write Γ_h^D for the resulting (2.2) layers, with an empty layer for h>j.
