Put I=Y^{~−2} and C=Y∖I, using the M₁ cubes specified by the source. Then (2.14) says precisely

\[
 B\mathbin{\cup}B_j(Y)=(B\cap I)\cup(B_j(Y)\cap C),\qquad
 \Gamma_h^{\rm joined}=(\Gamma_h\cap I)\cup(\Gamma_h^D\cap C).
                                                               \tag{4.24}
\]

These equalities apply to points, bonds and plaquettes using the original incidence convention. CMP109 §0, printed p.251, assigns a bond or plaquette to a continuous domain when it intersects that domain. Thus a boundary-crossing bond can meet both masks. Set union counts an identical pair (h,b) once. Different-scales paths may use a common fine link. Each such path retains its own physical support and scale. CMP119 prescribes restriction to the individual boundary layers j and summation of the RHS when ∂Y meets several layers. Outside Y the corresponding localized problem has its source exterior conditions.

**Proposition 4.10 (L/P, enumeration at the first R join).** In LFR II (1.61) take Y=Y₁ and B=B″_k. On I the retained rows are precisely Mʰ on Γ″_h∩I. For a boundary piece in old layer j the collar consists of the identity rows on Γ₀^D∩C, the rows Mʰ on Γ_h^D∩C for 0<h<j, and rows Mʲ on D_j⁽ʲ⁾∩C. For h>j the only possible rows on that piece come from Γ″_h∩I. Apply the union convention in (4.24) at the seam. The corresponding field values, including the old relative gauge on the collar, are those in (1.61). Thus each artificial boundary constraint is a row of the minimal determining hierarchy. The operation replaces the old collar constraints. The one-row Schur comparison in §4.9 can still be needed when comparing to a *distinct saved packet record*. It is unnecessary merely to define the joined minimum.

**Proof.** CMP119 (2.11) assigns Mʰ independently on each layer of (2.2). Substitution of these layers in (2.14) gives (4.24) for each (h,b), including the terminal jth layer D_j⁽ʲ⁾. Formula (1.61) applies this exact construction and prescribes the target values. A target's retained frame changes the value and its derivative, while its native bond and depth follow directly from (4.24). The coincidence region Y₁⁰ can extend into the collar wherever the records agree. The guaranteed core I suffices for the enumeration. ∎

**Proposition 4.11 (P, a mixed first join with boundary in the top layer).** Suppose the first localization boundary lies in Γ″_k, and the two-layer collar belongs to the old top layer. The interior may contain any number of old native layers and the Z-components met by X. For site domains, the joined hth retained region is

\[
             \Omega'_h=\Omega_h''\cap D_h,\qquad 1\le h\le k.
                                                               \tag{4.25}
\]

Indeed D_h contains I for h≤k. On C the old Ω_h″ contains C because the old layer is k. Thus (4.25) reproduces the two pieces of (4.24); bond and plaquette rows are selected by (4.24) itself. For h<k its spacing obeys

\[
 \sigma_h^{-1}\operatorname{dist}(\Omega'_{h+1},(\Omega'_h)^c)
 \ge\min\{b_{\rm old},LM_1\},                                \tag{4.26}
\]

where b_old is the actual old spacing in these units. This follows from the union formula for the complement of an intersection and the two original buffer inequalities. The exterior Dirichlet boundary has its prescribed source condition. Once the fixed number on the RHS passes the summability and support-radius tests of §§3.9,4.10, the complete flat reconstruction estimate (4.21) holds for this actual spliced record at all depths. Old mixed layers and live components inside I remain in the estimate. The fixed parameter test is retained.

For a single boundary piece j<k the intersection argument applies through h=j. An additional spacing to explicitly check is the distance of retained Ω″_{j+1}∩I from D_jᶜ, in units σ_j. The literal formula gives the needed seam quantity even when more than two old layers occur inside I. If Y and the original Ω″_{j+1} are unions of compatible cubes of side at least Mσ_{j+1}, separated boundary faces have distance at least Mσ_{j+1}. The retreat of D_j from ∂Y is at most two native M₁-cube layers, or 2dM₁σ_j in the ℓ¹ metric. That seam spacing is then at least LM−2dM₁. On multi-piece boundaries one must apply the same incidences to each piece and its junctions. The primary (2.14) settles row membership on all these pieces. This last numerical spacing check is stated with its actual grid hypotheses, distinct from the weaker 2M₁ localization eligibility test alone.

### 4.12 A nonlinear current estimate from the Wilson first variation

The physical current of a constrained critical field can be bounded using a right inverse of the actual first constraint derivative. This avoids estimating second derivatives of the reconstructed potential. Let R_y be the native cell at y, and use the d=4 native units above. Suppose a regular critical field W on the complete record obeys |1−W(∂p)|≤ε_F η²/σ_y² for its fine plaquettes. Use the equivalent inverse-plaquette bound when W is complex. Keep the fixed exterior in every trial. Denote the derivative of its physical row, in potential units, by Q_s(W), so the pairing in its Euler equation is Σσ_s⁴λ_s Q_s(W)V. A unit *angle* trial K_r has σ_s Q_s(W)K_r=δ_{sr}.

**Lemma 4.12 (P, exact multiplier test).** Suppose these trials satisfy

\[
 |d_WK_r|_{R_y}\le C(\sigma_y\sigma_r)^{-1}e^{-c d(y,r)},
 \qquad \sup_r\sum_y(\sigma_y/\sigma_r)e^{-c d(y,r)}\le C.
                                                               \tag{4.27}
\]

Here d_WV is the oriented, parallel-transported tangent sum around the actual plaquette divided by η, so that the variation of its word times the inverse word is η²d_WV up to the conventional i. The trial may be taken modulo a gauge tangent for which both the row equation and the variation of the action are respected. Then

\[
                  \sigma_r^3|\lambda_r|\le C\epsilon_F.       \tag{4.28}
\]

**Proof.** Test the Euler equation on K_r. The result is σ_r³λ_r. In the Wilson first variation the transported tangent is traceless. Thus the constant identity term in the plaquette trace cancels, leaving the factor |1−W(∂p)|. In the holomorphic extension the inverse-word term is treated by the same identity. Summing η⁴ over each native cell costs at most Cσ_y⁴. Consequently the absolute variation is at most Cε_F Σ_y(σ_y/σ_r)e^{-cd(y,r)}. This proves (4.28), even when the field, the trial and the row data are complex. The pairing is the holomorphic bilinear pairing throughout. ∎

**Proposition 4.13 (P, nonlinear commuting centered rows).** Consider the Cartan restriction of the native centered constraint problem of CMP95 (1.11),(1.18) in the gauge of CMP99, with fixed log branches and compatible exterior data. For a critical field in that chart, (4.28) and the scaled physical current bound σ_y³|J_phys(W)|≤Cε_F hold at arbitrary native depth under the fixed spacing tests above. This applies on the full set of complex Cartan targets for which the given critical branch satisfies the displayed curvature condition.

**Proof.** Commuting logarithmic relative averages have on the fixed branches exactly their linear variation Q. The columns of H₀ from (4.20), normalized for angle targets, are therefore the trials K_r. Cartan parallel transport on Cartan vectors is the identity, so d_WK_r=dK_r. Equation (4.20) and the scale-weighted counting theorem prove (4.27). The full nonlinear Wilson first variation gives (4.28), and the Euler equation J_phys(W)=Q∗λ together with the incidence calculation of Proposition4.9 proves the claimed current bound. This proof retains the sine of the original Wilson angles in the current. It uses the centered rows in (1.11). The center-based full path (1.8) has the two additional endpoint prefixes. Transferring this estimate to such rows requires their common nodal gauge transformation. ∎

**Proposition 4.14 (P/L, noncommuting centered rows in one weak chart).** Let W be an actual critical field of the centered native constraint problem, with the same curvature bound, on a hierarchy passing the native weighted counting tests. Suppose a single compatible gauge, preserving the exterior, gives W_b=exp(iη A_b) with |A|≤u/σ_y on each native cell and the neighboring blocks used by its original rows. For complex fields assume the same bounded holomorphic local chart and the first-derivative regularity of CMP99 (3.37) when its quoted perturbation result is used. Take u smaller than a constant depending on the fixed source parameters. Then the genuine linearized centered rows Q(W) have angle trials satisfying (4.27), and

\[
 \sup_r\sigma_r^3|\lambda_r|+
 \sup_y\sigma_y^3|J_{\rm phys}(W)|_{R_y}\le C\epsilon_F.       \tag{4.29}
\]

**Proof.** Let H₀ denote the flat right inverse, with its angle-column normalization. For row s of depth j, CMP99 printed p.406, (3.78)–(3.81), gives the one-step perturbation at internal level n of size C u L^{n−j}, times a fixed positive path majorant. The one-step majorant and its physical-measure transpose have bounded sup norm depending only on d,L: their row is a finite average of paths in a fixed L block and its immediate neighbor, with a finite Lᵈ measure conversion. The straight flat average and its transpose have norm at most one, by positivity and the partition of unity of their hat coefficients. Multiplying the one-step bounds gives ∏_{n<j}(1+C uL^{n−j})≤e^{Cu}; telescoping the perturbations gives C u for the full difference. This reasoning applies on the blocks determining each row and to the transpose with the physical pairing. In complex coordinates transpose means the analytic transpose, as distinguished explicitly below CMP99 (3.81).

Set E_{sr}=σ_s Q_s(W)H_{0,r}−δ_{sr}. The original composed paths remain in a fixed multiple of the native block. On their support, (4.20), the buffer comparison (4.23) and the preceding perturbation bound give

\[
                 |E_{sr}|\le C u(\sigma_s/\sigma_r)e^{-c_0 d(s,r)}.
                                                               \tag{4.30}
\]

Here c₀ can be chosen strictly below the flat rate δ₀/2 by a fixed amount. Conjugate E by the scale weights to remove σ_s/σ_r. Both weighted exponential row and column norms of this conjugate are ≤C u by Theorem3.9, with a fixed smaller rate. For sufficiently small u its Neumann series converges in that algebra. Hence the columns of K=H₀(I+E)⁻¹ obey the same potential and first derivative estimates as (4.20) at a smaller fixed rate. Indeed the factor 1/σ_s in H₀'s sth angle column multiplies σ_s/σ_r from the corrected target, leaving exactly 1/σ_r. The covariant plaquette derivative costs at most C(|∇K|+u|K|/σ_y), by expanding the four transported factors in the common chart. This proves (4.27) with a rate leaving room for the σ_y/σ_r count. Lemma4.12 proves the multiplier bound.

The original composed transposes have bounded sup norm in physical measures by the one-step product above. Only neighboring native scales can meet a fine bond in their support, by the same buffer argument as (4.23), using a fixed enlargement of the spacing test when the actual prefix support requires it. Applying this bound to J_phys(W)=Q(W)^*λ proves the current estimate. The conclusion uses actual Wilson stationarity, a genuine centered derivative, and the complete original native hierarchy. It involves no bound on a covariant second derivative of a Green kernel. ∎

**Proposition 4.15 (P, raw physical current and the exact nodal gauge).** Let W be a critical point of the original Wilson action with raw group-valued row map m and fixed exterior. Use the diagonal reference W, so the actual compatible row has the form m_c(V)=m(V)^{G_W(V)} with G_W(W)=1. Here G_W is one nodal gauge on the complete determining record; it is the identity at prescribed exterior nodes. This is the CL3–CL15 definition, using its composed endpoint factors. Put y=m(W), and use the holomorphic left tangent angle at y. Denote the physically normalized raw and compatible derivatives by Q_raw and Q_comp. For every variation v their exact difference is

\[
 (Q_{\rm comp}-Q_{\rm raw})_s v
 =\sigma_s^{-1}\{\zeta(s_-)-\operatorname{Ad}_{y_s}\zeta(s_+)\},
 \qquad \zeta=(DG_W)_Wv.                                    \tag{4.31}
\]

If λ is the raw physical multiplier, one has the exact equality of fine covectors Q_rawᵗλ=Q_compᵗλ, including the physical pairings. Hence the nonlinear estimate (4.29) applies to the raw current wherever the diagonal compatible derivative satisfies the trial and transpose bounds of Proposition4.14.

**Proof.** Each admissible coarse nodal generator z is the restriction of a fine generator fixing the prescribed exterior. At finite cutoff, prescribe its values on those fine sites, zero at fixed sites, and extend arbitrarily on all remaining sites. The extension is needed only to test an exact identity and can have arbitrarily large norm. Gauge covariance of the original raw row map gives its tangent angle z(s_−)−Ad_{y_s}z(s_+). Wilson gauge invariance gives zero first variation along this fine gauge direction. Therefore raw stationarity implies

\[
 \sum_s\sigma_s^3\langle\lambda_s,
       z(s_-)-\operatorname{Ad}_{y_s}z(s_+)\rangle=0
                                                               \tag{4.32}
\]

for every such z. Equation (4.31) follows by differentiating the exact left and right endpoint actions. Set z=ζ in (4.32) and keep the factor σ_s⁻¹ in (4.31). This proves the fine-covector equality with the original σ_s⁴ pairing. The identity holds for real fields, and extends algebraically to holomorphic critical branches and allowed complex fine gauges. It includes all endpoint prefix derivatives through ζ. The identity uses one value of ζ at each common node, even when several incident rows have different meshes. ∎

The same calculation applies to holomorphic families on a physical stationary graph at each point. Differentiating (4.32) then differentiates the actual multiplier, transport, target and generator together. The nonstationary changed-base residual (4.7) still has its full DT and De terms. This result reduces the raw current question to the compatible derivative on one common weak chart and the existence of the actual branch on the whole wall domain. The stored gauge-invariant source values on an admitted chart can be taken before the final nodal gauge restoration. Independent source current arguments and moving gauge readouts still require their own derivative bounds.

### 4.13 The original variational theorem and replay of an old collar row

**Checked native admission theorem (L).** CMP102, printed pp.277–279, (1)–(10), covers the actual Wilson action with fixed exterior on Ω₀, the prescribed nested hierarchy, its original separation RM₁, and the raw averages on its native bonds. Test the real target V on every native plaquette by (7). At a seam it is essential to complete a missing bond of the plaquette word by the actual average of the finer stored V, exactly as described on p.278. If all these words satisfy |∂V−1|<ε₁≤a₁, Theorem1 constructs the minimal orbit in (8). It is the unique critical orbit in the regular class (6) when B₃ε₁≤ε₀≤a₀. The two inequalities in (2) bound the fine plaquette by B₃ε₁η²σ_y⁻² and its covariant divergence by B₃ε₁η²σ_y⁻³ in the original conventions. In particular the Wilson current, obtained from the antihermitian traceless part with its η⁻² normalization, has the corresponding native σ_y⁻³ bound. The local gauges (9)–(10) have the source's explicit cube, enlargement and size restrictions. This construction requires no previously given admitted minimum for that V.

**Checked complex response (L/P).** For such a real target V₀ take U₀=U_k(V₀). For V=V′V₀ on the same record, (172) bounds the dimensionless V′−1 by C₁ε₁, with the logarithmic variable B on its prescribed branch. Proposition9, pp.305–309, constructs the Landau relative field exp(iηℋ(B)) with the bounds (19)–(21), including the covariant divergence and Laplacian terms of size Cε₁σ_y⁻³. Its derivative density at input site x of native mesh σ_x has five output rows in (190). Their weights are σ_y⁻¹, σ_y⁻², the stated cutoff-dependent Hölder σ_y⁻²⁻ᵝ, σ_y⁻³ and σ_y⁻³, all times σ_x⁻ᵈ exp(−δ₀d(y,x)/8). The last two rows are the covariant D∗D and Laplacian acting on the differentiated relative potential. Multiplication by the literal target measure σ_xᵈ cancels the input density. The paper states the long intermediate kernel calculation (189) without displaying it term by term and includes (190) in its published Proposition9. Equation (181) gives the original blockwise axial gauge covariance on its complex orbit extension. Dependence on an external field requires the specific regularity conditions quoted in Proposition9. These statements are about the original physical variational solution on its specified domain. Localization wall variables can enter it through an analytic target family only when that family's actual image lies in this domain.

For a joined history this is an implementable test. Compute v₀=q_oldΓ_real with all finer seam completions, and test (7). Compute v(s)=q_oldΓ_s including its exterior and all endpoint frames. Test the perturbation v(s)v₀⁻¹ by (172) uniformly over the original simultaneous walls. With the same action, boundary conditions and record, Theorem1 and Proposition9 then construct P_old(v(s)) and control its native response. For histories passing these tests compare the resulting native bounds with the stored age, physical mesh and strict radii of §2.1. The last comparison is required even if the native orbit exists.

**Proposition 4.16 (P/L, completion of coarse rows from a mixed record).** On the original compatible cubical hierarchy let the row data be m_B(W). At each h, every h-bond b which does not intersect Ω_{h+1} can be evaluated as an exact holomorphic function of the stored rows and the fixed exterior, wherever the original means and logs are defined. At h=k this recovers every top-scale row on the relevant determining and exterior region. No replacement of the iterated mean by a single mean is made.

**Proof.** At h=0 the required identity rows are stored or fixed. If b intersects Ω_h but avoids Ω_{h+1}, it is a stored Γ_h row by (2.2). If b avoids Ω_h, both h-blocks at its endpoints lie outside Ω_h, since that region is a union of aligned h-blocks. The original one-step mean uses the finer bonds in precisely these two blocks, including the full paths from the endpoints, the translated path between them, and the original reference bond. This is CMP109 (0.4),(0.11),(0.12). The child bonds needed for this computation avoid Ω_h and are therefore known by the h−1 step. Apply that actual one-step mean to them. This proves the claim by induction, including any fixed exterior bonds encountered. The face convention acts on the bonds themselves; assigning vertices first and deleting crossing bonds would give a different recursion. Holomorphy follows by finite composition on the original log branches. ∎

**Corollary 4.17 (P, first-join collar reconstruction).** On a boundary piece with original depth j, every original j-row in I is already stored by (4.24). Consider an original collar j-bond avoiding I whose two aligned endpoint j-blocks lie in the localization domain outside I. Its complete descendant computation uses the minimal record in C. Hence if the prescribed collar data equal Mʰ(F) of one physical field F at those stored descendants, the missing old row is exactly Mʲ(F). This equality also holds for a boundary row if its full required exterior data are those of F. The claim applies to the identity, the raw group means and all their complete prefixes. Equality of the analytic functions implies equality of their finite derivatives with the original wall variables.

**Proof.** The child contours stay in the two endpoint blocks, which avoid I and remain in the admissible collar/exterior region under the hypothesis. Apply Proposition4.16 there with terminal depth j and compare to F at each stored descendant. The two recursive evaluations are identical. In the unweakened (1.61) these data are explicitly M^•(U_k⁰), so the first-join case is an exact physical instance. With localization walls present, the source prescribes the relative words in (1.61), including u_k⁰ and the complete H″_k. The replay remains exact on these prescribed data. Representing them as Mʰ(F_s) of a single field on the whole actual exterior requires compatibility of their endpoint gauges and log branches there. Even without that identification the recursion expresses the old row in the actual compact/complex descendant variables, independent of unknown fine values of the joined minimum. ∎

**Proposition 4.18 (P, quantitative old collar target from a field at the original collar scale).** Fix an old j-plaquette in the collar. Take the complete descendant blocks of its four bonds, including the finer completions required by CMP102 (7). Suppose they admit a contractible rectangular enlargement of diameter Cσ_j with the original exterior. Let a real collar field F₀ satisfy |1−F₀(∂p)|≤εη²/σ_j² there, and let the complex collar family have F_s=exp(iη b_s)F₀ with σ_j‖b_s‖∞≤δ in its common transported frame. On fixed sufficiently small ε,δ, the replayed original coarse plaquette and its perturbation satisfy

\[
 |1-\partial M^j(F_0)|\le C'\epsilon,\qquad
 |M^j(F_s)M^j(F_0)^{-1}-1|\le C'\delta.                     \tag{4.33}
\]

The constants depend on N,L,C and the original one-step mean, uniformly in j and η. This proves the local part of the real test (7) and complex test (172) for these old collar rows when C′ε≤ε₁, C′δ<C₁ε₁. If the matched source target also carries an explicit nodal frame variation, include its two actual endpoint changes in δ. A single fixed compact gauge conjugation preserves the estimates.

**Proof.** Choose the ordered-coordinate axial tree in the stated rectangle. At a fine bond the two tree paths differ by a strip of at most Cσ_j/η plaquettes up to unitary conjugations. The product estimate gives |1−F_{0,b}^{tree}|≤Cεη/σ_j. This is one compatible real gauge for all descendants. Thus F₀ has potential of size Cε/σ_j there. The complex perturbation in that same frame has size δ/σ_j.

For completeness one must control the *raw* iterated mean in this estimate. At each step use the actual relative endpoint gauge defined by averaging the path ratios in CL3–CL4. At the identity its derivative removes the two endpoint prefix means, leaving the positive straight normalized average of physical potentials. This follows by differentiating the normalized one-step mean at equal arguments, where its derivative is the arithmetic average by (0.8) of CMP109. Its remaining Taylor derivative in physical units is bounded by C_L σ_{n+1}‖a‖∞ on the block, since the one-step paths have fixed length in L-lattice units and the output potential is divided by σ_{n+1}. Starting from a potential of size u/σ_j, the successive centered physical potentials have norm at most 2u/σ_j. Indeed the product of their inductive bounds is at most exp(2C_L u Σ_{n<j}σ_{n+1}/σ_j), which is ≤2 for a fixed sufficiently small u. The blockwise endpoint gauge at step n differs from identity by ≤C_L σ_{n+1}u/σ_j. Gauge covariance restricts each previous endpoint gauge to the next nodes; their whole product differs from identity by ≤Cu because the sum of these lengths is bounded by Cσ_j. This restores the actual raw iterated mean together with both endpoints, and establishes a holomorphic, uniformly bounded raw output on a ball of fixed radius in σ_j times the potential norm. Cauchy's estimate inside half that ball gives a uniform Lipschitz bound in this scaled norm. At the identity the raw mean is the identity. Apply the bound first to F₀ in the tree gauge and then to F_s relative to F₀. Each of the four bonds costs Cε in the first comparison, and their product gives the first bound. The second is the corresponding Lipschitz estimate under the perturbation. Corollary4.17 identifies these values with the actual replay. ∎

**Native recorded readouts (L/P).** There is a separate direct use of the full LFR member-domain statement. The range (1.64)–(1.69) in LFR I explicitly tests the reconstructed fields U_{p,Y}(M^•(Γ)) at each recorded p, with their actual native record, and the corresponding curvature bound. LFR II (1.65) puts its output in that range at n=N. Consequently if a saved readout has exactly this determining set, action, boundary prescription, relative gauge orbit and localization domain Y, its curvature is already bounded by the appropriate row of (1.64), (1.66) or (1.68) on the original simultaneous member domain. This is a result for W itself. The relevant factor is the displayed α_{0,m} or α_{0,j}, multiplied by the source's L₀ power on the two affected regions. The largest such power may cost a factor of order R_k by LFR I p.192. In common current units an own-scale bound c_Δ≤ε_m still contributes σ_m^{4−2p}ε_m^p per assigned cell. Thus an exact record match settles a genuine curvature row and the original CMP102 response there; the mixed-cell sum and the smaller saved radius still require their own quantitative comparison. In particular q>p₀ alone does not compare an arbitrary α_{0,m} logarithmic exponent q₀ and an R_k loss to the saved q.

**Which distance is available.** The component of Z meeting X is contained in Y₁, hence its inner Λ has distance to Y₁ᶜ at least its distance to that component's exterior. A collar two M₁ layers wide reduces that distance by at most 2dM₁σ_j on a j-piece. LFR I (1.73) enlarges the inner rectangle by one M cube; this enlargement must be deducted in using the preceding ordinary-history buffers. On an ordinary-recent region with the original separation dist(Ω_kᶜ,Λ_k)≥2MR_kσ_k, nesting Ω_k⊂Ω_{k₀+1} and the one-M enlargement give dist(Λ,Zᶜ)≥(2MR_k−dM)σ_k, provided the same component is used. This is an actual separation of the integrated source region from the artificial collar for that class. There is no asserted separation of X itself from the collar. The zero-fluctuation interface forcing is given in LFR II (1.54)–(1.55) by both the averaged argument and a projected current supported at its inner seam. That projection/localization identity must be applied before using a native decay factor. Identifying every complex wall's old collar forcing with the single controlled field in (4.33), and comparing it with the last admitted birth chart, are the remaining application steps.

### 4.14 The zero-fluctuation current forcing at the physical first join

**Proposition 4.19 (P/L, the physical kernel after the original projection).** Consider the physical first join (localization parameters at their physical endpoint), on an admissible original native hierarchy satisfying §3.9 with enough fixed spacing to absorb scale ratios through power four. Use the real regular background U₁,₂ of LFR II (1.53), with its stated regularity, and the actual small source parameters of (1.54). Denote by S the union of the full supports of the second averaged argument of (1.54), of θJ₁,₂ in (1.55), and of the complete adjoint Q∗ applied to the cutoff θ₁ there. Enlarge S by the fixed neighboring native cells used in these rows. Let ε bound the original weighted target angles and the σ_y³-scaled current sources after this projection. Then, in the source relative gauge, the zero-fluctuation correction in (1.54) satisfies

\[
 \sigma_y|H''_k|_{R_y}+\sigma_y^2|\nabla H''_k|_{R_y}
       \le C\epsilon e^{-c d(y,S)}                           \tag{4.34}
\]

for sufficiently weak fixed ε and a fixed c>0. This addresses the changed base at B=0, where the first ordinary fluctuation term (1.51) has vanished but the correction (1.54) remains.

**Proof.** Use the physical LFR II (1.55) first. It is an exact identity obtained by inserting the critical projection for U₁ on the coinciding record before multiplying by the original covariance G″. The source thereby becomes (1−θ)Q∗θ₁H∗J₁+θJ₁,₂, with the native Q,H and their full adjoints as printed. Its support is contained in S by locality of the averaging rows. The first term retains the whole H∗J₁ inside θ₁. For regular native data its σ_y³ bound follows from the H kernel and the native sum, or from the explicit H∗J estimate following CMP99 (3.136). The averaging transpose maps this bounded multiplier to a bounded force using the physical one-step transpose calculation of §4.12 in the regular local gauges of the source. One needs such a gauge only on each complete individual row support to estimate its norm, since the real transports are isometries. The second term has precisely the native regular current bound stipulated by the background in (1.53). Complex variants require the source's quantitative complex regularity on the same blocks.

Here are details of the spatial estimate. Give a potential the cell norm n_y(A)=σ_y|A|+σ_y²|∇A| and give a force the norm σ_y³|f|. CMP99 Theorem3.13 gives the first two (3.42) estimates for the physical constrained covariance 𝔊 of (3.148). For force in a cell z the scaled output costs at most C(σ_y/σ_z)³e^{-δd(y,z)} times the input norm. This ratio, and those from the angle lifting operator H₁, are absorbed by the fixed scale-weighted sum. Thus the inhomogeneity T=H₁B−𝔊J in CMP102 (111),(116) has sup_y exp(c d(y,S))n_y(T)≤Cε. This use of the source current projection is essential: J₁,₂ before (1.55) need not be supported in S.

Write Y=A₁+H₁B. Equation (116) becomes Y=T−𝔊 V′(Y). To estimate the difference of its nonlinear terms use CMP102 (189) on the segment between two native-small fields, with the original regular external background. Decompose variations with a smooth partition supported on the enlarged native cells. Such a partition can be constructed from cutoffs equal to one on each native cell and supported on its fixed enlargement. The enlarged cells have bounded multiplicity and neighboring scale ratios by (4.23); normalizing by their sum preserves the bound C/σ_y on their first difference. Consequently the local scaled n norms of the pieces are bounded by the neighboring input norms. Equation (189) maps each piece to force bounds Cε₃e^{-δd/4}. Compose with 𝔊 and use Theorem3.9 at a smaller fixed rate. The operator norm in the exponentially weighted n norm is at most Cε₃. The smallness conditions of CMP102 Proposition6 can be imposed with this fixed constant so that Cε₃<1/2. Since V′(0)=0 by (98), contraction on that weighted ball gives sup_y exp(c d(y,S))n_y(Y)≤2Cε. The actual potential is Y−HD(Y) by (112). The derivative bound (73) of Proposition3 has density Cε₃σ_r^{1−d} times its exponential. Integrating over a cell of volume O(σ_z⁴) against an input O(σ_z⁻¹) leaves the ratio (σ_z/σ_r)³, again covered by the fixed weighted sum. Composing with H gives the same potential/first-derivative norm. This proves (4.34) on the source physical regular branch. ∎

For a top-layer collar y in the class of Proposition4.11, (4.34) controls its complex logarithmic perturbation in original collar units whenever the projected source S is separated. The regular, real physical branch supplies a concrete instance; an admitted small complex parameter in the same physical kernel obeys the identical analytic estimates. For the LFR wall polydisc, one must localize the *already projected* expression of (1.55), with its actual linked walk carriers and radii. The unweakened identity between separately multiplied factors does not persist after independent wall operations. This is the remaining distinction between (4.34) and an all-wall saved estimate.

### 4.15 Physical curvature on a refined collar, uniformly in its depth

**Proposition 4.20 (P/L, collar smoothing for the original nonlinear variational problem).** Take the mixed top-boundary localization of Proposition4.11 with top index j=k, core I and refined collar C. Assume the seam has a top-layer buffer: a native path from the refined collar to the mixed interior meets the top layer, up to a fixed number of neighboring native cells. This holds, for example, when the old top layer contains a full top block neighborhood of ∂I and the minimal record reaches its top layer there. Keep the original hierarchy spacing b. In this assertion take the fixed native counting tests with enough reserve for weights through power six, e.g. (2.59) at α=1/32 and 8 log L/b≤δ₀/64, with b interpreted in the same native metric. Choose one regular source reference F satisfying the hypotheses for the reduction (14),(19)–(21) and contraction (113)–(121) of CMP102. It may be the physical reference from a parameter family point by point. Its actual curvature and Wilson current in C obey

\[
 |1-F(\partial p)|\le \epsilon\eta^2/\sigma_j^2,
 \qquad |J(F)|\le\epsilon/\sigma_j^3,                         \tag{4.35}
\]

and on I obey the source's ordinary native bounds with ε. The target variable B is the actual one in (20),(111), with the original exterior. Assume B vanishes in C and is bounded by ε on I, or more generally |B_s|≤ε(σ_s/σ_j)² on C and |B_s|≤ε on I. Thus, for example, the inside record can differ from its reference while the entire refined collar uses the reference rows. In applying this criterion to a raw row, its actual Landau target and exterior are required. On the stated source domain the reconstructed critical orbit W has a relative representative W=exp(iη A)F with

\[
 |A|_{R_y}\le C\epsilon\sigma_y/\sigma_j^2,
 \qquad |\nabla_F A|_{R_y}\le C\epsilon/\sigma_j^2
                         \quad(y\in C).                        \tag{4.36}
\]

In particular for a region D whose complete plaquette stencils lie in C, with the assignment convention of Proposition3.8,

\[
 \sum_{\Delta\subset D}\sigma_\Delta^{4-2p}c_\Delta(W)^p
       \le C_p |D^+|(\epsilon/\sigma_j^2)^p,
                  \qquad p>8.                                \tag{4.37}
\]

Here D⁺ accounts for one fine plaquette collar. The real curvature is gauge invariant in norm. For complex data (4.36)–(4.37) are in the single source relative representative used for the whole D; transfer to another complex representative retains its conjugation bound. At the current top scale σ_j=1 this has exactly the AE8 power of the actual weak ceiling ε. The result concerns the reconstructed W and includes every assigned fine cell in this collar.

**Proof.** Give the native potential norm n_y from §4.14 the additional weight w_y=(σ_j/σ_y)² on C, w_y=1 on I. For y,z in C the number of separating layers gives w_y/w_z≤L² exp((2 log L/b)d(y,z)). For y in C and z in I the top-layer seam gives the same estimate up to a fixed factor: any connecting path must rise from the native index of y to the top layer. The reverse quotient is at most one. Thus the ratio of weights across the entire hierarchy can be absorbed in a fixed fraction of the exponential kernels of §4.14. This step is what permits a finer artificial mesh.

For an input current on C, w_zσ_z³|J(F)_z|≤εσ_z/σ_j≤ε, and on I the native current bound suffices. The actual angle data likewise have bounded weighted sup norm. Use the physical 𝔊 of (3.148) and H₁ in T=H₁B−𝔊J(F). Their estimates used in §4.14, with the weight ratio just proved, give sup_y w_y n_y(T)≤Cε. Equation (189) and the partition argument of §4.14 give the same bound Cε₃ for the derivative of 𝔊V′ in this weighted norm. Choosing the admissible coupling/regularity ceiling sufficiently small once, apply the original contraction to Y=T−𝔊V′(Y). The transformed field A=Y−HD(Y) has sup_y w_y n_y(A)≤Cε by (73), with the same scale-ratio calculation as in §4.14. This gives (4.36). The source variational theorem identifies the resulting field, modulo its allowed gauge, with the original critical orbit in its prescribed regular class.

For a plaquette, expand the four relative exponentials around its original transported factors. The covariant first differences contribute at most Cη²‖∇_F A‖. The remaining commutators and quadratic terms are bounded by Cη²‖A‖² on the small source chart; cyclic transport around the plaquette costs an additional Cη‖A‖ |1−F(∂p)|, covered by (4.35). Hence |1−W(∂p)|≤Cεη²/σ_j². The matrix log on the fixed branch gives |F_a(W)|≤Cε/σ_j². The exact assigned-cell identity (3.23) then proves (4.37). All estimates remain uniform for a holomorphic family satisfying the same hypotheses at every point of its original domain. ∎

This argument identifies a specific route through the artificial mesh. A source perturbation supported in the mixed interior is attenuated by the rise to the old collar scale. It therefore costs no negative power of the smallest artificial cell in (4.37). For the complete LFR history, the reference candidates in the three splices are the actual fields in (1.61)–(1.63), with (1.55) for their changed base. The unresolved checks are (4.35) for those fields on the whole original simultaneous domain, their actual centered target and exterior at every junction, and the comparison with any distinct saved readset crossing the genuine rough core. A collar estimate alone cannot cover an independently pinned original fine cell in that core.

### 4.16 Which mesh controls the first output, and a rough history with a crossing birth record

**Lemma 4.21 (L/P, the matching reconstructed current).** In the top-boundary first join put e=k, and retain Y=Y₁ and the actual record B″_k(Y₁). LFR II (1.65) bounds Γ on the spaces (1.64)–(1.69) of LFR I. Their rows are evaluated on the original regions Ω_m,Ω″_m intersected with Y. The finer layers D_h introduced solely to localize at ∂Y are distinct from these regions. On a portion of Ω_k∩Y take n=N,j=m=p=k in (1.66). In current units σ_e=1 the output satisfies

\[
 |1-\Gamma(\partial p)|\le C\alpha_{0,e}\eta^2,
 \qquad |J_{\rm phys}(\Gamma)|\le C\alpha_{0,e}.             \tag{4.38}
\]

The real representative of the original complex decomposition has the same curvature ceiling. The complex perturbation has potential and first covariant derivative ceilings Cα₁,e on that portion. All complete stencils used in these inequalities must lie in the indicated range. Formula (4.38) holds for the full original LFR member-domain preparation and localization parameters in (1.65). An additional source wall that only varies the carried function leaves this statement valid on its prescribed disc.

**Proof.** In (1.66) the factor L₀^{2(j−m)} is exactly one. Its curvature row has native factor η²/(Lᵐη)²=η² at m=k. For the current use the *fourth* row, J_{p,Y}(M^•(Γ)), rather than the independent third row J. CMP109 (1.8),(1.15) defines this fourth quantity as the physical projected divergence at the actual constrained reconstruction, with the pth mesh and its explicit η⁻² normalization. At p=k, the record used in this reconstruction is precisely B″_k(Y₁). Equation (1.61) constructs Γ as the original critical configuration on that record at its prescribed target, so reconstruction with M^•(Γ) selects the same critical orbit and the same axial representative. This equality continues in the original holomorphic branch. The fourth-row factor for p=m=k is L^{k−min(k,k)}(L^kL⁻ᵏ)⁻³=1. This proves the second inequality of (4.38). The result uses the actual first output, with all target pieces and the retained gauge already present in (1.61). It does not identify an arbitrary independently chosen current with J_phys. ∎

**A nonempty rough first-join geometry.** Use the original logarithmic averaging (CMP109 (0.4), equivalently the commuting log-mean version of (0.11)–(0.12)) and a contractible part of the torus well away from its periodic identification. In a single original fine block take the Cartan-valued link cochain t times the oriented boundary of an interior plaquette, zero off those four links. Choose t so its central holonomy has norm strictly between ε₀ and 2ε₀ while the other affected plaquettes are below ε₀. This is possible at weak coupling since the central angle is 4t and the other angles sharing one link have modulus t. Choose the plaquette inside a single initial P₀ cube. Its two parallel links in each direction have opposite signs, identical longitudinal coordinate and adjacent transverse coordinates in the same block. Hence their flat straight one-step averages cancel exactly. The full raw Cartan mean differs from the straight mean by the gradient of the averaged endpoint prefixes, so its coarse field is a pure coarse gauge. Extend that coarse gauge at the retained nodes to a fine gauge inside the flagged cube. The resulting field has M¹=1, all higher Mᵐ=1, and the same nonzero plaquette. This calculation uses commuting words on their original near-identity logarithmic branches.

The initial bad plaquette generates one rectangular large-field component. All prescribed variables outside it are flat. At later ordinary steps take the coarser fields equal to identity and the allowed small fluctuations equal to zero. In (3.2),(3.3),(3.16) of CMP119 all newly inspected blocks outside the inherited component then see identity data. Equivalently, the seed plaquettes and their whole incident stars are inside the fixed level-zero part, and identity off those sites is an exact minimizer on the free part: the pinned action is fixed, and every remaining plaquette term attains its minimum. Thus P,Q,R outside the inherited component are empty. The inherited component evolves by S, namely coarse covering followed by ten layers, exactly as in LFR II preceding (1.80). CMP119 (3.5),(3.20) give Ω_eᶜ by eight layers and Z_e=Λ_eᶜ by ten when no new bad fields occur. A rectangle stays a rectangle. On a weak trajectory interval where R_m is constant, the side in current units obeys w_{m+1}≤w_m/L+22MR_m, since coarse covering adds at most one block at each end before the ten layers. A single initial flagged cube with the source's fixed padding has side below 100MR₁, and this bound is preserved for the allowed L. One may take weak-coupling subsequences away from the discrete thresholds in (2.5); over O(R₁) steps the relative change of log g⁻² tends to zero by I-0c. At the first allowed waiting time the component satisfies LFR I (i),(ii), with the choice N=R₁ in LFR II (1.80). The exterior selector (1.75) admits the flat extension and the zero fluctuation satisfies (1.82). The seed is a strictly large physical plaquette in the original fine-mesh criterion. This example establishes a possible geometry. The following uniform bounds use (1.65), and so include its allowed retained variables and original walls rather than just the displayed seed.

Take X to be two adjacent Mσ_{j₀} birth cubes on opposite sides of a flat face of I=Z_e^{~−2}, away from the corners; j₀ is fixed, for example j₀=1. Use the actual aligned partitions. For e−j₀ sufficiently large the chosen face lies on a birth grid face. Let S_old include both cubes and the complete fixed number of neighboring birth blocks needed by the actual determining, exterior, curvature and current stencils. The first localization domain containing X and the component it meets is Y₁=Z_e. The complete S_old lies inside the outer two MR_e layers of Z_e and hence in Ω_e, while it avoids the earlier inherited Z_{e−1}: the predecessor is inside the coarse cover before the ten new layers. This is a first touch. It crosses the artificial join seam and the record has a genuine rough component in its interior. Since I⊂D_e and D_e has a full M₁σ_e separation from D_{e−1}ᶜ, the small birth-sized neighborhood at this seam meets only the top two auxiliary layers. This supplies the top-layer barrier for paths into I. No separation between this readset and the seam is asserted.

**Proposition 4.22 (P/L, fixed birth depth and unbounded waiting age).** In this crossing geometry use the original local Wilson determining problem of depth j₀ on the indicated birth record, with its exact oriented means, identity rows, fixed exterior and nonredundant gauge quotient. Put r=L^{j₀−e}. In birth units the fine mesh is L⁻ʲ⁰. On the entire member domain just specified, write τ_e=C(α₀,e+α₁,e) for its actual ceiling. There is one transported local tree chart on the complete record in which

\[
 \|A_F\|\le C_{j_0}r^2\tau_e,\quad
 \|A_W-A_F\|+\|\lambda\|\le C_{j_0}r^3\tau_e,
 \quad\|J_{\rm old}(W)\|\le C_{j_0}r^3\tau_e,
 \quad F=\Gamma,\quad W=P_{\rm old}(m_{\rm old}F).             \tag{4.39}
\]

The constants are uniform in cutoff and entry e at the stated fixed j₀, with dependence also on the fixed carrier and group. This distinction matters: the preliminary waiting depth and the ratio of entry to birth mesh can diverge here, although the old nonlinear problem itself has fixed finite depth.

**Proof.** Use F itself as the reference of the old problem, retaining its exterior. It is the already constructed first-join output. The old target defect is exactly zero on *every* row because the true old target is m_old F. No comparison of the finer collar rows to independently prescribed zero angles enters. Bound (4.38) supplies curvature and current on the whole required enclosure. Fix the ordered tree on this finite contractible enclosure and transform the exterior and endpoint frames along with the field. The transformation is a holomorphic product of the actual complex links throughout the original domain. Norms of its conjugations and inverses are at most exp(Crα₁,e) by the compact real component and the physical lengths of the tree paths. For a non-tree bond the resulting word is the product of transported plaquettes in a strip with at most C_{j₀} faces. Each angle has size O(r²τ_e) in the fixed birth coordinates. The tree representative therefore gives the first estimate. Fixed birth differences of every order needed here cost at most constants depending on j₀. Working in this moving chart transforms the prescribed exterior covariantly; it does not release it.

In the old variables center the physical KKT system at (F,0). Its residual is exactly (DS_old(F),0). At a free bond the old Wilson action includes all incident plaquettes by the original incidence convention. The scale change of the same action and current in four dimensions gives J_old(F)=r³ Ad_h J_phys(F) in birth units. Hence the residual has norm at most C_{j₀}r³τ_e. The fixed, boundary-gauged flat KKT matrix is invertible on the actual nonredundant quotient, either by the finite coercivity and row calculation of §4.8 or by the original constraint linearization at the identity. There are finitely many complete row and boundary stencils at this fixed depth and size. In this chart their holomorphic Wilson/row derivatives and their inverse norms are bounded on one fixed open ball. The first estimate makes F lie in that ball, uniformly, without shifting the prescribed exterior. The contraction of Theorem3.3, centered at F, now gives the actual analytic old critical branch with the second bound in (4.39). The finite-dimensional physical current map is uniformly Lipschitz in these birth coordinates. Comparing its full incident-star word to that of F gives the last bound, also at pinned bonds when the prescribed exterior star is included. This avoids identifying an independent current parameter with the physical one. ∎

For the M8.16 saved radius ρ_{j₀}=c_{j₀}g_{j₀}(log x_{j₀})^q, all constants fixed, (4.39) gives strict spare margins on this record for a sufficiently weak original ceiling. Indeed τ_e≤C g_e(1+log x_e)^P for a fixed P from the actual α's, the ratio g_e/g_{j₀} is bounded by C√(1+e−j₀), and the allowed waiting N=R₁ dominates any fixed power of log log x_e. Thus r²τ_e/ρ_{j₀}→0. More specifically R₁≥(log x₁)^u with u≥2 under (2.5), and in the constant-R ordinary example e−j₀=R₁+O(1). Consequently every positive unused power of r absorbs *any fixed power* of x_e and of log x_e. This uses the actual original waiting choice; the weaker sufficient lower bound on N following (1.29) by itself only absorbs a specified logarithmic power. The readout's AE8 sum in entry units follows from (4.39) and the curvature comparison with F. The scale conversion returns |F_a(W)|≤C_{j₀}τ_e and |J_phys(W)|≤C_{j₀}τ_e on the whole S_old.

**The fixed-spacing check.** For variable old depth, let b denote the actual native buffer and K(a)=12c₀(a/2)^4. The CMP96 proof requires aδ₀b/4>8log c₀(a/2)+1. When conjugating a source Green row by a power-three mesh weight, its own scale factor and the added weight together cost at most L⁶exp((6log L/b)d). The potential Hessian (189) starts with exp(−δ₀d/4). For example (2.59) at a=1/32 and 6log L/b≤δ₀/16 leave a summable positive rate for both factors, also after the fixed enlargements in the partition. The operator norm of the derivative in the weighted fixed point is bounded by C_{d,L,b} K(1/32)^2 ε₃; it is below 1/2 only after this specific constant is included in the original weak-field cutoff. None of (1.65), CMP102 (1), or the α=1/2 example below CMP96 Lemma2.1 gives the numerical inequality at a=1/32 for an arbitrary already chosen b. In the top-boundary splice the available b is min(b_old,LM₁) by (4.26). These are explicit original-parameter tests for Proposition4.20. The fixed-birth proof (4.39) is independent of this stronger counting hypothesis, since the old matrix is genuinely finite with j₀ fixed.

### 4.17 A protected crossing record of arbitrary birth depth

The fixed-depth restriction in Proposition4.22 can be removed for physical curvature and current on an older record whose entire free domain and prescribed collar lie in the same original top-region cube. Here the explicit fixed-spacing test at the end of §4.16 applies to the old record. This treatment uses the complete first output F=Γ of (1.61), after the fields in (1.62),(1.63) have been inserted. Choosing an intermediate uncorrected U₁ in its place would prescribe different actual target data on the collar. The zero-fluctuation forcing (1.55) is included in the first output. The exterior and every old target in this argument are taken from that same F.

**Lemma 4.23 (P, the external complex regularity test on the protected cube).** Take a single cube of the LFR I (1.67) class whose intersection with Y₁ contains the whole old record, its prescribed exterior and a full fine plaquette neighborhood. The crossing example has room for such a cube since the ordinary Ω_e rim has width 2MR_e and (1.67) permits the gauge on the intersection with Y₁. Work in entry units σ_e=1. In one compact gauge on that cube the first output admits F=exp(iηa), with

\[
 |a|+|\nabla a|+|d^*d a|\le C_M\tau_e.                      \tag{4.40}
\]

Thus, using the identity as the real comparison field in that gauge, F satisfies the complex background conditions (3.37),(3.38) of CMP99 on every old cell in the cube. This statement is a norm estimate using a permissible compact gauge at each point of the parameter domain. It does not assert that the compact gauge supplied by (1.67) varies holomorphically.

**Proof.** Equations (1.66),(1.67) give F=exp(iηa′)exp(iηa₀) in this gauge, with the potential and first differences of a₀,a′ bounded by C_Mτ_e. Covariant and ordinary first differences differ by at most C|a₀||a′| here. The near-identity product logarithm has the same first-order bounds for a. Expand the plaquette word to second order, keeping the oriented linear difference intact. Its projected imaginary part divided by η² is da+i[a_μ,a_ν] with the appropriate orientation convention, plus an error of norm at most Cη(‖a‖∞‖∇a‖∞+‖a‖∞³). Indeed moving the quadratic factors to one corner costs η‖∇a‖∞ per shift, and the cubic remainder has the additional η factor. The difference of the error is bounded by twice its sup norm divided by η. The difference of the commutator is bounded by C‖a‖∞‖∇a‖∞. Changing the divergence to its F-covariant version costs at most C‖a‖∞ times the plaquette curvature bound. Therefore d*d a differs from the physical Wilson current by O_M(τ_e²+τ_e³). Use (4.38). This proof does not differentiate a second spatial difference of a through an uncontrolled norm. ∎

**Proposition 4.24 (P/L, physical readout response in a protected older record).** In the crossing example take any actual older local determining hierarchy of depth j whose full free domain, finite extensions and boundary conditions are contained in the cube of Lemma4.23. Its largest mesh in entry units is r=L^{j−e}≤1. Assume the original CMP102 spacing, the fixed stronger weighted summability test of §4.16, and the stated nonredundant original gauge and target conventions. Then its old Wilson readout at the actual target m_oldΓ exists on the original native member family. In the source's relative representative it obeys

\[
 |F_a(W)|\le C_M\tau_e,\qquad |J_{\rm phys}(W)|\le C_M\tau_e,
 \qquad \sum_{\Delta\subset S_{\rm old}}\sigma_\Delta^{4-2p}c_\Delta(W)^p
 \le C_{p,M}|S_{\rm old}^+|\tau_e^p.                          \tag{4.41}
\]

Complete stencils and the finite exterior are included in S_old⁺. For the current on a bare fine pinned edge include its incident neighboring cells; at such an interface the native length is at most a fixed L multiple of the fine mesh. Equation (4.41) holds in a representative with bounded conjugations; the gauge-invariant trace functions have their original values there. The stronger paired norms of a specified saved packet remain to be tested.

**Proof.** Use F=Γ as the reference of CMP102 (14),(19)–(21). Lemma4.23 verifies its regularity in the common cube, together with the fixed original exterior. Its target is m_old F. The actual relative target B in the centered equation is zero: F itself is feasible, its relative potential is zero, and (20) applied at zero gives B=0. The source J(F) in (116) is precisely the physical Wilson action derivative. All plaquettes incident to an unfixed bond are retained in the original regional action under the incidence convention; bonds farther out already belong to the fixed exterior. Therefore |J(F)|≤Cτ_e on this entire problem.

Give n_y(A)=σ_y|A|+σ_y²|∇_F A| the global weight w_y=σ_y⁻³ on the *old* hierarchy. There is no interior-to-collar jump in this weight. The scale ratio w_y/w_z is absorbed by the fixed spacing test as in §4.16. Since sup_y w_yσ_y³|J(F)_y|≤Cτ_e, the weighted contraction for (116) and the (73) change of variables give

\[
 |A|_{R_y}\le C\tau_e\sigma_y^2,\qquad
 |\nabla_F A|_{R_y}\le C\tau_e\sigma_y,\qquad W_{\rm rel}=e^{i\eta A}F.
                                                               \tag{4.42}
\]

The critical point and the relative-to-axial gauge are those of the original variational reduction, not a new quadratic action. Equations (4.38),(4.42) give |F_a(W_rel)−F_a(F)|≤Cτ_eσ_y on each fine plaquette after its actual relative transport, by the four-word expansion used in §4.15. This proves the curvature claim and the sum by (3.23).

For the current at unfixed bonds use the genuine angle trial K_s of §4.12 for the compatible derivative at W_rel. It applies on this cube because (4.40),(4.42) give a common small potential and a bounded first difference. The residual raw endpoint gauge term vanishes in the physical multiplier pairing by (4.32). Split DS(W_rel)[K_s] into DS(F)[K_s] plus the difference. The first part is bounded by Cτ_e Σ_y σ_y⁴σ_s⁻¹e^{-c d(y,s)}≤Cτ_eσ_s³. The last inequality uses the fixed scale-ratio count through power four. In the difference of Wilson first variations the curvature difference τ_eσ_y multiplies a covariant derivative of K_s bounded by C/(σ_yσ_s) times its exponential. The change of derivative/transport from F to W_rel adds at most C|A||K_s| times the original curvature. Each of these terms has the same or a smaller bound after multiplying by σ_y⁴ and summing. Therefore σ_s³|λ_s|≤Cτ_eσ_s³, hence |λ_s|≤Cτ_e. Applying the actual compatible transpose with its bounded physical counting norm (§4.12) gives the bound for J_phys(W_rel). At a bare fine pinned edge use the incident plaquette differences from (4.42) directly. They have size at most Cτ_eη in curvature units, since its adjacent meshes are at most C_Lη by the native buffer. The physical divergence divides by η once, giving the same bound. This also includes the exterior plaquettes where the difference vanishes.

In the analytic relative-to-raw conversion, each endpoint correction is a product of actual node transports in its original block. On a block of mesh σ_y the sum of internal step lengths is at most C_Lσ_y; integrating (4.42) along these paths and using the physical real-reference transport bound gives uniform bounded conjugations for a sufficiently small fixed τ_e. The original blockwise covariance (181) and reconstruction of CMP102 Proposition9 supply the axial version on its declared branch. Pointwise compact gauges used to estimate norms need not be differentiated in this calculation. For the explicitly fixed-birth case §4.16 already gives one holomorphic analysis gauge and its finite derivatives. An all-depth boundary-compatible holomorphic Sobolev chart with all weighted absolute anchor derivatives is a separate requirement of the complete interface. ∎

### 4.18 Radius, coupling and age for the crossing birth packet

Use the fixed j₀ crossing readset of §4.16. Its original tree chart has old potential O(r²τ_e), current O(r³τ_e), and strict spare radius by (4.39). The rough fine plaquette is in the same first R component, while every stencil in this particular old record lies in its regular rim. The carrier really crosses the artificial core/collar seam. The distinction matters for summing many such carriers.

**Proposition 4.25 (P, scalar rows of a single crossing clean-born packet).** For an actually stored stable H coefficient on this carrier, use its original homogeneous quadratic and cubic pieces and its analytic fourth-order remainder, with their original physical caps. This assumption is the saved input at birth, and does not assume a mixed-history transport theorem. In the notation of (1.2),(2.1) their caps are A_jg_j⁻², A_jg_j⁻²α⁻¹ and A_jg_j⁻²α⁻². The exact lower-dimensional center and independent contact terms remain their separate records. For m=2,3,4, a single original root makes the following contribution after the dimension-six normalizer r⁻²:

\[
 |T^{(m)}_{\rm root}|\le C A_j\rho^2u^{m-2}
                     r^{2m-2}(\tau_e/g_e)^m.                 \tag{4.43}
\]

For the fourth row, T denotes the entire fourth-order analytic remainder. In this genuine waiting example each of the bounds in (4.43) is at most C A_jρ²u^{m−2} r^{γ_m}Q^m with the saved γ₂=s, γ₃=β, γ₄=2, where 0<s<1 and 0<β<1 are the original stable exponents. These are precisely the three original scalar weights for this one root. No original birth weight was reissued.

**Proof.** All components of the old physical input are O(r²τ_e) by (4.39), with the stronger current bound there. At a fixed old root the original Taylor caps then cost respectively (r²τ_e)^m g_j⁻²α^{2−m}. Multiplication by r⁻² gives (4.43). The cap for the remainder applies on the original spare disc already verified. Put P for the fixed logarithmic exponent in τ_e/g_e≤C(1+log x_e)^P. For a single root, 2m−2>γ_m. Since r=L^{−R₁+O(1)} in the selected waiting history, the spare power r^{2m−2−γ_m} absorbs this logarithm. The original Q=q_H≥1 already includes the pinned and exterior canonical data. This establishes the claimed scalar comparison with Q itself. ∎

**Multiplicity check (P).** In one current-sized face patch, the number of birth carriers of this fixed shape meeting the seam is ≤C r⁻³ because their centers lie in a birth-sized slab about a three-dimensional face. Edges and corners only lower the power. Thus summation of (4.43) by absolute values yields instead the powers 2m−5. The cubic gives r¹, stronger than its saved r^β; the fourth-order remainder gives r³, stronger than its saved r². Their spare factors absorb the same finite logarithmic costs. For the quadratic row this crude sum gives r⁻¹, which does not establish r^s. Across a four-dimensional region the full root count is r⁻⁴, as in MT42. Establishing the quadratic seam sum needs the actual stable kernel's moment cancellation, together with its contact and boundary partners. A single-root gain is not a substitute for that cancellation.

For a fixed set of derivative labels the holomorphic tree chart in (4.39) allows Cauchy on any of the original available larger physical discs. The exact factors g_e^m and the inverse powers of those discs are retained on conversion to canonical variables. In the selected waiting example a fixed power loss in x_e can be paid by any positive spare power of r in (4.43), but a Cauchy estimate on unspecified radii cannot be used. In particular these scalar and finite-label statements do not bound the complete simultaneous weighted absolute-anchor norm N_I over arbitrarily many input roots. That part remains open even after admission of this crossing record's physical fields.

**A second rough history (P, ordinary merger at the first return).** Place two of the Cartan seeds from §4.16 on aligned blocks whose centers are separated by about 14MR_eσ_e along one axis and are aligned in the transverse axes. Keep the same constant-R weak interval, with the containing torus large in entry lattice units. The two fine supports are disjoint, their straight averages each vanish, and their coarse endpoint gauges can be extended disjointly. At ordinary steps through e−1 their padded rectangles remain disjoint: their sizes in e-units are bounded by CMR_e/L while the chosen separation is a fixed multiple of MR_e. The final ten-layer covers overlap, since each extends by at least 10MR_eσ_e along this axis. With the transverse alignments their convex rectangular completion has side below 100MR_eσ_e; coarse-cover rounding costs at most two additional MR_e blocks. No new bad field is inspected inside the inherited regions, and no further P,Q,R is created outside them. Thus the same N-step ordinary criterion gives an eligible merged component at the first return. These constructions are stable under small changes that keep the finite strict selector inequalities; a configuration on a delta-constrained retained fiber is interpreted in the relative support of that fiber.

Choose the crossing pair of birth cubes near the *outer end face* of the merged rectangle. In that neighborhood the eight- and ten-layer fronts are exactly those of the nearer inherited component, so its full recorded stencils are in Ω_e. Both smaller integrated rough cores are now present in the containing first-join region. Equations (4.38)–(4.43) apply to this older readset, uniformly on the documented original LFR wall family, with the same constants. This example uses the whole merged determining record and the genuine changed-base fields of (1.55),(1.61)–(1.63). A carrier reaching either interior pinned seed does not satisfy the top-region hypothesis. At the physical parameter endpoint its fully pinned seed plaquette has c_Δ of order ε₀ on a fine cell, so the contribution σ_Δ^{4−2p}c_Δ^p cannot be bounded by a coarse ceiling merely by refining the artificial collar. This fact demonstrates why extending the proof to the rough interior requires proving that the *actual saved record* reads that plaquette, or proving a different aggregate estimate there. It does not construct such an interior-read old clean coefficient.

**Proposition 4.26 (P/L, first touch of an unchanged complete readset).** Consider the same matched first output p=k. Let an older determining record and its *complete* exterior, raw word descendants and incident stars be disjoint from Z_{e−1}, with the immediately preceding ordinary survival and no relocation of that readset. Then the whole record is in Λ_{e−1}⊂Ω_{e−1}. The original Ω_{e−1} erosion leaves the native cube room used in (1.67). There are at most two original layers on this readset, m=e−1,e. For m=e−1 the factor L₀^{2(j−m)} in (1.66) is L₀² and the physical curvature/current conversions cost respectively L² and L³. All are fixed constants. Thus (4.38) holds on such a record with a changed fixed constant. This assertion concerns the original layers at the fine bonds being tested, even if the localized older variational problem uses many auxiliary meshes.

For an old hierarchy satisfying the original CMP102 admissibility hypotheses, its real and small-complex target tests (7),(172) follow here without one global gauge on a possibly long carrier. Indeed every individual old plaquette and its complete averaging descendants fit in its own top or adjacent-top regular cube. Use the real representative U of the LFR output decomposition. The real tree argument of (4.33) bounds the completed h-plaquette of m_old U by Cτ_eσ_h², and the analytic raw mean comparison bounds |Mʰ(Γ)Mʰ(U)⁻¹−1| by Cτ_eσ_h, in entry units. Both estimates are unaffected by the compact gauge chosen on that row. At an incomplete plaquette one first uses the exact finer-row completion of Proposition4.16. The original exterior is included in these tests; if a free bond has an exterior stencil outside the protected region this proposition does not apply. With max_hσ_h≤r, take ε₁=C rτ_e in the source theorem. It tends to zero along the weak trajectory and contains both tests with the original fixed constants. Theorem1 and Proposition9 therefore give the analytic old critical branch for the actual raw target throughout the documented family, regardless of how many such row patches are needed. Holomorphy is a property of the branch as a function of the raw target, by Proposition9 and (181); the auxiliary real comparisons need not form a holomorphic family. The estimates (4.39) apply at fixed birth depth. Where a single enclosing regular cube and the stronger fixed spacing test hold, (4.41) gives the physical estimates at arbitrary birth depth. Native existence on a longer covered record by itself leaves the absolute marked and common-gauge bounds to be proved.

### 4.19 A continuously live birth record at its first completed return

**Proposition 4.27 (P/L, physical obstruction in the genuine core).** Take a fixed first-step birth box X with a level-one plaquette P and its complete fine descendant words in its two-layer interior. There is an ordinary history with Λ₁ equal to the torus, a new Q₂ component containing X, and no subsequent new defects through its first eligible return k=2+R₂, on which the following hold. Four level-one rows of the actual first output (1.61) retain a compact coarse plaquette of size at least c t, where t=g₁¹⁰. The original q_old of X retains the same four rows. At the physical real endpoint both the first output Γ and every real old reconstruction W on that record satisfy, for p>8,

\[
 \sum_{\Delta\subset D}\sigma_\Delta^{4-2p}c_\Delta(\Xi)^p
 \ \ge\ c_{L,N,p}\,\eta^{4-2p} t^p,\qquad
 \Xi=\Gamma\ \text{or}\ W,\qquad\eta=L^{-k}.                 \tag{4.44}
\]

Here D includes the complete tested descendants, and η is in *return* units. This lower bound holds in particular in a unit return box containing those descendants. Along the weak trajectory it exceeds every fixed multiple of the real return ceiling τ_k^p, or a complex return ceiling with fixed logarithmic exponent. It rules out an extension of the return-unit collar bound (4.37) over a continuously live genuine core. For LM47 the first mixed touch of this record is b=2; its regular pre-entry age is b−1, not k−1.

**Construction through the selectors.** Choose one Cartan generator T of norm one, and put an angle tT times the oriented link boundary of P on the level-one lattice, with zero on the other links. Put P in the interior of one level-two block and away from the boundary of X. The straight normalized block mean of this cochain vanishes, since the two links in each direction occur at equal longitudinal height with opposite signs. For commuting fields in the logarithmic mean the endpoint paths give an exact coarse gradient, as in §4.16. Extend its inverse nodal gauge inside the affected coarse blocks. The resulting level-one field Ṽ₁ has M(Ṽ₁)=1, all angles O_L(t), and Ṽ₁(∂P)=exp(4itT) up to orientation. Now apply one compact nodal gauge of angle 3.5δ₁T at a nonpivot interior site, identity at all level-two nodes. Denote the result by V₁. Choose a nonpivot incident bond in the supremum of CMP119 (3.3). Then M(V₁)=1, the same plaquette has norm distance ≥ct from identity, and that bond has norm distance >2δ₁. The axial path variables of the block have norm distance ≤3.5δ₁+C_Lt<ε₁, with the original large ratio A₀/A₁. One can place the two compact supports in the interior of the same partition cube. The construction uses the soft axial cutoff of (1.5), which admits this gauge spike.

At the preceding step use the exact simple lift Q^{s*}V₁: internal block bonds equal identity and each crossing bond equals its prescribed coarse bond. Its raw mean is V₁ and the fine axial path variables equal identity. Undo the block-constant extension of the nodal gauge just used. All its physical fine plaquettes then have norm deviation O_L(t), hence the original P₀ test is small. In the same gauge the simple lift and the first-step global and regional axial minima are within C_L t of identity on each tested link. This follows from CMP102 Proposition 9 at identity for the one-step target O_L(t); the source cap (173),(19) is independent of the number of blocks. Equivalently the real regional equation has the small lift as its feasible reference and a bounded one-step inverse. Restoring the compact block-constant gauge transforms both fields together, as stated just after CMP119 (1.17). Thus the first-step P₁, Q₁ cutoffs are small. On the nonpivot independent bonds the true scaled fluctuation has size at most C_L t/g₀, below the cutoff in (1.18) since t=g₁¹⁰ and g₀/g₁ tends to one. This verifies Λ₁=T and permits the clean birth record X there.

Put V_m=1 for m≥2. These values satisfy the retained averages including M(V₁)=V₂. For step two the regional comparison U_{2,□′}(1)=1 on each test cube, so (3.2) has no defect and (3.3) records a genuine Q₂ failure at the chosen bond. The other coarse-angle changes of order t cannot cause a remote failure. The underlying retained level-one target, after undoing the compact gauge, differs by O_L(t) at finitely many bonds and all other targets equal identity. Its completed native target plaquettes in CMP102 (7) have size O_L(t), including the interfaces: the entire perturbation fits in the interior Q₂ patch. The same assertion holds on every subsequently prescribed ordinary hierarchy. Thus the regular real minimal orbit exists there by CMP102 Theorem 1 for the ordinary separated native layers. Its original top-layer regularity is within the fixed ceiling used in CMP119 (3.12).

There is a more direct check of the later R tags than comparing gauges of a global minimum point by point. On each cube where a new P or Q is inspected, the regional test (3.2)–(3.4) with V_{m+1}=1 has the identity background; V_m=1 there for m≥2. At step two the same assertion holds outside the spike. On an interior test cube of (3.16), the retained local comparison V^{(m)}_{□′} is the identity and the actual V_m is identity there. CMP119 (3.17)–(3.19) bound the *global* comparison to this local one by C(1+β₀)ε_m exp(−R_m), with the actual localized gauge included. At step two any such test cube touching a nonzero link is either excluded by Ω₂ or the link can be placed strictly inside the Q₂ patch. Thus the unscaled logarithm in (3.14) is ≤C ε_m exp(−R_m). The linearizing map changes only the pivot bonds, by the paragraph after (1.14), and (3.16) tests the independent bonds. Even reading (3.16) literally as a cutoff δ_m on the *scaled* variable, its size is bounded by C g_m⁻¹ ε_m exp(−R_m)<δ_m. Indeed R_m≥(log x_m)^u, u≥2 and x_m=g_m⁻², while ε_m and δ_m differ only by their fixed ratio. This proves that these actual fluctuation selectors have no further failures. It also explains why replacing the true fluctuation everywhere by zero would not establish the claimed history.

Use the rounded weak-coupling plateau and width recurrence in §4.16, with its single initial component now at scale two. The small initial component remains rectangular of width <100MR_m. The first ordinary waiting criterion in LFR II, printed p.384 before (1.80), is met after N=R₂ further steps. There is room on the fixed physical torus since k grows polylogarithmically in x₂, while the number of available ultraviolet steps grows on the scale x₂. The return has flat current coarse outside data. The extension V_Λ=1 passes (1.75); B′=0 passes (1.82). We take the unweakened real parameter values. All original strict selector inequalities in this construction have margins. The retained constrained values are interpreted on their actual fibers.

**Rows retained by the return and by the old readout.** Write h=k−N=2. In LFR I (1.10)–(1.17), the original Ω_i with i≤h are unchanged; no preliminary variable on Γ₁ is integrated. The region Ω₃ᶜ contains the eight-layer ordinary cover of Z₂ from CMP119 (3.2)–(3.5). Inside the chosen component, LFR I (1.11) sets Z″₃=(Ω₃^{~5})ᶜ. Taking off the additional two native layers in (1.61) still leaves the central original Q₂ patch in (Ω″₃^{~2})ᶜ. Here the tilde operations are those of these native cubes; the two old bond stars are chosen strictly interior to the seed region. The four level-one bonds of P belong to Γ₁=(Ω₁∖Ω₂)^{(1)} there. They lie in Y₁⁰, away from the artificial two-layer seam since the localization domain contains the entire returning component. Consequently the last V″ piece of (1.61) prescribes exactly V₁ on them. In its physical axial representative,

\[
 M^1(\Gamma)|_{\partial P}=V_1|_{\partial P},\qquad
 M^1(W)|_{\partial P}=M^1(\Gamma)|_{\partial P}.             \tag{4.45}
\]

In a different admitted representative both plaquettes are conjugated at their basepoint. The second equality uses the actual first-step old variational constraint on the interior of X. Its free real branch exists in this fixed-depth box. For example apply the curvature row of LFR I (1.65)–(1.69) to the joined output on its original fine layer in native units. The allowed L₀ factor is at most C R_k as explained on printed p.192. The resulting bound on each native plaquette is a fixed logarithmic power times g₁, tending to zero. Each old row, its fine completion and its exterior live in this fixed birth patch. Tree gauge on that patch gives small native plaquettes for the completed real old target, including the exterior, by the fixed-depth word estimate (4.33). CMP102 (7) and Theorem 1 give the old real orbit. This argument uses smallness in *old* units only. It does not identify the orbit with a different localized minimum.

Let d_f be the maximum fine plaquette deviation on all complete fine descendant blocks of ∂P, including the one-block rectangular filling and its required exterior, within X. When d_f is below a fixed constant, compact axial tree gauge on this fixed L-sized contractible patch gives fine links within C_L d_f of identity. The original raw logarithmic one-step mean and its endpoint words are uniformly Lipschitz there, and so its coarse plaquette differs from identity by at most C_L d_f. If d_f is above that fixed constant the same lower conclusion follows immediately. By (4.45), d_f≥c_L t for each of Γ,W. On the real unitary group every logarithm of the plaquette in its admitted curvature chart has norm at least its norm distance from identity. One fine plaquette in (AE8.7) therefore costs at least c η⁴(t/η²)^p, proving (4.44) with the actual disjoint cell assignment. With k=2+R₂, the factor L^{(2p−4)R₂} dominates every fixed power of x₂ and log x₂, while g_k/g₂ stays bounded by the original finite-step running law on this interval. This proves the ceiling comparison. ∎

**What is tested.** X is an allowed clean birth determining record and its actual oriented old coarse plaquette is retained. The obstruction holds for the field readout on this record independently of the numerical value of a coefficient filed there. No nonzero value for a particular sourced coefficient is inferred. In birth units, the one-plaquette lower bound is of order t, which is strictly inside ρ₁. Thus (4.44) distinguishes a failed return-unit M8 extension from LM47 at the true first touch and from an admission construction that keeps the old marked record in its native coordinates through the live residence. A later R-domain's bank and that record's protected pre-entry age have different origins.

### 4.20 All-wall screening by the actual retained one-step rows

**Proposition 4.28 (P/L, native readmission inside a continuously live core).** In the interior geometry of Proposition4.27 let D₁ be a rectangular union of level-one blocks about the complete old X. Take its width a fixed positive fraction of MR₂ in level-one units, small enough that the one-step stencils of all blocks in D₁ and a fixed neighboring collar belong to the original Γ₁ piece of (1.61). This choice exists by the eight-, five- and two-layer calculation in §4.19. Fix the real retained core variables on the fiber of that proposition. Let F₁ be the full ordinary first-step critical configuration with the same retained V₁. On the full original external variables and walls of (1.61)–(1.65), one can make one joint holomorphic choice of relative fine block-axial gauges, with uniformly bounded adjoints, in which

\[
 \|\Gamma-F_1\|_{X^+}+\|J_{\rm old}(\Gamma)-J_{\rm old}(F_1)\|_{X^+}
 \le C_L e^{-c_L d_1(X^+,\partial D_1)} E_1.                 \tag{4.46}
\]

Here the fine mesh in old units equals 1/L, X⁺ includes its complete old current and exterior stencils, and E₁≤C_L(g₁(1+log x₁)^P+t) is an actual native bound; P depends on the fixed source exponents only. On this whole old record W is gauge equivalent to Γ. For the retained V₁ of §4.19, the field and physical current in a joint local birth chart are bounded by C_X(t+e^{-c_LMR₂}E₁). The common physical chart is strictly inside the M8.16 radius ρ₁. This is positive admission in the birth units of an actual previously live core. It applies also to each additional source wall that changes only the marked function evaluated on this preparation. The counterexample (4.44) simultaneously holds at the real endpoint in return units.

**Which target is held fixed.** By LFR II (1.33)–(1.35), the *new* determining set in Z has only k-rows. Its old Γ₁ variables in the last piece of (1.61) belong to the integrated A,B,V″ record. They are not rows of the outer field. Printed p.377 immediately above (1.68) explicitly retains localized A,B,V″ in addition to the analytically extended new field (U,J). In particular the replacement by corresponding M^•(U) at the new outer rows in (1.63)–(1.65) leaves these old core integration variables as parameters. The mixed representation (1.46) also displays the V″ argument separately. Thus on D₁ the output Γ is the actual Wilson critical point, subject to exactly the same level-one target as F₁, throughout the original external domain. The changes passing through (1.55),(1.62),(1.63) can affect D₁ through the solution on its boundary. No bounds on external variables outside the documented member domain are used. If one varies the retained V₁ itself, the comparison on the right must be made with F₁ of that same V₁.

**Native coordinates on the whole interior.** Undo the one compact nodal spike jointly by its block-constant fine extension. Use in each fine block the rooted axial tree, identity at its coarse node, on both configurations. The gauge is the product of the actual fine links along that tree and so is analytic on the complete parameter domain. On D₁ the old mesh is fixed, every tree has bounded length Ld and (1.65) places each complex fine link in a native small relative neighborhood of a compact link. Hence the products and inverses are bounded. An internal nontree link is the product of a fixed number of transported plaquettes.
