Two parallel crossing links of one face differ, up to those internal links, by a strip of at most C_L plaquettes. The logarithmic one-step mean at equal crossing links is that link and its derivative in their common direction is the identity. The compact/complex Lipschitz estimate on the finitely many normalized words now shows that each crossing link is within C_L times the native plaquette bound of its actual retained link. In this gauge V₁ has norm distance O_L(t) from identity. The curvature and complex-part row of LFR I (1.65)(i) on m=1 gives E₁ after conversion to birth units: k₀=k−N₀≫1, so the exponent max(0,m−k₀) in that row is zero. Using the weaker C R_k enlargement would also be enough here. All fine coordinates in D₁ are therefore small uniformly. On the full first-step torus the similarly gauged F₁ obeys the sharper O_L(t) bound by CMP102 Proposition9 at the identity for this target of size O_L(t). The common gauge used in the estimate is a section of the original fine gauge orbits. Returning to another representative transports the exterior and endpoint frames at the same time. In particular no old boundary condition has been freely changed.

Use independent fine coordinates in this tree section. There is one pivot for each coarse face row, as in CMP119 after (1.14). Distinct faces use distinct crossing bonds; a raw coarse row uses the interiors of its two endpoint blocks and its own face. At identity the straight normalized mean contains its pivot with a positive coefficient depending only on L. The derivative of one face row in the pivot of a different face is zero. The analytic implicit function theorem eliminates the physical pivot exactly as a function of the independent links in these two blocks and their fixed row. Its neighborhood and all derivatives through order three are uniform, because each neighborhood has fixed size and the computed pivot derivative is bounded below. Reconstruction, including the true endpoint words, is finite range. Restrict the Wilson action by this actual coordinate map, dropping tree coordinates only. Denote its Hessian at flat data by H₀, in first-step native units.

There are constants 0<c_L<C_L independent of D₁ and of the torus such that c_L I≤H₀≤C_L I. For the lower bound consider a tangent with all tree links zero and all coarse differentials zero. Every internal link is bounded by the finite sum of curls on a filling of its tree loop in that block. The difference of two crossing links on a face is bounded by curls in the two adjoining blocks plus the just bounded internal links. Its zero coarse row says that their normalized weighted average, plus a bounded linear combination of internal links, is zero. Consequently every crossing link is bounded by the sum of curls in those blocks. Square, sum and use fixed overlap to get ∑|a_b|²≤C_L∑|da_p|². The restricted flat Wilson Hessian is a fixed positive multiple of this curvature energy. The independent coordinates are a subset of the fine coordinates and the local pivot inverse has bounded norm. This proves the lower bound and the finite-range upper bound. Extension of independent coordinates by zero proves the same lower bound for every principal compression. This argument uses the physical constrained Hessian; an unconstrained axial Wilson Hessian need not have this gap.

**Boundary estimate.** Interpolate in the independent coordinates of F₁ and Γ on D₁, keeping all actual coarse target rows fixed. The interpolation need not be a critical field. Its coordinates and the reconstructed pivots remain uniformly small. The local Hessians on this segment differ from H₀ by at most C_L E₁ in row and column sum, by finite range and the third-derivative bound. Let I be the indices in D₁ with their full finite dependency neighborhoods in D₁. The compression H_{II} of the averaged Hessian has smallest singular value at least c_L/2. Both endpoint Euler derivatives vanish at every such index: its compactly supported variation preserves the actual q₁ targets and all the global joined rows. The difference u of their independent coordinates therefore solves H_{II}u_I=−H_{I,Iᶜ}u_{Iᶜ}. The right hand side is supported in a fixed-width boundary annulus and has sup norm at most C_L E₁.

For each boundary index z, conjugate H_{II} by the weight exp(c dist(·,z)) in the ordinary level-one block distance. Finite range and the bound on both absolute matrix sums give a perturbation of operator norm at most C_L(exp(c C_L)−1). Choosing c>0 so this is below c_L/4 gives |(H_{II}^{−1})_{yz}|≤C_L exp(−c dist(y,z)), also for the complex symmetric Hessians here. Summing over the boundary sites costs at most a polynomial in the distance on the four-dimensional lattice, absorbed by reducing c. The same bound holds on the finite periodic torus, since a ball contains at most C_L(1+r)^4 sites. This proves the potential estimate on X⁺. Reconstruct each physical pivot by its actual analytic local map and compare the two Wilson current words, with all incident plaquettes and exterior. At the fixed fine mesh 1/L these are uniformly Lipschitz operations, proving (4.46). The small inverse and the decay rate require only the fixed one-scale curl gap; no weakened-rate mixed-mesh spacing test occurs in this estimate.

**The old fiber.** Embed an old admissible variation on X into the large determining problem by leaving its prescribed exterior untouched. Every genuine Γ₁ row wholly in the old interior is fixed. In the artificial collar the missing level-one rows are reconstructed from its complete fixed fine children by Proposition4.16; rows crossing X use those same fine children together with their prescribed exterior. The larger rows that can see the variation in this interior slab are precisely these level-one rows. Thus the embedded variation preserves all constraints. Since Γ is critical, its restriction is critical for the old regional action with that exterior. Its smallness, the old target test (7),(172) and uniqueness in the analytic regular class of Theorem1/Proposition9 identify it with P_old q_oldΓ, up to the actual fixed-boundary gauge. This argument uses one common constrained field on X and its full collar. A comparison among independent root-dependent minima would not have this property.

The error in (4.46) is exponentially small in MR₂. The direct flat first-step term is O_L(t) in any fixed birth-sized patch after transporting its fixed exterior, and its finite-difference physical current is O_L(t) there. Analytic tree coordinates and all original finite birth-unit Sobolev norms on X have fixed equivalence constants because the old depth and X are fixed. The bound divided by ρ₁ tends to zero, giving the asserted margin. At b=2 the canonical conversion has the original bounded factor L and the pre-entry age b−1. Holomorphic Cauchy estimates apply to each fixed finite set of derivatives on its actual larger discs. No count of arbitrary marked derivative anchors or accumulated live normalization is implicit in (4.46). ∎

**Extension to nonflat old compact regular data (P/L).** Keep this same fully retained deep one-step slab and Λ₁=T, but allow V₁ from a general clean first step. The original test on each regional first minimum controls its fine plaquettes by C_L ε₁ there. The actual coarse mean plaquette, being the mean of that same regional configuration on its prescribed row, is ≤C_L ε₁ by (4.33) with j=1. Thus the full real V₁ satisfies the actual CMP102 test (7), including every row of this full first-step torus. Its global ordinary F₁(V₁) exists, and (8)–(10) give curvature and current O_L(ε₁) in birth units. In the rectangular D₁ place V₁ in a compact coarse tree gauge. Each resulting coarse link lies within C_L diam(D₁) ε₁ of identity by a strip filling. This still tends to zero because diam(D₁)=O(MR₂) is only a fixed logarithmic power. Use its block-constant extension jointly on Γ and F₁ and their original fixed exterior. The same small Hessian perturbation argument applies with E₁ enlarged by C_L MR₂ ε₁. After (4.46), measure the original saved local bound on X⁺ in its own fixed-size transported compact tree. F₁ there has potential and physical current bounded by C_X ε₁; the new localized difference stays exponentially smaller. Consequently this entire retained deep subset has strict physical birth-radius margin for ρ₁=c₁g₁(log x₁)^q with q>p₀. The compact real comparison may depend on the retained real V₁, which is a parameter of integration. It is fixed as a function of the complex external walls. Uniform holomorphicity of all derivatives in a varying complex V₁ outside its actual original stored discs, or an unlimited sum of source anchors, is not claimed.

### 4.21 Absolute derivative kernels of the retained core

**Proposition 4.29 (P, original level-one core rows).** Take the true local independent coordinates of §4.20. In the Euler map on I use as coordinates the actual level-one target rows v in the retained slab and the fine independent boundary data b from the real or complex critical representative on its neighboring annulus. Let Ψ denote its solution and its local current in X. For each fixed order m≥1, each fixed moment order p and 0≤κ<c_L/4 (decreasing c_L once for graph-range constants), the physical derivative coefficients satisfy

\[
 \sup_x\sum_{a_1,\ldots,a_m}e^{\kappa\tau(x,a)}(1+R(x,a))^p
 \big|\partial_{a_1}\cdots\partial_{a_m}\Psi_x\big|\le C_{L,m,p}.
                                                               \tag{4.47}
\]

Here a runs through the actual v,b slots, x is a bond/current root in the slab, and τ is the spanning-tree length of the displayed slots in the old block metric. R is the maximum distance from x. If at least one input is a boundary slot, the left side at x gains a factor exp(−c′_L dist(x,∂I)). Physical angles are the input units. Replacing each input angle by g₁ times its canonical variable multiplies the mth bound by g₁^m. For a canonically normalized old output one divides by its actual g₁. These results are uniform at the wall points used in Proposition4.28. A required fixed weight of the stored-record norm must lie in the displayed rate range; a weight choice outside this range cannot be inferred from the proof.

**Proof.** At the actual solution the inverse interior Hessian has the exponential coefficient estimate proved for (4.46); the same proof applies to the Hessian at that point itself. On the uniform small field chart, the differentiated reduced Euler residual of order d uses only coordinates within a fixed distance C_L, with bounded coefficients through order m. This follows from the local analytic pivot reconstruction and the original finite Wilson words including their exterior. The first response is a sum of an inverse-Hessian kernel times such a local derivative. For the mth response differentiate the same equation m times. Its term containing the unknown mth derivative is H_{II} times that derivative. Every other term is an actual multilinear residual derivative at one local cluster, applied to derivatives of order strictly less than m or to a direct target or boundary slot. Solve by the inverse Hessian and iterate this finite recursion. Each term is a connected tree of local vertices joined by kernels C_L exp(−c_L d), with a finite combinatorial constant depending on m. Identical input indices retain their ordinary derivative multiplicities.

For fixed x and arbitrary input sites, the displayed spanning-tree length is at most twice the length of such a tree before suppressing its internal vertices: traverse each edge twice and shortcut by the metric. Local vertices add at most C_{L,m}. The polynomial moment is bounded by a constant times a polynomial in the sum of the edge lengths. After paying the weight in (4.47), each inverse edge still has summable exponential decay on the old four-dimensional lattice. Sum the vertex and input sites along the tree, from its leaves to the root. If a boundary slot is present there is a path from x to that boundary of at least dist(x,∂I)−C_{L,m}; retaining a further fixed fraction of decay on this path gives the extra factor. Current words and the recovery of eliminated pivots are additional bounded finite-range vertices. This proves the asserted absolute coefficients without converting a directional multilinear norm into a sum of absolute coefficients. ∎

For the actual outer variables, the boundary inputs in this proposition are the boundary traces of the joined field, and the chain rule inserts their own derivative kernels. Formula (4.47) supplies the internal factor and its suppression before those exterior kernels are summed. It proves arbitrary fixed joint derivatives in actual retained v and marked boundary slots viewed as the inputs of this regional map. It leaves the outer kernels of the intervening joins, and any weak derivatives of the inherited sharp selector, intact. In particular no original fine or coarse boundary labels have been collapsed into one scalar variable.

### 4.22 One common constrained field across the artificial seam

**Proposition 4.30 (P, exact fiber inclusion on the protected crossing patch).** In the one-component case of §4.16 take a fixed current-sized open patch about a flat face of I=Y₁^{~−2} well away from its corners and from ∂Y₁. Make it small enough to lie in the previous ordinary-survival front and in the original top or immediately adjacent regular layer. Every small old X wholly in this patch lies in Z, so its smallest Y₁ in (1.61) equals the same component Z. On its entire fixed-depth completed record and exterior,

\[
 W_X=P_{\rm old,X}q_{\rm old,X}\Gamma=\Gamma|_X
                                                               \tag{4.48}
\]

in its fixed-boundary orbit, simultaneously for these X. This holds on the whole original member domain, including the changed-base targets in (1.61)–(1.63). Thus the potential and current in the trace of each such old gauge-invariant coefficient come from one common configuration. The assertion applies in particular to j₀=1 and fixed finite path stencils within this patch.

**Proof.** Allow an admissible old variation in X with its full exterior fixed. The old interior rows fix every original j₀-average there. Where the old boundary construction instead fixes finer rows, completing their original children reconstructs the j₀-averages, by Proposition4.16 with its actual endpoint paths. Outside X the fine links do not move. Thus every global j₀ row that can be affected is fixed. In the crossing geometry the complete stencils of any joined row meeting this support have terminal depth at least e−1: on the interior side this is the original top portion, while D_e contains I and D_{e−1} has the buffer of Proposition4.11 at the artificial seam. The physical support of one iterated averaging row is within a fixed multiple of its terminal size by (3.32); choose the fixed interior patch and original M₁ buffer to contain these complete neighborhoods. If that geometric allowance fails for a different choice of constants this proof applies only where the complete stencils satisfy the indicated two-layer test. The original coarse rows of depth ≥j₀ compute their value from the now fixed j₀-averages, with their complete exterior. No finer unconstrained joined row meets the variation. The embedded old trial therefore preserves the full joined constraint, so the actual joined Wilson critical field is critical on the old fiber with the prescribed exterior. The already verified real/complex small old branch (4.39) and native uniqueness in that class give (4.48), including physical current words of bonds whose full stars were retained. Different axial sections conjugate all stored endpoints and exterior together. ∎

In this patch the artificial inside/collar split does not create two original old coefficients or two return fields. If a translation-covariant quadratic kernel with fixed rooting phase is actually filed across these roots, both sides have the same birth, the same true first touch e and the same (1.61) readout. A smooth current-unit localization supported in this patch is allowed in evaluating the sum of both assignments. This observation does not combine an original fixed sharp source with its complement. At the genuine outside boundary of Z, disjoint old X can follow a different branch and (4.48) does not identify the two fields there.

**Quantitative part for the all-wall complex field (P).** On a smaller fixed patch write F for the based fine plaquette logarithm in entry units. In a common transported local gauge its increments obey, for each γ<1,

\[
 |F(x)-F(y)|\le C_\gamma\tau_e |x-y|^\gamma.                 \tag{4.49}
\]

The same estimate, up to C_{L,γ}τ_e r^γ, applies to the curvature F₁= r⁻² log M¹(Γ)(∂p) of the old depth-one rows and their bounded-length old path transports at separations of order r. Constants are independent of the age in this crossing example.

To see (4.49), take an ordered fine-lattice tree in a fixed rectangular enlargement of the patch on which (4.38) holds, with an extra incident-star collar. Path products of Γ and their inverses are bounded there: the original relative decomposition has potential O(τ_e) in entry units and a compact real part. Products over distances O(1) therefore have bounded adjoints. Filling each tree loop by its plaquette strip gives fine links exp(η A) with |A|≤Cτ_e in this one joint analytic gauge. All estimates use the same transformed physical current, whose norm is ≤Cτ_e by (4.38). Let d,δ be the ordinary cubical exterior difference and its adjoint at spacing η. The elementary ordered cube identity gives |dF|≤Cτ_e². Indeed the transported six face logarithms cancel linearly; returning them to a common untransported frame costs O(η) times A times F, and the quadratic face word costs O(η) times F² after the difference is normalized. For δF use the Wilson current variation: projected curvature differs from F by O(η⁴τ_e³), the two transported differences differ from ordinary differences by O(τ_e²), and a fine difference of the first error costs at most 2/η. Thus |δF|≤Cτ_e. These bounds use ordinary differences of the matrix coefficients in this fixed gauge. With a smooth cutoff equal to one on the inner patch, both d(χF) and δ(χF) are bounded by Cτ_e.

Extend χF by zero to the fine infinite lattice. By the cubical Hodge identity, each coefficient of χF is a sum of convolutions of these two forms with a first difference of the scalar lattice Green kernel. The kernels with one or two differences have bounds C(|x|+η)^{−3} and C(|x|+η)^{−4}. For completeness the Fourier multiplier has denominator 4η⁻²Σ sin²(ηp_μ/2). On a dyadic frequency annulus of scale μ≤C/η its differentiated symbol of order a=1,2 has derivative bounds C_b μ^{a−2−b}. Integrating by parts in the periodic Fourier integral yields C_J μ^{2+a}(1+μ|x|)^{−J}; summing the annuli proves the displayed kernel bounds, including the top periodic band. For a displacement h, sum the first kernel absolutely within 2|h| to get C|h|, and use |h| times the second bound farther away. On the bounded support this costs C|h|(1+log(1/|h|)) for η≤|h|≤1/2. This proves (4.49).

At one old plaquette, make a separate compact-sized tree with root in the preceding gauge on its fixed L-block stencil just for the local word computation. Its nontree link logarithms are sums of the transported fine face logarithms with finitely many coefficients, plus O_L(r⁴τ_e²); this follows by filling their finite tree loops. Expanding the actual raw mean and its coarse plaquette on these links gives the same translation-invariant finite linear combination of fine curvatures at every old root, plus O_L(r²τ_e²) in curvature units. The transport back to the common entry chart changes the neighboring fine values by at most C_L r τ_e² since |A|≤Cτ_e. The oscillation of that combination at bounded old displacements is O_{L,γ}(τ_e r^γ) by (4.49); the actual short old adjoint path costs the same or less. This proves the asserted increment bound without replacing the original mean by a straight noncovariant readout.

**Sharp boundary term (P).** Consider a scalar even finite-range reversible quadratic kernel K(h) with ΣK=0, a fixed symmetric invariant bilinear form B and reversed translated paths for opposite h. Put f for any actual root assignment and T_h for the based transported shift. Algebra on one common physical field gives

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \sum_{x,h} f_x K(h)B(F_x,T_hF_x)
  ={}&-\tfrac12\sum_{x,h} f_xK(h)B(T_hF_x-F_x,T_hF_x-F_x)\\
    &+\tfrac12\sum_x (K*f)_x B(F_x,F_x).
 \end{aligned}                    \tag{4.50}
\]

The sums are on the complete lattice with the actual support cutoffs included; f may be extended by zero. The formula follows by expanding the square, using invariance under the actual group transport and shifting the translated norms. It holds for the complex bilinear extension as well. With fixed bounded displacements, a birth-sized face slab contains O(r⁻³) old roots. The old physical F brings r² per factor, followed by the separate r⁻² dimension-six normalizer. Hence (4.49) gives for the squared-difference sum on the slab the bound C g₁⁻² τ_e² r^{2γ−1} at its original quadratic grade. For any saved s<1 there is γ<1 with 2γ−1>s, absorbing the finite logarithmic costs on this ordinary waiting trajectory. The second line of (4.50) is an actual boundary-test term with no squared increment. Under a split f=f_inside+f_collar of the *same* stored root family, its two contributions sum to K*f; the artificial cut does not survive. This equality does not estimate K multiplied by an arbitrarily different test on just one side. A full sharp native boundary partner must be kept with that row.

### 4.23 A full reversible quadratic age on the commuting part of the rough crossing

**Proposition 4.31 (P, actual Cartan first return with a smooth root test).** In the protected patch of Proposition4.30 choose the Cartan sector of its original real and complex variables, leaving all scalar native preparation walls available. Take a smooth root test f supported in a strictly smaller patch and a finite-range even tensor kernel K whose zeroth and second translation moments vanish (componentwise after the original fixed contraction). Use one fixed coarse rooting phase and the same kernel on both sides of the artificial seam. Then for j₀=1, r=L^{1−e} and every v<1/2, the original reversible quadratic expression satisfies

\[
 \left|g_1^{-2}r^{-2}\langle fF_1, K F_1\rangle_r\right|
 \le C_{L,v,f,K}\,g_1^{-2}\tau_e^2 r^{2v}.                 \tag{4.51}
\]

The inner product is the physical lattice sum with cell volume r⁴ and the fixed invariant trace pairing. This includes, for example, the fourth-difference row with weights 1,−4,6,−4,1 along one actual coarse direction. Since 2v can exceed any fixed s<1, (4.51) retains the scalar stable quadratic age and coupling group (g_e/g₁)² on this rough history, with the spare power absorbing the prescribed finite logarithmic factors. The assertion is for an old reversible row having these actual moments and phase; it does not assert that an arbitrary stored old insertion has this form. No I-1 estimate is used.

**Proof.** Choose diagonal retained configurations and independent currents and diagonal compact comparison fields. The original gauge-covariant branch is invariant under every constant diagonal conjugation by uniqueness of its axial representative. Its links, averaged links and currents therefore lie in the common complex Cartan group. In this sector the logarithmic local mean is exactly the linear mean of the angles, with its raw endpoint gradient restored. Parallel transport of a Cartan curvature is trivial. The elementary cube relation gives dF=0 on a small common chart.

We give the extra fractional estimate needed to sum the full smooth patch. On the joined minimizing fiber, the physical Wilson current annihilates variations in the kernel of the actual differential of the retained means. In the Cartan slot it is consequently the transpose of that finite-dimensional differential applied to a multiplier. This assertion is linear algebra on the actual gauge quotient; no bound on a general multiplier inverse is necessary. The constraint is linear in Cartan logarithmic potentials. All fine site gradients are allowed as simultaneous gauge variations of action and targets. Therefore its multiplier pairs to zero with every permitted coarse gauge gradient, by extending the node values as in (4.32). In the protected patch the divergence-free pairing removes the two raw endpoint gradients exactly. More explicitly expand the raw row as its straight combined mean plus a coarse difference of one scalar prefix average at each node. In the full multiscale hierarchy several depths can share a node. The scalar prefix averages at such a node need not be identical for different depths. For a fine site in the patch one may instead retain these terms: in the Cartan case every prefix coefficient comes from a finite set of ordered coordinate segment sums in an aligned block, averaged over block sites. Along each varying coordinate its discrete adjoint consists of a box or a linear ramp on that block, times possible Kronecker constraints on the coordinates fixed to the node. Endpoint terms concentrated on lower-dimensional subsets cannot be called bounded-volume box functions without cancellation. The following depth separation avoids that issue completely.

Choose the protected patch of Proposition4.30. Every intermediate row relevant to its fine sites stops at depth e−1 or e. Since I⊂D_e and the separation between D_e and D_{e−1}ᶜ is at least M₁ in entry units, a fixed enlargement of this inner face patch avoids all smaller terminal cells under the same complete-stencil allowance. No single terminal depth across the entire seam is assumed.

For these two terminal depths use the sequential group-centered construction of §4.12 at *every* intermediate step. For levels h<e−1 every row relevant to the patch continues to the next level. Incorporate all fine prefix endpoint gauges into the evolving coordinates of that next-level field; gauge covariance carries the old gauge at common nodes forward by restriction. Only at level e−1 are some rows stopped. At a node incident to both stopping and continuing rows the accumulated level-(e−1) endpoint gauge is one and the same node variable, so its dual coefficient vanishes by the full joined gauge divergence there. The final single e−1→e step can have its own endpoint difference on the terminating e mesh. After expanding any uncanceled part at this *single* step it is a vector on the e−1 lattice, before the earlier straight adjoints. It therefore causes no narrow fine-scale prefix singularity. Equivalently, in the Cartan case first factor the actual fine variation into its straight mean up to level e−1 and the common level-(e−1) nodal gradient. On the continuing rows raw gauge covariance transports that gradient to its final endpoint nodes, and on the stopped rows it stays at its own nodes. The multiplier annihilates this one simultaneously induced gauge variation, since those node values extend to the fine lattice. The remaining dual input on the e−1 lattice includes both stopped rows and the actual last raw transpose of continuing rows. This proves that locally the fine current is the straight physical adjoint from one depth e−1 applied to some level-(e−1) cochain. A node gauge extension localized to the patch can be used on the infinite or periodic lattice; its full coarse support plus all relevant backward stencils are within the stipulated enlargement. This proof neither deletes the last raw row nor assumes that the two different terminal node prefix averages agree.

The combined straight mean is a box average and one parallel segment average by direct base-L digit expansion of its normalized one-step weights, as in the flat original CMP95 row (1.11). Its physical adjoint is constant across the transverse block slices and piecewise linear in the longitudinal direction, with breaks on a fixed number of grid offsets per coarse interval (a segment count changes linearly as its endpoints slide). Therefore on each line in a bounded patch the Cartan coordinate current has at most C_L such pieces. Its supremum is ≤Cτ_e by (4.38); its total variation per line, counting the jumps and restricting to the patch, is at most C_Lτ_e. This remains true for complex values, since each affine segment has variation bounded by twice its supremum. It yields, after a smooth cutoff,

\[
 \|J(\cdot+h)-J\|_{2}^{2}\le C_L\tau_e^2 |h|,\qquad
 \|J\|_{H^v}\le C_{L,v}\tau_e\quad (v<1/2).                 \tag{4.52}
\]

Indeed the first estimate follows by bounding one difference by its supremum in one factor and summing the telescoped absolute line variation in the other. The discrete fractional difference square with denominator |h|^{4+2v} is summable at h=0 after this gain. The estimate controls the actual physical current; there is no inferred bound on arbitrary coefficients of a poorly conditioned representation of the same piecewise function.

The physical Cartan divergence uses the projected sine of the fine curvature. Replacing it by F inside δ costs first difference and fractional norm at most Cη^{−1−v} times the O(η⁴τ_e³) uniform sine error on the bounded patch. Thus δF is bounded in H^v there. The local cutoff terms in d(χF),δ(χF) require F in H^v on a slightly larger patch, supplied by (4.49) for γ>v. The exact Fourier cubical Hodge identity then gives ∥χF∥_{H^{1+v}}≤C_{L,v}τ_e. On the fixed one-step Cartan average, the old plaquette curvature is a finite normalized linear combination of fine plaquettes in its complete stencil (the coarse curl kills the endpoint gradient). In local Fourier coordinates, decimation by fixed L folds at most L⁴ aliases into each coarse frequency; their physical frequencies dominate the wrapped coarse frequency up to a constant depending only on L. Cauchy–Schwarz and Parseval therefore carry this bound to χ′F₁ on the old mesh, taking the first cutoff equal to one on all required fine stencils.

Finally the even Fourier symbol satisfies |K̂(θ)|≤C|θ|^{2+2v} Σ_h |K(h)|(1+|h|)^{2+2v}, by Taylor expansion through order two, evenness and the moment hypotheses. The original smooth f and a larger cutoff equal to one on its complete finite stencil have bounded multiplication norms in H^{1+v}. Apply the Fourier multiplier inequality between the two H^{1+v} factors, retaining their real or complex trace pairing, to obtain (4.51). Every old root in this support has the same field by (4.48). On a one-sided sharp marked test, the additional term in (4.50) must instead be kept. ∎

### 4.24 Noncommuting wall fields and the fourth-difference Wilson row

**Proposition 4.32 (P, a local multiplier at the common intermediate scale).** Keep the protected patch of Proposition4.30 and enough of its fixed enlargement for the full backward stencils. Put t=1/L in entry units. All joined constraints meeting the patch factor through the actual intermediate raw field M^{e−1}(Γ) there. At every point of the original simultaneous domain its physical current on the smaller patch satisfies

\[
 \|\chi J_{\rm phys}(\Gamma)\|_{H^v}\le C_{L,v,\chi}\tau_e,
 \qquad 0<v<1/2.                                             \tag{4.53}
\]

This is for a fixed smooth cutoff with its full stencil in the protected patch. The bound uses the actual two terminal depths, either t itself or one further average, not a flat whole-torus replacement of the joined problem.

**A right inverse supported on one actual coarse face.** In the analytic tree gauge of (4.49) vary only the fine links crossing one oriented face between the two adjacent terminal t-blocks for a row c. Give each crossing link the same left group tangent θ in this frame. Other t-rows are unchanged: their complete two-block words do not cross this face. For the affected raw row the derivative in this tangent equals (1+E_c)θ with |E_c|≤C_L tτ_e. Here is a check independent of the number of fine steps. After coarsening to any intermediate spacing σ, the tangent is still supported on the links crossing the same large face. For each such output link, every normalized one-step comparing path crosses the face once, and both endpoint prefix paths remain within their endpoint blocks. At the identity the resulting derivative on face tangents is a positive average with total one (the derivative of the normalized log mean of coinciding values). The one-step derivative coefficients differ from these by C_L στ_e in absolute row sum, since all the actual inputs in its two blocks have angles O_L(στ_e). This last bound follows from the centered one-step iteration and its restored endpoint gauges in Proposition4.18 on the complete t-block stencil. If the incoming face tangent differs from θ by δ, the outgoing error is at most (1+C_Lστ_e)δ+C_Lστ_e|θ|. Summing the geometric sequence Σσ≤C_L t proves the estimate, with the initial fine face tangent measured in left group coordinates. Thus the inverse of 1+E_c realizes an arbitrary *actual* t-row angle on that face. Its total physical L¹ cost as a fine potential is at most C_L t³|θ|: there are (t/η)³ crossing links, each of physical volume η⁴, and a link angle θ corresponds to fine potential θ/η. This count retains every fine bond.

Take a compactly supported fine variation in the smaller patch and subtract these face trials for all its induced t-row variations. The difference fixes the full intermediate field on every affected row, hence fixes every joined target and is an admissible variation of the original minimum. Pairing with the Wilson first derivative gives locally \(J=M_t^*\lambda\) in the physical pairing, where λ_c is obtained by testing J on the exact face trial and dividing by t³ for an angle variation. By (4.38) and the preceding L¹ cost, |λ_c|≤C_Lτ_e on every needed row. Fine gauge variations lifted from the intermediate nodes give zero derivative of the action. Consequently this λ kills simultaneous intermediate node gauge gradients on the relevant supports. This argument works also for the analytic bilinear pairing at complex Γ and keeps the original exterior fixed. It establishes a local multiplier for the common intermediate *raw* rows; it does not invert a mixed global Gram matrix.

**Fractional regularity of its transpose.** Use the centered representatives for the successive averages as in §4.12. Differentiating the compensating endpoint gauge gives precisely an intermediate site-gauge variation, whose pairing with this multiplier vanishes by the preceding horizontality. The endpoint adjoints and final logarithmic differential remain bounded since the terminal angle and accumulated frame changes in this weak gauge are O_L(tτ_e). In physical variables the centered derivative of each level n has the form T_n+σ_n E_n, where T_n is its normalized straight action on potentials and E_n and its physical transpose have ℓ² operator norm ≤C_Lτ_e. This follows from the one-step analytic derivative at equal arguments and its fixed range, with the neighboring physical measures in ratio L⁴. The iteration may be computed in a fixed frame on the needed finite neighborhood and extended for the estimate; the combined backward stencils in the smaller patch are unchanged. The product of the subsequent derivative norms is bounded by exp(C_Lτ_e Σσ_n), because the straight normalized maps are contractions in the physical ℓ² norms.

The product T_{η,s} of straight maps from fine to spacing s is the combined box average and parallel segment average. For its physical transpose on a coarse ℓ² field w,

\[
 \|T_{\eta,s}^*w\|_{H^v}\le C_{L,v}s^{-v}\|w\|_2\qquad(\eta\le s\le 1).
                                                               \tag{4.54}
\]

In fact the transpose of the box average is constant on each coarse block. Its squared physical ℓ² translation increment at shift h is bounded by C min(1,|h|/s)∥w∥², by counting the fraction of each block crossed and applying the squared triangle inequality at its two sides. The parallel segment average is a normalized convolution and cannot enlarge this bound. Summation in the fractional difference norm gives (4.54) for v<1/2; at s=η the inverse inequality is sufficient. Expand the full adjoint product by telescoping one perturbation at a time. Its contribution at s is \(T_{\eta,s}^* s E_s^*\) times the remaining adjoint product on λ. Inequality (4.54) and Σ_s s^{1−v}≤C_{L,v} now bound its H^v norm by C_{L,v}τ_e. Extend λ by zero outside the actual enlarged patch for this calculation (its ℓ² norm there is bounded by C_Lτ_e); only unchanged complete stencils enter the smaller cutoff. Comparing logarithmic-coordinate and group-coordinate fine currents costs I+O(ητ_e). The difference in H^v is bounded by the fine inverse inequality η⁻ᵛ times its O(ητ_e²) local ℓ² norm. Multiplication by the fixed smooth cutoff completes (4.53). ∎

**Proposition 4.33 (P, full-wall quadratic scalar age for a nonabelian stable family).** Under the actual protected first-join geometry and original wall domains of Proposition4.32, take any fixed finite even kernel along one old coordinate direction, with zero sum and zero second moment. Use the true straight axis path, its reverse and one common invariant bilinear trace contraction of the old plaquette curvature. For a smooth fixed root test f in the smaller patch the genuine rough-sector sum obeys (4.51) for every v<1/2, on noncommuting complex fields as well. This supplies all saved scalar powers s<1 on that family, at their original grade. A finite sum over axes and fixed component polarizations has the same result.

**Proof.** We first verify ∥χF₁∥_{H^{1+v}}≤C_vτ_e in the actual local frame, without a global potential regularity assumption. Choose v<γ′<γ<1. In the ordered axial gauge of (4.49), |A|_{C^γ}≤C_γτ_e on a smaller rectangle. To check the increment, order the tree so its last coordinate links are identities and the next coordinate links are identities on the zero last-coordinate face, recursively. Along the last direction the earlier link is the corresponding link on that zero face times an ordered strip of elementary face words, by the exact plaquette recurrence in this gauge. Iterate on the lower-coordinate faces down to the tree. Each product has at most C/η factors, each within Cη²τ_e of identity. Shifting its endpoint by h changes O(|h|/η) factors at the ends, and matched interior factors differ by at most Cη²τ_e|h|^γ by (4.49). Product telescoping, bounded adjoints and division by η to recover the link potential yield Cτ_e(|h|+|h|^γ). A fixed number of coordinate strata changes the constant only. This proves the claimed Hölder estimate for the same frame used throughout.

The covariant cube identity expresses dF as a finite analytic function of nearby A,F: after dividing by η³ the leading terms are products of A and F arising from the connectors, while nonlinear face products have an extra η. All these coefficients are bounded in C^γ by Cτ_e². The analogous difference between ordinary and transported divergence is a finite sum of the same Hölder products. The projected sine curvature differs from F by O(η⁴τ_e³); on the bounded patch its divergence and H^v cost at most Cη^{−1−v}. By (4.53), δF has local H^v norm ≤C_vτ_e. A C^γ function of bounded support lies in H^v for v<γ by its difference quotients. Taking the cubical Hodge identity for χF, with its cutoff factors on the slightly larger patch as in §4.23, yields the desired H^{1+v} bound for the fine F.

For fixed old depth j₀=1 use the actual local expansion underlying (4.49). After a tree gauge rooted in its one-step stencil each nontree fine link is a product of finitely many transported fine plaquettes. Their logarithms have C^γ norm O_L(r²τ_e). The linear term of F₁ in these local links is a fixed finite linear combination of fine F in the original entry frame. Restoring the short transports changes each such F by a function of C^γ norm O_L(rτ_e²): a short transport is I+O_{C^γ}(rτ_e) by the just proved bound on A. The higher terms of the local log mean and plaquette are O_{C^γ}(r²τ_e²) after division by r². Consequently F₁ is this fixed fine linear combination sampled at the old sites plus r times a bounded C^γ error. The combination and fixed L-fold sampling preserve H^{1+v} with constant C_L by the alias calculation of §4.23. The localized error has H^{1+v} norm at most C r^{γ′−v}τ_e², since it has H^{γ′} norm O(rτ_e²) and the remaining derivatives cost at most r^{−1−v+γ′}. The old link potential from the one-step raw mean has C^γ norm ≤C_Lτ_e by its finite analytic word divided by r. These estimates cover all complete old current and exterior stencils needed in the smaller patch.

Let T be translation by this one old axis link with its adjoint transport, and D=T+T⁻¹−2. The transpose relative to the holomorphic invariant trace pairing is T⁻¹, exactly, also for complex links. Denote ordinary shifts by T₀. Expanding either adjoint link about identity, the two first-order terms in D F are r times the commutator of the local old A with the difference of the two translated F's, plus r times the increment of A times a bounded F. On the fixed patch these are bounded by C r^{1+γ}τ_e²; the remaining terms cost C r²τ_e³. The ordinary second difference (T₀+T₀⁻¹−2) costs C r^{1+v}∥F∥_{H^{1+v}} in ℓ² by its exact Fourier symbol. Thus both ∥D F₁∥₂ and ∥D(fF₁)∥₂ are at most C_{L,v,f} r^{1+v}τ_e, using a slightly larger cutoff for the first norm.

A finite even kernel K(T) is a Laurent polynomial in T invariant under inversion. Its vanishing zeroth and second moments make it divisible by D²: its symbol as a polynomial in z+z⁻¹−2 has constant and linear coefficient zero. Write K(T)=D² P(T), where P is a fixed finite Laurent polynomial of the same shift. All transports on its finitely many extra bonds are bounded on the actual complex patch. Exact summation gives ⟨fF₁,K(T)F₁⟩=⟨D(fF₁),P(T)D F₁⟩. Inserting the two ℓ² estimates proves (4.51). The common Y₁ and (4.48) ensure that both fields in this pairing are the ones read by the actual old roots; no cross-wall replacement of one field was made. This calculation bounds the complete reversible quadratic scalar for the displayed family. Other paths may carry a curvature-area cubic center; independent current derivatives, sharp primitive source tests and unrestricted absolute anchors are additional rows. ∎

### 4.25 An old-depth-uniform holomorphic chart on a protected patch

**Proposition 4.34 (P/L, physical first-touch admission on one whole regular patch).** In the protected first-return patch of §4.22 allow any old j≤e−1 and any birth record therein whose complete readout, prescribed exterior and fine plaquette stars fit inside a fixed smaller patch. Use its original admissible old hierarchy with all its complete means and their finer collar completions. There is one holomorphic gauge on the containing patch, depending on the actual composite Γ, such that in entry units

\[
 \|A\|_{C^{1,\gamma}}\le C_\gamma\tau_e,
 \qquad |J_{\rm phys}(\Gamma)|\le C\tau_e,
 \qquad 0<\gamma<1.                                         \tag{4.55}
\]

All prescribed exterior data are transformed by the same gauge. Its adjoints and inverse adjoints are uniformly bounded. The original old physical readout is this same field on the old record in its required relative representative. In old units its potential and its first derivative on a bounded birth patch are at most C rτ_e and C r²τ_e respectively, with r=L^{j−e}, and its physical current is at most C r³τ_e. On a single fixed birth-sized patch the same joint gauge can further be normalized at one point, giving potential O(r²τ_e) there as well. In particular the M8 physical spare-radius test holds at every depth for which the actual C rτ_e lies inside the stored radius by the stated strict margin. This gives the entry-unit AE8 and physical local Sobolev estimates on such complete old readsets, without the weighted old contraction spacing test of Proposition4.20. The statement does not assert a single small chart on a region winding round the torus or across a genuinely rough original fine core.

**Proof of the chart.** Work in the joint ordered tree gauge of (4.49). The strip recurrence in Proposition4.33 gives ∥A₀∥_{C^γ}≤C_γτ_e. Let χ be a smooth cutoff equal to one on the original full patch and supported strictly in its regular enlargement. Regard χA₀ as a periodic potential on an auxiliary torus of fixed side, choosing its size compatible with the fine mesh. This torus is used only to *compute a gauge choice*. The group fields, the Wilson action, all old constraints and the actual exterior on the original patch are never replaced by this extension in their defining equations. The curvature of this cut potential has C^γ norm ≤Cτ_e on the auxiliary torus: the BCH expansion of the true plaquette subtracts its linear curl with remaining analytic products of uniformly bounded C^γ norm, and the differences of χ in the discrete product rule are uniformly smooth.

On this fixed periodic torus the operator P=d(δd)⁻¹δ on one-forms, taking mean zero for the scalar inverse, has C^γ norm ≤C_γ independently of the fine mesh. To check this, its kernel is a second difference of the periodic scalar Green kernel. Away from its fine singularity it and its first differences are bounded by C(|x|+η)⁻⁴ and C(|x|+η)⁻⁵ up to fixed smooth terms, by the dyadic multiplier derivation of (4.49) with the zero mode omitted. In the periodic case the dyadic discrete-frequency bound follows either by discrete summation by parts or by periodizing each smooth annular kernel; the noncentral translates of sufficiently rapidly decaying annuli are summable. Sums of this second-difference kernel on cubes about the origin are uniformly bounded: sum one finite difference onto the boundary and use the first-difference Green bound there. These estimates give the C^γ bound by subtracting the value of the input at the central site in each convolution. For increments separated by u split at radius 2u. The near sum is O(u^γ), using the Hölder increment of the input, and for the far sum integrate C u |x|^{γ−5} in four dimensions. The change in the subtracted central value is multiplied by a uniformly bounded truncated kernel sum. The fine-site term and the finite smooth tail have the same uniform bound. This proves the asserted discrete projection estimate and also the identical bound for any fixed component of a second-difference scalar Green operator.

Write the additional gauge as exp(i h(x)), with mean-zero h on this auxiliary torus. The actual normalized logarithm of the gauged link has the form

\[
 \mathcal A(h)=\chi A_0-dh+\mathcal R(h,dh,\chi A_0).
                                                               \tag{4.56}
\]

On a small ball of C^γ one-forms the remainder has bound C(∥h∥+η∥dh∥+η∥χA₀∥)(∥χA₀∥+∥dh∥), with the same Lipschitz derivative bound in h,dh. This follows from the convergent group word e^{ih(x)}e^{iηχA₀}e^{-ih(x+ημ)}, using h(x+ημ)=h(x)+η d_μh(x). At η=0 the word is identity for every h(x), so division of its logarithm by η is analytic with a uniform bound; its first terms are exactly those displayed. Periodic Poincaré on the fixed torus bounds the C^γ norm of mean-zero h by C∥dh∥_{C^γ}. The equation δ𝒜=0 is equivalent to dh=P(χA₀+ℛ). On the ball ∥dh∥_{C^γ}≤2C_γ∥χA₀∥_{C^γ} its right side is a strict contraction for the original sufficiently weak field ceiling. It yields a uniformly convergent holomorphic gauge and its inverse. No reduced wall domain was selected in this construction.

For this gauged potential the actual plaquette curvature has C^γ norm ≤Cτ_e by conjugating the cut curvature with exp(i h). The same finite-word expansion used above now gives ∥d𝒜∥_{C^γ}≤Cτ_e. Since δ𝒜=0, the cubical Hodge identity and the previously proved second-difference bound imply ∥∇𝒜∥_{C^γ}≤C_γτ_e, with the harmonic constant harmless. On the original patch this is a gauge of the actual Γ, proving (4.55). The rooted tree products and this last gauge are holomorphic jointly in the original preparation-wall parameters. Both are defined on a common enlargement including each required exterior value and path frame. If the gauge used to evaluate a particular old representative must be the identity at its fixed boundary, these computations are a simultaneous change of coordinates of the prescribed boundary, the target and the field; returning to the prescribed representative restores that boundary. They do not transform just the interior and then impose the old exterior in the new frame.

**Identification and scaling.** The argument of (4.48) works verbatim for this original j: a local old admissible variation fixes the full j-average wherever it could be affected, by complete descendant replay, and every joined row that sees the variation has terminal depth ≥e−1≥j. Its embedding is therefore tangent to the actual joined fiber. On real data, the field is critical in the old problem. Its curvature and divergence satisfy the original CMP102 (2) throughout the complete old hierarchy by (4.38), since σ_h≤r≤1 on that hierarchy. For the unprojected divergence in (2) the omitted Hermitian quadratic plaquette error costs at most Cη³τ_e² after one physical difference; the traceless projected part is the bounded physical current. The real uniqueness in (6) identifies the old orbit. The complex old branch on these targets exists by the native old-target test of Proposition4.26: each complete old plaquette has its actual finer completion, and the real and complex perturbation tests are O(r²τ_e) and O(rτ_e) in a compact transported local gauge. All its exterior rows and stars lie in this same regular patch. Equality of analytic readouts follows by continuation from the real admitted variables on each original connected wall chart. Equivalently the joined restriction supplies the regular continuation of the same old constrained orbit. This preserves the exact physical current because the entire incident star belongs to the retained old action.

Scaling (4.55) gives ∥A_old∥∞≤C rτ_e and ∥∇_old A_old∥∞≤C r²τ_e on one common patch for all roots. Their finite local W^{1,p} norms follow with the actual old-domain volume. If a single birth patch of diameter C r is under consideration one may compose with the exponential of the affine function of coordinates with gradient 𝒜 at its center, in the sign convention canceling that constant potential. On this patch its word differs from identity by O(rτ_e); expanding the link gives residual entry potential O(rτ_e+ητ_e²) from the bounded derivative and the commutators. Since η≤r, the old potential is O(r²τ_e). Apply this last gauge to the old exterior jointly, and use one common choice when a packet combines several roots. Curvature and current are conjugated, with current scaling r³. Equation (3.23) gives the asserted AE8 sum over the complete patch. Only an actual numerical comparison to the saved radius yields its margin; in the waiting example this holds at all older depths with a positive spare age power as in §4.18, or whenever the particular stored ceiling already satisfies C rτ_e<ρ_j with the required margin. No absolute Sobolev or anchor norm across arbitrarily many cells follows by dropping their volume. ∎

### 4.26 The reversible scalar at arbitrary old mesh on this rough patch

**Proposition 4.35 (P, arbitrary old depth of the protected reversible family).** In Proposition4.34 let the entire iterated old averaging stencil for each tested coarse plaquette and axis path fit in the protected patch, with a fixed larger collar and the smooth entry-unit root test of §4.24. Assume the actual old record is the one identified in (4.48), now of any j≤e−1. Then, with r=L^{j−e},

\[
 \|\chi F_j\|_{H^{1+v}_r}\le C_{L,v}\tau_e,qquad
 |g_j^{-2} r^{-2}\langle fF_j,K(T)F_j\rangle_r|
 \le C_{L,v,K,f}\,g_j^{-2}\tau_e^2 r^{2v},\quad 0<v<1/2.  \tag{4.57}
\]

The constants are independent of j,e, cutoff and external wall point in this actual geometry. K is any finite even single-axis kernel whose zeroth and second moments vanish. This is the scalar quadratic grade with its actual weak ceiling. It gives the saved age s<1 after choosing 2v>s, whenever the canonical ceiling τ_e/g_e is already charged in the saved Q or the actual spare factor r^{2v−s} absorbs it as in the waiting example. It does not assign a logarithmic ceiling to a smaller Q by definition.

**Proof.** Work in the common patch gauge (4.55). Use a smooth extension to the fixed periodic calculation box as in its proof, taking it equal to the original field on every backward stencil in the statement. The fine curvature on this extension has H^{1+v} norm O(τ_e) on its inner patch by (4.53) and the Hodge proof of Proposition4.33. For the initial ordinary fine curl on the calculation box, choose first a cutoff equal to one on all those stencils, supported where (4.53) holds. The product rule and (4.55) show that its ordinary curl has H^{1+v} norm O(τ_e) globally. Here is the check for the nonlinear curvature conversion. For a mesh s and a potential A with ∥A∥_{C^{1,γ}}≤Cτ_e, the log plaquette normalized by s² equals d_s A plus its fixed coincident-site quadratic commutator plus s E; the sign of the commutator depends on the path convention. Replacing a translated A by A in each quadratic term costs s times a bounded C^γ function by its first-difference bound, and higher BCH terms have an additional s. Thus ∥E∥_{C^γ}≤Cτ_e². Choose v<γ′<γ<1. The bracket is C^{1,γ} and the smaller H^{γ′} bound on E followed by the inverse inequality gives s∥E∥_{H^{1+v}}≤C s^{γ′−v}τ_e². Multiplication by the smooth cutoff and the shifted cutoff derivatives preserves these bounds.

In left endpoint-corrected coordinates the actual one-step mean on potentials is T_n A_n+s_n N_n(A_n), where s_n=L^{n−e} and T_n is the normalized straight average of §4.24. The nonlinear term is a fixed analytic function of finitely many translates of A_n with ∥N_n(A_n)∥_{C^{1,γ}}≤C_L∥A_n∥_{C^{1,γ}}². Indeed differentiating the normalized mean at coincident identity arguments gives its straight arithmetic array once the two original endpoint prefix means have been incorporated in the nodal frames. Each remaining term of degree d≥2 in the link variables carries s_n^d; division by the output mesh leaves s_n^{d−1}. The first discrete differences of these analytic arrays are sums of translated first differences of their inputs by the product rule, with Hölder bound from the same rule. A coarser first difference of a straight average is an average of translated finer first differences, by summing the intervening fine steps; its Hölder seminorm is bounded under this averaging. Hence the successive centered potentials have norm ≤C_Lτ_e, using Σ_n s_n≤C_L and the fixed small ceiling as in Proposition4.18. At level n its endpoint frame differs from identity in C^{1,γ} by O_L(s_nτ_e), from the finite actual prefix map. The accumulated gauges from the original raw family are restricted at the common nodes and multiplied. Their C^{1,γ} norms and inverse norms are bounded uniformly by this same geometric sum.

Taking the ordinary curl commutes with the straight potential average, with a normalized area average S_n on the curls: the segment in the transverse finite difference contributes an additional segment average, by telescoping. The kernel of S_n is nonnegative, a box average followed by parallel segment averages, and is normalized in both physical row and column measures. Thus it contracts physical ℓ² by Jensen. For each first or second output difference the same telescoping gives a normalized average of the corresponding fine difference with the requisite additional parallel segment factors. These averages again contract physical ℓ². Consequently every product S_{n:j} contracts the Fourier H¹ and H² norms defined by the weights (1+Σ|d_μ(p)|²)^{1/2} and (1+Σ|d_μ(p)|²); these squared norms are exactly sums of the indicated component difference squares with fixed multiplicities. Interpolation in the two weighted finite Fourier ℓ² spaces gives H^{1+v} contraction of the whole product, with no factor repeated j times. Equivalently this interpolation follows by conjugating the product with the two Fourier weights to complex powers on the strip and applying the three-lines inequality to each tested bilinear form.

Now propagate the ordinary fine curl. Its nonlinear error created at step n is s_n d_{s_{n+1}}N_n, with C^γ norm of the last factor ≤C_Lτ_e². On the output mesh its H^{1+v} norm costs at most C s_n^{γ′−v}τ_e² by the preceding inverse inequality. All subsequent straight area maps contract that norm. Summing these errors over n is bounded independently of j since γ′>v. The old centered ordinary curl therefore has H^{1+v} norm ≤Cτ_e. Restoring the coarse bracket and remainder at s_j by the same BCH estimate gives this bound for its centered old plaquette curvature. The accumulated endpoint gauge is a uniformly bounded C^{1,γ} multiplier on the localized H^{1+v} norm: apply the first-difference product rule and the fractional increment estimate with v<γ to its factors. This gives (4.57) in the actual transported frames; one may equally perform the invariant quadratic pairing entirely in the centered frame.

The covariant second difference D=T+T⁻¹−2 on the old axis now has norm ≤C r^{1+v}τ_e on F_j and fF_j by the argument of Proposition4.33. For the estimate in the centered frame its old link potential is bounded in C^{1,γ} as just proved, and the fused original paths have the same invariant trace value. Factor K(T)=D²P(T) and use its actual reversed path transpose in the holomorphic pairing. This proves the second inequality of (4.57). It involves the full root sum on a smooth patch across the genuine two-sided artificial junction, on the same field at every root. ∎

### 4.27 Oriented quadratic and cubic terms on the same full-wall patch

**Proposition 4.36 (P, the total area center and two fractional factors).** Use the original old mesh r and common field of Proposition4.35. Allow finitely many oriented old paths P of signed axis steps with length n(P), endpoint displacement h(P), coefficients K_{abP} on curvature components a,b, a smooth fixed root test f, and the same physical trace on the complete path. Include cubic coefficients D_{abc,P,Q} with bounded invariant trilinear tensors C_{abc}. All their entire stencils must fit in the protected patch with its complete collar, and r times each path length must be bounded by a fixed patch constant. Assume the actual stored coefficients satisfy

\[
 \sum_P K_{abP}=\sum_P h_\mu K_{abP}
 =\sum_P h_\mu h_\nu K_{abP}=0                               \tag{4.58}
\]

for every displayed component and that their *one total* local cubic center vanishes identically:

\[
 \sum_{abP,\mu<\nu}K_{abP}\,\mathfrak a_{\mu\nu}(P)
   B(F_a,[F_{\mu\nu},F_b])
 +\sum_{abc,P,Q}D_{abc,P,Q}\,C_{abc}(F_a,F_b,F_c)=0.       \tag{4.59}
\]

The orientation is fixed by T_μT_ν=Ad(U_{μν})T_νT_μ, and the area is one half the sum of ordered step wedges. The quadratic normalization is r⁻² and the cubic one is unity. For any 0<v<1/2, their complete sum obeys

\[
 |R_{j,e}(f)|\le C_{L,v,f}
  \left[\tau_e^2 r^{2v}\sum_{abP}(1+n(P))^3 |K_{abP}|
       +\tau_e^3 r\sum_{abc,P,Q}(1+n(P)+n(Q))|D_{abc,P,Q}|\right]. 
                                                               \tag{4.60}
\]

This proves the saved quadratic and cubic scalar ages s,β<1 on the actual rough patch, with the original coefficients and physical moment factors, under the same canonical-ceiling comparison as (4.57). It does not assert (4.58) or (4.59) for a coefficient whose complete lower, phase or contact partners have not yet been identified.

**Proof.** Pick 2v<γ<1. In the common centered old frame Proposition4.35 gives A=A_j with ∥A∥_{C^{1,γ}}≤Cτ_e and ∥χF_j∥_{H^{1+v}}≤Cτ_e. Write each oriented transport as a matrix on the Lie algebra at the root followed by ordinary translation by r h. Index the signed steps s_i=±e_μ. The link used in a negative step is based at its end, so its base displacement relative to the root is ξ_i=Σ_{l<i}s_l−1_{s_i<0}e_μ. Write B_μ=ad A_μ in the group exponential convention. Expanding the ordered product of the actual link exponentials gives

\[
 T_PF(x)=\{I+r h_\mu B_\mu(x)+r^2 C_P(x)+E_P(x)\}F(x+rh),
 \quad |E_P|\le C\tau_e(r n(P))^{2+\gamma}.                 \tag{4.61}
\]

In fact expand A(x+rξ_i)=A(x)+r ξ_i^ν d_ν^+ A(x)+O(τ_e(rn)^{1+γ}) by telescoping fine first differences at this old mesh. Each occurrence of a link contributes another r. In a two-factor product both A's may be evaluated at x with error O(τ_e²(rn)^3); all terms with three factors have this or a smaller bound by the exponential-series remainder. The stated bound on rn controls the exponential product even for a complex field.

Compute the coefficient C_P. Its derivative terms have coefficients Σ_i ξ_i^μ s_i^ν. For μ≠ν this is h_μ h_ν/2 plus the signed area; for μ=ν it is (h_μ²−h_μ)/2, the last summand coming from the negative-edge basepoint. Its connection products are Σ_{i<l}s_i^μs_l^ν B_μB_ν plus the diagonal half-exponent terms. Thus after (4.58) their only sum is

\[
 \sum_P K_{abP} C_P=
 \sum_{P,\mu<\nu}K_{abP}\,\mathfrak a_{\mu\nu}(P)
       (d_\mu^+ B_\nu-d_\nu^+ B_\mu+[B_\mu,B_\nu]).       \tag{4.62}
\]

This displays the actual orientation contribution. The BCH conversion at the old mesh in §4.26 replaces the last parenthesis by ad F_{μν} with error O(rτ_e²), including the negative-step convention already counted above.

For the identity term in (4.61), its pure translation symbol on the old Fourier lattice is ΣK_{abP} exp(i r ξ·h). Moment cancellation (4.58) gives the absolute bound C|rξ|^{2+2v}Σ |K|(1+n)^{2+2v} by Taylor expansion at zero and the bounded exponential for real ξ at larger argument. Use compactly supported extensions fF_a,χF_b that agree on every input site. Plancherel and Cauchy–Schwarz split this Fourier weight between the two H^{1+v} fields, before taking spatial absolute values. This supplies O(r^{2+2v}τ_e²) times the displayed moment. The first connection term has a translation symbol weighted by h_μ. Its zeroth and first moments vanish, so its bound is C|rξ|²Σ|K|(1+n)^3. After pairing between f B_μ^t F_a and F_b in H¹ and keeping the additional factor r, it costs at most C r³τ_e³ times that moment. Multiplication by B_μ preserves this H¹ bound by its established C^{1,γ} estimate. The transpose here is the analytic bilinear transpose.

In the term with coefficient r² C_P replace F_b(x+rh) by F_b(x); its local ℓ² difference is ≤C r n τ_e from H¹, while |C_P|≤C n²τ_e. After the normalization r⁻² this costs O(r τ_e³) times the third path moment. The remaining term is exactly (4.62) paired with fF_a,F_b, hence the area term in (4.59) up to O(rτ_e⁴). The error E_P after normalization contributes at most C r^γτ_e³Σ|K|n^{2+γ}, bounded by the first term in (4.60). In the cubic row replace each transported slot by F at its original root. Each difference in local ℓ² is ≤C r n τ_e by ordinary H¹ increments and the norm bound on the actual short adjoint word. The other slots have the pointwise curvature bound Cτ_e. The resulting error is the second term of (4.60). Equation (4.59) cancels the two remaining local cubic polynomials before absolute values, proving the result. ∎

The preceding estimate is stronger in the quadratic exponent than estimating a single differentiated transport difference. Its use of two fractional factors takes place in the pure translation multiplier. The noncommuting ordered path has been expanded only in the common physical chart on this protected return; the independent canonical-current and weak-selector jets remain separate records.

### 4.28 The true outer boundary at the unweakened physical endpoint

**Proposition 4.37 (P/L, physical equality across ∂Z).** In either ordinary first return of §§4.16–4.18, take a fixed current-sized patch K across an outer flat face of the returning component Z, with the complete footprints away from the preceding rough components. Use the original real unweakened endpoint at zero B in (1.50), with the actual permitted retained data. For every old j≤e−1 whose complete record lies in this patch and fixes the j-averages as in Proposition4.34, the physical old readout, on both sides of the true ∂Z, is the restriction of the *same* U_k⁰. On the patch these old physical curvatures obey the scalar reversible and total-center estimates (4.57),(4.60) under their stated source hypotheses. In this endpoint statement K may be localized smoothly across the true outer boundary.

**Proof.** At these physical parameters the left side of (1.61) is the global variational field U_k⁰ defined by (1.50) with B=0. The determining problem before its localization is B″_k. Printed pp.372–373 displays the value at U_k⁰ as the first term of (1.59),(1.60) for disjoint X and then represents this same U_k⁰ by the determining set (1.61) for intersecting X. The latter equality is a physical restriction identity on each stated Y₁. Thus the common unfiled old field exists prior to choosing a different Y₁ for each X. In the chosen outer flat patch the original ordinary domains have no genuine finer layer: the outer two layers of the ten-layer Z_e enlargement lie in Ω_e, and the exterior continuation here is ordinary. The nearest genuine finer piece of B″_k lies farther than any of the relevant bounded backward stencils. This also holds on an enlargement of K crossing the flat face in the selected examples. Consequently the original global constraints meeting an admissible old trial on K are top-scale rows. Complete j-averages and the unchanged exterior fix every one by recursive averaging. This argument is made on B″_k *before* any artificial localization. It remains valid when an individual B″_k(Y₁) places finer auxiliary rows immediately next to its own boundary. Native real regularity, the old target test and uniqueness as in §4.25 give W_X=U_k⁰ on all these patches. The global field has curvature and physical current ≤Cτ_e there by the matched native range; one can use a containing localization domain that includes the entire enlargement. Propositions4.32–4.36 apply to this single regular field. Independently of the later expansion into branches, this proves the asserted unweakened physical endpoint estimate. ∎

**Exact cost off that endpoint (P).** It is important to retain the distinction between this global source field and two independently localized branch arguments. At the true boundary, an old X wholly inside Z can have Y₁=Z; one spanning X can force Y₁ to contain an additional coarse localization cube. The former minimal hierarchy can have terminal rows down to the fine mesh next to this same face, while the latter can have only e-rows there. Formula (1.61) on the smaller domain uses the boundary data containing u_k⁰, the complete H″_k and U₁; formulas (1.62),(1.63) then use their own localized determining sets. The identity just proved at the physical endpoint does not equate these independently weakened maps on an arbitrary outer pair.

For a concrete scale check, let F, F̃ be two returned curvature readouts on an affected birth-sized seam slab, in jointly compatible physical frames, with ∥F∥+∥F̃∥≤Cτ_e in entry units. Include the complete transported other slot in this comparison and set d_s to the supremum of the actual differences of both slots on the slab. For bounded path moments, the scalar quadratic discrepancy has size at most C g_j⁻² r⁻¹ τ_e d_s. Indeed each old slot carries r², each pair carries the r⁻² engineering normalization, at most C r⁻³ roots meet the slab, and the product difference has the two indicated terms. Thus an off-endpoint comparison d_s≤C τ_e r^{1+s} (or a sharper summed identity for the actual flux) would pay the scalar quadratic age for these boundary roots. A bound from a truly remote source e^{-cMR_e}τ_e would suffice in the waiting example only after checking cM R_e≥(1+s)(e−j)log L plus the logarithmic margin. The changed-base formulas localize their displayed defect sources, but this inequality must also treat the projections of the outer pair onto the two different artificial determining sets. Even when an outer field has all its coarse averages prescribed, its finer boundary rows on one of these sets need not be determined by those coarse averages alone. This is the exact additional boundary comparison for general original complex walls; the physical endpoint has d_s=0.

### 4.29 A true-boundary discrepancy in the second and third joined readouts

**Proposition 4.38 (P/L, two of the original three joins).** In the ordinary Q₂ return of §4.19 take its pure-gauge version, namely retain the same nodal spike with the Cartan plaquette t there set to zero. It is still an actual large fluctuation under (3.3). Choose a flat outer face of its Z and a transverse interior portion of an adjacent current localization cube. Let Y=Z and Y⁺ be Z together with this adjacent cube. An old X in Z can have smallest Y₁=Y, whereas a crossing X has smallest Y₁=Y⁺. Take the exceptional choices with all the additional Y′ domains empty, explicitly present in LFR II pp.376–377. Choose an admissible original fixed M₁,M for which a complete pair of level-two blocks fits strictly inside the fine pinned layer of Y and in the interior of Y⁺ beyond its two-layer collar. For example the size comparison M₁>C_L L and M>C_L M₁ suffices; these are fixed geometry tests on the selected example.

There is an allowed common outer pair (U(z),0), |z|≤c τ with τ=min(α₀,k,α₁,k) on these original top-scale records, such that the actual localized U_{k,Λ} of (1.63) takes two different values on this patch for Y and Y⁺. Their old depth-one curvature readouts differ there by c_L r|z|, r=Lη. The discrepancy also occurs in U₁ of (1.62) for these two domains. Thus a uniform O(τ r^{1+s}) comparison of the two intermediate readouts fails for any s>0. This proposition concerns these actual intermediate functions. The last H″_k and endpoint frame in (1.61) still have to be included before drawing a conclusion for the completed Γ or W.

**Proof.** Fix the old real history and V_Λ=1, B=0 as above. Its new determining set has only k-rows in Z and ordinary k-rows outside, by (1.33). The previous pure gauge makes no old physical curvature. The outer analytic variable U is a separate member-domain argument. On the fine pinned strip just described put a Cartan level-one cochain of strength η³z times the link boundary of a level-one plaquette P. Use the compact/complex extension of the coarse endpoint correction of §4.19 to make its next raw level-two mean identity. Its level-one plaquette holonomy is exp(4iη³zT), up to orientation. Use the simple lift Q^{s*} of this level-one field to the fine mesh; call it U(z). Then M¹(U(z)) is exactly the prescribed level-one target, M^h(U(z))=1 for h≥2, and all fine and level-one link changes have size ≤C_L η³|z|. The entire change and its fine incident stars fit inside the pinned layer and inside the stated Y⁺ top region. In particular there are no changed star plaquettes next to a free fine bond of the minimal Y hierarchy. The link and endpoint changes form an analytic Cartan family and do not change the retained real variables of the old Q₂ event.

We verify that such thin data belong to the original outer member space, rather than imposing a new weaker domain on its boundary. Relative to identity, all its actual native targets on any of the required localized records have angle ≤C_Lη³|z|: the only possible nonidentity rows have terminal depth zero or one. The missing finer-row completions are the actual means of the same U(z) and satisfy the same finite-depth bound. For each of the native auxiliary reconstructions U_{p,D}(M^•(U(z))) apply CMP102 Theorem1 and Proposition9 about the real identity with its literal ε₁=C_Lη³|z|. The dimensionless fine plaquette deviation is ≤Cη³|z|, even on the finest resulting layer, by (2),(19)–(21) and the local plaquette word. For the required physical divergence, even dividing this finest plaquette bound by three powers of the finest entry-unit mesh η gives C|z|. On rescaling the pth native problem to its own terminal units its fine mesh is ≥η, so this crude upper estimate remains valid there. All localization-completed target constraints are the ones prescribed in (7),(172). For the trial outer field itself its potential in entry units is O_L(η²|z|), its first difference O_L(η|z|), and the compact real comparison can be taken to be identity for its complex part. The independent current can be set to zero. These inequalities give the bounds required in the original top-layer member class with a strict margin for c sufficiently small. More precisely, at the original top n=k the full pth plaquette ceiling in CMP119 (2.35) is αη² and the p-unit current ceiling in (2.37) is α(L^{p−k})². The above plaquettes are O(η³τ), and the currents after conversion to p-units are O(τ(L^{p−k})³), within both ceilings. The new top hierarchy and its original native comparisons are therefore respected. All constants here are independent of waiting depth.

Consider first (1.63). Its argument of H_k in (1.64) vanishes: the current k-row field on Λ is identity and so is V^{(A)} there. Consequently its boundary representation has zero relative perturbation and identity change-of-frame for this comparison. The input on the artificial boundary is the actual M^•(U(z)); interior new rows are identity since M^k(U(z))=1. For Y⁺ every affected fine link lies well inside its region of top rows, so *all* its row values are independent of z. Its critical field is the flat one. For Y the altered links and their entire affected stars lie in its finest fixed piece. The configuration U(z) itself is critical on this fiber. Every varying star sees only flat data, and the altered stars consist entirely of fixed fine bonds. The remaining target values are exactly those read from U(z). Native uniqueness and analytic continuation identify the original U_{k,Λ}(Y) with this configuration on the affected patch and identity elsewhere. This argument also verifies the changed-base branch by the physical variational equations themselves.

Now apply (1.62). The argument of H_{B₁} in (1.57) is the log of the required averages of U_{k,Λ} on Z″_k. Those averages are all identity for both domains. The old thin perturbation on the outermost face and all its complete stencils are separated from this inner region. Hence that relative function vanishes, with identity change-of-frame. The distinct strip is in Ω″_{h+1}, while the coarse core argument of U₁ is identity outside the separated U₂ region in this pure-gauge history; all retained V″ on Λᶜ are identity. Thus the pinned part of (1.62) on Y again records U(z) on the changed fine bonds, and the same free-star argument makes its actual field flat elsewhere. On Y⁺ it again has flat data. Therefore the level-one plaquette P in either intermediate comparison differs by angle 4η³z up to the same compact frame. Dividing by r² gives 4r z/L³, proving the discrepancy. For real z choose |z|=cτ/2; the conclusion also holds on the stated holomorphic family. ∎

The example isolates a real cost of putting differently localized critical projections on opposite sides of a sharp test. The final (1.61) contains both the projected physical-current term (1.55) and the full endpoint gauge. Calculating their first variation on this explicit thin outer field is now a concrete test for the last join. A discrepancy at the two intermediate maps is neither a contradiction to (4.48) on its interior patch nor a proof that the full marked estimate must fail.

### 4.30 The final joined field on the empty native wall subset

**Proposition 4.39 (P/L, discrepancy for the prepared field itself).** In Proposition4.38 take the empty subset in each native fundamental-theorem-of-calculus localization expansion. This is an original simultaneous wall value. On the smaller branch the final Γ in (1.61) has the altered depth-one plaquette on P. On the larger branch Γ is independent of z. The corresponding old W has the same respective dependence of this *coarse plaquette* whenever its complete word lies in the old record. In particular, one can take a birth box on the inner side of the true face and a birth record enlarged across it, both reading this P. On these two actual records the variation about z=0 of the final curvature discrepancy has size 4r|z|/L³ in the Cartan component. Thus the branchwise bound d_s≤C τ r^{1+s} from §4.28 cannot hold uniformly on the original simultaneous domain. This conclusion is a statement about different filed records with overlapping reads, not two values assigned to one fixed determining problem.

**Proof.** The needed wall rule is given explicitly in the primary CMP116, printed pp.2–4, equations (1.2)–(1.9). Each walk term of a propagator is multiplied by the product of the parameters s(Δ) for chosen cubes intersecting its localization domain. Equation (1.9) includes the empty differentiation subset, with zero values on all other assigned cubes. LFR II printed p.373 applies this localization to H″_k on the set of cubes disjoint from Λ; its later formulas use the same expansion for H_{B₁} and H_k. The exceptional terms with all additional Y′ empty are explicitly included immediately after (1.67). The numbering (6.10) cited in LFR II refers to this localization construction; the published second part uses the renumbered (1.9)–(1.10). The following argument uses the zero value itself, not a derivative term in which a boundary parameter could be differentiated.

Every walk with output on the thin outer strip, or within any of the original k-blocks required to restore its endpoint frames, must meet a cube disjoint from Λ carrying the zero parameter. The whole fixed neighborhood is separated from Λ by the ordinary MR_e buffer; the complete path and block support is included. Consequently every such output kernel of each of H₀(s),G(s),H(s) in (1.2)–(1.4) vanishes. In the relative solution H″ the linear target term has outermost H₀(s), its nonlinear unknown A₀ has outermost G(s), and its linearizing correction has outermost H(s). Thus H″_k vanishes there identically at this wall value. In particular the exact projected-current input (1.55) is retained: its outermost G″(s) has zero output there regardless of the multiplier or the current on the interface. This support argument does not discard that term at any other parameter value. The source describes u_k⁰(x) after (1.61) as the frame determined from H″_k on the original block of x. On these complete blocks the relative potential is zero, so its normalized frame is identity; a common residual compact frame gives the same traced coarse plaquette.

On the finest pinned strip of Y, the boundary part of (1.61) is therefore exactly the bare output of (1.62), including the complete fine stars of the changed patch. By Proposition4.38 that is U(z). On Y⁺ the variation in U(z) is invisible in every raw outer target of (1.63), the old retained coordinates are fixed, and all ensuing localized operations have identical inputs with and without z. The pure-gauge core at z=0 gives zero physical curvature in this patch: U₁ is identity, U₂ is its compatible compact pure gauge in the core, so their glued real field has zero current in (1.55), and the other target A₀ is zero. Both are supported away from the tested outer face. At this base point the actual resumed relative branch at zero forcing is zero. This proves the asserted dependence of Γ, including its exact coarse plaquette on both branches.

For the old orbit take the entire first-step word and its rectangular filling inside each relevant record.
