Each of its raw old bonds is either a stored depth-one row or the genuine replay of its fixed fine children with the prescribed exterior, so M¹(W)(∂P)=M¹(Γ)(∂P) in the transported old frames. At the real z used here the old target words on a fixed birth box satisfy the native small real plaquette test: the source output on this ordinary outer region is in the range (1.65), its tested stars in Y have the top-layer (4.38) bound, and fine fixed boundary data on the indicated strip are exactly the small U(z). Its explicit exterior can be continued by those same bare boundary data on the neighboring stencil where required. The fixed-depth compact tree tests of §4.13 give (7), and CMP102 Theorem1 gives the regular old real orbit. Equivalently, the plaquette identity is necessary for *every* old field solving those constraints, independently of its other rows. Taking nonzero real z=cτ/2, the exact log plaquette after division by r² is 4η³ z/r²=4r z/L³. This is larger than Cτ r^{1+s} along the actual waiting sequence as r tends to zero. ∎

Neither (4.44) nor this outer-face example says that a specific original stable source coefficient has a nonzero one-sided boundary flux. Equation (4.50) retains the partner that an actual completed coefficient may supply. Proposition4.39 identifies the cost on a saved coarse readout itself; the next question is how the complete marked localization, including its original difference and contact terms, combines the two different reads *before* the packet norm takes absolute values.

### 4.31 The discrepancy survives the count on a true flat face

**Proposition 4.40 (P on the fixed native spacing test, a quadratic partial row).** Use the true face and empty native wall of §4.30. Fix the original admissible spacing at the outset so that the weight-three estimate of §4.17 and its power-four sum hold for the auxiliary native reconstructions in the original outer member test. There is a single allowed real outer family with Θ(r⁻³) old depth-one plaquettes on a bounded face patch, each shared by an inner and a crossing birth record as in Proposition4.39. Let Q_i(z) be the difference of the *quadratic plaquette traces* in these two records, for that same outer argument. Then for fixed real z=cτ>0, in the actual nonzero weak background constructed below,

\[
 g_1^{-2}r^2\sum_i |Q_i(z)-Q_i(0)|\ge c_L g_1^{-2}\tau^2,
 \qquad
 g_1^{-2}r^2\sum_i |\delta F_{1,i}|^2\le C_L g_1^{-2}\tau^2 r. 
                                                               \tag{4.63}
\]

Here δF is the actual inner field change about z=0; the competing larger record has no change on these reads. The factor r² consists of the old r⁴ physical cell volume and the separate r⁻² dimension-six normalization. Thus estimating this unpaired quadratic boundary trace by absolute values consumes all the scalar age in a single depth sequence. A squared *change* retains a power. No assertion is made that the partial trace in this calculation is the entire stored stable quadratic packet.

**An admissible nonzero physical comparison.** In entry units take the normal direction to be 1 and put A₂(x)=c₀τ sin(2πx₁)T, A_μ=0 otherwise, relative to the identity. Coordinates are translated so the chosen flat coarse face has x₁=0. This is periodic on the entry lattice torus and its derivatives through order two are bounded by Cτ. All fields are Cartan. Its raw k-average is identity: the discrete average of one full sine period on every terminal block is zero, and the raw endpoint prefix mean has the same value at every terminal node by translation periodicity. Thus the current outer k-target, V_Λ and the retained pure-gauge Q₂ history are exactly those used above. Its old depth-one 12-plaquette curvature has the sign of T and size ≥cτ on the indicated thin strip. Indeed the coarse Cartan curl is a normalized finite area average of its fine curl, which equals the sine difference quotient and is positive there for all sufficiently small r.

Place translates of the compactly supported test perturbation of §4.29 at a fixed number of level-two block widths apart in each of the three directions parallel to the face. Restrict to one fixed transverse subpatch away from corners. There are between c_Lr⁻³ and C_L r⁻³ such translates. Their complete supports and first incident stars are disjoint. Their actual angle increments add exactly in the common Cartan group. Every increment has M^h=1 for h≥2 (as an increment), hence it remains invisible in the larger joined exterior; on its central P it changes the fine-to-old curvature by +4r zT/L³. This is an array on one allowed outer field, not a supremum chosen separately for each root.

To check the outer member class in this nonzero comparison, use the field F consisting of this sine and all the bumps as the feasible reference for each required real regional reconstruction. Its potential in the single global analysis chart is bounded by Cτ, its first and second physical differences by Cτ. A bump has potential O(η²τ), so even its second fine difference is O(τ); the disjoint supports preserve the sup bounds. Its curvature and true Wilson current are bounded by Cτ in entry units. At every actual region and p-record the target is its own raw M^•(F), so the relative target B in the native equation is zero. This reference satisfies the original real regularity and small external smooth-reference conditions used in Proposition4.24, with room from the fixed small coefficient in τ. The proof of (4.41) applies directly with the global weight w_y=σ_y⁻³ on each of these component hierarchies, using the displayed fixed-spacing tests. In that proof the reference needed precisely its own common small potential, first derivative and bounded physical current with its actual exterior. Here these hold directly, uniformly in the number of bumps. It follows that the critical field has physical curvature and current O(τ); the physical fine plaquette is ≤Cτη². Rescaling a p-record to its pth terminal units only decreases these physical bounds (by the square and cube of its terminal length). The extra lower-p weights in the top member tests do not strengthen that ceiling. The explicit gauge and first-difference requirements for the *outer* F itself hold in the displayed sine chart. Reducing c₀,c once puts the family inside the original real domain with a common margin. The argument uses a sufficient original parameter setting, not a retroactively widened collar or an assumption that every fixed spacing already passes this test.

**Quadratic trace calculation.** On the empty native wall all three output corrections vanish on the complete fine pinned strip of Y, by the walk support proof of Proposition4.39. Therefore its final bare fine values there equal the outer ones, also in the sine background. For Y⁺ the perturbations make no change in any of its seen new targets: they have trivial h≥2 means and support in its top interior. All ensuing localized functions on this branch have exactly the same inputs for z and zero. The retained old mean at each indicated P therefore has precisely the change just computed in Y, and zero change in Y⁺. Use the real invariant positive trace on antihermitian curvature. Its increment in the first record is twice the inner product of the baseline F₁ with δF₁ plus the square of δF₁. Each baseline has size at least cτ in the same Cartan direction, so for z=cτ the trace increment is at least c_L rτ² at every one of these roots. The competing trace is constant in z. Summation proves the first inequality in (4.63). Squaring the explicit change before summing instead gives the second inequality. ∎

This rules out a proposed absolute estimate for the unresolved *separate* boundary-center slot even after the native face multiplicity is included. An original completed packet might cancel this slot algebraically, record it as a removed lower-dimensional term with its own grade, or use a genuinely summed marked integral. Its exact contact and root assignment have to be checked to decide which applies.

### 4.32 Existing crossing packets and the cost of proposing a new union

**Proposition 4.41 (P, a common original crossing record).** Suppose a complete stored packet already has a determining carrier X spanning the true face in the returning history above. Let its outer part lie away from the transverse edges of one adjacent current localization cube, and include that cube in its smallest Y₁. Assume the full old source and exterior stencils being tested lie in a smaller patch across this face with the fixed backward-stencil allowance of §4.22. Then on *all* of its original simultaneous walls, that packet has one physical old field on this patch. The chart and scalar conclusions (4.55),(4.57),(4.60) apply to its admitted graded families there, with their actual saved weights and radius tests.

**Proof.** The branch choice is made once from this given X, so its Y₁ includes Z and the adjacent coarse cube. On a flat subpatch of their common face the distance to ∂Y₁ exceeds two M₁ layers plus the fixed complete stencil radius. This uses the assumed transverse allowance and the fixed initial M/M₁ separation. Thus the whole patch lies in Y₁⁰. The genuine B″_k heritage there is top scale, by the original eight- and ten-layer geometry at ∂Z. Every joined row seeing an old variation can be reconstructed from the old retained j-averages and their full exterior. On the actual branch (1.61), (4.38) applies to all those stars, including the ones just outside Z. The proof of (4.48) and then §§4.24–4.27 gives the claimed readout on this one original X. All roots in the packet use that same Y₁; neither the collar field belonging to a different X wholly inside Z nor an auxiliary finer trace from that different record is substituted. This proves an off-endpoint assertion *across the true face* for the stated crossing records. It has no extra incidence cost because the complete carrier and its coefficient were already filed together. ∎

**Incidence of a different proposal (P).** If separate old carriers X_i are instead replaced by one union X_*, an old weight exp(κ d_j(X)) cannot simply be inherited. For any weighted coefficient sum one has exactly

\[
 e^{\kappa d_j(X_*)}\sum_i |a_i|
 =\sum_i e^{\kappa d_j(X_i)}|a_i|
                 e^{\kappa(d_j(X_*)-d_j(X_i))}.              \tag{4.64}
\]

In particular bounded-diameter old carriers tiling two points at fixed nonzero entry distance have union tree size at least c/r in unnormalized birth-block units. The new factor in (4.64) can be exp(cκ/r). No fixed polynomial reserve in r⁻¹ pays it. In a scheme where root translations have a pre-existing current-unit test weight, their individual old carrier weights must remain attached to the original X_i and the current test sum accounted separately; (4.64) then prevents treating those X_i as one newly cheap old polymer. The equality concerns an actual refiling that charges the union size. It does not obstruct Proposition4.41 for a packet whose common original determining carrier is already given. This distinguishes the verified crossing case from the unrestricted common-union route mentioned in the stored-record construction.

### 4.33 An allowed one-sided boundary wave with a complete reversible quadratic readout

**Proposition 4.42 (P on the fixed native spacing test, a true-boundary mode).** Use the original parameter setting and empty-wall history of §§4.29–4.31. There is a real Cartan outer field admitted on both of the actual domains Y and Y⁺, with unchanged M^k=1, such that the old depth-one curvature on a thin inside layer of Y has tangential increments of order τ, uniformly as r=Lη tends to zero. The larger Y⁺ does not see this mode. For the single-axis fourth difference K(T)=(T+T⁻¹−2)² along the face, with its actual reversed straight paths, its old quadratic pairing on the inner small-Y layer is positive and at least c_L g₁⁻² r⁻¹τ² after the full face count. This tests a *complete even kernel with zero zeroth and second moments*. The restricted quadratic expression is an explicit Wilson readout. Its occurrence as a particular nonzero term of the prescribed original frustration/source packet, together with that packet's actual birth incidence, is a separate question.

**Construction.** Use x₁ inward normal to the flat face, x₂ tangential, and let integer n=x/η in a translate of the fine lattice. Write s=L²η and take the only initial nonzero potential component to be

\[
 A_3(n)=c\tau s\,g(n_1)\,cos(2\pi n_2/L^2)\,phi(n_2,n_3,n_4)T.
                                                               \tag{4.65}
\]

Here T is the fixed Cartan direction. The envelope φ samples a smooth tangential bump on complete s-blocks and is constant within each such block. It equals one on a fixed subpatch and is supported strictly inside the adjacent added M cube, away from the other faces. Its jumps between s-blocks are O_L(η). Take g(n)=exp(−λ n), where cosh λ=2−cos(2π/L²), on the inside and on a fixed number C_L of additional fine layers beyond the true face. A fixed fine-step translation of the normal origin is immaterial. Cut g off to zero just beyond this exterior strip, and use a smooth cutoff at fixed positive entry distance on the inward side. There the exponential is smaller than every fine-mesh power. The construction lifts periodically without meeting a torus identification. The exterior extension may have a large divergence immediately *outside* Y. We will check the actual reconstruction tests on Y⁺, not assert a physical-current ceiling for this raw exterior field.

The discrete dispersion equation for λ makes the product g(n₁)cos(2πn₂/L²) harmonic for the ordinary fine Laplacian wherever the cutoffs are constant. On the inside and its needed fine incident stars, |A|≤C_Lτs, |∇A|≤C_Lτ and |d*dA|≤C_Lτ. Indeed a first fine difference of the block envelope is O_L(1) and a second one is O_L(1/η); this last term multiplies a factor of size O(ητ). The mixed divergence terms involving direction 3 obey the same bound. The dimensionless fine Cartan plaquette angles are O(η²τ); replacing the linear curl by the projected sine in the Wilson current costs at most Cη³τ³ after one normalized physical difference. On the *whole* torus, including the short exterior cutoff, the potential and its first derivative still have the preceding bounds. The constants may depend on the fixed exterior number of fine layers, independent of k.

The straight combined two-step mean of (4.65) is zero: each transverse s-block contains one full cosine period, with φ constant there. In the Cartan raw mean restore the inverse endpoint gradient on the level-two nodes by its block-constant extension as in §4.29. The resulting U has M^h(U)=1 for every h≥2. This compact gauge changes each fine link by at most C_Lη²τ because its node angle involves paths of length O_L(s) times a potential O_L(sτ). The resulting potential and first derivative remain bounded as stated, and in Cartan logarithmic coordinates d*d is unchanged by this exact gradient. The correction is supported on the same region with a fixed s-block enlargement. Choose the tangential support and exterior extension with this margin. The independent source current is zero. The real fine holonomy and the coarse Cartan plaquette curvatures are unchanged by this correction.

**The member tests, including the lower p powers.** On each domain W=Y,Y⁺ the conditions are the actual ones in CMP119 p.261, (2.34)–(2.39), with the new top hierarchy in the returning component. At n=j=k, ξ=η, the bound in (2.35) on each dimensionless reconstructed plaquette is proportional to τ L⁻²ᵖ(LᵏL⁻ᵖ)⁻²=τ η² for *every* p. In (2.37) the normalized reconstructed p-current has ceiling proportional to τ L^{k−p}(L^{k−p})⁻³=τ r_p², where r_p=L^{p−k}. This is stronger than bounding a raw plaquette by τL⁻²ᵖ alone. The direct raw tests, the independent current and the real local gauge (2.38) follow from the explicit potential and first derivative above on both domains. For the real example U′=1 in (2.39).

For W=Y use U itself as the feasible reference for each actual regional determining set. Its raw target defect is zero. On this region and the required neighboring fine stars, (4.65) has the displayed small potential, first derivative, bounded d*d and bounded Wilson current in entry units. These are precisely the data for the global weight σ_y⁻³ calculation in Proposition4.24, with the assumed original spacing and the true fixed exterior. Its estimate for the critical configuration gives physical curvature and current ≤C_Lτ. This verifies (2.35), and the current in p-units is ≤C_Lτ r_p³≤C_Lτ r_p². No Hölder bound on the first derivative of the reference was used in (3.37)–(3.38): those primary conditions test its potential, first derivative and the indicated covariant second row, here the bounded d*d. For W=Y⁺ all nontrivial support is strictly inside its top interior. For p≥2 all observed rows are identity: in its interior M^p(U)=1 and the smaller artificial rows are near the outer boundary, outside the support. The actual regular reconstruction is flat. For p=1 the rows of depth zero or one have angle ≤C_Lη²τ, including all seam completions. CMP102 Theorem1 at the real identity comparison gives fine plaquette size ≤C_Lη²τ. In p=1 units the mesh is 1/L, so the crude entire finite divergence of this fine plaquette array is ≤C_Lη²τ≤C_L r²τ, as required. This also verifies the domain on the full top torus if its test is made there, by the same p≥2/p=1 argument. Reducing c in (4.65) puts all these bounds inside the original margins. The old real selectors are unchanged since the current k-average and the original retained coordinates were unchanged.

**Readout and quadratic size.** On the empty wall all response output kernels vanish on the outer strip and its complete frame blocks, as proved in Proposition4.39. The smallest hierarchy on Y has a fine pinned strip of width at least M₁Lη. On a fixed normal old layer there, with all required fine children and old plaquette stars inside the strip, the final bare Γ therefore equals U. On Y⁺ every actual input to (1.63) is unchanged from its flat value, and the other joins have the same retained pure-gauge data. Its output is flat on the tested patch. This conclusion does not use a Hölder extension of the Y field beyond the true face.

Take the 23-curvature of its original one-step mean. In the subpatch with φ=1 its value on that fixed layer is

\[
 F_{1,23}(m)=c'_L\tau\,sin(2\pi m_2/L+\theta_L)T,
        \qquad |c'_L|>0.                                     \tag{4.66}
\]

Indeed the transverse L-site box mean of the fine cosine has nonzero Fourier factor sin(π/L)/[L sin(π/L²)], the old direction-2 difference has nonzero factor 2 sin(π/L)/r, and the normal L-site average of exp(−λ n₁) is positive, with a fixed positive value on this layer. The longitudinal segment average in direction 3 is constant. Raw endpoint gradients disappear in the coarse plaquette. Fixed phases and the chosen finite layer only change c′_L and θ_L. The Cartan adjoint transport acts trivially. Consequently K(T) acts by the strictly positive number (2 cos(2π/L)−2)². A positive fraction of the Θ(r⁻³) roots has curvature magnitude at least c_Lτ. Their dimension-six normalized quadratic sum on this actual layer is ≥c_L g₁⁻²τ²/r. For each constituent old record reading complete plaquette words, their depth-one values are the genuine M¹(W)=M¹(Γ) values, whether the record stores coarse bonds or replays fixed finer children. A single small-Y family can thus have large tangentially paired quadratic values on its true boundary despite the vanishing translation moments.

There is also a lower bound with a smooth tangential test on the small-Y half-patch when the quadratic family is tested on *one common* Γ from that branch. Let ζ be a real smooth current-unit cutoff equal to one on part of the above strip, with transverse support away from all edges, and f=ζ². Sum its old roots on the inner side of the face; the normal half-patch indicator commutes with the tangential shift T. On every root in this half-patch |F₁|≤Cτ by (4.38) and the fixed one-step complete-word estimate. In Cartan coordinates put D=T+T⁻¹−2. Exact summation along each tangential line gives ⟨ζ²F₁,D²F₁⟩=⟨D(ζ²F₁),DF₁⟩. The commutator has pointwise bound C r|ζ|τ+C r²τ by the finite product rule and the supremum of F₁. Multiplying by DF₁ and using the squared inequality yields a lower bound ½∥ζDF₁∥²−C r²τ² on this fixed-volume patch. On the tested layer ∥ζDF₁∥²≥c_L rτ² by (4.66). Hence the normalized common-branch half-patch scalar is at least c_L g₁⁻²τ²/r−C g₁⁻²τ². This is a tangential reversible operator, so no normal jump of the test was differentiated. If the actual complete packet assigns some of these roots to a different localization, those assignments and their original differences must still be included; this argument does not identify a newly chosen half-patch sum with a specific completed R_j.

This example explains the fixed external collar in Proposition4.35. On a genuine outer boundary, bounded divergence and curl *inside* a region do not give an interior-scale Hölder estimate up to that boundary without control of the trace. The mode (4.65) has its rough trace at the old scale on an allowed original empty wall. It is distinct both from the continuously live pinned return in (4.44) and from the smaller change in (4.63).

### 4.34 The actual matching-force observable on the pinned old plaquettes

**Proposition 4.43 (P, the compact H readout, before its old integration).** On the real true-boundary layer of Proposition4.42 let h_b be the original nonnegative Wilson matching-frustration density evaluated on the old level-one links of the actual W, with β₁=2N/g₁². On a positive proportion of its direction-3 bonds,

\[
 h_b(M^1 W)\ge c_{L,N}\beta_1 r^4\tau^2,\qquad
 r^{-2}\sum_{b\ \mathrm{on\ the\ layer}} h_b(M^1 W)
        \ge c_{L,N}\beta_1\tau^2/r.                         \tag{4.67}
\]

This is the compact H observable itself at old mesh, not just an auxiliary fourth-difference kernel. The notation denotes the readout on the old coarse links and its full matching-star support. It does not replace the previously completed coefficient R₁ by this unintegrated observable.

**Proof.** On the indicated complete star use a single Cartan node gauge to remove the raw endpoint gradient of the first mean. Let v be its dimensionless old link angle in direction 3. In the subpatch with envelope one it is a nonzero fixed multiple of

\[
 r^2\tau\, e^{-\lambda Lm_1}\cos(2\pi m_2/L+\varphi_L)
\]

in the generator T. Other link components are zero in this calculation frame. At b in direction 3 the transverse plaquettes in each matching come from directions 1,2,4 respectively. The projected tangent force in plane μ3 is, up to a common orientation, the traceless antihermitian part of the two based oriented plaquettes. In these Cartan coordinates its linear term is the directional second difference Δ_μ v(m)=v(m+e_μ)+v(m−e_μ)−2v(m). This follows directly by writing the two angles v(m+e_μ)−v(m) and v(m−e_μ)−v(m). Replacing a projected sine by its angle costs O_{L,N}(r⁶τ³). Hence the 13 and 23 forces have linear terms 2(cosh(λL)−1)v and 2(cos(2π/L)−1)v of opposite signs; the 43 force is zero. The coarse mode need not be exactly harmonic after one averaging step. On a fixed positive fraction of tangential phases the difference of its first two forces is at least c_Lr²τ.

To see the exact Wilson factor, write u=U_b, w_{i,±} for the two staples in the matching plane, K_i=w_{i,+}+w_{i,−}. In the real matrix scalar product normalized by the Lie-generator norm κ_T, the sum of the squared generator derivatives of S_i−S_j equals κ_T times the squared tangent projection of K_i−K_j at u. Thus h_b=(κ_Tβ₁/3)Σ_{i<j}∥Π_{T_u}(K_i−K_j)∥². This is exactly the identity derived also in T3 §4.60; the short algebra above establishes the required linear tangent directly here. The normal part starts with the square of a plaquette defect, because the Hermitian part of U_p−1 equals −(U_p−1)(U_p−1)^*/2, and the determinant identity gives a scalar trace quadratic in the defect. Its squared contribution is ≤C_Nβ₁ r⁸τ⁴, which cannot remove the displayed leading quadratic value. There are at least c_Lr⁻³ tested bonds on the bounded face patch. Positivity and multiplication by the specified engineering normalizer prove (4.67). ∎

**Status of the stored-source comparison.** Equations (4.66) and (4.67) concern actual plaquette and matching-force values on W and its raw stored means. They show that bounded *total* physical current on the native outer domain permits opposite large directional contributions on the old boundary trace. For the original already integrated H coefficient, its separately retained dimension-six local derivative-curvature center and all proper differences must be evaluated at their own birth records. Its mean at a coarse target is an integral over the fine fluctuation variables, not substitution into h_b(M¹W). In particular the full ordinary Ward source also includes the Casimir/contact combination prescribed in the question. Equation (4.67) alone proves none of these further identifications.

### 4.35 An exactly formed first-step insertion for the broader source class

**Proposition 4.44 (P, purpose-built Wilson birth and its real root incidence).** Consider the class allowing an arbitrary local bounded gauge-invariant bare observable whose restriction to the first clean chart is analytic and whose physical normalization is the quadratic one in (1.2). There is such an observable whose *already performed first-step normalized source derivative* is the complete reversible depth-one kernel tested in Proposition4.42, with compact sine plaquettes in place of logs. With the natural complete root filing described below, its actual small-Y empty-wall deterministic old row on a true face half-patch has size ≥c g₁⁻²τ²/r−C g₁⁻²τ². Thus a general assertion assigning the positive stable scalar age to every such deterministic old row on every original simultaneous wall is false. This extends the obstruction to a genuinely integrated first-step source in this broader class. It does not identify that source with the prescribed frustration H, and it says nothing about a cancellation after adding other separately formed terms in a total connected pressure.

**Birth.** For an oriented old 23-plaquette write S_m(W) for the traceless antihermitian part of its compact holonomy, based at the lower vertex. For an old direction-2 bond let T denote translation by that bond with its adjoint parallel transport. Define the complete rooted function

\[
 \Theta_m(W)=g_1^{-2}B\bigl(S_m(W),[(T+T^{-1}-2)^2 S(W)]_m\bigr),
                                                               \tag{4.68}
\]

with invariant real trace B and its holomorphic extension using inverse words. The onsite, both nearest-neighbor and both second-neighbor terms are kept together at this one root. All coefficients and path lengths are fixed. In terms of plaquette logarithms S=X+O(X³). The quadratic translation kernel has moments zero through degree two, straight reversed paths enclose no area, and this sine replacement introduces only fourth and higher terms in the paired curvature expansion. It therefore has the clean stable scalar row of (4.57) on the protected families where that estimate was proved, with bounded finite physical birth coefficients at the displayed grade. In particular the initial local analytic amplitude and fixed absolute carrier moment norm are O_L(1) after removing g₁⁻² and the appropriate two powers of the physical birth radius. A change of the group action at the negative edge is not used in (4.68).

On the fine torus insert Σ_m f_m Θ_m(M¹U) into the exponent. These are finite gauge-invariant local bare observables, expressible as continuous functions of the actual raw one-step mean on its chart. If a global compact definition of that mean is unavailable, multiply the local expression by a gauge-invariant smooth cutoff supported where all its finitely many relative comparing path holonomies lie in their fixed logarithmic chart, and extend by zero. The cutoff is one on the complete local first-clean selector: in a fixed L-block pair their relative loops are products of a fixed number of its small plaquettes, with their compact transports; choose the original fixed weak ceiling so these loops lie strictly in the unit portion of this cutoff. The compact observable is bounded at each fixed cutoff and agrees with its analytic expression on that original clean neighborhood. This construction does not alter a selector or require holomorphy of a compact cutoff away from the clean chart.

On the global first clean event used in the Q₂ example the exact one-step Wilson integral has its original delta constraint M¹U=V₁. Consequently its sourced mass at fixed V₁ equals

\[
       \exp\!\left(t\sum_m f_m\Theta_m(V_1)\right)
                       \times T_0(1)(V_1).                    \tag{4.69}
\]

Differentiating its logarithm gives exactly these completed R₁ functions, with no additional first-step field-dependent scalar at this order. This uses the true integration and its actual hard cutoff. It is not an evaluation of a fine primitive in lieu of integration. On a smooth physical test anchor the usual dimension-six conversion is the r⁻² scalar factor in (4.42)–(4.43); equivalently the dimensionless link values in (4.68) on this family are r² times the old curvature values. The bounded fixed-stencil moment constants at birth do not contain a power of the future waiting depth.

**Incidence on the first return.** File Θ_m with its entire signed five-term stencil in the smallest union X_m of its original birth M-cubes containing the coarse edges and the full fine descendants of every raw level-one bond used. The 23-plaquette and all the direction-2 transport edges lie in one normal level-one block layer. A prefix may travel in direction 1 inside such a block, but none of these true two-endpoint-block words can cross its normal face into the next normal block. Since both the old birth M-grid and the true Z face are aligned in this history, for a root on the inner normal side and away from the transverse ends of the chosen patch the resulting X_m is entirely in Z. Its smallest original Y₁ in (1.61) is Z, for *all* its directional terms and neighboring roots in the same tangential slice. The old constrained representative may also require fixed exterior stars; the value and all directed old holonomies used in (4.68) are fixed by the stored rows or by their exact finer children with that exterior. Thus no extra exterior dependence has been inserted into this retained function's support. Along these cube roots the first mixed touch really is e, since the preceding ten-layer predecessor is far inside the present face. This is the original filing for this chosen observable. It does not merge independent old carriers and charge them a new union size.

**Size.** Use a smooth nonnegative f=ζ² with ζ supported transversely away from the corners and ζ=1 on a positive portion of the layer. The part whose roots are filed in the common small Y is exactly the inner normal half-patch, up to the ordinary endpoints of the test support. The tangential K does not cross that normal partition. On all these roots the output is one Cartan field and all holonomies in (4.68) are the ones from Γ of (1.61). Its curvature is bounded in this patch by (4.38). For the sine field in (4.68), r⁻²S=F₁+O(r⁴τ³). This uniform error and its tangential second difference contribute at most C r²τ⁴ to the normalized smooth sum on a fixed patch. The summation-by-parts bound at the end of Proposition4.42 therefore gives

\[
 r^{-2}\sum_{m:\,Y_1(X_m)=Z} f_m\Theta_m(W)
       \ge c_L g_1^{-2}\tau^2/r-C_Lg_1^{-2}\tau^2.           \tag{4.70}
\]

The sum on the left retains the actual unnormalized old discrete sum; the r⁴ factor from the two sine plaquettes is exactly its physical volume factor on the right of (4.51). The signed expression has been estimated as a whole before taking absolute values. An absolute-root norm cannot reduce this positive lower bound. For this test the canonical first-order field sizes are O(τ/g_e): (4.65) has |A|≤C_L rτ and |∇A|≤C_Lτ in entry units, also in the bounded exterior extension. Primary own-scale rescalings by lengths ≤1 only reduce these bounds. The current input is zero and the integrated fluctuation is zero. The only old Q₂ core spike has compact pure gauge and own-scale angle O(δ₁), with δ₁/g_e a fixed logarithmic power. The number of current localization cells of the selected component grows at most polynomially in R_e. Thus the canonical sup-first-norm control (the stored-record construction, (2)) and the indicated test and carrier moments grow at most by fixed powers of log x_e, not r⁻¹. The claimed positive-age right side for this separate row cannot bound (4.70) along r=L^{1−e}, e=2+R₂.

**Scope.** The observable in (4.69) is a deliberately chosen local fine observable in the broader class, and its first-step activity is explicit. If the intended interface is restricted to a particular original H coefficient or fixed specified polynomial representatives, the existence of (4.69) does not put this chosen function into that smaller list. The strictly positive readout (4.70) on an actual empty-wall summand also need not survive the full sum of localization differences, scalar subtractions or connected innovations. A physical theorem phrased only for that total sum requires its own proof. ∎

### 4.36 A principal first-step H calculation after compact lower extraction

**Proposition 4.45 (P, the first constrained Gaussian principal).** Take the homogeneous flat principal Hessian of the original first Wilson integration. Let H₁ be its true constrained mean map and C₁ its covariance on the physical fine kernel of the original level-one average. For the quadratic matching-H form with its actual matching planes, the field-dependent part of its normalized first conditional Gaussian mean is the quadratic matching-H form evaluated on H₁v; its other part is a v-independent trace. After subtracting any fixed finite collection of compact coarse lower representatives, this principal quadratic field dependence cannot vanish on every sufficiently slow Cartan boundary Bloch wave. The conclusion allows coefficients of the representatives to vary; finitely many fixed test frequencies detect a uniformly nonzero residual from their entire finite-dimensional span.

**Proof.** For the linear Cartan field with only a direction-3 component take fine coordinates in one-step fine lattice units and write

\[
 a_3(n)=u^{n_1}\Re(e^{i\varphi}v^{n_2})T,\quad
 v=e^{i\theta},\quad u=e^{-\lambda(\theta)},\quad
 u+u^{-1}+v+v^{-1}=4,
 \quad \lambda(\theta)=2\operatorname{arsinh}(\sin(\theta/2)).    \tag{4.71}
\]

For positive small θ this is a transverse discrete Maxwell harmonic field. Its full one-step coarse straight angle is a fixed nonzero normalization and phase times the product of the two box factors

\[
 B_L(u)B_L(v),\qquad B_L(w)=L^{-1}\sum_{n=0}^{L-1} w^n,        \tag{4.72}
\]

at the coarse normal and tangential Bloch momenta u^L,v^L. The other factors in the block and parallel segment averages are constant for this polarization. Restoring the exact raw endpoint means adds a coarse gradient only. Constants and origin shifts in (4.72) do not affect its zero. The physical traces and matching forces are invariant under these simultaneous node gauge changes.

These exponential modes are used as *local analytic Bloch symbols*, not as infinite probability samples. In the homogeneous flat periodic problem fix the fine block tree and eliminate the one pivot per face as in §4.20. Its constrained Hessian has a size-independent positive gap. The unit-cell matrix in a Bloch fiber is invertible for real momentum and remains so in a fixed complex strip by its finite range. Thus the linear constrained mean and its physical curvature have analytic symbols there. The field (4.71), transformed to that block section, satisfies both the actual fixed rows and the homogeneous Euler equation with zero multiplier. Uniqueness in this fiber gives H₁(Q¹a)=a up to the fine gauge for sufficiently small θ. This step does not claim that the propagator remains bounded for every λ in (4.71).

Write B_match for the collection of differences of matching linear forces, with their fixed positive normalization. The exact constrained Gaussian trace identity is

\[
 \mathbb E\big[\|B_{\rm match}A\|^2\mid Q^1 A=v_1\big]
    =\|B_{\rm match}H_1v_1\|^2+
                \operatorname{Tr}(B_{\rm match} C_1 B_{\rm match}^*).
                                                               \tag{4.73}
\]

It applies also as a sum over the fine roots of one old cell, keeping their offset phase. Average the source phases φ in the real quadratic principal at the mode (4.71); equivalently pair the opposite tangential phases with the same normal decay. Its two nonzero matching forces in directions 1,2 have amplitudes +(2−2cosθ) and −(2−2cosθ), times the fine field; the third is zero. The fine source sum over a single whole cell has a field-dependent coefficient

\[
 E_L(\theta)=c_L (2-2\cos\theta)^2
                     \sum_{n=0}^{L-1}u^{2n},\qquad c_L>0,    \tag{4.74}
\]

up to an additional fixed positive power of u if the cell origin is translated. This is the cell value at the original source subcell sampling, not a single selected alias. It is real analytic for 0<θ<π and strictly positive there.

A fixed compact coarse lower representative has at its quadratic Taylor order a finite bilinear form in the coarse input at finitely many sites. Evaluate it on Q¹a and average over the same phase. Its value is real analytic on that interval. At θ*=2π/L, its gauge-invariant field-dependent value is zero: B_L(v)=0 in (4.72), so the coarse physical field has become a pure gradient. This applies also to a total finite linear combination of such compact representatives. By contrast E_L(θ*)>0. If a combination agreed with (4.74) for all sufficiently small positive θ, analytic continuation of these *explicit finite expressions* along (0,2π/L] would give a contradiction at θ*. Neither the actual Wilson source cumulant nor the Bloch Green function has been continued to that point. This proves nonidentity of the principal residual.

For the finite-frequency version take a small open interval inside the Bloch strip. A finite-dimensional span of continuous functions on a compact subinterval is closed. One may choose finitely many evaluation points, even from θ=2πl/L^m, so that the evaluation of E_L lies outside the evaluated span: choose points of full rank for an independent basis enlarged by E_L, using continuity and density of these L-adic angles. Then the distance in the finite sup norm is strictly positive. At least one of these fixed wavelengths detects the residual, for any coefficients of those compact coarse representatives. A finite wavelength L^mη replaces L²η in the previous construction with constants depending on the finite m. Its first m−1 auxiliary records have fixed relative mesh ratios and the same direct native small-target tests as the p=1 check above. ∎

**Application test for the actual formed H, C.** Suppose its true first-step full homogeneous quadratic kernel, after multiplying by g₀², differs from the matching Gaussian principal in a lifted old exponential two-anchor norm by O(g₀²), with a fixed rate and a separately controlled periodic winding term. Suppose the same continuous compact lower extraction is used. Each of the finitely many Bloch evaluations just chosen is bounded in that norm by absolute convergence at small |log u^L|. Therefore its nonzero principal residual persists for sufficiently weak g₀. T1 (7.123) proposes precisely a physical first-step comparison of this type, obtained by differentiating the actual saddle, Jacobian and determinant on a spare analytic chart. This paragraph does not assume I-1 or substitute a Gaussian measure for the original Wilson law. A finite periodic symbol still needs its genuine lift comparison before such an evaluation; direct substitution of a growing normal exponential into a periodic real source is invalid.

The remaining incidence in a *given* H is substantive. Formula (4.74) concerns its complete homogeneous cell sum. Its old effective curvature kernel can have carriers reaching beyond that cell. At the true face, the small-Y branch keeps only those old carriers wholly on the inner side, with the others filed on different joins. These removed terms have their original source offset, live weights and reference partners. Their separate boundary contribution is not (4.74). Recovering this exact half-space sum, or showing its required cancellation on the original unsimplified marked integral, is the next test for the intended H. The explicit source (4.69) had no such long first-step carrier remainder.

### 4.37 The actual homogeneous H Hessian on finite periodic real probes

**Proposition 4.46 (P with the verified homogeneous first-step comparison, not I-1).** On the homogeneous first clean Wilson family of T1 Corollary7.109, take a fixed compact lower moment extraction on the actual quadratic H birth. For all sufficiently weak g₀ there is one of finitely many real, bounded periodic boundary-wave probes on which the full-cell quadratic Taylor coefficient remaining after that extraction is nonzero. Quantitatively, for a probe whose dimensionless coarse amplitude is r²τ, its g₀²-normalized phase-averaged Hessian on a fixed old cell has magnitude at least c_L r⁴τ². This statement uses the finite periodic compact first-step law itself for the comparison. It neither evaluates its kernel at an exponentially growing periodic test nor assumes a winding lift. It is a result for the complete homogeneous cell coefficient; the restricted small-Y sum of its individually filed old carriers is a different quantity.

**Proof.** The needed separate first-step result is (7.174) of T1, under the original clean smallness and carrier margins of its Theorem7.57. Briefly, its (7.123) differentiates the actual constrained saddle on a fixed spare external disc. The leading term of the mean is β times the matching function on the actual critical field. The Jacobian and Hessian determinant supply rooted analytic one-loop terms bounded there independently of g₀, followed by a rooted O(g₀² log²(1/g₀)) error. Taking two upper-target derivatives at zero on the spare disc and paying the fixed two anchor moments gives an O(g₀²) absolute kernel error *after multiplication by g₀²*. The matching force vanishes at identity, so the leading second derivative is its bare quadratic form on the linear constrained mean, with the fixed g₀²β factor. The sharp first-step selector remains in this estimate. This is first stated on the homogeneous CMP109 fiber. The corresponding newly verified selector comparison for the actual CMP119 first birth of §4.19 is Lemma4.52 below. The Hessian is at identity and only bounded infinitesimal physical target probes are used. We invoke this specific proved first-step comparison on its hypotheses, not the all-history transport interface. An argument outside its homogeneous chart would still need a new comparison.

Choose the finite small L-adic frequencies from Proposition4.45 with λL less than half a fixed one-step inverse decay rate. Use a fine periodic real array equal to (4.71) on D extra old block layers around the chosen cell, with its actual two tangential phases, and cut it off outside those extra layers. It is enough that the fine Maxwell residual d*d a vanish on that neighborhood and be at most C_L exp(λ L D) times the center amplitude elsewhere. Such an array is obtained by extending the exact difference-harmonic normal exponential D layers before cutting off, as in (4.65); a smooth larger tangential cutoff is equal to one on the needed blocks. Cell-block averaging gives the exact factor (4.72) and its complete relative endpoint gauges in this neighborhood. Choose one fixed number of further layers if the compact lower representatives have a larger stencil. Large finite tori contain this construction without wrapping the support. Any overall compact gauge change for the raw endpoint gradient transforms all data together and does not change the cell observable.

For clarity the Gaussian conditional mean for this *periodic real* array is close to the Bloch principal on the chosen cell, uniformly in the torus size. In the flat fine block-tree section write its difference from the given trial as K u, with K the finite-range reconstruction of independent zero-target coordinates (including the true pivot corrections). Then H₀u=−K^t d*d a. Passing the trial into the tree section changes it by a gradient and leaves this residual unchanged. The one-step lower gap and exponential inverse in §4.20 apply on the periodic lattice. The residual vanishes on D layers, so the first few fine curls and matching differences at the central cell differ by at most C_L exp[−(c_L−λL)D] times the center amplitude, after decreasing c_L once for the polynomial sphere count. This establishes the finite real probe approximation without a complex infinite-volume comparison.

Take D fixed and large enough that this error, after the cell and phase averages, is below one quarter of the positive distance of (4.74) from the finite compact span on the previously chosen frequencies. The compact representatives in this neighborhood are evaluated on exactly the stated coarse trial, not on a growing test imported from another space. The rooted finite-torus estimate (7.174) now adds at most C_{L,D}g₀² times the square of the center amplitude. Taking g₀ weak enough preserves the required lower bound for at least one frequency, independently of which coefficients of the given compact extraction occur. Phase averaging is a sum of the actual fine roots of the cell and of real target phases, so some actual phase attains the bound in absolute value. Multiplication of the trial amplitude by r²τ gives the declared normalization. ∎

In particular the principal calculation in (4.74) is not being used as the Wilson law. The true finite-cutoff first H Hessian has now been compared on bounded real arrays. Inferring the sign or magnitude of the whole small-Y old marked R term from this cell Hessian would require control of the old carriers that meet the physical outside of Z; (7.174) alone does not separate those carriers by their localization support. The possible cancellation of their original root phases on the half-space is the remaining application step.

### 4.38 The prescribed H subtraction in the actual fresh R integral

**Proposition 4.47 (P, the pinned part cancels from the within-history innovation).** Fix the original returning component and one discrete history class in (1.71), retaining its true joint selector, reference factors, coarse target, B and V″ variables. At an empty wall let A_Y(z) be the portion of the already formed H quadratic/cubic readout and its actual compact marginal/contact subtraction whose full *field slots and paths* lie on the pinned strip. Include its original coefficients and test phases. Then A_Y(z) is independent of the newly integrated variables in this history. Its contribution to the same-mass connected pairing with the full inherited real or complex vertex V is identically zero for every interpolation t. On the competing Y⁺ record the boundary-wave variation is zero for the whole record at fixed integration variables. In particular the discrepancy of the two fully pinned field variations does not survive as a new within-history covariance term. It does survive in an undifferentiated marked mean unless another actual summand cancels it there.

**Proof.** On Y the zero-walk support of §4.30 fixes the entire strip, including its frame blocks, independently of the value of the forcing in (1.55) and independently of which allowed integrated fields occurred in the fixed history class. All finite old plaquette slots and their prescribed transports there are functions exclusively of the outer z. This applies to the covariant quadratic/cubic kernels of the actual H birth, including the original fixed compact lower polynomials, by applying the statement to their indicated slots. For the larger Y⁺ the change in z in §§4.29–4.33 does not change any of the raw arguments seen by that record, including the extracted V_Λ, so its variation at fixed ξ vanishes. For any nonzero-mass complex functional T on this same joint integration, the definition gives exactly

\[
 T(A_Y V)-\frac{T A_Y\,T V}{T1}=A_Y TV-A_Y TV=0.           \tag{4.75}
\]

No positivity is needed for this algebraic cancellation. Equivalently extract exp(t_src A_Y) from the integral before differentiating. Linearity applies to the signed H and marginal difference together, without an extra rare factor. The covariance identity concerns an old tagged term with the new vertex. It does not remove a first source derivative of the logarithm of the mass itself, nor a connected response in which the large-record kernel depends on other exterior arguments.

For clarity, if discrete history labels vary in the same integration, write m_h=T_h1, a_h=T_hF/m_h and w_h=T_hV/m_h. One must add to the sum of the within-h covariances the between-history term

\[
 \sum_h m_h (a_h-\bar a)(w_h-\bar w),\qquad
 \bar a=\frac{\sum_hm_ha_h}{\sum_hm_h},\quad
 \bar w=\frac{\sum_hm_hw_h}{\sum_hm_h}.                       \tag{4.76}
\]

It vanishes for a single identical pinned scalar shared by all those branches. It need not vanish if source incidence, the endpoint data or the old source birth varies with the history. Equation (4.76) is exact also for a complex measure on a common nonzero-mass branch. ∎

**Proposition 4.48 (P/L, one-reserve raw payment for the actual low H packet in this waiting class).** Consider the one-component first return e=b+R_b above, b=2, j=1, on the constant-R weak interval. Take the actual homogeneous first-birth covariant quadratic/cubic H kernels on their verified native carrier norm, their fixed compact marginal/contact representatives, and the signed inner/outer component filings. Test their *raw* connected pairing with the full inherited vertex of the original R body. On its documented outer phase domain, for each fixed finite collection of these scalar rows one has the bound

\[
 |\mathcal C_{t,z}(F^{H,\mathrm{low}},V)|
 \le C A_1\,g_e\,(g_e/g_1)^2\, r^s e^{-p_0(g_e)}            \tag{4.77}
\]

for the part of the body in this waiting class and a fixed current-unit test patch, with changed fixed powers for the cubic grade as specified below. This estimate counts one genuine component reserve. It gives the same numerical age or any other fixed finite age power for these raw rows in this specific short-in-log-depth waiting class. It does not give a bound of (4.77) after division by the mass of the rare component. The physical upper-target derivatives of a sharp indicator or an independently localized vertex are not asserted to satisfy this scalar estimate automatically.

**Incoming compact low rows.** A finite-step curvature representation with the original absolute moments has per-root coefficients bounded by fixed multiples of g₁⁻² and its actual finite radius/grade factors (a conservative g₁⁻³ can be used for a cubic). On the compact real branch the sine plaquette, each normalized fixed-order trace and each compact representative are bounded, and its adjoint transports are unitary. A retained plaquette-log slot on its original small branch has a uniform bound as well. At old depth one, any physical Wilson current word on its fine grid has a fixed mesh bound from its finite incident star. Thus after the separate dimension-six normalizer the absolute sum over all possible old roots on this current patch is bounded by C A₁ g₁^{-m} r⁻⁶ times a fixed polynomial in R_e, for a fixed m determined by the displayed H rows. This deliberately rough estimate keeps the actual r⁻⁴ root multiplicity and r⁻² normalizer. The finite compact marginal or cubic-area polynomial is included with its sign and its original coefficient. The same estimate applies to a difference of two such actual records; it does not identify their different fields. On the complex native extension the real/complex bounds of LFR I (1.64)–(1.69) put every fine link within O(g₁ times a fixed logarithmic power) of a compact link on its own mesh. The level-one raw words and their complete endpoint paths have fixed length in fine lattice units. Their adjoint norms cost exp(C_L ε n) on an old path of length n, with ε tending to zero. The fixed original exponential carrier and path moment absorbs this factor on its original spare rate. This gives the same rough compact-kernel bound for those covariant H rows throughout their original target ranges. This argument concerns their actual finite physical coarse slots; a different independent-current or noncompact analytic tail without this representation requires its own estimate.

**Raw connected calculation.** Write M_t for the genuine positive mass of the interpolated original real operation and σ_{t,z} for its small complex variation. The interpolation uses the absolute full inherited bound (1.69), as in the stored-record construction, (8.2)–(8.6): the parameter t multiplies that whole inherited interaction with 0≤t≤1, preserving its original positive majorant and the same Wilson and determinant cancellations. The small phase and the exact common-mass bound give

\[
 |\mathcal C_{t,z}(F,V)|\le C_\epsilon M_t\sup|F|\sup|V|.   \tag{4.78}
\]

The bound follows by expanding the two terms of the raw covariance, using |T1|≥c_ε M_t on the common positive reference. For a positive subfamily of histories the same calculation applies to its own raw covariance; the full between-family term in (4.76) must be retained when the body is reassembled. In fact each term in the full raw pairing with F restricted to this subfamily has an upper bound of the same form with M_t replaced by its subfamily mass and sup|V| taken over the complete body. This follows directly from T(FV) and T(F) TV/T1, with the denominator bound on the whole body. It does not lower-bound the subfamily's mass.

Here |V| in (1.69) is at most C times the number of old determining bonds in the component. Even the brutal upper estimate counting *all* bonds at every level is ≤C(1+e)(1+MR_e)^4η⁻⁴ in the stated waiting component. All their actual incidence multiplicities are thereby covered without treating them as new failed sites. On the original interpolated positive majorant the proof of (1.89), printed p.387, gives M_t≤exp[−2(1+β₀)⁻¹p₀(g_e)] for this component; this use of interpolation requires the same absolute full-V majorant (not replacing it by a signed selected subfamily). The paper chooses β₀ small, for example 1/7 in its estimate before (1.95). In that estimate the source also checks ¼p₀(g_e)≥1+2κ(100R_e)^4. Consequently p₀ controls every fixed multiple of R_e and of log x_e on this trajectory. The incoming engineering cost and the number of bonds above have logarithm O(R_e+log x_e+log(1+R_e)); r=L^{−R_b+O(1)}. Therefore the strictly positive surplus 2/(1+β₀)−1 of the single exponent pays these costs, the fixed original coupling/radius powers, one g_e and any required r^s (or r^β, r² when those are the actual incoming rows). The regular coupling ratio g_e/g₁ stays bounded above and below on this interval. Inserting into (4.78) proves (4.77). A fixed deterministic scalar common to the body multiplies this inequality once if it was not included in T. The proof spends no fabricated reserve per old source root. ∎

The distinction in this test is quantitative. The actual deterministic pinned discrepancy cancels exactly from its within-history fresh covariance. The remaining signed compact low H contribution, including a history-dependent part of (4.76), is small *after* the original raw one-component integration in this waiting profile. The normalized unpinned H covariance and the entire original H source with its analytic remainder, earlier proper differences and weak selector/contact derivatives are not decided by these two facts. An earlier birth with e−j much larger than R_e also cannot charge an arbitrary earlier age to this one reserve.

### 4.39 The actual exterior completion and the signed healing bracket

**Proposition 4.49 (P/L, proper signed returning contribution of the prescribed low H rows).** In the Q₂ example of §§4.29–4.35 fix the actual returning component X_* as a geometric set, including its home, and take the healing sector with no still-live component. Keep all other compatible returns in the original signed bracket of LFR II (1.90). At the empty wall a classical first-clean H term whose complete depth-zero fine star is pinned on the small Y has zero *proper* return term. A marginal or contact term supported on the same retained fine data also has zero proper term. Each remains once in the exterior action. On the one-cube extension Y⁺ the old-minus-completed term is a distinct insertion and has to be retained. For the actual compact quadratic/cubic H rows and compact lower partners of Proposition4.48, the first and mixed second logarithmic source rows of this signed healing bracket, tagged on these records at X_*, are bounded by (4.77), with the incoming finite products of caps and grade powers appropriate to the labels. This also holds for a formed contact row with its own incoming cap. The tag may be in a crossing Mayer vertex. This is a scalar estimate at fixed external state, with the original signed denominator. It does not assert cancellation of the exterior action or admit the independent old current jets of an arbitrary inherited analytic member.

**The exterior term.** LFR II printed p.377 adds the missing old terms on their new determining record to the effective action and subtracts those same terms from the corresponding V(Y) before (1.70). Write C_X(U,J) for this extension of the particular old sourced function and O_{h,X}(U,J;ξ) for its actually prepared contribution with discrete record h and integration variables ξ. With the sign convention of the logarithmic source, its proper tag is
\[
 D_{h,X}=O_{h,X}-C_X.                                      \tag{4.79}
\]
Use this formula *before* the final Mayer resummation. The extension C_X is fixed in the integrals at the given outer pair. Even if the new physical critical field at MᵏU=I is flat, its analytic argument on the prescribed outer member domain has fine slots. For the local classical part β₀H_b(U) of the actual first-clean coefficient, formula (7.123) of T1 identifies those exact slots as its original fine plaquette star. The proof requires only the local saddle term of that formula, independently of the all-history I-1 assertion. Choose the old first-step minimal boundary strip to meet the true face in the example and place b and its whole star in the interior of that strip, also in the corresponding finest strip of Y. Both joined and old projections literally keep these fine bonds, their endpoint transports and their prescribed exterior there. Thus O_{-,b}=β₀H_b(U)=C_b for every ξ, so D_{-,b}=0. Any finite lower representative on precisely the replayed data obeys the same identity with its actual sign. On Y⁺ the wave has MᵏU=I and lies strictly inside the top-row piece. Its variation is invisible to the old readout at fixed ξ by §§4.29–4.30. Consequently Δ_z D_{+,b}=−Δ_z C_b whenever this filing occurs. No Y⁺ record is inserted for a carrier whose prescription uses only Y. This computation uses the actual fine pins, stronger than the preservation of a coarse plaquette. On the mode (4.65) the fine matching quadratic on nonzero phases is of order β₀τ²r⁴ per root. The cancellation on the small branch is exact even at this order. Equations (4.75)–(4.76) still apply to covariance with the fresh vertex. The constant C_X cancels in every normalized within-history covariance, whatever its outer value.

**Signed denominator and a marked connecting vertex.** Write B for the actual bracket in (1.90), w_P for its polymer (1.91), and B_{¬P} for the same signed gas with neighbors incompatible with P excluded. The convergent connected logarithm in (1.98) proves, by subtracting its expansion with these exclusions,
\[
 B_{¬P}/B=\exp[-\sum_{\mathcal T:\,\mathcal T\text{ hits the exclusion of }P}
                          \phi_{\mathcal T}],\qquad
 |B_{¬P}/B|\le e^{c|P|}.                                    \tag{4.80}
\]
Here |P| counts the native M cubes; the rooted absolute cluster sum in (1.97)–(1.99) bounds c and it can be made smaller than the spare polymer decay. This is the same argument as T1 (7.183), keeping the whole signed gas. For a tag inserted *inside* T′(X_*) use its absolute supremum in the positive reference majorant (1.73). For a tag filed instead in the actual enlarged domain Y⁺, differentiate its Mayer factor before the component integration: the first derivative of exp(V)-1 is exp(V)D, and the mixed second derivative is exp(V)(D₁D₂+D₁₂). Its support remains the original Y⁺. If a derivative creates a selected edge absent from a zero-source graph, include that edge in its graph before assigning the union P. The unmarked incidence constraint is unchanged in this expansion over selected edges. On (and only on) this actual one-cube extension of the one fixed component, the native relative distance (1.67) is bounded by a fixed constant, since Y⁺ outside X_* adds one current M cube. Choose the fixed small upper majorant α in (1.68) once on the weak interval. An upper bound K on D, or K₁K₂+K₁₂ on the two rows, therefore replaces the single α exp[−(1+2β)κd_{k,Z}(Y⁺)] by at most a fixed multiple of K (respectively K₁K₂+K₁₂) times that *majorant*. This compares to the positive graph bound, never to the possibly vanishing value of the old Mayer factor. It pays a fixed α⁻¹ for each designated edge. Other selected edges and their connectivity retain precisely their factors in (1.92)–(1.94).

For these finite compact signed rows one may take K=C A₁g₁^{-m}r⁻⁶ poly(R_e) by the real/complex old-scale word estimate in Proposition4.48, including its actual marginal/contact term, roots, engineering factor and subtractand C_X. Product bounds suffice for fixed two labels. This assertion is for the globally bounded physical compact low polynomials or for actual readouts certified on a spare old chart. In particular it does not replace that certification for a general noncompact remainder. It uses the actual single birth at j=1, the marked current-scale patch, and the original complex path lengths. Exterior link frame words cost exp(C ε times path length), paid by the spare old rate there. On the selected boundary-wave curve the compact old slots used in the exact pinned cancellation vary by at most C_L r²τ, inside any of their saved radii ρ₁ with C_L r²τ<ρ₁; this comparison is explicit and is satisfied on the fixed spare first-clean radius (and on the g₁ log-power radii with positive margin in §4.8). The bulk rough low-row supremum does not require all off-shell targets to obey this stronger pin estimate.

A geometric reserve survives the signed normalization. Before (1.95) each of the q returning components has exponent at most −3p₀(g_e)/2 after paying its diameter completion. In the sum with the *specific geometric component X_* fixed*, reserve its full exp[−3p₀/2]. Sum the other q−1 as in (1.96), each with its usual summation cost; there is no count of shapes of X_* at this place. More explicitly, order this component first in the unordered family. For q−1 others drop the incidence restrictions after charging the common tree decay in (1.95), just as in (1.96). Their remaining positive sum is bounded by its original exponential in |P|, canceled by the same spare size-decay in (1.95)–(1.97). The factor for the fixed geometric component is constant in this sum and is not spent a second time. Selecting its position in an ordered convention costs at most q, equivalently changing q! to (q−1)!, already included in the remaining exponential series. Thus the rooted gas sum containing this mark has, at a reduced fixed tree rate, majorant C K exp[−3p₀/2]. A fixed mark on an adjoining cube has the same bound since P still contains the home of X_*. This argument need not hold with this surplus when one sums freely over all possible distinguished shapes.

Differentiate the original signed polynomial in its activities. One derivative of log B is the sum of w_{P,1}B_{¬P}/B. Two derivatives give its single-polymer w_{P,12} term, a sum for distinct compatible polymers, and the subtraction of the product of first rows. Both designated labels require a polymer containing the same home X_*, so the distinct compatible term is empty here. Formula (4.80) absorbs in each single-polymer sum. One obtains
\[
 |\partial_1\log B|\le C K_1e^{-3p_0/2},\quad
 |\partial_1\partial_2\log B|
 \le C (K_{12}+K_1K_2)e^{-3p_0/2}+C K_1K_2 e^{-3p_0}.
                                                               \tag{4.81}
\]
All masses and exclusions are those of the actual same signed (1.90); there is no use of a lower estimate on T′(X_*)1. Constants in (4.81) include the fixed majorant expense of the one or two differentiated short edges. The logarithms of the finite K products, inverse incoming coupling/radius factors, r^{-s}, and one g_e^{-1} are O(R_e+log x_e+log(1+R_e)) in this waiting profile. As in Proposition4.48, the spare p₀/2 dominates them on a sufficiently weak interval. Restoring the chosen incoming caps and grade gives the bound in (4.77) for the corresponding signed normalized coefficients. This also treats a finite mixed tag with V in the T′ exponent by inserting its actual polynomial supremum from (1.69). A long separately localized V(Y) requires its native connecting decay, beyond the single adjacent edge just proved. ∎

The exact answer on this returning geometry depends on which quantity is estimated. The pinned classical H and matched lower proper tag vanish on the small record. The distinct large record can retain a deterministic exterior difference, but its *signed returning* logarithmic coefficient has its saved scalar age by (4.81). No term of that coefficient can cancel the already separated exterior C_X simply because it is large on an auxiliary wall. The physical density evaluates the completed coefficients at the actual new critical orbit. Estimates for their off-shell exterior continuation must respect their own original comparison maps. Neither the whole all-wall outer current/anchor norm nor an arbitrarily long prior lifetime follows from (4.81).

### 4.40 Screening the fresh integration at a nonpinned boundary readout

There is a separate estimate which does not use the probability of a rare return. It applies on the genuine empty-wall Q₂ geometry and Cartan outer curve of §4.33. It compares two critical fields of the *same* Y, not the critical fields of Y and Y⁺.

**Lemma 4.50 (P/L, inner cut for a native annular problem).** Work with the original fixed spacing and summability test of Proposition4.24. Let Γ be the field (1.61) for either exceptional Y in this returning geometry. All retained ξ of the given one-component geometry may vary on the original selector. On the part of Y outside an aligned rectangular inner cut in its outer top-scale ring, Γ is gauge-equivalent to a solution of a native CMP102 determining problem with the following targets. Its genuine outer boundary targets are M^•U, its middle k-targets are MᵏU=I, and its inner cut targets, after a simultaneous change of frame there, have log norm at most Cτ_e. Here the rings and outward comparison are measured in entry units. This auxiliary determining problem is used only to estimate the original solution.

**Proof.** For the small record use the rectangular Z built from the ordinary eight and ten layer construction. The inner Λ of the R operation, including the one-M enlargement of LFR I (1.73), is separated from its outer face by at least 2MR_e−dM. The old non-top layers are also inside the eight-layer enlargement described in §4.16. Choose a rectangular cut aligned in whole M cubes roughly MR_e inside the outer faces, with the fixed complete block buffer at both boundaries. Its entire boundary is in the regular top ring and misses the inner Λ and the seam of the glued old determining sets. For Y⁺ use the same cut, with its boundary still in the common top ring. D is the part of the respective Y outside the cut. Its two boundary components are separated by much more than four M₁ layers. Install the minimal CMP119 (2.13) collar at the cut and keep the original collar at the true outer boundary. In the middle the actual hierarchy is the original top-scale hierarchy; the joined nested domains are intersections with the minimal domains of these two collars as in (2.14). The local distances at the two boundaries are exactly those of a top-layer minimal record. A path crossing between the collars traverses the intervening top layers. The native fixed-parameter summability test already used for the top collar therefore applies. This argument does not apply where an inner boundary itself meets genuine finer old layers.

On every complete block in the true collar the empty-wall H_k, H_{B₁}, H″_k and their local endpoint frames vanish, by the zero outermost walk argument of §4.30. At (1.63) the outer targets are consequently the actual M^•U. At (1.62) and (1.61) the preceding critical fields obey that *same* minimal B(Y) collar, so taking their prescribed raw averages replays those targets exactly. The source explicitly identifies V″ on Λᶜ with the data of the new determining configuration, LFR II p.370 after (1.50); p.374 replaces that data by M^•U in the extended (1.63). Thus the middle k-rows outside the cut are unchanged k-rows of the new field. They are I for our wave, for all inner ξ. The correction (1.55) has not been suppressed on the inner side. Its output on the complete true collar is zero on this specified wall only. On the new inner collar use the actual descendants of Γ, retaining its complete exterior fine stars at the analysis cut. Any variation preserving the new inner rows and fixed boundary also preserves the old middle k-rows by the original raw descendant recursion. Its action variation on D is a permitted variation in Y extended by zero through the pinned stars. Since Γ is the critical orbit in (1.61), its restriction is critical for this more constrained native problem.

Here is a uniform choice of inner raw gauges, needed because small plaquettes alone do not bound individual open links in the given gauge. Cover both sides of the cut's fixed block allowance by the original k-blocks. In each use an ordered tree based at its k-node, with node frame identity there. Use this change jointly on the complete artificial inner target and its prescribed exterior, and leave the outer collar unchanged. The middle k-rows remain I by node gauge covariance. In a two-block rectangle containing an inner face, one can instead choose a single common ordered tree based at the left k-node, reaching the right k-node by an original coordinate path. In this latter gauge every fine angle is O(ητ_e) by strip filling, because the genuine physical plaquettes obey (4.38) on its entire extended rectangle. For complex Γ the same computation uses its real comparator with the original analytic perturbation, or the analytic tree itself: the norms of the finitely long physical path conjugations are at most exp(Cα₁,e). In this tree the raw k-angle is O(τ_e), by the centered-mean product and the sum of its actual endpoint fields in the proof of (4.33). In the original node gauge that same k-row is I. The transformation between the two k-node frames therefore differs from identity by O(τ_e). Within each block the ratio of the common and the separately rooted trees differs from its value at the rooted k-node by O(τ_e), by the two physical path bounds or the enclosed plaquette filling. Hence on their whole union the relative gauges at all fine nodes differ from one constant by O(τ_e). Every actual raw h-bond in this pair in the common tree has angle O(τ_e) by the same raw-mean estimate through h≤k. Returning to the separate block trees changes its two endpoint factors by O(τ_e), up to a common bounded conjugation. This establishes the asserted bound on every cut target including bare fine bonds and their prescribed exterior. The argument is on adjacent original blocks and does not multiply one fixed Lipschitz loss at each age. Enlarging the selected set of blocks by a fixed collar permits all its required descendant stencils. Across the outside of this gauge patch no newly prescribed fine rows occur; all middle constraints are k-rows with identity endpoint frames.

The regular-orbit uniqueness in CMP102 applies on D for real data: in the cut and middle rings the original fine plaquettes are O(η²τ_e), meeting every local native regularity ceiling there. On the true boundary Γ is the original weak member on its original collar with (1.65), which meets the native regularity test. For the complex outer wave the same identification follows by continuation from the real data, with the cut tree gauges and the complete raw targets continued together. This argument compares physical orbits. It does not identify the varying inverse axial frame (106) with a transformation fixed just at the coarse nodes. ∎

**Proposition 4.51 (P/L, scalar same-mass innovation on these actual records).** For each fixed original depth-one H carrier with all old determining rows, path prefixes and exterior in the outer half of this annulus, take its actual readout on Y or Y⁺. There is a deterministic reference readout for that carrier on this original outer wave. For compact finite H, marginal and contact rows summed over a fixed current test patch of such roots, including their old curvature and physical currents on this depth-one scale, the normalized fresh covariance with the inherited component vertex obeys
\[
 \left|\frac{T(FV)}{T1}-\frac{TF\,TV}{(T1)^2}\right|
 \le C A_1g_1^{-m}(1+R_e)^C r^{-C_0}\tau_e
                      e^{-c M R_e}.                          \tag{4.82}
\]
Here m,C₀ are fixed finite constants for the stated rows. The same estimate applies to the fixed signed proper difference (4.79). No rare exponential or tiny denominator is used here. On original parameter choices with cM larger than (C₀+s+1)log L, (4.82) retains the fixed positive scalar age r^s in this waiting example, and the fixed coupling/radius factors in (4.77) apart from its rare exponential. The rate condition is a test on the original parameter choice for this last numerical consequence, not a change to the source domain.

**Proof.** On D define a reference native problem with the same true outer collar, all middle k-rows I and *identity* artificial inner collar rows and exterior. It is deterministic for fixed Y and U. It is important to keep the nonzero true outer wave on the small record. Its raw angles are bounded by C_Lr²τ on this particular curve (for (4.65) only levels zero and one can vary; the fixed longer wavelengths of §4.36 use finitely more levels). The wave and its complete stencils are interior top data on Y⁺; the corresponding reference is flat there. The targets of Γ furnished by Lemma4.50 and those of the reference are both in a fixed small complex log chart about the real identity on the *same* auxiliary D. The only differences in these tuples are supported at the artificial cut. On the line joining them use CMP102 Proposition9, (190), in its stated relative Landau representative. The physical orbits of the two restricted solutions are represented in this comparison. Every contributing input on the cut has dimensionless angle at most Cτ_e. Its native distance to the outer tested region is ≥cMR_e, because no native mesh exceeds one and a complete cube step changes physical position by at most a fixed constant in the source metric. Using its derivative bound and the native density of the input, the difference of the dimensionless fine link angles in these representatives is at most
\[
 C(1+R_e)^4\eta^{-8}\tau_e\exp[-c M R_e]                     \tag{4.83}
\]
uniformly on the indicated outer region and a fixed complete enlargement. A deliberate overestimate here counts all bonds on every cut mesh: at most C(1+R_e)^4η⁻⁴ by geometric summation in the mesh, and even if one discards the native integration volume and retains the whole σ_x⁻⁴ from (190), this costs at most η⁻⁴ more. The output potential factor σ_y⁻¹ cancels against the η of a fine link, since η≤σ_y. One may instead perform the normalized native sum of §3.9, gaining sharper mesh powers; (4.83) already suffices for the displayed parameter test. The reference alone has uniform dimensionless fine angles ≤C r²τ, by integrating (190) on its small genuine outer targets with the native measure and summability in §3.9. This bound follows in the Landau comparison about identity. A simultaneous gauge change of every input and boundary of a physical scalar has the same value, irrespective of how the raw axial frame would vary.

In these common chart coordinates the true P_old q_old reconstruction at depth one uses fixed-length words and their complete exterior on the specified patch. Its two raw target tuples at each old node differ by at most a fixed multiple of (4.83). Their absolute angles are bounded by C(r²τ plus the right side of (4.83)); even without numerical comparison of the rate to η⁻⁸ the native-measure sum in the previous paragraph gives an O(τ_e) sup bound. CMP102 (190) on this one-step original record, with all meshes comparable in birth units, propagates the difference to its old fine field and physical current with the same bound. The old current is a fixed incident-star Wilson word at this finite depth; the native k reconstruction current is not substituted for it. A coarse or fine value fixed at a boundary is replayed directly. All these statements use gauge-equivalent representatives with their prescribed exteriors transformed also. For any fixed-size old determining support the reference has old potential and current O_L(r²τ) in its transported chart. Thus the explicit saved radius test here is
\[
 C_X\{r^2\tau +(1+R_e)^4\eta^{-8}\tau_e e^{-cMR_e}\}<\rho_1.  \tag{4.84}
\]
It holds with strict margin on weak trajectories for the displayed first-clean spare radii and sufficiently large fixed cM. For a support with an extra global volume norm its volume factor must be restored. This is a birth-unit estimate at nonpinned old bonds on the true-boundary records, not an AE8 sum on their rough interiors.

Evaluate each compact physical H/compact lower row at its corresponding deterministic reference to get F_ref. The value may differ between Y and Y⁺. For a given filed tag it is fixed independently of ξ and includes the actual C_X with its sign if appropriate. The mean-value bound for each finite path/word uses its correct path length; a polynomial in length is paid by the original spare exponential moment. With at most C r⁻⁴ old roots, the r⁻² dimension-six normalization, the rough old coupling/cap factors of §4.38, and the absolute bound on the complete V from (1.69), the product sup|F−F_ref| sup|V| is at most the right side of (4.82), with enlarged finite C₀. If a X extends into the inner half of the annulus while its root stays on the outer face, it has diam₁ X≥cMR_e/r. For a globally compact old polynomial with the original exponential carrier norm, the crude supremum from Proposition4.48 then gives an exponentially smaller tail.
