This tail assertion concerns genuine compact rows, not evaluation of a general analytic activity outside its original domain. For fixed old supports fitted to (4.84), a genuinely analytic H one-loop member on its original spare chart obeys the same mean-value argument with its actual Cauchy radius and all its indicated old slots.

Finally subtract F_ref inside the same-mass covariance. Formula (3.1) bounds the normalized absolute variation by a fixed constant on the source phase tube. This proves (4.82), including all retained integrated fields of the selected geometry. It also allows a mixture of histories for which this *same tag and reference* are present on the same geometric record. In general the two different references at Y and Y⁺ must be retained in the between-history term (4.76). Since r=L^{−R₂+O(1)}, R_e is the original scale of order (log x_e)^ν, with fixed ν>1 in CMP119 (2.5) and log g_e^{-1}=O(log x_e), the explicit rate comparison after (4.82) yields the stated scalar age. The original M and decay after the native summability test determine this comparison; (4.81) is the independent single-reserve bound when this comparison has not been verified. ∎

### 4.41 The actual first-selector and the whole depth-one readout

**Lemma 4.52 (P/L, the homogeneous CMP119 first selector).** On Ω₁=Λ₁ equal to the torus and R₁ empty, the canonical integration cutoff in CMP119 differs from the fixed physical cutoff used in the homogeneous CMP109 comparison in Proposition4.46. On the first clean physical graph, with the strict homogeneous carrier and Gaussian margins used there, this changes its g₀²-normalized two-field Hessian for one H tag by at most C exp[−c p₀(g₀)²] in a smaller fixed two-anchor norm. In particular the finite periodic full-cell conclusion of Proposition4.46 holds also for this particular first birth in the actual CMP119 history. The first-step background selector remains an exterior factor.

**Proof.** Printed pp.249–250 of CMP119 distinguish the two coordinates exactly. The linearizing map affects only each b₀(c). After A'=CA solves Q̃A'=0, the letter A in (1.15) denotes integration variables on Ω₁*=Ω₁ minus those b₀(c). Formula (1.18) bounds the sup of |A(b)| on the *starred* subsets of the covering cubes. On the homogeneous R₁=∅ sector their union covers all the independent bonds, giving the common canonical threshold P=g₀⁻¹δ₀=A₁p₀(g₀), in the source's own convention for the positive constant. The threshold tends to infinity. The second indicator in (1.19) is removed on these small cubes by the source's own comparison, and with Λ₁ the whole torus the (1.22) boundary change is absent. Thus the A domain of this conditional coefficient is independent of the upper field. The remaining exterior small-field selector on that upper field restricts the density, and is not included in its holomorphic coefficient. This is the reason Cauchy differentiation of the coefficient in its upper graph is permitted here; one could not apply it to a moving, undifferentiated sharp indicator.

We detail the tagged-selector comparison on that physical graph. Use the same canonical coordinates and same signed connected localization for the two thresholds, actual P and fixed physical P_out=ε₁/g₀. A possible fixed equivalence between the finite-dimensional Lie-algebra norms only changes constants in the lower threshold for a failed site. Take an independent source holomorphic radius |s_b|≤c g₀² **for this comparison only**. The literal perturbation s_b β₀H_b of the fine Wilson integrand is then a small local vertex on the unchanged canonical contour, including in the fixed outer physical chart containing the swept constraints and the true coupled plaquette words. The finite words and the finite corridor reconstruction at the original one-step scale have a uniform bound on the spare physical domain of the reference expansion. Their derivatives with respect to native weakening parameters have the same anchored Cauchy estimate by expanding the actual walk/determining inputs. No translated source saddle, derivative of the sharp cutoff or comparison at a displaced contour is required. On the unmarked physical graph the dangerous linear action term vanishes by stationarity, as stated immediately before (1.17). The original strict Gaussian and small-vertex majorants therefore remain available on a fixed sufficiently small physical graph chart for these sources. Equivalently expand the one or two local source factors first as in the finite local-species argument for T1 Lemma7.25, using the displayed smaller radius in place of a fixed physical source radius. This step applies on the homogeneous physical graph and on its certified native analytic chart, not to arbitrary large independent off-graph currents.

In that expansion replace each broad selector by itself plus its difference from the narrow selector. A term with an actual difference has an independent coordinate of norm ≥cP. The changed factor is tested on the original real canonical coordinate in the conditioned interior, before exterior Gaussian whitening. With the native quadratic reserve on the joint real integration this inserts exp(−cP²) per distinct constrained coordinate, leaving a smaller strict reserve. This is the tagged-cutoff argument of T1 (7.76): multiplying the changed local factors by a separate scalar ζ gives an absolutely convergent rooted expansion on |ζ| exp(−cP²)≤c₀. All original connected coefficients then have a rooted analytic majorant there. Subtract ζ=0 from ζ=1; Cauchy's formula retains C exp(−cP²) on a smaller fixed carrier rate. This compares rooted logarithms, avoiding a torus-volume bound on the probability of any failure. The same proof on a smaller physical upper disc gives its uniform carrier supremum, since none of these new real indicator coordinates depends on the analytic upper parameter. In real variables it agrees with the source's original integral wherever its genuine exterior selector is one; the integral with the fixed independent-coordinate cutoff constructs its analytic coefficient separately on that disc. Cauchy on |s_b|≤c g₀² costs C g₀⁻², canceled by the g₀² normalization in Proposition4.46. Two derivatives at zero in the physical upper chart cost fixed factors and the two original anchor moments; the usual source (190) homogeneous one-step reconstruction attaches their real anchors with exponential tails, as in T1 (7.174). This proves the stated estimate. Since P grows as a positive power of log g₀⁻², the cutoff difference is smaller than g₀² here. The compact lower extraction and the finite real-probe argument of §4.37 are unchanged. ∎

The literal β₀H_b(U) on the pinned record in Proposition4.49 may in any event be split off by adding and subtracting its zero-fluctuation value *inside the first source expectation*: at first order this is an exact identity for either canonical selector. The lemma supplies a source-specific comparison for the complete homogeneous Hessian. A later R history has its own selectors and cannot be brought under this calculation simply by quoting the result on Ω₁=Λ₁.

**Proposition 4.53 (P/L, first-birth physical readmission throughout the positive old layers).** Let the entire first-step readset, its fixed exterior stars and the complete depth-zero/one raw stencils lie in the portion on which the actual output Γ of (1.65) satisfies one of LFR I (1.64), (1.66), (1.68), with positive layer index. Include their literal fixed exterior whenever the old problem takes a value from outside Y. Denote the actual source ceilings there by α_{i,n}, with the constants and tilde versions of the defining member. Put
\[
 d_1=C_L\sup_{1\le n\le e}\vartheta^n(\alpha_{0,n}+\alpha_{1,n}),
 \qquad \vartheta=\max\{L_0^2/L,1/L\}<1.                    \tag{4.85}
\]
For sufficiently weak coupling all these *entire* original depth-one readouts are on a single regular physical critical branch. In local old birth-unit charts their group curvature, holomorphic relative field, and the physical Wilson current of that same minimum are bounded by C_Ld₁, independent of the number of original layers, old carrier length and Λ geometry. The constants for a fixed local chart include its prescribed M₁,L. On the standard radii (2.28), d₁≤C g₁(1+log x₁)^q with q the larger actual exponent. The same statement with that envelope applies to the ordinary Q₂ return, including true readsets inside its rough core, on *every original simultaneous wall* for which the unmarked (1.65) holds. This establishes an old physical birth radius and local current bound. The strict saved radius criterion is C_Ld₁<ρ₁ with the indicated spare margin; a fixed small physical old chart satisfies it at weak coupling. A smaller shrinking old radius with the same leading log exponent is not automatically larger than this estimate.

**Proof.** Use the six separate rows on printed pp.190–191 of LFR I, rather than an entry-unit AE8 estimate. At a fine link the complex part is exp(iηA′) times the compact real comparator. In (1.64) its dimensionless perturbation is bounded by Cα₁,m L₀^{max(0,m−k₀)}L^{-m}. The same bound for the real fine plaquette carries L^{-2m} instead of L^{-m}, with α₀,m. In (1.66) the two factors become Cα₁,j L₀^{2(j−m)}L^{-j}, respectively Cα₀,j L₀^{2(j−m)}L^{-2j}. In (1.68) the factors are Cα₁,m L^{-m}, Cα₀,m L^{-2m}. All are bounded by the appropriate sup in (4.85), including the source constants c=1,3 on the last layer. The displayed convexity prefactors in the source are bounded by constants. This comparison uses the residual scale factor even where a true core fails the return-unit ceiling. The running bound (2.6)–(2.8), or the stopped inverse-coupling bound of I-0c, gives g_n/g₁≤C(1+n)^C, and log x_n≤C(1+log x₁) on the weak interval; the geometric θ^n pays this polynomial. In the Q₂ class the first step was homogeneous and there was no earlier bare failure. The first nonempty old large component occurred at step two, so the stored global Ω₁ covers the torus throughout its ordinary survival before this return. On a finer artificial boundary its depth-zero values that fall outside the ordinary global determining support are the prescribed outer fine values. Test those, and their old exterior stars, by the same original positive-layer member bounds for the given outer pair. A longer X exhausted by the physical localization boundary can be handled only when its actual exterior is supplied by those retained values. No bound is claimed for independent untested level-zero rough data.

Now compare M^•Γ on each true old raw bond to M^• of the *same* real comparator U. With only one averaging step, each such complete word and derivative has finitely many factors in two old endpoint blocks, including the original endpoint prefixes. In a local ordered tree the complex/real bond discrepancy is C_L d₁ by the finite raw-mean Lipschitz bound on its genuine branch. The compact local coarse plaquette (including a missing bond reconstructed from its finer fixed children) differs from identity by C_Ld₁: in an enlarged plaquette block its real fine field is in a tree gauge with all links O_L(d₁) by the finite fine strip filling. This verifies the native real test (7) and the complex relative test (172) on every old row, without one absolute gauge over a potentially winding large X. A prescribed bare exterior bond uses the original complex/real comparison directly; a prescribed exterior plaquette uses its complete incident stars. These are all the input tests for the original one-step orbit, including the corrected means at a boundary plaquette. The CMP102 real minimum for the comparator and Proposition9 on the complex relative tuple give the actual old critical orbit. At fixed old depth all native meshes are between 1/L and 1 in birth units. Its real curvature estimate and its local gauge estimate follow from Theorem1. Its complex relative angle and local first differences are bounded by C_Ld₁ by (19)–(21) and (190). For the old physical current one may simply bound the finitely many incident projected plaquettes, since its physical mesh factors are fixed in birth units. This includes prescribed boundary stars. A local compact tree on a fixed old cube gives the declared real chart; complex representative changes and boundary data are transported simultaneously. On overlapping real-centered charts uniqueness of the regular orbit and analytic continuation on a smaller common neighborhood identify the same physical scalar. This gives holomorphy in the actual prescribed outer parameters, even though a real comparator used for the bounds need not be chosen holomorphically.

The constants for uniform local norms are independent of |X|. A global unnormalized Sobolev norm on X must still include its volume, and a long word consumes its original path weight. For old charts with separate real and complex ceilings the bounds can be separated: the real minimum uses only the α₀ part of (4.85), and the analytic *relative* response only the α₁ part. The total physical current at the complex minimum can depend on both. These are local scale-one admission statements for the actual operator P_old, not a proof of its arbitrary-age stable kernel cancellations. ∎

**Consequence for the formed H on its actual first-step chart (P with explicit original margins).** In the Q₂ class take old first-step coefficients on the certified small physical chart of Lemma4.52 and the original unmarked source construction. For members whose source-free carrier constants and real/complex ceilings have spare room there, Proposition4.53 now supplies the actual physical readouts on the whole positive-layer support and not just on the boundary-wave curve. To bound their first two source jets it suffices to use the small source radius c g₀² in Lemma4.52: on that radius the physical H enters as a bounded local vertex on the unchanged contour, so fixed-order costs are at most Cg₀⁻² and Cg₀⁻⁴ times the original rooted carrier weights. This proves polynomial, volume-independent per-root suprema for the true integrated marks where those carrier margins hold. It is enough for (4.81); no weak-field expansion identifying the whole mark with its classical value is used for this upper bound.

The short-edge restriction can also be removed for the old-carrier localization cost at the *empty additional-wall domains* of this geometry. For an original connected depth-one carrier X touching X_* with n old native cubes, its minimal Y contains X_* and the current M cubes touched outside it by X. Trace a spanning walk of X in the original old cubes, length at most 2n original cube steps. Its image in entry units has length at most C r M n; cut it into segments of length M. Each lies in a fixed number of current M cubes. Connecting the cubes it actually touches in the same traced union makes a graph of length at most C M(1+rn), allowing a fixed number of corner adjustments inside the union. This bounds d_{k,Z}(Y) in (1.67) by C(1+rn); segments inside X_* are also traced rather than joined through points exterior to Y. A spare original carrier weight exp(−κ₁n) therefore pays the marked-edge replacement α⁻¹exp[(1+2β)κd_{k,Z}(Y)] from §4.39 at the actual weak r. It leaves a positive old summation rate. One can sum X and its two source anchors with their retained original weights before (4.81). ### 4.42 A long pre-entry age at the actual outer face

The native distance in (4.83) can be much larger than the physical separation. This matters when the old source was born long before the first large fluctuation. At the bth step the spike is identity on b-nodes but changes the inspected old **unaveraged, nonpivot** bond by more than 2δ_{b−1}, as in (3.3) and §4.19. Choose it in a single Q_b inspection cube. The subsequent identity coarse fields and zero new fluctuations again give the ordinary rectangular eight-/ten-layer geometry, with a fixed physical return shape and its annular cut. For these comparisons only the initial geometry is selected by this example; the estimates integrate all allowed ξ of that genuine class. An arbitrarily large early torus can accommodate the current test patch on the fixed reference torus by taking b sufficiently below the terminal step.

**Proposition 4.54 (P/L, the small-record fresh scalar through arbitrary prior clean ages).** Use the empty-wall Y=Z record and harmonic outer wave of §4.33 at this first Q_b return.  \left|\operatorname{Cov}_{T/T1}(F,V)\right|
 \le C A_1 g_1^{-m}(1+R_e)^C\tau_e\,r^{\gamma-5}
                 \exp[-cMR_e+C_L R_e],

\]
For a full current-volume root test in the boundary band one can harmlessly replace 5 by 6. Here γ is proportional to δ₀ times the *actual fixed native separation* divided by log L, with numerical fractions specified in the proof. In particular, if γ exceeds 5+s and the original cM dominates the fixed cost C_L, the small-record fresh quadratic scalar retains the desired age r^s, including the extra fixed coupling/grade factors by spending a further arbitrary fixed margin in γ on this long-gap class. Unlike (4.77), this argument can gain the arbitrarily long pre-entry age without charging it to a rare return. The strict physical readmission at these old boundary rows also holds there with reference term O(r²τ). The large record and the separately filed exterior completion require their own comparison.

**Proof.** Let y be a native site in an old support of n birth cubes rooted in this boundary band. Include the fixed exterior and any finite prefix buffer in n.  c MR_e L^{e-\ell},\qquad S_0(\ell-h(y)-2)_+ .              \tag{4.87}
\]
The first bound is physical distance divided by the largest mesh visited. The second follows by following the contour from the first h(y) layer to the first visit to ℓ, applying the interlayer separation in §3.9. This argument allows a shortest path to leave D through its much finer exterior. It does not assume ℓ=e. On the original geometry cMR_e≥2S₀ at weak coupling. Since L^d≥1+d, the first bound pays the missing S₀(e−ℓ) even on a contour avoiding the top middle layer. Splitting the two bounds into fixed shares gives

 d(y,S)\ge c' MR_e+c''S_0(e-h(y)-2)_+                       \tag{4.88}
\]
with absolute positive c',c''. For example after replacing c by its stated lower geometric constant, take c'≤c/4 and c''≤1/4 in the preceding split. This verifies the path cost inside the genuine two-collar determining hierarchy used for the analysis.

In (190) sum the σ_x⁻⁴ density with its *literal native input measure* σ_x⁴ over the inner cut targets. Reserve exp(−δ₀d/16) for the summability in (3.26). The other half of its rate δ₀/8 and (4.88) give

 C\tau_e \exp(-cMR_e)\,r^\gamma(1+n)^{C_1},

for the dimensionless fine link difference in the common Landau representatives. The output factor η/σ_y is bounded by one. The polynomial in n includes the fixed initial shifts and h(y) bound. No η⁻⁴ input count occurs in (4.89). The reference angles are O(r²τ) as in §4.40, independently of b. On a fixed old star the saved physical radius test is consequently C_X(r²τ+τ_e e^{-cMR_e}r^γ)<ρ₁. This is a genuine arbitrary-age original boundary readout. Restoring the old one-step field and its physical current uses the same native density in birth units and bounded L as in Proposition4.51. If a carrier reaches the inner half-annulus from the designated old root it has at least c MR_e/r old cubes. The number of roots in a fixed thickness old face band is at most C r⁻³ times a fixed number of layers. Include the r⁻² dimension-six normalization. A tagged mixture with a common geometry and source record inherits this proof; different reference means are still retained by (4.76).

For explicit bookkeeping, the inverse coupling costs are polynomial in x₁ and x_e and logarithms of these quantities. The first term is paid by any positive exponential spatial margin in R_e, and log(1+e) by an arbitrarily small positive part of the old r-power. A weaker real grade or a fixed finite product requires its corresponding numerical margin. Equations (4.86),(4.89) are valid without increasing the actual native spacing. To infer their simplified saved-age corollary one must check the displayed inequality for the originally selected spacing. There is a family of examples at every a with a genuine rough component meeting the readset. Thus the controlling native ranges on the old core have n≥a on every enlarged stencil, including the core's genuinely retained a-rows. **Proof of the scaled input.** In cases (1.64) and (1.68) the controlling n is m; for (1.66) it is the stage j with m≤j. At the remaining special j piece of (1.64) take n=j as specified by the source. All multipliers L₀ in these inequalities are at most L₀^{2N₀}. **Holomorphic chart and changed old minimum.** In the common tree on the regular enlargement all non-tree link logs are bounded by C L^{-a}u from a finite-physical-length strip of fine plaquettes, with uniformly bounded conjugations. The lattice Bianchi identity and the physical Wilson current give bounded curl and divergence of the projected curvature in this frame: reordering face products costs quadratic plaquette defects and each change of parallel transport costs a bounded old potential times curvature. Fine gauge variations remove the intermediate endpoint gradients in its pairing, by (4.32). The actual first physical curvature packet has exponential pointed path moments on its native source chart. For the actual analytic fourth-order remainder with its stored cap, put a short old carrier in its compact axial tree on the complete b-unit patch. The radial Schwarz estimate on the original spare chart after the covariant quadratic and cubic subtractions gives at most C A₁g₁⁻²α⁻²(1+n)^C t⁸u⁴ times the old rooted weight. On a fixed small physical first-source radius one can split at c/t, since n≤c/t implies Cnt²u≤Cctu there. An age-dependent old radius requires its own analogous Taylor-radius comparison. The current engineering normalization differs from each patch's t⁻² by s⁻². This proves (4.91).

 \sum_{Y\supsetneq X_*}|V(Y)|\le C\alpha_0 |\partial_M X_*|.

Thus its total positive weight divided by the complete real B is bounded by exp[−(1+δ)p₀(g_e)] for some fixed δ>0, from the genuine single (1.89) with β₀=1/7. In this physical recombination the sharp Y/Y⁺ mismatch disappears as an artificial boundary of the complete old source. ### 4.45 A genuine long continuously occupied history

**Proposition 4.57 (P/L, a delayed first physical return).** The real recombined first-birth result of Proposition4.56 also applies to a class where the elongated component survives an arbitrarily large number e−b of ordinary steps before its first eligible R. The old homogeneous prefix and the occupied interval can both be long. Here it is the actual extended set of Q_b failures and its native history weight, not one hypothetical failed site for each old source anchor, that pays the cost s⁻⁶ of (4.91).

**Construction and proof.** On a constant-R weak interval write R=R_b. In the b−1 lattice place pure compact nodal gauge spikes of §4.42 in a straight string of adjacent originally inspected Q_b cubes; one spike at a nonpivot site in a fixed interior portion of each of a positive fraction of these cubes suffices. The spikes can have disjoint supports, occur along one Cartan subgroup, and be identity on all b-nodes. Extend them block-constantly into every preceding fine lattice as in §4.42. All earlier physical plaquettes and fluctuations then have their flat clean values, while M V_{b−1}=I. The inspected bonds with angle >2δ_{b−1} are genuine Q_b failures. Neighboring ancestors whose inspection windows also see one of these spikes may enlarge the string by a fixed number of the defining cubes. The old b−1 background is a pure gauge and has zero Wilson plaquette action. The nonconstant node gauge does carry the actual soft axial weight of CMP119 (1.5)–(1.7), retained at the bth step; the gauge-orbit normalization is part of the native Q estimate. Q_b here is the actual fluctuation failure, not a claim of macroscopic plaquette energy. The neighboring layers and associated gauge restrictions are precisely those of the native Q construction. Choose the string long enough that its connected enlarged component has length D MR in the b-unit lattice, width at most CMR in the three transverse coordinates, and at least cD distinct charged initial cells. Take the current torus large enough for its final contracted localization cube with periodic separation. At each following ordinary step choose the new inspected data flat outside the inherited component and zero new fluctuations. There are no new failures. The region evolves by the original operation S, coarse cover plus ten layers. It stays within a rectangle with scaled length bounded above by C(1+D L^{-i})MR and bounded below by c D L^{-i}MR at step b+i, and width O(MR), by summing the geometric costs of rounding the covers. One can use all the consecutive defining cubes to make the initial thickened string rectangular; the native small-component rectangular completion when it first fits is part of the original construction. The first eligible e then satisfies e−b=log_L D+O(R+1) when log_L D is much greater than R: before fitting a 100MR box condition (i) fails, and after fitting an ordinary clean wait of at most R+O(1) gives (ii) with h=e−N. This proves an unbounded class. Constant R can hold on intervals containing a fixed positive fraction of the available weak steps by taking a subsequence away from its discrete thresholds; the running x values may vary by a bounded ratio there, so their logarithms differ by O(1). One may equivalently use a slightly shorter interval with both gap lengths tending to infinity. In particular D can be chosen large compared with the polynomial in R used below.

For this fixed discrete string geometry, sum the unmarked costs in (1.79) on its actual ordinary history rather than treating all R′ terms as statistically independent. ### 4.47 An eligible return that stays live, and exact physical conditional transport

**Proposition 4.59 (P/L, a nonempty still-live branch).** In the actual one-Q_b eligible geometry there are strict real coordinates on which the component takes the second branch χ_k^c(Y^-6) of LFR II (1.72). Its new interior has a nonzero compact plaquette, yet the next ordinary transformation need have no new failure outside the inherited body. There is also an explicit variant with a new, adjacent genuine Q at the next step, a merger, and a forced ordinary waiting interval before a second eligible R.

**Proof.** Let k=b+R_b in the one-rectangle weak example, denote its B″ inner integration domain by Λ, and keep the old Q_b history with all original k-level data flat outside Λ as above. The χ_{k,Λ} selector from LFR I (1.75) asks for an extension of these *outside* data; take the flat one. Then V_Λ=I in the indicated rep. LFR I pp.200–201, (1.99)–(1.101), inserts a **new** V′_k in Λ after integrating the old field there. It is constrained to the graph section δ_{G₀}, with log bound M₀ε_k. This is different from the old integrated B′ with its own χ′ in the last integral of (1.100). LFR II p.378 fixes M₀ of order B₅M⁶; choose the original parameters with M₀>3. The rectangular Λ meets the inner tested Y^-6 well away from its boundary, since the ordinary Λ has distance ≥2MR_k−dM from the true outer face and contains a full M cube. Choose one full aligned L-block at the new k-scale with its immediate plaquette stars inside this region.

There exist two free parallel internal bonds of that block with the same longitudinal index and adjacent transverse positions. Indeed, there are 4(L−1)L³ oriented internal bonds and the restriction of the axial tree to its L⁴ sites has at most L⁴−1 edges. For any of the 4(L−1) choices of direction and longitudinal index, the possible transverse positions form a three-dimensional L-grid. A set without adjacent positions has size at most ⌈L³/2⌉, by pairing consecutive vertices on a serpentine Hamiltonian path. For L≥3 the total number of free edges strictly exceeds 4(L−1)⌈L³/2⌉. This proves the claim without treating an eliminated tree bond as a degree of freedom.

On these two bonds put opposite angles ±2ε_k in one unit Cartan generator. Set all other new bonds to I. The axial graph delta is satisfied and both logs are strictly below M₀ε_k. The plaquette between the two bonds has angle ±4ε_k and hence norm distance greater than 2ε_k at weak coupling. On p.378 the implication from the first small χ_k to |∂V_k−1|<2ε_k on that component is explicit. Our tested plaquette thus forces the actual χ_k^c. All matrices are still in the native weak open set for the heat comparison. The old χ′ and old Q_b variables can retain their earlier strict values, since they are integrated in the OTHER factor of (1.100). Their physical gauge-fixed independent-coordinate neighborhood has positive finite-cutoff measure. The heat density in the new independent graph variables is strictly positive at this configuration. Thus this is a nonempty branch of the original law with an open set in its actual graph section.

The next straight Cartan mean vanishes since the two opposite parallel links occur at the same longitudinal height in one block. The actual raw mean adds its endpoint gradient, so M V_k is a compact pure gauge, supported in a bounded number of adjacent new blocks. All subsequent iterated means are still pure gauges by covariance. Their nonidentity support has bounded native width: after a coarse cover its diameter obeys w_{m+1}≤w_m/L+C_L because the complete raw row stays in its two endpoint blocks. These blocks are deep inside the inherited body; outside its successive covers the inspected fields and their local backgrounds are flat. The original next P and Q tests in CMP119 (3.2)–(3.5) are applied outside four and six padded layers of the old Z. The R test (3.16) compares to the true local minimum; (3.17)–(3.19) bound its remote correction by Cε exp(−R) before the fixed canonical rescaling. It is inside the original threshold on the weak interval. The k-th coarse plaquette just planted, though a genuine large-χ event, is not mistaken for a new outside failure in these tests. Thus one obtains the claimed ordinary no-new-outside-failure transformation after an eligible still-live step. Further ordinary steps with no new R apply whenever their eligibility conditions permit.

One can force a longer interval without a convention about whether the last χ_k^c alone counts as a fresh birth in condition (ii). Superpose a second pure nodal gauge spike in an originally inspected (k+1)-block *outside* the sixfold cover of this Y. Put its inspected little cube in the seventh or eighth MR_{k+1} layer from the coarse cover, away from transverse corners. The allowed original M≫LM₂ gives room for the complete (3.3) small cube there. Extend its node gauge block-constantly backward through the clean exterior up to k, as in §4.42. It is supported away from the first component, is identity at k+1 nodes, and changes no physical plaquette there; all earlier local tests are the flat ones by the simultaneous coarse/fine gauge covariance. At step k+1 its independent nonpivot bond violates (3.3) strictly while the other newly inspected regions stay flat. The Q′ two-layer enlargement in (3.5) now touches the ordinary eight-layer old enlargement; (3.20) puts both in the same connected new component. Its width is still below 100MR_{k+1} by the fixed geometry. Thereafter take the pure-gauge higher means and make no further new defects. The actual new Q prohibits R for the mandated preceding clean wait by LFR I condition (ii); (3.2)–(3.20) give ordinary survival until the next eligible interval. The first interior pair and this exterior nodal change need disjoint pads through their different earlier stages, available on a sufficiently large current lattice inside the fixed reference torus. This establishes an actual still-live, merging, subsequent-wait geometry without assigning a nonzero value to an unspecified generated boundary polymer. ∎

**Proposition 4.60 (P, physical source across the genuine R reintroduction).** Fix the complete retained *exterior* and the discrete local history/graph choice of an eligible component in LFR I (1.99)–(1.102). Before the later localization, let F be the complete spectator physical old H coefficient, including the physical lower and contact partners to the order under consideration. The same assertion holds for a finite joint old-source germ or the actual old compact observable integrated in that sector. Conditional on this exterior and history, its normalized law is independent of the newly inserted V′_k inside Λ. In particular its mixed connected response with a source depending only on that new interior and its subsequent source-free transitions is exactly zero. This includes restricting the new field to the still-live event of Proposition4.59. Exterior-dependent means and history-label variations need not cancel.

**Proof.** Denote all retained exterior parameters and the prescribed geometry/G₀ collectively by v, and the old integration variables in this component by ξ. Write m_v(dξ) for their undivided positive physical measure before introducing the new variables. The numerator/denominator on the first line of (1.100) is exactly a probability kernel p_v(dw) on independent graph coordinates of the new w=V′_k in Λ: it is the same χ(Λ) exp[−g_k⁻²A(ζ,U_{k,Z}(wV_Λ))] in both parts, with the same axial graph and Haar measure. The denominator is finite and strictly positive by the native construction. The other integral in (1.100) contains the old ξ and old χ′. The normalization equality (1.102) is after integration of the new field, so this proof starts from the actual factorization there, not from equality of two unintegrated densities before this replacement. For several components p_v is a product with all exterior data held fixed. Thus for old sources f_i(ξ,v), including formed conditional H derivatives, and a new label G,
\[
 \int m_v(d\xi)e^{\sum s_if_i}\int p_v(dw)P_v(w,dy)e^{tG(w,y,v)}
   =Z_{\rm old}(v,s) Z_{\rm new}(v,t).                         \tag{4.94}
\]
Here P_v is any subsequent genuine positive propagation which, conditional on v and the new fields, does not consult the eliminated old interior. Its mass may also include a positive future selector or integration; this changes only Z_new. Conditioning on χ_k^c(w) as in Proposition4.59 changes only that factor, whose mass is nonzero on the admitted example. Replacing the old exponential by its original joint sourced density preserves the product. Old formed source second rows and direct contact derivatives stay in their original Z_old. At finite cutoff differentiation of the log of (4.94) gives zero mixed old/new cumulants. No individual signed V(Y) or T′ logarithm is being equated to that old physical factor.

Equivalently the old normalized mean G_old(v)=m_v(F)/m_v(1) passes through this actual still-live reset as a function of v only. Positive kernels subsequently integrating v contract its supremum; they also include its nontrivial conditional variance by the ordinary total-covariance formula. For the homogeneous-prefix real sector of Proposition4.56 one has the physical preintegration supremum (4.91) on its stated old chart. That bound therefore carries to G_old through this still-live choice and to its future conditional means without a new inverse probability for the reset. It is not valid to repeat the factor exp(−p₀) every time one conditions on the same history. In the example with the genuine Q_{k+1} merger that later event has its own charge. A global normalized spatial variance estimate would additionally need the distribution and correlations of the exterior v and its possible history labels. This physical cancellation avoids the unresolved independent off-shell current derivatives; it does not prove them. ∎

On this graph use the *unlocalized endpoint* Γ(v,ξ) of (1.50). **Normalized analytic scalar.** For this fixed geometric sector let ν_{v,h} be its undivided (1.72) component integral after the physical exterior completion is factored as in §4.44. \[
 \left|\int F(\Gamma(v,\xi))\,d\nu_{v,h}\right|
 \le C\,\sup_{\xi}|F|\, e^{-(1+\delta')p_0(g_e)}
\]
as in §§4.38,4.44, for the fixed single shape and no other returned body in this sector. On the complete signed gas (1.98) rooted convergence gives |B(v)^{-1}|≤exp[C n_e⁴exp(−p₀(g_e))]. At the fixed terminal offset this is a uniform constant. One may first perform this argument with the physical old generating germ and differentiate its source once; no independently normalized rare denominators are introduced. Denote the resulting quotient by G_h(v). The same calculation holds for the marked product with the fixed internal vertex described in §4.44. Its small-sector probability is paid once. The old true age follows by the s,t conversion after (4.91).

For m exterior current-coordinate derivatives on a concentric ball of radius δ/2, Cauchy gives a factor at most C_mδ^{-m}. The sum over their current sites costs at most C_m n_e^{4m}, and even an exponential connecting-tree weight on these m fixed anchor sites costs at most exp(C_m κ n_e). These constants are bounded at the prescribed offset; the finite δ expense is also affordable from the native weak bank if its g_e dependence is retained. A physical current computed on the graph is included by differentiating the genuine composed scalar there. An independent off-graph current is not identified from these coordinate derivatives.

One may now define the source-only extension G_h(M^eU) for outer pairs whose actual raw current-scale means are in this coarse ball, keeping every endpoint gauge in those means. It has exactly the old physical source at the real graph, and is identically unchanged on the null-average family of §4.46. Its derivatives with respect to fine external coordinates still include the full derivative of M^eU; only the stated finite current-coordinate anchor norm has been proved here. In particular the estimate does not use the bound exp(C n_e⁴e^{-p₀}) on a growing current torus. Localization of this source-only representation with cutoff-uniform fine-anchor norms, and its propagation through unrestricted mixed boundaries, are further obligations for this alternative. ∎

### 4.49 The large-record same-mass mean at arbitrary prior age

There is one part of the sharp comparison that has no short-wait restriction. In the genuine Q_b family of §4.42 use the null-average wave of §4.46 for the fine-slot H and the indicated coarse projection. On the actual Y⁺ record the support of the wave is disjoint from every perturbed fine boundary slot. Every positive-level mean is unchanged. Therefore the old O_{+,X} has *exactly the same value* for U_ζ and U₀ at fixed ξ throughout that record, for every b≥2. This observation does not require a lower bound on the native distance from the core. For each true crossing term put
\[
 D^\Delta_{+,X}(\zeta,\xi)
   =D_{+,X}(U_\zeta,\xi)-D_{+,X}(U_0,\xi)
   =-[C_X(U_\zeta,0)-C_X(U_0,0)].                           \tag{4.95}
\]
The incidence and the residual fine quantum bound in Proposition4.58 for the right side also have no short-wait restriction. They are calculations on the explicit *outer* pair in C_X, not on an approximation of the inner minimum. Thus the sum over genuine crossing fine stars and their native quantum/subtracted coarse partners has a phase-averaged quadratic coefficient at most −c g₀⁻²τ²/r. This includes the case of fixed terminal offset and b−1→∞. A finite real old contact of bounded coefficient changes the o(g₀⁻²) part. As throughout §4.46, a further fine-slot β₀-order lower term would require its own symbol.

Fix the one-component record and the actual unexpanded (1.72) weight of that record at the same ζ, retaining its exp V(Y⁺), all other vertices and true T′ body. Normalize by its *own* nonzero mass. At real weak data positivity ensures this denominator is strictly positive. Inserting (4.95) leaves exactly its deterministic value after that normalized integration. Its connected product with every other variable in the same fixed body vanishes, by (4.75). For an exact logarithmic mark attached to this finite difference only on a designated real subfamily of weight fraction π, the first two label derivatives are mπ and m²π(1−π), as in T1's signed exclusion calculation, with m the value in (4.95). No independence of separate rare masses occurs in that statement.

The bracket of *all* histories and the change of the unmarked measure when comparing two exterior states are distinct. In particular,
\[
 E_{U_\zeta}D(U_\zeta)-E_{U_0}D(U_0)
   =E_{U_\zeta}[D(U_\zeta)-D(U_0)]
     +(E_{U_\zeta}-E_{U_0})D(U_0).                            \tag{4.96}
\]
The first term on the chosen member is (4.95); the second is an exterior score, and the two expectations in it really have different weights. This prevents interpreting the nonzero same-mass mean as a lower bound on the whole physical susceptibility. At the real coarse graph the physical exterior completion and the exact reset yield (4.91),(4.94). Conversely a proof of the original branchwise all-wall scalar norm cannot use vanishing of a connected covariance to discard the separate nonzero mean (4.95).

### 4.50 The physical source with arbitrarily many other returning bodies

**Proposition 4.62 (P/L, inclusive real normalization).** At a fixed real physical current graph in the no-still-live bracket, let X_* be a fixed eligible returning shape. Consider its contribution from actual homogeneous-prefix histories for which the original mixed background in the whole physical test has no determining row below d=b−1 (in particular, with the homogeneous clean prefix on the torus). Other compatible returning components can be present in arbitrary number. The absolute first logarithmic contribution of that sector's *complete physically projected first H* obeys (4.91) times C exp[−3p₀(g_e)/2]. This bound does not depend on the number of other components or on the volume of the current grid, for the same fixed-size test. For e=b+R_b the surplus pays the recent inverse s-powers, leaving the saved pre-entry age, the appropriate coupling/grade and a single exp(−p₀). The bound also holds for the internal fresh V(X_*⁻) tag with its actual determining-bond supremum as in (4.86). These are normalized real physical sector contributions, not conditional expectations divided by the rare sector's own mass.

**Proof.** On this physical class all global original determining rows factor through M^d. The proof of (4.91) uses local estimates on smooth d-unit patches, the common physical orbit and complete original source translations; none of them counted other components of the current Z. The higher old cell count in (1.69) at the specified component uses only the lower level index d. Thus its deterministic bound remains valid throughout that class, at arbitrary real integrated variables. If other histories have a finer genuine layer, do not use this uniform supremum there.

Introduce an independent affine label 1+t for the *presence* of the specified shape X_* inside its original T′ body, with all compatible other returns retained. At real v every original term of (1.72) before the Mayer expansion is positive, so its normalized first logarithmic derivative at zero is the actual conditional probability P_v(X_* present). The tag is bounded by 1, supported on that geometric body, and is identically absent on all other families. The same signed denominator (4.80)–(4.81) at K=1, now used for this literal occupancy marker, gives
\[
  P_v(X_*\ {\rm present})\le C e^{-3p_0(g_e)/2}.              \tag{4.97}
\]
This application does not require F itself to be a local Mayer variable. For an event E contained in that presence event, real positivity gives |E_v[1_E F]|≤sup_E|F| P_v(X_* present), which proves the assertion for the inclusive class. The same use of (4.80) does not estimate complex conditional expectations by these real probabilities.

There is a direct check of the incidence behind the probability estimate at fixed arbitrary other body variables. If the specified X_* is removed, terms with Y disjoint from it are unchanged by the record locality stated below (1.72). For a changed term with a proper domain Y containing X_* and another region, an outside M cube touching X_* roots the tree count in (1.67). All the outside cubes of Y lie on the tour of a shortest tree to within a fixed cube-step allowance. For a tour of n native M-length units there are at most C^n choices of the touched outside cubes and their masks: split the tour into pieces of length ≤M, each of which hits at most a fixed number of lattice cubes. Every fixed distinct component included in Y must be adjacent to such an outside cube, since different Z-components do not touch. Their choices therefore also cost at most C^n, regardless of how many remote bodies are present. For terms of the family without X_* which are altered because their domain intersects X_*, instead root at one of its previously ordinary M cubes. The source fixed counting slack pays these exponential counts with the native tree factor. The change in the real sum of proper potentials is bounded in magnitude by Cα|X_*|, not by the current volume. Source (1.95)–(1.98) implements precisely the convergent estimate even when it is preferable to avoid reasoning about deletion of histories. This check explains the lack of a volume denominator in (4.97); the proof above uses the exact signed excluded gas rather than positing an independent law for the other bodies.

For two fixed distinct compatible shapes X₁,X₂, the normalized mixed derivative of B itself with respect to two presence labels is their joint probability. In the one-polymer term of (1.91) both geometric bodies are now fixed, leaving q−2 shapes to sum in (1.96); their two exp(−3p₀/2) factors remain. The distinct-polymer term has the same two factors, each on its corresponding polymer. Bound the two exclusion ratios by (4.80) and its union version. This yields P_v(X₁ and X₂ present)≤C exp(−3p₀). For incompatible shapes the probability is zero, and a repeated label on the same shape uses (4.97) only once. These joint bounds give the corresponding estimates for products of bounded physical projected source rows or internal vertices on their actual positive events. They do not imply spatial decay of an arbitrary source covariance merely by multiplying the covariance of the two attendance indicators: the physical fields themselves can depend on other bodies. ∎

### 4.51 Real spatial covariance of the full H birth in a mixed returning family

We now condition on a homogeneous prefix and consider the entire remaining real return gas at fixed weak current v. This is a different conditioning from dividing by an individual rare component. All admissible internal states and their original unmarked weights remain in the calculation.

**Proposition 4.63 (P/L on the specified separated, homogeneous-prefix family).** Use the no-still-live factor of (1.72), at a real current v in a small physical neighborhood of identity. Hold the exterior term fixed and take no exterior ineligible component. Restrict each candidate returning body's histories to a homogeneous prefix up to d=b−1; all genuine rows have depth ≥d, and there was no earlier R of the deterministic H birth being followed. Use the ordinary aligned eligible rectangles of side ≤100 MR_e. Require a gap of at least four M cubes between distinct bodies so an auxiliary collar can fit outside either one in the true top region; if not already imposed by the native partition, condition on this separated event. The empty family is admissible. Normalize the resulting **actual positive restricted gas** by its own full partition. It can have arbitrarily many components. This statement does not transfer a conditional covariance through an unsummed distribution of such prefixes.

On the first-eligible waiting trajectory e−d≤R_e+C and the first-source chart of §4.44, let F_o be the full physically projected first H scalar on a smooth current-size test centered at the current M cube o, with its complete old root translations and pointing offsets. For two such unit tests choose an exponent 0<s₀<min(2v,d₃), allowing a strictly larger intermediate exponent in (4.91). For the fixed physical first-source caps and a small fixed rate c>0 the restricted law satisfies
\[
 |\operatorname{Cov}(F_o,F_{o'})|
 \le C A_1^2 (g_e/g_1)^4 r^{2s_0} e^{-p_0(g_e)}
             e^{-c\,\operatorname{dist}_M(o,o')}.             \tag{4.98}
\]
The constants are independent of the number of current sites/candidates and the earlier clean-prefix length. For distinct incoming cubic/analytic caps keep the corresponding products from (4.91); extra fixed bounded cap inverses at this true first physical source only affect C. The spatial metric in (4.98) counts current M cubes, not old fine sites. This controls a real connected returning contribution of this marked physical family. It is not the full unsourced Wilson susceptibility theorem.

**Changing one actual body.** Outside a changed rectangle Q all other choices of component variables are held fixed. Before localization the mixed (1.50) determining rows and their actual data there are identical: on Λᶜ the smooth V″/new target equality is explicit after (1.50); in every other integrated component its retained variables and local R reference U_{k,X}(V_Λ) are unchanged. These preparatory references and integrals factor in components in (1.100),(1.71). Replacing Q by its absence sets the rows in Q to the outside top hierarchy, and replacing its internal state changes rows only in that rectangle. This comparison is for the real unweakened physical source, with fixed genuine new v; local representations on independent off-shell old charts do not have this property.

Remove Q with one extra aligned M layer and use the complement D on the torus as the CMP119 localization domain. Its new boundary is entirely in the top region, with the original 2M₁ clearance and all original complete stencils, by the stated separation (enlarge the fixed gap once if the original M/M₁ ratio requires it). On D form the one true (2.14) splice with the common determining set; use the actual raw data at the new inner boundary and its prescribed exterior separately for the two states. As in Lemma4.50, any admissible replacement on this new fiber preserves the old rows and external stars. Both restrictions are the regular minima of their more constrained problems. Only the new inner boundary data can differ. In the complete top blocks meeting this cut both states obey the e-unit (4.38) estimate. Use the separate k-node rooted block trees and the two-block comparison from Lemma4.50, with the common k-row v now small in a fixed physical upper gauge. In the two-block tree the comparison of the two node frames acquires the extra actual angle |log v|. All cut tuples are therefore small on the same local log chart and differ by at most C(τ_e+||log v||). Gauge changes in this operation fix the middle k-nodes and do not rotate the data of another region. The gauge and native spacing tests at this cut are independent of the number of remote rough components.

Use the real regular minimum of the unchanged state as the CMP102 reference; it need not be globally flat. Its genuine old constraints and endpoints on the other components have their native weak regularity by the same LFR member tests used in (4.90). Within the added cut collar the top physical bound implies the smaller native tests directly. The relative perturbation on the differing cut rows is small for (172), with no perturbation elsewhere. Formula (190) in this mixed hierarchy bounds the actual difference of the fields in relative Landau representatives. In a complete old star a distance D_o from the cut this gives, for the dimensionless fine link, at most C(τ_e+||log v||)exp(−cD_o); its native output η/σ_y is ≤1. The input measure is the original one and (3.26) sums it after spending part of the decay. The native path length is at least the physical separation because the mesh never exceeds one. This comparison also applies to two internal states of the same rectangle. One may join their cut-target logs inside the CMP102 small chart without interpolating the discrete histories. For an old stencil outside the analysis collar all true layers are ≥d: if needed for native chart comparison the smaller factor η/σ_y≤L^{-d} is available. The physical gauge-invariant first-source scalar is independent of any simultaneous representative transformation.

The first old readout has fixed depth one, a finite raw stencil per link and finite local-current words. Its pointwise rooted first-source derivative on the stated fixed spare physical chart has cap C A₁g₁⁻² with its exponential old support weight. Compare its arguments on a short original carrier by the preceding estimate, including its native upper mean; compare longer carriers by that spare weight as in §4.40. More explicitly if the extent of a carrier reached half the separation its old size would be ≥cD_o/r, so the corresponding tail already has the same current distance exponential. One may equivalently compare the two old fields in a local real tree and use the mean-value estimate on the fixed small chart there. Long contractible paths pay any polynomial tree/slot costs with their original spare weight. The native full-carrier suprema on long/winding carriers are part of the stated first physical chart, not a comparison to a global flat chart in a topologically nontrivial sector. Counting the r⁻⁴ old roots and r⁻² normalization gives the deliberately rough oscillation C A₁g₁⁻² r⁻⁶ exp[−c dist_M(o,Q)+C R_e]. Here dist to the home cube of Q and to its true boundary differ by at most C R_e. For an overlapping body simply use (4.91).

For all compatible configurations we also have |F_o| bounded by (4.91), since all true determining rows factor through M^d. Write s=L^{d−e}, t=r/s as before. Up to its fixed first-source cap and polylogarithmic factor, that supremum is C A₁g₁⁻² s⁻⁶u²t^{s₁}, with s₁>s₀ chosen below min(2v,d₃); u is small so the higher rows have the same upper estimate on fixed physical caps. Taking the minimum of the two estimates, and interpolating with a fixed small weight λ on the rough one, gives for changing the state at Q
\[
 \operatorname{osc}_Q F_o\le C A_1(g_e/g_1)^2 r^{s_0}
                      e^{C R_e}e^{-c_1\operatorname{dist}_M(o,Q)}.    \tag{4.99}
\]
Indeed choose s₁(1−λ)−6λ>s₀, and use r=ts. The cost s to any fixed inverse power has logarithm O(R_e), as do (1+log x_e)^C and the finite inverse g_e power from interpolation of u². All nearby/overlap cases fit the same bound. This argument retains positive age by spending some of the stronger regularity in the physical sum, rather than multiplying the covariance of an indicator by a presumed local value of F.

**The positive Gibbs comparison.** Represent each possible current rectangle Q as a coordinate whose safe state is absence and whose present states comprise its actual discrete histories and real T′ integration variables ξ_Q. There are at most C(1+R_e)^{C} candidate rectangles per fixed home cube: a rectangle of bounded side is determined by its finitely many grid endpoints. Its actual positive one-body base measure has total mass at most exp[−2p₀/(1+β₀)] by (1.89); restricting its internal histories only lowers this bound. The remaining log weight Φ is the sum of (1.68) terms over proper connecting domains Y. It depends on a body's state only when that body's support meets Y, as stated below (1.72). Incompatible rectangles and the stated separation are hard exclusions. The conditional odds to insert a compatible Q are consequently its base measure times exp(Δ_QΦ). The tour-count check of §4.50 gives |Δ_QΦ|≤C α |Q|, with |Q|≤C R_e⁴. The small native α and the p.389 inequality therefore bound the conditional probability of *any* present state by p_*≤exp[−(1+δ₁)p₀] for a fixed δ₁>0. Nothing here divides a signed polymer activity by its mass.

Let c_{QJ} be the supremum of total-variation change in the one-coordinate conditional at Q on changing the state at J only. At near or incompatible J the common absent atom gives c_{QJ}≤2p_*. At distance greater than C R_e the hard exclusion does not change. Only potential terms whose supporting domain intersects both bodies can contribute to Δ_JΔ_QΦ, including terms removed from the former domain list when a rectangle is inserted. In any of the four original domain lists there is an outside M cube within a bounded M-distance of each body, or a cube inside the absent body itself counted as outside. Hence the tree in (1.67) for such a Y has length at least their separation minus a fixed boundary allowance. Spend part of its decay for that distance and count the rest by the same rooted tour estimate; this yields
\[
 |\Delta_J\Delta_Q\Phi|\le C\alpha(1+R_e)^C
     e^{-c\,\operatorname{dist}_M(Q,J)+C R_e}.
\]
At separations above a sufficiently large multiple of R_e this bound is small. Comparing the two positive conditional odds and their normalizers gives c_{QJ}≤Cp_* times that bound there; this follows also from |e^x−1|≤e^{|x|}|x|. Choose a small c₂>0. Both row and column sums of the matrix c with weights exp(c₂ dist_M(Q,J)) are at most C p_*e^{CR_e}(1+R_e)^C<1/2. Distances here may use home cubes; the adjustment from a rectangle costs at most C R_e. These estimates are uniform in continuous internal-variable dimensions. The same conditional kernels may be defined for off-support exterior lists by ignoring exclusions not involving the update site; the safe absent state and the same bounds suffice for the comparison on admissible lists.

For clarity the covariance estimate follows directly from heat-bath updates on this finite set of candidate shapes. Let K_Q integrate the one-coordinate conditional and let L=Σ_Q(K_Q−I). Each K_Q is the positive conditional projection for the true restricted measure. A maximal conditional coupling or the ordinary total-variation bound gives
\[
 \operatorname{osc}_J K_Q f\le\operatorname{osc}_J f+
                 c_{QJ}\operatorname{osc}_Q f\quad(J\ne Q),
 \qquad \operatorname{osc}_Q K_Q f=0 .
\]
Thus for P_t=exp(tL) its oscillation column is bounded by exp[t(c^t−I)] acting on that of f; this follows first for one small random-site Euler step and then by taking its exponential. On admissible configurations one can compare compatible lists through the all-absent list. Equivalently extend oscillation comparisons by coupling the conditional updates, deleting incompatible neighboring sites to the safe state when necessary. For a pair differing at J, the identical update at Q couples except with probability c_{QJ}; when both different states at J,Q are present and conflict across the two lists, deleting Q in both lists permits the path comparison with cost at most 2 osc_Q f instead of osc_Q f. Thus the displayed estimate with 2c in place of c is always sufficient on the hard-constrained state space, and the weak bound above may be made <1/4. The weighted row/column contraction implies convergence of P_t f to its mean for finite volume and integrability of the oscillation bound. Finally
\[
 \operatorname{Cov}(f,g)=\int_0^\infty\sum_Q
          E[\operatorname{Cov}_Q(P_t f,g)]\,dt .
\]
This is obtained by differentiating E[(P_t f)g] and using the conditional projections. At Q the absent atom has probability ≥1−p_*. Subtract each function's value at that atom to bound the absolute conditional covariance by 2p_* osc_Q(P_t f) osc_Q g. The inverse (I−2c^t)⁻¹ has bounded exponential row/column sums by its Neumann series. Inserting (4.99) for f=F_o,g=F_{o'} and summing their two exponentially decaying profiles gives a factor exp[−c dist_M(o,o')] at a smaller positive rate; the polynomial number of rectangles per home costs (1+R_e)^C. All factors e^{CR_e}(1+R_e)^C fit in the surplus δ₁p₀ on the actual weak interval. This proves (4.98). ∎

Equation (4.98) controls correlations *inside* this genuine positive family at fixed current v. Unconditioning the exterior and the earlier scale histories introduces the covariance of their conditional means. Histories with finer retained layers than d, intertwined previous R operations, and the independent fine-slot complex source remain outside this result.

### 4.52 Different genuine first-entry scales in the same positive gas

**Proposition 4.64 (P/L on the explicitly strengthened native reserve margin).** In the real no-still-live gas of §4.51, drop the requirement of a common d=b−1 and the short residence bound e−d≤R_e+C. Keep the same physical first H birth, originally made in a homogeneous clean first step, its certified fine/carrier chart, the separation for the comparison cuts, fixed weak v and the actual restriction to histories of this deterministic contribution. In particular one may take all eligible histories with the initial homogeneous first step and no earlier R of this source birth, allowing arbitrarily long waits and mergers at different scales. Only the present deterministic first-H term is estimated; additional marked coefficients emitted at earlier integrations are not asserted to have this form. Suppose the original fixed birth/renewal/merger inequalities (1.81)–(1.88) have their native strict margin after the fixed increase in occupied-volume cost in the proof below. Then (4.98) holds uniformly for this positive family, independently of the number, earlier ages and positions of the other returns. No spatially distant old row is charged to the root merely because it is the oldest row in the whole history.

**True occupied-age tail.** For a present body Q let d_Q be the smallest genuine pre-R determining depth inside Q, omitting the artificial collars used to estimate a comparison. Put n_Q=e−d_Q. The genuine first clean step gives d_Q≥1. To see this from the original rules, later ordinary steps add a coarser domain, and (1.33) at an R replaces all lower rows within the healed/reintroduced set by its current top rows. LFR I (1.10)–(1.11) leaves the domains below the first participating depth untouched. A true level-d_Q row has an old site in Ω_{d_Q} outside Ω_{d_Q+1}; the subsequent determining domains are nested and the small integration domains Λ_n lie in Ω_n. Thus this site, with the original covering-cell convention, is retained in an occupied ancestor at each scale d_Q+1,...,e. If a previous R had overwritten this site, (1.33) would instead give it that later or a coarser determining depth. This counts one genuine occupied ancestor per scale of that row, also through a merger; it does not count artificial fine boundary rows as past failures.

For each fixed A>0 needed below, the real one-body T′ measure therefore satisfies, on the specified margin,
\[
 \int L^{A n_Q}\,d\zeta_Q\le C\exp[-2(1+\beta_0)^{-1}p_0(g_e)].
                                                               \tag{4.100}
\]
Here is the precise use of the unmarked bank, rather than a uniform bound asserted on each chronology. Assign the log cost A log L once on those occupied steps; it is bounded by A log L times the sum of the actual occupied native site counts in this body. Printed p.380 charges the larger type of cost C log(g_j⁻²)|Z_j|_j; the positive expense in (1.79) is then C₀Σ M⁴R_j⁵d′_j(Z_j). Increase C₀ to C₁=C₀+C_geom A log L. The future sum from (1.81) is at most C C₁M⁴L⁵R_j⁶(d′_j+1), and the merger expense in (1.87)–(1.88) is at most C C₁(100M)^4R_{j+1}⁶. Require the original birth credit and overlap surplus to dominate these respective costs with their stated fixed slack. Equation (1.83) then pays a retained wait from that one existing bank; (1.85)–(1.88) combine the two child banks at a merger and pay the same enlarged future expense. At exit K=0 in (1.80), giving (1.89) with the displayed multiplier inside its history sum. This is the elementary fixed-cost argument of T1 Proposition7.38 on its actual native hypotheses, not the use of an all-history marked transport theorem. The weak threshold can be chosen initially for this finite A where the original exponent choices permit these strict inequalities. If they have not been checked for an earlier fixed parameter set, (4.100) is an identified condition for that set.

In the positive conditional odds of §4.51 the proper outside potential costs exp(Cα|Q|). Consequently for some a₀>1 at the stated weak margins
\[
 P(n_Q\ge n,\ Q\text{ present}\mid\sigma_{\ne Q})
        \le C e^{-a_0p_0(g_e)} L^{-An}.                      \tag{4.101}
\]
This is valid under the actual restrictions of the positive family, since restricting a body's base measure decreases its numerator and absence remains admissible. It uses the true conditional gas, not the ratio of an isolated rare numerator to another physical sector.

**Local scales.** Enlarge the support of the current unit test at o by a fixed number of current M cubes. Include all neighborhoods of the original raw row support needed for the face trial and Hodge cutoff; a full iterated row's descendant stencils lie in its two endpoint blocks, of physical diameter at most a fixed constant in entry units. Call this fixed region N_o. Only actual bodies meeting N_o can create non-top genuine rows relevant to these tests. Set n_o=max n_Q over such present bodies, with value zero when none contributes an older row, and d_o=e−n_o. For each positive n choose the first maximizing body in a fixed ordering and write E_{o,Q,n} for this event. Use also E_{o,0}. They partition the actual configurations and their indicators involve only candidate rectangles meeting N_o.

The entire old endpoint is a genuine first homogeneous minimum on its first coarse data because all actual rows have depth at least one. The stronger physical Hölder/current argument on this test, however, requires depth d_o only *locally*. On its complete enlarged patch every genuine determining row whose descendants meet a compactly supported variation has depth ≥d_o. A zero-M^{d_o} variation on those complete intermediate stencils thus preserves every global row it can change. This is exactly the local test in (4.53)–(4.54); no comparison with unrelated finer targets outside N_o is made. The source ceilings (4.90) at indices ≥d_o, the common local gauge, and the physical Hodge/current proof of §4.44 apply on each d_o-unit patch. A long original first carrier leaving the fixed enlarged region has size ≥c/r and is bounded by its genuine rooted first-chart tail as before. This proves the same bound (4.91) with d=d_o on E_{o,Q,n}. In particular if s₁ is strictly between the desired saved exponent s₀ and min(2v,d₃), then
\[
 |F_o|\,1_{E_{o,Q,n}}\le
 C A_1 (g_e/g_1)^2 e^{C R_e} r^{s_1} L^{B n}1_{E_{o,Q,n}}   \tag{4.102}
\]
for a fixed B. Take B>6+s₁ with slack. Indeed s=L^{-n}, t=rL^n; the source u_{d,e} is bounded by C g_e(1+log x_e)^C uniformly. For example the function g(1+log g⁻²)^q is increasing for sufficiently small g, and the actual running bound at the two endpoints allows this comparison up to uniform fixed constants. The larger native L₀ factors are included in (4.90). The higher first-source caps are fixed physical constants here. The same estimate with n=0 holds on E_{o,0}.

**Summing the real covariance.** For the truncated variable f=1_{E_{o,Q,n}}F_o, changing a remote rectangle outside N_o does not change the event. When it is present in both configurations, the mixed minimum comparison on the one legal top cut in §4.51 still gives the rough bound C A₁g₁⁻²r⁻⁶ exp[−c dist_M(o,J)+CR_e] for a change at J. The other body's additional older rows are retained on the common mixed hierarchy; this proof uses (190) on the genuine compact real weak reference, not flatness of all intermediate regions. Source (1.64)–(1.69) there has native regularity since the worst L₀ factor is only a fixed logarithmic power of the weak terminal coupling. At the artificial cut the original top bound and common small coarse v give the same relative chart as before. Where J meets N_o, use (4.102) for the nonzero value(s) even when the event itself changes. Interpolation with a fixed small λ as in (4.99) gives
\[
 \operatorname{osc}_J f\le C A_1(g_e/g_1)^2 e^{C R_e}
              r^{s_0} L^{Bn}e^{-c\operatorname{dist}_M(o,J)}.  \tag{4.103}
\]
For E_{o,0} this holds with n=0. The heat-bath proof of (4.98) therefore gives for two pieces of ages n,m a covariance upper bound with factors
\[
 e^{-(1+\delta_1)p_0+CR_e}\, L^{B(n+m)}e^{-c\operatorname{dist}_M(o,o')},
\]
in addition to C A₁²(g_e/g₁)^4r^{2s₀}. There is a different bound for the same two pieces: use |Cov(f,g)|≤sup_E|f|sup_{E'}|g|[P(E∩E′)+P(E)P(E′)]. By (4.101), P(E∩E′)≤min(P(E),P(E′))≤C e^{-a₀p₀}L^{-A(n+m)/2} when both labels are nonempty. When just one is nonempty its one probability supplies at least the same decay with the other age set to zero. Thus, apart from the same physical factors and exp(CR_e), the absolute bound in these cases is C e^{-a₀p₀}L^{(B-A/2)(n+m)}. For the two empty labels use the spatial estimate directly. Taking the geometric mean of the two estimates retains exp[−c dist_M(o,o′)/2] and L^{(B-A/4)(n+m)} for the remaining cases. Pick A>4B+4. Both age sums converge. There are at most C(1+R_e)^C candidate rectangles meeting each N_o, and their polynomial count and exp(CR_e) fit in the remaining positive rare surplus. This proves (4.98) for different, unbounded local residence times. ∎

The new input here is an assignment from a smaller *genuine row* to its own occupied history. A fine collar inserted solely for a localized comparison carries no such assignment. Restoring arbitrary exterior histories, a possible originally rough first-step source, earlier generated R source activities, and independent fine-coordinate analytic derivatives remains a different problem.

### 4.53 The normalized exterior derivative on the physical coarse graph

**Proposition 4.65 (P for the normalized derivative identity and value bound on §4.52; C for the spatial physical derivative under the raw-frame response specified below).** In the setup and explicit native margins of Proposition4.64, let v be a real current field in a sufficiently small coarse link ball about identity, with the stated first-source margins. Put
\[
 G_o(v)=E_v F_o(v,\sigma)-F_o(v,\varnothing).
\]
The second term is the physical no-return value on the same current graph. For any fixed unit tangent direction at the current link j, the normalized mean and its first true exterior derivative have the bounds
\[
 |G_o(v)|\le C A_1(g_e/g_1)^2 r^{s_0} e^{-p_0},\qquad
 |D_jG_o(v)|\le C A_1(g_e/g_1)^2 r^{s_0}e^{-p_0}
                       e^{-c\operatorname{dist}_M(o,j)}.      \tag{4.104}
\]
Take s₀ strictly inside the available first-source exponent interval so that the finitely many interpolations below can spend a fixed power. All constants are uniform in current volume and the earlier cutoff. Powers of the inverse current physical radius and fixed original cap constants are paid from the declared rare surplus; if these are to be displayed separately retain them in the inequalities before that payment. The current-coordinate absolute anchor sum with any fixed polynomial weight follows by summing (4.104) over j. These are first derivatives of a **positive physical sector** with its exact changing normalization. Independent fine (U,J) derivatives on arbitrary simultaneous walls require a different argument.

**Normalization and domains.** Write w_v(σ) for the unexpanded density over the compatible rectangles, including their original independent real integration variables. At a fixed real interior v choose its source-free reference measures on these same coordinates. LFR II (1.71)–(1.74) separates the v-dependent exponential from these measures; their old selectors and coordinate graphs are fixed for this analytic coefficient. External characteristic functions are held outside it. Work on a current link disc of radius δ=c g_e² or smaller fixed multiple for which the strict physical graph test of §4.48 holds. In particular every native selected weight depends analytically on the physical coarse coordinate here. The derivative of the ratio is the exact formula
\[
 D_jG_o=E_v[D_j(F_o-F_o(\varnothing))]
                 +\operatorname{Cov}_v(F_o,S_j),\qquad
 S_j=D_j\log w_v.                                            \tag{4.105}
\]
The derivative of log B is E_v S_j. Thus a coarse-field scalar common to all records disappears from the score covariance, not from each raw term separately.

We detail a deliberately crude finite mesh estimate used only to establish *locality*. The homogeneous terminal orbit at a coarse link perturbation obeys (190). Near a physical output cube y the fine link and its first difference in a suitable relative representative change by at most a fixed mesh power times exp[−c dist_M(j,y)] per unit j perturbation. The physical current can here be bounded by differentiating its finite Wilson plaquette stars, losing at worst a further fixed η⁻³, without a false uniform Laplacian estimate. Simultaneous pure gauge changes of all the native slots have zero effect on each gauge-invariant scalar; for gauge-fixed component coordinates use simultaneous compact covariance or keep its native physical representative as explained below. There is a coarse Cauchy bound independent of location as an alternative to this rough estimate.

For clarity about that mesh power, on a bounded piece of a **homogeneous top** physical chart an independent perturbation of each fine link/current slot of size δ η^P lies inside the native member chart for some fixed P. Start with ||v−I||≤cδ so the unperturbed graph has strict room in the real/complex α ceilings. Each of the e raw averaging steps has uniformly bounded one-step first derivatives on its fixed L-block small relative chart. A constant to the eth power is a fixed inverse power of η. Apply the bound to the actual real reference in its transported frames; reduce the perturbed radius by that inverse power first so all intermediate group logs stay on their required branch. The same physical upper data are used for the prescribed exterior. On the auxiliary p-records (190) and the original density give a native bounded linear response, or a cruder fixed inverse mesh power by summing all levels. The old p-unit current can be bounded by at most the fixed power η⁻³ of the fine link error and an incident-star count. The strictest original top p-current and plaquette ceiling here loses at most two further powers of η. The physical perturbation and its first difference lose at most η⁻². Choosing P larger than this sum ensures the claimed local radius; on a larger carrier any remaining fixed polynomial in its size is retained and paid by the native connected exponential. This argument is a conservative sufficient chart estimate for the *new top physical output* where (1.65) is tested, not an unrestricted spare-radius assertion for the old mixed readouts.

One subtlety is a raw axial datum outside a source region. It can be avoided in the rough estimate for scalar scores as follows. For a physical coarse perturbation at j, represent the two homogeneous coarse configurations in the relative Landau representatives about the common real orbit using (190), rotating every coarse/fine datum in the region together. If the R integrand contains gauge-fixed coordinates, also rotate those integration variables by the compact coarse site frames for the real perturbation, preserving Haar measure and the real indicators under the allowed simultaneous covariance; alternatively perform this comparison after their integral. To get a *pointwise* score for the spatial heat-bath estimate it is enough to fix a common frame on all terminal blocks and use the derivative of the raw frame there. At the homogeneous current hierarchy each block frame is local on its own block of physical width one, with identity value at zero relative potential (LFR II p.373 after (1.61)). A crude polynomial bound on this derivative suffices. The location of this remaining frame assumption is explicit below; where a fixed native raw frame lacks that verification the pointwise-score version of (4.104) is conditional on it.

On this physical source chart write S_j as the sum of its one-body scores and the derivatives of the proper potentials V(Y), leaving out any common constant. For a one-body density (1.73) its v-dependent log variation has a bounded analytic difference on the actual member tube about a real comparator. The W-dependent quadratic and V(X⁻) are exactly the two terms identified below (1.72), including the changed quadratic coefficient and its integrated B factors. The fixed one-link physical Cauchy disc gives a bound Cδ⁻¹ uniformly in the canonical integration variables. Estimating instead the tiny change of all target slots in its actual domain by the crude local chart radius above gives Cδ^{-C}η^{-P'}exp[−c dist_M(j,X)] times a fixed polynomial in R_e. Equivalently use Cauchy along that small induced tangent vector in the full local member chart, amplifying it only to its verified δ η^P radius. For V(Y) the same two bounds multiply its actual Cα exp[−(1+2β)κd_{e,Z}(Y)] upper majorant (and a fixed size polynomial if needed); do not divide by a possibly zero V. Take the minimum of the uniform and localized bounds and interpolate with a sufficiently small weight on the latter. As j=1 is the fixed birth depth, η=r/L, and for each prescribed ε>0 this gives a bound with an r^{-ε} loss, some positive spatial rate c_ε and a fixed inverse g_e power. Summing the proper supports which change at a site Q gives
\[
 |S_j|\le C g_e^{-C}r^{-\epsilon}e^{CR_e},\qquad
 \operatorname{osc}_Q S_j\le Cg_e^{-C}r^{-\epsilon}e^{CR_e}
                  e^{-c_\epsilon\operatorname{dist}_M(j,Q)}. \tag{4.106}
\]
Here the absolute bound is for the nonconstant score. To check the sum, each altered proper domain touches Q. Its outside cubes lie along the (1.67) tree; every included rectangle is within its own O(R_e) diameter of an outside cube. Consequently diam_M Y≤C(1+R_e+d_{e,Z}(Y)) for these proper domains. A small part of the tree rate converts the factor for dist(j,Y) to the displayed one for dist(j,Q). The remaining rooted count is that of §4.50, and summation over candidate homes gives the absolute bound. This uses the full exponent of the actual positive gas, not a separate model for the exterior score.

**Explicit derivative of the physical insertion.** For one real j perturbation in its fixed disc, the old global pre-R target data can differ only in that coarse neighborhood and in a component whose new local reference uses it. At most one separated rectangle meets this neighborhood. If necessary excise it and a fixed top guard, or just a top cube about j when there is no such component. Every other mixed target agrees, using (1.50) and the componentwise boundary reference in (1.100); the source Φ and the fixed discrete geometry do not impose new old rows there. The proof of §4.51 on the common exterior problem gives the same crude exp[−c dist_M(j,o)+CR_e] old-source comparison, with fixed η powers and the true native cutoff, for this real perturbation. At every fixed such configuration F_o extends holomorphically in the one coarse physical parameter on a smaller uniform disc and is bounded there by (4.91)/(4.102) at the actual local age. These pointwise bounds use the unweakened physical graph only; no complex total-variation estimate on a gas of growing volume is required.

Here is the real-to-complex step used *only* for that derivative.
