If a holomorphic h on |z|≤δ is bounded by M there and by m≤M on the real diameter, its value on a concentric sufficiently small disc is at most M^{1−θ}m^θ for a fixed θ>0. Apply the subharmonic maximum principle to log|h| on the upper and lower half-discs, using the positive lower bound on the harmonic measure of the diameter in the smaller discs. Cauchy on the smaller disc gives |h′(0)|≤Cδ⁻¹M^{1−θ}m^θ. For h=F_o(v_z,σ)−F_o(v_z,σ^{−Q}) the good real bound from the comparison at Q supplies decay in dist(o,Q); the complex bound has the same local age and may drop only this distance decay. For h=F_o(v_z,σ)−F_o(v_0,σ) the real comparison at j instead gives decay in dist(o,j). Take that latter estimate for both configurations. Combining the two derivative bounds by a geometric mean gives decay at positive rate in the *sum* of the two distances. In these comparisons first interpolate the crude mesh bound with the stronger deterministic coefficient at an exponent s₁>s₀, as in (4.99); choose the weights so a strictly intermediate exponent still remains after the r^{-ε} cost in (4.106). The resulting quantitative estimate for a deletion, with n the largest local genuine age in either configuration, is
\[
 |D_j\Delta_Q F_o|\le C A_1(g_e/g_1)^2 e^{CR_e} r^{s_0}
         L^{Bn} e^{-c[\operatorname{dist}_M(o,j)+\operatorname{dist}_M(o,Q)]}.
                                                               \tag{4.107}
\]
When j or Q meets the enlarged test, use the same Cauchy bound without the corresponding distance factor. Constants include the finite inverse g_e cost of δ. Deleting a body cannot increase the local oldest age, so along a telescoping deletion to the empty state all n are bounded by the original local maximum. The very same unconjugated real/complex source formula is used for both marks before this comparison.

Take expectations of the telescoped (4.107). On the event of a positive local oldest age n, (4.101) bounds its probability by C e^{-a₀p₀}L^{-An} times the polynomial count of possible maximizing bodies. The sum in Q of the distance weight in (4.107) is bounded by a polynomial in R_e, even if Q-presence is then ignored. On the age-zero event keep the indicator of the individual deleted body Q and use its own probability ≤Ce^{-a₀p₀}. All terms thus retain one actual rare surplus. This proves the first term in (4.105); the same deletion without differentiation proves (4.104)'s value estimate. No global no-return probability was divided out.

For the score term apply the heat-bath comparison of §4.51 to F_o truncated by the events E_{o,Q,n} and to S_j. Equations (4.103),(4.106) give a spatially decaying estimate for each pair with factors r^{s'}r^{-ε}L^{Bn}p_* for an intermediate s'>s₀. The probability bound for the same covariance uses the absolute nonconstant score in (4.106), (4.102), and P(E_{o,Q,n}); it retains L^{-An}. The interpolation and sum of §4.52 thus apply also to this one-sided truncation. The age-zero case uses the spatial heat-bath bound directly. All exp(CR_e) and fixed current inverse-radius expenses are absorbed in the true surplus above p₀. This proves the second term in (4.105), with the same spatial rate after reductions. ∎

### 4.54 An explicit imaginary wall and the required old directional radius

**Proposition 4.66 (P/L on the fine-slot native H chart and fixed geometry of §4.46).** Take the Cartan first-average-null wall U_ζ of Proposition4.58 with the same smooth supported test and original Q₂ record. There exists a fixed a>0, independent of the cutoff, such that the actual nonreal wall ζ=i a has
\[
 |F_-(i a)-F_-(0)|\ge c_a g_1^{-2}\tau^2/r,\qquad
 |D_+^\Delta(i a)|\ge c'_a g_1^{-2}\tau^2/r.                \tag{4.108}
\]
Here F_- is the same small-Y, properly first-coarse-projected deterministic old H sum **before** the exterior subtractand, and D_+^Δ is the same crossing proper mean difference (4.95). The prepared coarse graph sees M¹U_ζ=I for the entire curve; the fine-slot terms explicitly tested here do not. This is a complex point of the prescribed simultaneous domain with the assigned localization walls zero. It is not obtained by enlarging that domain.

**Proof of the uniform Taylor control.** On the genuine small Y outer annulus, compare Γ with the deterministic reference of §4.40. The only nonzero reference targets are the bare finest boundary angles of the wave, of size C r²τ|ζ|. Every positive-depth raw target is I; the artificial inner cut is the identity reference. In common relative Landau coordinates, integration of (190) and native density summability imply for a fine link at y a difference from I bounded by C r²τ|ζ|exp[−c d(y,S₀)], where S₀ consists of the actual nonzero outer fine slots and their fixed exterior and the harmless factor η/σ_y≤1 was dropped. The actual cut error at any site of the full fixed test enlargement is at most τ_e η^{-P}poly(R_e)exp(−cMR_e) uniformly on the original complex ζ-disc by (4.83). For the selected Q₂ and large original M this is at most C r^{K}τ at any fixed required K. At zero the actual field in the outer comparison is flat up to its reference frame. Use analytic continuation of the fixed real-centered representatives or a smaller disc and Schwarz for the difference from its zero value. This comparison is for invariant scalar pairings; no inverse raw frame derivative enters it.

Let x be inward physical distance from the tested true face, measured within its central flat tangential patch. On this portion the minimal domains D_h retreat from the face by at most C M₁σ_h, by (2.13) and its integer rounding. Thus the native index at y is at least log_L(1+x/(C M₁η)) minus a fixed constant until the top layer is reached. The normal width of the test is a fixed current distance. Its enlarged short-carrier support lies away from every other true face and from the artificial cut. From the native interface toll in (3.25), the distance to any finest boundary input is at least S₀ times the number of intervening layers minus a fixed allowance. (In this proof the constant S₀ in the toll is the spacing; it is distinct from the just described set of input slots.) Consequently on the test enlargement
\[
 e^{-c d(y,\,\mathrm{outer\ fine\ inputs})}
       \le C(1+x/\eta)^{-p}                                 \tag{4.109}
\]
for p>1 at the assumed original spacing. For x already in the top interior the saturation at h=e costs only a fixed factor on this bounded-width patch. This is an upper estimate, not an assertion that the oscillatory physical solution equals its harmonic linearization.

At old depth one, the complete old means and currents on a short actual carrier, in its physical representative, have fine-angle differences bounded by the same r²τ profile and the small cut error: (190) on that old record has meshes between 1/L and 1. Its fine and coarse slots are bounded by a local weighted sum of the actual data; one may bound these on a carrier of n old cubes by an extra (1+n)^p times the right side of (4.109) at the root. This just uses 1+x/η≤(1+C_L n)(1+x'/η) between two of its slots, and costs only its original spare moment. If it leaves this fixed enlargement, its diameter is ≥c/r and the proved physical native sup/derivative tail of §4.41 pays every fixed power. The compact real comparison and complex relative frame also give the same bounded group conjugations. At zero all the tested stars are physically flat. In a local gauge each gauge-invariant source member has zero linear field jet there, including its independent-current color slots. Its absolute second-order rooted bound on the spare first-chart is at most C g₁⁻²; this upper bound, unlike the sharper o(g₁⁻²) residual in §4.46, applies to the entire first H. Hence the normalized short-carrier variation is bounded by its old weighted moment times g₁⁻²r⁴τ² times the square of (4.109) at its root, plus the negligible cut error. The sum of those squared profiles over a fixed current patch has at most C r⁻³ effective roots, because the convergent normal sum Σ_{n≥0}(1+n)^{-2p} is bounded. With the dimension-six normalizer it follows that on one smaller fixed ζ-disc, uniformly,
\[
 \sup_{|\zeta|\le c_*}|F_-(\zeta)-F_-(0)|
                  \le C g_1^{-2}\tau^2/r .                   \tag{4.110}
\]
The current-unit smooth f, the actual orbit gauge covariance, and the first-source physical chart assumptions are those of §4.46. This argument does not rely on a sign at complex fields.

The coefficient [ζ²] in this same difference is real and ≥c_*'g₁⁻²τ²/r by (4.93), and its linear coefficient vanishes at the flat record. Cauchy from (4.110) bounds the sum of all orders ≥3 at |ζ|=a by C g₁⁻²τ²/r·(a/c_*)³/(1−a/c_*). Choose a fixed positive a<c_*/2 with this last factor <c_*'a²/2. At ζ=i a the quadratic term is negative with the same modulus, proving the first assertion. For the large proper s-difference there is an even simpler proof: (4.95) is minus the actual outer completion at U_ζ. Its fine classical matching square continues to the holomorphic trace pairing; a real angle is replaced by i a times that angle. The original fine quantum residual has the smaller rooted Hessian bound and its higher terms are bounded on the same fixed disc. The explicit decaying outer fine-layer profile gives (4.110) also for this large-record difference, and its quadratic coefficient from §4.46 has the opposite sign. The same fixed a, decreased if needed, proves the second lower bound. The complete physical normalization still obeys §§4.44,4.47; a nonreal deterministic H fiber has not been assigned a positive probability.

**Missing radius in the original units.** If the branchwise saved scalar inequality (2.1) for this filing held on a ζ-disc of radius h with the same old grade, Cauchy's coefficient estimate and (4.93) would require
\[
 h\le C\frac{g_e}{\tau} A_1^{1/2}Q_*^{D/2}\,
                r^{(1+s_0)/2},\qquad
 \rho_{\mathrm{old,wave}}\le
       C_L A_1^{1/2}Q_*^{D/2}g_e r^{(5+s_0)/2}.              \tag{4.111}
\]
Here Q_* bounds the actual finite canonical polynomial over the proposed disc, including its contact/grade constants; it is only polylogarithmic on this selected Q₂ curve. The second inequality is the corresponding maximal allowed amplitude in the original birth-one potential/plaquette units. The fine link angle of the chosen probe and its first old curvature are each of order r²τ per unit ζ (fixed L); multiply the first inequality by that number. The true imaginary probe has that original size times a fixed a. It can be well within the ordinary microscopic analytic radius ρ₁. It is the *paired domain supporting the saved positive age* that would need the extra factor r^{(1+s₀)/2}. The original simultaneous domain has a fixed ζ-disc and supplies no such reduction. A successful derivative bound for G_o(M^eU), which is constant on this very wave, cannot yield (1.1)–(1.2) for these independent fine-slot filings. ∎

### 4.55 Which packet is tested by the imaginary wall

**Proposition 4.67 (P, quantifiers and trajectory of the test).** Equation (4.108) disproves a separate positive-age scalar bound on the indicated uncompensated small-record filing, or on its indicated large-record proper difference, with the native fine-slot extension, the first-coarse moment filing of §4.46 and the canonical inputs of §4.35. It does not disprove (9.1) of the stored-record construction. The two statements differ already at the defining source map and at the set of carrier roots to which the projection is applied.

**Identification.** The stored-record construction, (9.1), displays a finite current/source-jet and weighted absolute anchor norm of the literal single composition \(R_j(P_{\rm old}q_{\rm old}\Gamma^R,J_{\rm phys}(P_{\rm old}q_{\rm old}\Gamma^R))\). That display denotes the weights by indexed coefficients and do not give a universal numerical list of the jet/anchor weights. For the clean-born degree-two stable H scalar, (1.2)–(1.3) of that construction and RG31 give the specified coefficients and powers explicitly:
\[
 (a_{0,2;j,e},a_{0,3;j,e},a_{0,4;j,e})=C A_j(g_e/g_j)^2
 \bigl(r^s,\ (g_e/\alpha)r^b,\ (g_e/\alpha)^2r^2\bigr)
 \ \text{with powers } m=2,3,4,                             \tag{4.112}
\]
where the display lists the three coefficients, and the powers are respectively 2,3,4. Here 0 labels the scalar row in this comparison. The incoming \(A_j\) is the old source norm in MT37/40, with its actual root, offset, mesh and test convention. For a derivative row one has to keep its particular lowered grade and radius cost; (4.112) supplies none of those for free. MT22 includes canonical first-order exterior coordinates and pinned data in Q. MT37–40 first take the own-current pullback on the physical coarse graph. The stable projection removes its exact dimension-four family and total dimension-six center with the pointed descendants, retains the covariant quadratic and cubic kernels with the area/commutator contribution combined, and defines the quartic remainder by subtracting both whole covariant completions. Lower, free and contact companions remain in the state. RG6's paired canonical norm is distinct from this physical jet norm.

LM42 and LM45–47 require one common original determining problem for all roots of a tested packet and keep separately born proper differences and lower/contact rows. The chosen physical source extension at birth is explicitly MT21/PA4. It repairs the outer input, substitutes its physical current in the completed inner response and repairs the resulting primitive field. In contrast (4.93) uses the finite fine-slot extension with classical term \(\beta_0 H_b(U)\) and a fixed first-*coarse* representative; a lower projection with the same flat coarse moments does not identify its extension away from the coarse graph. The small record uses the sum restricted by \(X\subset Z\). It omits the adjacent carriers and its literal exterior partner. The quantity \(D_+^\Delta\) is the later proper difference of a different determining graph, after subtracting that exterior partner. Neither expression is the **definition** of the stored stable coefficient in (9.1). No equality of one of these expressions to a complete named lower/contact row has been proved. The exact p.377 subtraction kills the leading small-record proper term on this test. The opposite difference of the large record is a genuine native boundary term, but fresh covariance and the completed exterior output cannot be deleted in asserting a statement about their sum.

There is a direct way to see the distinction even before localization. At the homogeneous first birth use its full periodic determining problem with no separately pinned fine exterior, all *birth source* walls one, and the same global independent Cartan input \(U_\zeta\) with \(M^1U_\zeta=1\). In the inner MT21 repair the prescribed averaged orbit is flat. The identity has zero Wilson action there. Uniqueness of the small critical orbit on this homogeneous regular graph puts the repaired input on the flat orbit for real \(\zeta\), and holomorphic continuation gives the invariant identity on the small complex disc. At B=0 the completed first response of that flat input is again flat, and so is its outer physical repair. Every local invariant matching frustration at this resulting source argument vanishes identically on the curve. In particular its classical quadratic coefficient is exactly zero, whereas the independent raw \(\beta_0H_b(U_\zeta)\) has the nonzero coefficient of §4.46. This shows explicitly why agreement of the two source prescriptions on true physical inputs cannot identify this countertest with the mandated prescription. At nonzero B the unmarked kernel still depends on the external background, and any changes of frame must also act on B. The vanishing just proved is for the deterministic flat source slot. A joined finite window may have prescribed fine exterior rows that the global homogeneous torus does not have. Neither its repaired output nor its quantum score has been set to zero by this argument.

**Actual cutoff sizes.** Write \(x_i=g_i^{-2}\). In the admitted weak flow each step changes x by an amount between two positive fixed constants in magnitude. On any stopped weak trajectory with fixed terminal coupling this gives \(x_2\asymp K\) and \(x_e=x_2+O(R_2)\) at the first return \(e=2+R_2\). CMP119 p.246 chooses the logarithmic range exponent \(\nu\ge2\), and (2.5) rounds \((\log x_i)^\nu\) upwards to a power of L. Choose K so that \((\log x_2)^\nu\) lies strictly inside one rounding interval, e.g. in a fixed interior fraction of \((L^{n-1},L^n)\). This is possible along any fixed-terminal family obeying the I-0c flow: summing the flow gives \(x_2=(b_0\log L)K+O(\log K+1)\) at that weak endpoint, so the interior range has arbitrarily many admissible K. Since \(R_2=o(x_2)\), rounding is constant from 2 to e there, and \(K-e\to\infty\) leaves ample room for the original aligned periodic boxes on the fixed torus. This is a subsequence calculation on a stated weak trajectory, not a construction of I-4. We can take the smaller trial amplitude \(\tau=c g_e^2\) throughout; it is inside both of the actual positive native radii used in §4.46. The native inputs of the test consist of the one old own-spacing spike of angle \(O(\delta_1)\), zero retained fluctuations, and the Cartan wave. Its potential and first derivative in the current-unit affected exterior are bounded by \(C r\tau,C\tau\); the fixed extension across the last exterior layers preserves these estimates. Block-scaled gauge and first differences on the actual spike cost at most \(C\delta_1\) in its own units. Its local canonical value is \(O(p_0(g_1))\), up to the bounded running coupling ratio. This verifies a fixed power of \(1+\log x_e\) for the **separate row's** sup-first-coordinate Q and the number of its current localization cells. It asserts no such formula for an arbitrary enlarged completed readset. Even a fixed additional power of x or R in this comparison is harmless since
\[
 \log(r^{-1})=(R_2+1)\log L\ \asymp (\log x_2)^\nu,
 \qquad g_1^{-2}\tau^2\asymp x_2^{-1}.                      \tag{4.113}
\]
Consequently (4.108) dominates the three coefficients (4.112) times any of these fixed Q powers for that stronger standalone test. This verifies the age/coupling comparison without absorbing a power of the unbounded earlier age into a rare factor. For the actual target the open issue is membership and control on its own completed determining domain. Merely enlarging the power of Q on the known test does not address that issue. ∎

### 4.56 A localized independent-current score without a change of axial frame

**Proposition 4.68 (P/L, on the separated real gas of Proposition4.64 and its declared numerical margin).** Keep the actual exterior field U of that proposition fixed in its chosen real frame. Vary the **independent current** in the extended unmarked weight by \(J(t)=J(0)+t h\), with h supported in one current M-cube c on the post-healing top member, \(\|h\|_\infty\le1\) in entry units, and J(0) the physical current there. Take the same small real coarse neighborhood with strict native margins as in §4.52, or simply its flat center. Let \(S_h=\partial_t\log w(t)|_0\) in the actual positive unexpanded return integral and use the real law at zero. Then the normalized derivative contributed by the unmarked weight alone satisfies
\[
 \left|{\rm Cov}_0(F_o,S_h)\right|
 \le C A_1(g_e/g_1)^2r^{s_0}e^{-p_0-c'\operatorname{dist}_M(o,c)} . \tag{4.114}
\]
This is an actual independent-current score row at a real base. For the derivative of a separately varying marked function its own response must be added.

**Proof.** CMP119 (2.34)–(2.39) separates the independent J ceiling (2.36) from the tests on U and its reconstructed currents (2.37). On the new homogeneous top graph the former is of order \(\alpha_{0,e}\) in entry units; on carriers not meeting c it is unchanged. For \(|t|\le\delta=c_*g_e^2\) it has the required strict margin. Every gauge, old reference and subsidiary U-dependent auxiliary field in this *external domain test* remains fixed. This verifies the direction without a derivative of (106). The internal weights have their usual J dependence through their true maps. The resummation immediately before LFRII (1.73) bounds their first complex log variation uniformly in the retained ξ on the complete prescribed body. On a rectangular return Q and t in this disc the oscillatory exponent has a bounded analytic variation; its derivative costs \(C/\delta\) by Cauchy and is zero when the actual rectangular determining support (including its fixed collar) misses c. A common additive log normalization depending only on the external field may be dropped from every covariance. The same Cauchy bound for a proper \(V(Y)\) costs \(C\alpha/\delta\) times its original (1.68) tree bound and is zero if the current support misses its actual Y. Here one differentiates V itself, never the logarithm of V or its possibly vanishing activity.

For a change of the state at a rectangle Q, only the one-body term there and proper supports touching that rectangle, or its exclusion neighborhood, change. A contributing support must in addition meet c. The diameter bound following (4.106) converts the first (1.67) tree length into \(\operatorname{dist}_M(c,Q)-CR_e\) with a fixed rate. The original rooted count summing the rest of the tree gives
\[
 \operatorname{osc}_Q S_h\le C\delta^{-1}e^{CR_e-c'\operatorname{dist}_M(c,Q)},
 \qquad |S_h-S_h(\varnothing)|\le C\delta^{-1} e^{CR_e} .   \tag{4.115}
\]
The second bound follows by deleting the rectangles and summing the first, since the number of locations/shapes per current cell is at most polynomial in R for the specified gas. Distinct interior ξ at the same rectangle are included by comparison through the vacant state. The fixed real positive law supplies the conditional covariance contraction of §4.51. For each local-age component in (4.103), combine (4.115) with that contraction. The convolution of the two spatial exponentials remains exponentially summable in four dimensions with a slightly reduced rate. The absolute covariance estimate instead uses the second bound in (4.115) and \(P(E_{o,Q,n})\), producing the saving \(L^{-An}\) of §4.52. Interpolate and sum as in (4.103), including the age-zero component directly through the covariance contraction. The single surviving occupancy surplus pays \(g_e^{-2}\), \(e^{CR_e}\) and the placement polynomial. This proves (4.114) for the stated margin. The same argument applies to real and imaginary parts of the directional score.

In particular, differentiating the unnormalized numerator gives \(E_0(F_oS_h)\), and differentiating its own normalizer subtracts \(E_0F_o\,E_0S_h\). This is one place where each separately averaged unmarked derivative would omit a required term. The actual physical variation in v also differentiates U, its auxiliary configurations and the gauges; (4.115) alone cannot establish (4.106) for that direction. On the independent complex walls the comparison is an identity of analytic integrals only on their established member charts. It does not provide a uniform positive covariance law there or extend MT21 through unverified old pinned data. ∎

**Corollary 4.68a (P, one absolute microscopic current-anchor sum).** In (4.114), the current on c is an arbitrary bounded array of microscopic bond values. CMP119 (2.36) has only a supremum of J. The first-difference conditions (2.38),(2.39) apply to the link potential, which is fixed here. Write \(k_{b,A}={\rm Cov}_0(F_o,S_{b,A})\) for the coefficient for one original fine-current bond b and Lie-algebra basis index A in this array, using a fixed orthonormal basis and the same entry units as (2.36). Given those coefficients, select in each c the complex phases of h so that each \(k_{b,A} h_{b,A}\) is nonnegative real, and divide by a fixed color-norm constant. A real-direction version follows by testing real and imaginary parts separately. Linearity of the score and (4.114) prove, for any fixed m and any \(0<c''<c'\),
\[
 \sum_{b,A}(1+d_M(o,c_b))^m e^{c''d_M(o,c_b)} |k_{b,A}|
 \le C_{m,c''} A_1(g_e/g_1)^2 r^{s_0}e^{-p_0}.             \tag{4.115a}
\]
Here \(c_b\) is its original top current-size anchor cube; summing over c uses the actual four-dimensional cube count. Taking the dual of the microscopic sup norm **before** summing cubes removes any inverse fine-mesh count. This proves an absolute derivative-anchor row of the actual changing unmarked normalization. Bases scaled by mesh volume use the same convention on both sides of its duality. For two free derivative indices a joint directional bound alone does not imply an absolute two-index tensor bound.

### 4.57 Linearization of the mandated first repair with genuine source walls

**Lemma 4.69 (P, exact flat response and a restricted near-endpoint estimate).** On the admitted homogeneous periodic first determining problem of §4.55 let \(a\) be the dimensionless fine-link tangent of its null-average test. For the first MT21 repair write \(T_s a\) for its output tangent. Fix the original collars and use their original MC weak vertex weights \(w_s(p)\), with weight one on the protected plaquette carriers. In flat one-step units and in a common infinitesimal gauge,
\[
 T_s a=a-\Pi_b\Gamma_{0,s}\iota_1 d^*\bigl(w_s\,d a\bigr). \tag{4.116}
\]
Here \(\iota_1\) inserts the force into the first augmented row, \(\Gamma_{0,s}\) is the original weakened five-row parametrix at identity, and \(\Pi_b\) takes its gauge-field component. A fixed Wilson normalization can equivalently be included in the flat plaquette form. This is a formula for the inner MT21 repair, irrespective of the unmarked exterior score.

**Proof.** At the trivial field the seed of MC9–12 is zero and the derivative of the nonlinear remainder in its unknown is zero. The derivative of the parametrix therefore multiplies zero. The derivative of the seeded force is the Wilson second variation with the original whole-plaquette weights, which gives the expression displayed. This order matters. For any flat test y the seed has bilinear form \(\sum_p w_s(p)\langle (dy)_p,(da)_p\rangle\). Even if \(d^* d a=0\) in a neighborhood, weights differing across its seam create a force on the seam edges. Dropping that boundary term would not describe this source prescription.

At all walls one (4.116) gives a pure gradient by the flat homogeneous result of §4.55. At fixed first mesh ratio L the original inverse expansion MB35–36/MC12 has exponentially summable first-step row and column kernels, uniformly throughout its fixed smaller wall polydisc. To see the norm here, each one-step local box has bounded size in bare units and bounded incidence. Each parametrix walk has a locally bounded endpoint factor and a product of commutator factors summable in the number of traversed boxes by the original Neumann contraction. Keeping a small exponential weight in the box distance before the summation gives row and column bounds independent of the periodic volume. Cauchy along the one-dimensional complex wall direction \(s=1+u h\), \(\|h\|_\infty\le1\), gives the same bounds for the derivative on any smaller disc. The finite-stencil \(d,d^*\) and the per-plaquette weight map have bounded operator norms in these fixed bare units. It follows by the Schur estimate that
\[
 \|d T_s a\|_2\le C_L\|s-1\|_\infty\,\|da\|_2.              \tag{4.117}
\]
Indeed subtract (4.116) at one before differentiating, so every term on the right is applied to \(da\), and use \(dT_1a=0\). For the actual exponentially normal-decaying test \(\sum |da|^2\le C\tau^2 r\) since a fixed normal layer has at most \(Cr^{-3}\) sites across the tested support and each plaquette angle there is bounded by \(C r^2\tau\) times that layer's exponential. This global sum includes the fixed number of extra harmonic exterior layers, their fixed cutoff and all tangential transitions. It is invariant if the initial endpoint gradient is restored. For the curvature matching quadratic form *of this inner repaired field* the resulting normalized Taylor bound is \(C g_1^{-2}\tau^2r^{-1}\|s-1\|_\infty^2\). In particular all fixed-order quadratic matching-force evaluations here retain the desired scalar order on sufficiently small near-one source-wall discs with the radius specified by (4.111). This is an upper estimate and does not impose that radius on the original domain. The exact \(\Phi_0\) and outer repair, lower/contact projection, changing unmarked density and localization of the retained mixed graph still enter the stored packet. Equation (4.116) supplies the actual boundary operator to carry into those maps. Its value cannot be inferred from harmonicity on either side of a sharp wall. ∎

### 4.58 The full periodic old readout on the rough first-return wave

**Proposition 4.70 (P/L, the common homogeneous birth graph at all birth source walls one).** Take the actual first globally clean step of the Q₂ history in §4.46, before any choice of different root localization windows. Its determining graph is the periodic torus with all first averages prescribed and no fixed fine exterior. For the small-Y determining branch at its true outer face, take the actual joined field on Y with its prescribed exterior field on the complement; call this single input \(\widetilde\Gamma_Y(\zeta)\). Use the original gauge at their prescribed seam, transporting both sides together. Reconstruct on the full old birth graph from \(M^1\widetilde\Gamma_Y\), including all its endpoint transports. Use this same reconstructed physical pair for *every* old root of the supported test. On the first-H physical chart and the original Q₂ spacing and plateau margins of §4.46, the wave's quadratic Taylor response in any fixed strictly interior portion of the tangential test plateau (including its normal face) obeys
\[
 \left|[\zeta^2]\,r^{-2}\sum_b f_b
      R^{\rm per}_{1,b}\bigl(P_1(M^1\widetilde\Gamma_Y(\zeta)),
                 J_{\rm phys}(P_1(M^1\widetilde\Gamma_Y(\zeta)))\bigr)\right|
 \le C_A A_1 g_1^{-2}\tau^2 r^A .                           \tag{4.118}
\]
Here A is any prescribed fixed power for which the original buffer/spacing inequalities in §4.46 have been chosen with sufficient room. The root function is the true first normalized physical H birth on the full periodic chart, with any of its specified fixed bounded physical coarse moment projections; keep their pointed offsets and complete first-source localization halos. The statement concerns the Taylor response, including the normalized quantum first-step coefficient, on this particular common old graph. It makes no identification of a smaller original pinned window with the periodic determining problem.

**Proof.** At the origin of this curve all the original targets are flat up to the single compact Q₂ nodal pure gauge supported in the inner region. Its endpoints preserve the coarser data; use its induced site representatives through the whole hierarchy. There is an admissible flat field with precisely these raw targets and exterior. It has action zero and is the unique weak constrained critical orbit. This also gives a flat base for the complete periodic level-one input, even if its raw first averages include a node gradient. Transform to identity for this linear calculation. Gauge changes do not affect the final invariant coefficients.

Write h for the variation of \(M^1\widetilde\Gamma_Y\) in old units. For all tested roots and a neighborhood of old radius \(t=D(1+e)\), with D any fixed constant, the linear comparison in §4.46 holds to a prescribed power \(P\). This neighborhood has width \(O(e r)\) in entry units. Its overlap towards the exterior uses the actual prescribed exterior; the fixed number of harmonic exterior layers is followed by the chosen cutoff there, and on that entire prescribed side the *straight first mean is exactly zero*, including the cutoff, by its complete tangential periods. The null-mean envelope must continue to be constant on first blocks in the tangential averaging direction there. Actual first rows straddling the seam are included in the old-scale comparison, since their full endpoint blocks use only a fixed number of extra fine stars. Thus on this neighborhood the true coarse curl \(dh\) is \(O(C\tau r^P)\), including any induced endpoint gradient. Outside it the coarse tangent has a crude bound by a fixed inverse power of r and g; for this explicit history use the full compact native member bound on a fixed \(\zeta\)-disc for the fine links, Cauchy, and the fixed-length level-one prefix words. On the prescribed exterior use the explicit array itself. There is no global volume count in this crude supremum.

For one anchor, use the radial coordinate tree on its old ball (or an enclosing cube) of radius t. Subtract its node gradient, extended by zero off the cube. Inside the concentric half-cube each corrected link is a sum of at most \(C t\) plaquette curls by the strip identity. The resulting inner supremum is \(C t\tau r^P\). On the boundary of the larger cube cutting off the gauge costs at most \(C t\sup|h|\). This change is pure gauge globally, so every physical gauge-invariant quadratic response at the flat orbit annihilates it in either argument.

The genuine periodic first-H physical conditional Hessian on its upper coarse links has exponentially summable two-anchor norm of order \(C A_1g_1^{-2}\) in its original mesh, by the first-step calculation of §§4.36–4.37 and the actual shrinking-selector comparison in §4.41. Equivalently its classical part is evaluated on the linear constrained response, and the normalized fluctuation derivative is the already bounded remainder; finite prescribed coarse completions keep these estimates. The first field gradient of each invariant scalar at the flat orbit vanishes by constant color rotation, so the second input variation is absent. The two small inner factors bound the inner-inner contribution. If either anchor is outside the half-cube, that Hessian has an exponential tail \(C e^{-ct}\), and the crude tangent bound costs only a fixed power of r and g. There are at most \(C r^{-4}\) tested roots in the fixed current region; the dimension-six normalization costs \(r^{-2}\). With \(t=D(1+e)\), choose D and P using the original margins to pay these powers and the requested A; (4.113) pays the fixed coupling powers and the declared \(\tau=c g_e^2\). This proves (4.118), uniformly in the full old torus because the two-anchor tail is already summed.

Thus the same rough return for which (4.108) grew has negligible quadratic response on the complete globally first-born periodic readout at the physical birth-source vertex. Coarse physical lower/pointed terms can be included here by their true upper-anchor Hessians; the argument requires no sign or special choice of their coefficients. The extension does not combine independently prepared root records of the p.377 localized action into one with a different incidence cost. Their actual fine pinned rows and the simultaneous earlier source walls are exactly where this calculation ceases to apply. ∎

### 4.59 A pinned near-window at the first birth: the seam force and its reprojection

**Proposition 4.71 (P/L, deterministic first wall jet on the pinned near-window slot).** Use the actual CMP109 (3.18)–(3.24),(3.33) near-window construction, with its own \(\square_0\), finest prescribed exterior and all the raw interior target rows. Choose a source root in the Q₂ small-record test plateau a fixed number of additional old layers inward, so that the *entire fixed-size first-birth window*, its first-mean endpoint blocks and its exterior plaquette stars are in the harmonic portion of the wave. There are such roots on the aligned grid: the old window has a fixed number of old cells, the inward plateau width in old units tends to infinity, and the transverse envelope is constant on a fixed entry rectangle. Keep a fixed inward layer beyond the window radius; its harmonic factor is a fixed nonzero constant, not a number decaying with e. At a fixed short window, the joined-input linearization agrees with the exact Cartan tangent a there to \(O(\tau r^P)\), modulo the same transformed fine boundary values, by §4.46 at the chosen margins. The result below states the exact harmonic calculation first and then the error for the actual input.

Let \(D\) be the dynamic links of this old problem. Extension by zero on its pinned links defines \(d_D^*\) as the transpose of the curl of these variations; exterior plaquettes incident on a dynamic link are included. Work at the flat orbit in a section of the true gauge quotient. The first-step raw differential modulo the node frame is Q; the last row of MC9 uses the corresponding reduced differential. Write \(G=\Pi_b\Gamma_1\iota_1\) for the physical-link component of the inverse of this exact augmented system acting on a pure force. The last row gives \(QG=0\) modulo a coarse node gradient, and its variations have zero prescribed fine trace. For a source wall direction h outside the protected source collar write \(w'_h\) for the derivative at one of the weights of the complete original plaquette vertices. Since \(d_D^*da=0\) on **all dynamic links of this window**, the first wall variation of the first inner repair is
\[
 v_h=-G d_D^*(w'_h da),\qquad (Qv_h,\ v_h|_{D^c})=(0,0)\quad\text{modulo frame}. \tag{4.119}
\]
Indeed the derivative of the parametrix multiplies the vanishing original seed. The effect of a sharp wall on the Wilson force remains in (4.119). Its bilinear pairing against a dynamic test b is \(\sum_p w'_h(p)\langle db,da\rangle_p\); moving the weight through the divergence would incorrectly discard a possible nonzero force. The reference harmonic field with those pinned values and first means is its own linear constrained minimum, by the same zero force and uniqueness on the zero-target space. These statements use no arbitrary boundary completion.

Now take the near-window **zero-fluctuation** term. The distinction between the following maps is material. CMP109 p.275, preceding (3.24), explicitly replaces the background in the fluctuation response and its averaging operations by \(U_{k+1}(\square_0,M'(U))\), extended by U outside the window. In the near term (3.24) the actual old \(U_j(\square_0,\dots)\) takes the resulting target field, and the perturbation of this target coming from the fluctuation is zero at B=0. At the first step \(j=0,k+1=1\), the remaining bare level-zero rows are literal identities. Thus the dependence on the repaired background in this term at B=0 is through the true finite-window map \(P_1q_1\), with its exterior trace, followed by the bare readout. At the outer MT21 repair the protected bare constraint is exactly \(q(b)=b=0\) (MC after (18), printed p.568): on the marked stencil the outer repair changes no bond, even with nontrivial outer source walls. This identifies the maps for this particular near summand; the other terms in (3.21) and the difference formula are kept in their own rows.

Since \(P_1q_1\) is invariant, up to its site frame, under a zero-target, zero-trace variation, its derivative annihilates (4.119). The first nonlinear H matching forces have holomorphic quadratic jet given by the bilinear sum of the three matching differences. Before this reprojection a force term \(2\sum_i\langle K_i(a),K_i(v_h)\rangle\) would be present. After reprojection the second argument is a fine pure gradient, so that term vanishes. For representatives with coefficients held fixed on this graph, the same identity applies to the complete pointed quadratic representative, the dimension-four representative and every physical current/contact scalar evaluated on this *same* twice-repaired input; they are gauge invariant and their first physical variation at the flat orbit is zero. The genuine path-area part and the cubic completion have ordinary field degree at least three and cannot contribute directly to \([\zeta^2]\). One keeps all these terms in the projected coefficient, rather than assigning the unprojected H square its saved age. A source-wall derivative of a projection coefficient formed from fluctuations on other input directions has its own term. It belongs to the complete normalized derivative below and has not been cancelled by (4.119). For a separately stored partner with a different determining graph this argument applies only after checking its graph and trace. On the actual joined trial the derivatives of the full fixed-window constrained maps are bounded at the flat point in its own mesh. The only uncancelled linear tangent in the same zero-fluctuation test therefore has size \(C\tau r^P\), before its pairing with an old physical tangent of size \(O(r^2\tau)\) on the window. The first-order wall variation of the principal \(g_1^{-2}\) classical coefficient at forbidden size is absent on this true pinned near slot. ∎

**Second wall variation (P, exact coefficient of the remaining linear map).** At harmonic input on the same window, let \(s=1+u h\) and \(\Gamma'_h\) be the original derivative of its weakened inverse. Its second variation in u has raw trace, modulo node frames,
\[
 Q\,\partial_u^2 T_{1+uh}a|_0
 =-2 Q\Pi_b\Gamma'_h\iota_1 d_D^*(w'_h da).                 \tag{4.120}
\]
The term with \(w''_{hh}\) has zero target by (4.119), and the term with \(\Gamma''_{hh}\) multiplies zero. The prescribed exterior variation is zero. After reapplying the physical \(P_1\) its first derivative in u is zero, so no square of that derivative survives in the classical H coefficient. Its second wall derivative pairs the unvaried harmonic H jet with the true minimizing lift of (4.120), with the same projected kernels. The complete same-box filing of MB35–36 identifies the support of this trace defect. Use AL21–25 with its sign convention \(K P_i=I_i-T_i\), \(\sum_i I_i=1\). The original weight is attached once to the union of boxes in a walk; multiplying separately weakened resolvents would count repeated walls incorrectly. If \(T_\omega\) is the ordered product of the residual boxes in a tail and \(X_\omega\) their union, direct telescoping gives
\[
 K\Gamma_s-1=\sum_{i,\omega}
   [w_s(X_i\cup X_\omega)-w_s(X_\omega)] I_i T_\omega . \tag{4.121}
\]
The empty tail has \(T_\emptyset=1,w_s(\emptyset)=1\). Indeed the negative term from \(-T_iT_\omega\) has the weight of its complete nonempty ordered tail and may be multiplied by \(\sum_i I_i\) before reindexing. The derivative of the square bracket at one sums h over exactly those wall cells meeting \(X_i\) that did not already meet the tail. Taking the coarse row of (4.121) on the first-force input gives the trace in (4.120) without any factorized-wall assumption. On any protected row whose complete inverse boxes retain weight one this is zero. The identity alone does not make it zero on all remote unprotected row coordinates of the finite window. It locates the first possible classical leakage in this composite at the second, rather than the first, source-wall derivative; no sign of (4.120) has been assumed.

### 4.60 A single source wall, the actual old normalization, and the matched exterior

**Proposition 4.72 (P, a whole single-wall segment on the harmonic pinned window).** Use the first-window setting of Proposition4.71. Vary one literal unprotected inner-repair wall cell Δ in the interior of that determining window, leaving all other repair walls at one. Take a cell for which the complete Wilson plaquette seed carrier reaches less than the inward cutoff margin of an inverse box, on both the fine and top layers it meets. On a first-step window this is a fixed geometric condition: the Wilson linear seed uses just the incident plaquette and its weak variation, the two native lengths have ratio L, and AL20 says that the support of each input cutoff is a fixed fraction of the inverse-box radius inside its complete box. Thus it suffices that the original \(c R_{\rm loc}/L\) exceed the fixed plaquette-carrier width in local units. The same test can be used farther inside a large native \(\square_0\) so that cutting at its boundary costs no margin. If no unfrozen source wall meets the old graph, the corresponding source derivative is zero directly.

On the harmonic tangent, the reprojection in (3.24) is independent of this single s parameter at first order in the external wave, throughout its full allowed disc, not merely at its center. Indeed write \(s_\Delta=1+u\). Its weighted first force is \(u f_\Delta\), \(f_\Delta=d_D^*(w'_\Delta da)\). Every bond in this vector lies within one seed-carrier width of Δ. In a nonzero AL22 inverse walk applied to this vector the last local inverse or commutator reads the input through its rightmost cutoff \(\chi_z\), supported strictly inside its full box. The stated margin puts Δ in that last box. Hence every contributing original walk contains this wall and, by AL23, carries it *exactly once*. Therefore \(\Gamma_{1+u_\Delta}\iota_1 f_\Delta=(1+u)\Gamma_1\iota_1 f_\Delta\). The repair variation relative to a is exactly \(-u(1+u)Gf_\Delta\) to linear order in the wave. Its full relative target and fixed exterior trace vanish. The following original physical near-window reprojection kills this variation, modulo its node frame, for every u. All orders in the single-wall variable of the classical \(\zeta^2\) coefficient vanish after this reprojection. This is an operator identity on the pinned harmonic graph. It is not the assertion that the unprojected fine H has a separate age. At the actual joined input the small nonharmonic error of Proposition4.71 persists uniformly in this disc on the fixed window. ∎

For two distinct wall cells the mixed trace instead includes \(-Q\Pi_b(\partial_i\Gamma)\iota_1 f_j-Q\Pi_b(\partial_j\Gamma)\iota_1 f_i\). A seed whose carrier meets both walls gives a further \(Gf_{ij}\) with zero trace. The derivative walk must connect the i-cell to the j-seed. AL22–24 therefore give a bound proportional to \(\exp[-c d(i,j)]\), up to the fixed seed and inverse-box widths, by retaining a small fraction of its length majorant. This gives no past-age suppression for two neighboring unprotected cells. In particular the one-wall identity cannot be applied successively while other adjacent walls are already weakened.

**Exact normalized coefficient at nonzero fluctuation (P).** Write m for the completed first-source insertion at the actual first integration variable B on the specified graph F, and \(w_z(B)\) for its complete unmarked density with its original selectors, constraint parametrization, Jacobian and exterior data. Work at the flat real origin. The chosen u here is a *pure source-repair wall*. Consequently the unmarked density does not depend on u. Let \(n=\partial_u m|_{u=0}\), \(S=\partial_z\log w_z|_0\), \(S'=\partial_z^2\log w_z|_0\); primes on n are z derivatives. All Hessian coefficients below use bilinear, not absolute, products. The first-source normalized integral satisfies
\[
 2[z^2 u]\frac{\int m(z,u,B) w_z(B)dB}{\int w_z(B)dB}
 =E_0 n''+2\operatorname{Cov}_0(n',S)
 +\operatorname{Cov}_0\bigl(n,S'+(S-E_0 S)^2\bigr).         \tag{4.122}
\]
This follows by twice differentiating the actual quotient. If n is an explicit difference of several previously born terms, each keeps its original defining integral and denominator. The source-independent exterior p.377 completion and a later signed healing ratio are not a second denominator of the incoming R₁ in (9.1). In a later operation the corresponding numerator/denominator response must again be taken there. Formula (4.122) uses the true original starred cutoff (CMP119 pp.249–250); in this first step it is fixed in the independent starred coordinates. At z=0, every B stays in the original real integral. At this flat base \(R_{1,s}(1)=1\) for all u, so n=0 for a source term whose only u dependence is through this repair; derivatives of independent lower-coefficient filings, if any, must be kept separately.

For the coarse projection, apply its fixed original linear jet/moment and rooted filing operation to this *whole* derivative. In particular the quadratic matching and dimension-four representative must be subtracted with their actual coefficient variations if the field in the representative is held fixed. For a representative with coefficient d and field functional L its u derivative is \(d' L+d L'\). The proved zero-target identity kills only the common-graph deterministic field part of \(L'\) at the harmonic test. In the direct physical field Hessian the pointed quadratic term includes its shifted test; it cannot be replaced by a sharp partial root sum. The path-area cubic, the remaining cubic completion and the quartic tail have no ordinary quadratic field jet on their original common physical graph. Their Wick/conditional lower and contact descendants at nonzero B enter the derivative of the actual stored lower rows, as do the Casimir action and reference terms wherever present. Thus (4.119)–(4.121) do not by themselves evaluate either covariance in (4.122) for a far or independently framed slot.

**Quantitative local consequence (P on this fixed finite-window chart).** For the first-wave coefficient on a single original bounded birth window one has the crude bound \(C_{\rm F}g_1^{-C}\tau^2 r^2\) after the physical \(r^{-2}\) dimension-six normalization, for the entire (4.122) and its fixed-order projected partners. Here is the scale count including normalization. In old units this graph has a fixed number of source roots, pivot fields, genuine prefixes, boundary links and anchors. Its input tangent has norm at most \(C_{\rm F}\tau r^2\) including every pinned value; the true near input differs by \(O(\tau r^P)\) and its relevant base is flat. Scalar invariant coefficients have zero first derivative at flat input by constant color rotations. Thus the second input variation does not enter their Hessian on the curve. On this fixed weak first-step graph there is a local polydisc of inverse-polynomial radius in \(g_1\), including a fixed smaller source-wall disc about one: the flat augmented Hessian with its redundant gauge rows removed has a bounded inverse at fixed L and box; all averaging denominators, pivots and compact chart Jacobians retain a fixed margin. The independent starred cutoff is at most a fixed logarithmic power in canonical units. On the real reference integral the exponent and all its finite-coordinate variations on that compact restricted domain cost at most a fixed power of \(g_1^{-1}\). Shrinking the input radius by one additional fixed inverse power makes the *variation of the full exponent* less than \(1/4\) uniformly at each B. The normalized complex denominator is then bounded below by \((2-e^{1/4})\) times its actual positive real mass; the absolute source-integral numerator uses that same mass. External characteristic functions remain outside the analytic coefficient. This argument retains fixed constants of the old chart under the bounded true source-wall maps; a physical absolute lower bound for an unrelated mass is not used. Cauchy on this genuine fixed-dimensional polydisc costs only a finite power of \(g_1^{-1}\), also for a fixed finite set of moment nodes and contact derivatives. The two input tangents contribute \(\tau^2 r^4\) and there are only \(O_{\rm F}(1)\) old roots and anchor offsets. Multiplication by \(r^{-2}\) gives the claimed bound. This beats the separate scalar saved power \(r^s\), \(0<s<2\), on (4.113). It is a bound on the tested Taylor coefficient on one full window, not on a current-size union of many different birth windows.

For the small-record p.377 proper difference the conclusion is stronger. In each complete short window on the chosen harmonic plateau, the reconstructed small-Y and the prescribed exterior completion have equal flat base and equal true tangent including the pinned exterior and all first rows up to \(O(\tau r^P)\) in old units after a common gauge. The stored marked function and its exact source walls, old projectors, measures and normalizing denominators are the *same function* of these two arguments in the additive p.377 completion. Apply its genuine finite-chart Hessian to the two inputs and subtract. The bilinear difference is bounded by \(C_{\rm F}g_1^{-C}\tau^2 r^{P+2}\), uniformly in the smaller original wall chart, including (4.122) and actual lower-coefficient variations. This follows from the polarization identity before dividing the compared numerators or taking absolute values; their densities need not coincide away from the base. The bound beats even the \(r^{-3}\) sheet count and \(r^{-2}\) normalization for any fixed required power when P has the stated original buffer margin. This gives actual proper cancellation on the matched short pinned windows with both repairs, instead of an assumption about their stand-alone suprema. On a large record with flat wave data the proper difference still contains the *negative exterior partner* with its own (4.122). Nearby distinct source walls, the normalized nonzero-B part of that large-record exterior term, the far rows and a common old graph with enough simultaneous anchors to realize the entire sheet remain to be analyzed. The separate fine-variable divergence of (4.108) does not settle those terms.

### 4.61 Fixed source-wall coefficients and the original number of roots

**Corollary 4.73 (P, original bounded first-birth near-windows).** On the rough test of §§4.59–4.60, sum the \(z^2\) coefficient over all current test roots of the original bounded first-birth near-windows with a fixed diameter ceiling in their own units. Fix one or more specified *old* wall cells and take a nonconstant Taylor monomial in those cells. Under the fixed finite-chart and complete window margins of Proposition4.72 the resulting normalized coefficient is bounded by \(C_m g_1^{-C_m}\tau^2 r^2\), with constants independent of the number of old sheet roots and of the periodic volume. This includes the finite physical lower projection and its coefficient derivatives on the same bounded filing, and the normalizer in (4.122). It also holds for a later linear p.377 proper difference of two such matched evaluations, with the stronger cancellation there.

**Proof.** A source-wall parameter of this fixed first-birth computation is indexed by its original old cell. A near term depends only on the original \(\square_0\), its complete finite collars and the source/inverse boxes on that old chart. Thus if the monomial contains the cell Δ, only windows meeting Δ with their collars can contribute. In a fixed old grid the count of these windows and of the marked roots inside them is bounded by a constant depending on the fixed diameter ceiling and offsets. This count also describes an actual single common original window: it contains only that bounded number of roots. All repeated derivatives in the specified finite monomial cost a constant on the smaller source-wall disc, or a fixed power of the weak old chart radius. The field, gauge and score derivatives on this graph obey the finite-coordinate bounds in §4.60, and all originally retained absolute anchor sums over this bounded window are finite with the same constants. Sum only these windows. In particular repeating one waveform along a macroscopic returning face does not repeat a *single specified old wall coordinate* at every old site. A current-scale preparation wall with much larger readset is a different variable.

The unweakened value and mixed variations over an entire simultaneously active wall set are separate questions. For a set of m distinct active old cells the difference of bounded-window coefficients from their all-one values on this test costs at most \(C g_1^{-C}\tau^2 r^2 m\): only the bounded number of old windows per active cell can change, and their values, not just their derivatives at one, obey the same fixed-chart Cauchy bound. Thus sparse simultaneous families with \(m\le r^{-(2-s-\epsilon)}\), \(\epsilon>0\), preserve the claimed scalar order on this sector of (4.113); this is an estimate for the wall-dependent difference. If each actual changed window reads at most one eligible interior cell satisfying the margin of Proposition4.72, its harmonic principal is exactly unchanged even for an arbitrarily large number of such separated cells. In contrast a dense pair pattern can affect order \(r^{-3}\) old roots of the sheet. Neither (4.120) nor (4.122) has been proved zero for adjacent distinct walls there. A bound for finitely many Taylor labels does not prove the norm on the simultaneous polydisc. ∎

### 4.62 A normalized single-wall coefficient including every first fluctuation

**Proposition 4.74 (P/L, an actual interior nonpivot H star).** In the pinned near-window of §§4.59–4.60, fix the other first-step fluctuation and source localization parameters at their physical values. Take a literal first-step matching-frustration summand whose complete fine plaquette and orientation/test-shift readout lies inside a single level-one block and contains no selected pivot \(b_0(c)\). For the harmonic tangent and one eligible varying source wall of Proposition4.72, the normalized \(z^2\) Taylor coefficient of this literal summand is independent of that wall, including the effects of the full first-fluctuation integration and of its normalizing denominator. This conclusion is for the indicated terms on the common original chart. Source projection coefficients or coarse-scale representatives with different readsets are retained in their own rows.

**Proof.** CMP119 (1.13) writes the first fluctuation in the original links relative to the first critical field. The change of variables immediately after (1.14), printed p.249, alters only the distinguished bonds \(b_0(c)\). Solving the equations \(Q A'=0\) changes only those same pivot entries. Thus on the stated star the physical direct bare readout is exactly \(e^{ig_0 A_b} C_b(z,u)\) with original independent starred A and the near-window background \(C=P_1q_1(R_{1,1+u_\Delta}(W_z))\). Prescribed fixed entries if present in the star are held at their actual values. The Wilson/Jacobian and canonical Gaussian weights are unchanged by the *pure source* wall. The protected outer level-zero repair has its bare identity on all the plaquette stencils. No auxiliary field is inserted in place of an actual pivot.

At z=0, C is the original flat configuration for every u in the small analytic disc. By Proposition4.72 its first z derivative changes, as u varies, only by a gauge of the coarse data with its prescribed trace. This is a raw pure node gradient, rather than a generic Landau-to-axial frame variation. CMP102 (181), printed p.307, gives the precise covariance of the axial minimum on the coarse-gauge orbit: the fine gauge is constant on each original determining block. Its infinitesimal gradient at identity therefore vanishes on every link of the selected star. Consequently \(C_b(0,u)\) and \(\partial_z C_b(0,u)\) there agree with their values at u=0 as actual link variables, for all u under consideration. This also holds for the finite pointed offsets within the same star.

For each A the difference \(m(z,u,A)-m(z,0,A)\) and its first z derivative now vanish at zero. In the second derivative, the two equal first link derivatives remove the Hessian-pair term exactly; the only remaining expression is the derivative of the same local compact source in a deterministic second link tangent difference. The real flat unmarked measure and the original starred selectors are invariant under simultaneous constant color conjugation. Hence its expectation applied to any one external Lie-algebra tangent is zero: this linear functional is adjoint invariant on \(\mathfrak{su}(N)\), which has no invariant nonzero covector. Apply this to each such deterministic tangent. Both denominator-score terms multiplying the value and first derivative differences are zero, as is the remaining expectation. This proves exact independence of the normalized quadratic coefficient from u, pointwise on its original one-wall disc. The same reasoning applies to a fixed compact source or fixed action-contact representative whose entire direct starred stencil satisfies this support condition. It does not identify the coefficient of a fluctuating lower projection with that direct representative.

This subclass is nonempty for sufficiently large allowed odd L. Discard a fixed number of outer fine planes of a level-one block to put every shifted plaquette star strictly inside. The surviving number of candidate roots is of order \(L^4\). Each old block or an adjacent block has only the fixed number of pivots attached to its oriented coarse bonds; one pivot excludes only a fixed number of finite star centers. For L above this fixed count one may select such a star in a harmonic near-window plateau. At the actual joined input the tiny nonharmonic tangent error is controlled by the same finite-chart Hessian used in §4.60. None of these interior stars is asserted to exhaust the physical matching source on the fine sheet. A crossing starred link can acquire a nonzero block-gradient rotation, and a solved pivot reads a larger constraint stencil. ∎

### 4.63 The crossing-star coefficient as an actual relative-frame trace

**Proposition 4.75 (P, harmonic fixed pinned near-window with one eligible inner source wall).** Keep the physical first fluctuation coordinates and all the other windows of Proposition4.74. Now allow a marked compact first H star to cross retained blocks and to contain solved pivots. Use the complete first constraint solve in the near elementary slot. The entire wave-quadratic coefficient has the following finite-matrix test. Put \(t=u(1+u)\) and let θ be the coarse site generator of the raw pure gradient of \(-G f_\Delta\) from (4.119). Include all its true endpoint frames, with its exterior constants fixed. Let \(\Theta\) generate the action on the *independent* starred coordinates of the inverse of this block-constant gauge. If \(m(C,A)\) denotes the exact compact source at background C in these coordinates, \(C_z\) its unvaried near physical background and w the original unmarked weight, set \(m_0=m(C_0,A)\), \(m'_0=\partial_z m(C_z,A)|_0\), \(S=\partial_z\log w_z|_0\). Then the change in the normalized quadratic coefficient between the wall value u and zero is
\[
 [z^2](R(u)-R(0))
 =t\{E_0(\Theta m'_0)+\operatorname{Cov}_0(\Theta m_0,S)\}
       +\tfrac12 t^2 E_0(\Theta^2 m_0).                        \tag{4.123}
\]
In particular \([z^2u^4]R=E_0(\Theta^2m_0)/2\). All expectations are on the actual compact first conditional integral with the original starred cutoff. This is a scalar formula for a literal H contribution; the extraction of the stable completed member must be applied as described below.

**Proof.** At this first bare step the nonlinear pivot correction is the coordinate change solving the exact average constraint relative to C. The full solution for the pivot depends on C and the independent A. It is covariant under their simultaneous compact coarse gauge, since the original raw averages are covariant, the axial condition is conjugated within each first block and the local solution is unique. CMP119 p.249 states that this change is the identity on the other coordinates. Thus \(m(C^{\tilde g},A)=m(C,A^{\tilde g^{-1}})\), where \(A_b\) is conjugated by the block gauge at its tail in the Lie-algebra chart (1.13). A solved pivot is reconstructed, not transformed independently. The equality uses the invariant compact Wilson source and the same prescribed boundary transformation; in (4.119) θ is trivial on the prescribed trace up to its common boundary stabilizer. It need only be differentiated for real small parameters; then the original normalized Haar and starred sup selectors are admissible.

By Proposition4.72 and CMP102 (181), the actual reprojected source background and the gauge transform of \(C_z\) by \(g_y=\exp(z t\theta_y)\) have the same first z tangent for every u on the stated disc. Their second tangents may differ. The term involving their difference in the normalized z-Hessian is the unvaried expectation of a first derivative with respect to a deterministic external colored link. It is zero by global conjugation at flat data, also after the complete pivot solve. The coefficient is therefore exactly the coefficient with this trial gauge. Expand \(m(C_z,A^{\tilde g^{-1}})\) first in \(zt\). The term linear in \(zt\) contributes the first expectation and its unmarked **normalized** score. The term quadratic in \(zt\) contributes the last expectation. This proves (4.123). On the nonpivot interior stars the same result vanishes by Proposition4.74. In particular no independent gauge variation of the unmarked denominator has been silently discarded on crossing stars.

Here is the weak-coupling principal of the highest single-wall coefficient on this fixed window. Let P be the true flat linear map from independent starred fluctuation coordinates to all fine coordinates, including its pivot rows. Write \(C_*\) for their actual constrained quadratic Gaussian covariance, including the real gauge quadratic term, and \(K_*=P^\top K_H P\) for the compact H quadratic matrix in these coordinates, with its original pointed test and Wilson normalization. Let \(y(i)\) be the determining block containing the tail of the ith independent bond. With the chosen Lie-algebra inner product let \(c_{\rm ad}\|v\|^2=-\operatorname{Tr}_{\rm ad}(\operatorname{ad}_v^2)\). Then
\[
 [z^2u^4]R=-\frac{c_{\rm ad}}{2}\sum_{i,j}(K_*)_{ij}(C_*)_{ij}
       \|\theta_{y(i)}-\theta_{y(j)}\|^2+o(1)\|\theta\|_\infty^2 .          \tag{4.124}
\]
Indeed the covariance is color diagonal at the flat orbit. Rotating the starred variables by independent block adjoints in the vacuum quadratic form gives the trace of the adjoint product at y(i),y(j). Its second derivative at zero is the negative stated squared difference even if the generators do not commute. On this fixed finite original chart the compact action, constraint solution, determinants and source admit their Taylor expansions on \(\|A\|\le C p_0(g_0)\). The higher Gaussian action and compact-H terms are bounded by \(g_0\) times a fixed Gaussian polynomial (a crude fixed cutoff power suffices); the complement has Gaussian tail \(C\exp[-c p_0(g_0)^2]\). Rotation derivatives add only finite powers of A and the two factors of θ. The genuine compact normalized integral therefore gives the displayed error as \(g_0\to0\). This is a finite-chart comparison, not a Gaussian replacement for a large-field history.

Equations (4.123)–(4.124) retain a possible crossing response even though the deterministic classical repair cancels. The quartic coefficient in u removes both score terms of (4.123); it does not remove the quadratic test from subsequent lower projection. For a fixed lower operator and a varying projection coefficient, the latter is the projection of the whole Hessian function on the physical coarse graph, not just the value of (4.124) on this one wave. Apply the specified pointed and dimension-four projections there and retain the independently filed contacts before testing its residual. A positive or negative sign for the unprojected quadratic trace, or a covariance of arbitrary substitute matrices, would not by itself decide the original completed norm. ∎

### 4.64 A genuine return-scale pinned window has no wave in its retained rows

**Proposition 4.76 (P/L, the original near-window incidence).** Choose the current-size cube \(\square\) of a plateau root at the actual outer face in the Q₂ example, in the top layer \(n=e\). In the near case of LFRII (1.59) the original window is \(\square_0=\square^{\sim5}\) and \(X\subset\square^{\sim2}\). Choose the existing compact wave with its inward cutoff and transverse bump as in §§4.33,4.46, strictly inside this window, beyond its finest pinned band by at least one full first block and all its endpoint stars. This is possible on the original aligned geometry: the outer current wave fits in the one adjacent M cube and a fixed inward patch, while the window adds five layers. At birth depth one the finest band of the minimal \(B_1(\square_0)\) is an old-scale collar, by the actual inward recursion (2.13).

Every positive first-level target of the *outer argument on this named LM45 graph* at \(U_z\) equals identity, by the full first-period average of each transverse wave and its restored raw endpoint frame. On every finest-band identity row, exterior bond and pinned prefix the perturbation is identically absent, since its whole support lies strictly in the first-level interior. Thus \(q_{B_1(\square_0)} U_z=q_{B_1(\square_0)}1\) for all z on the true wave disc, not only to first order. The unique regular minimizing orbit is flat. Evaluation of any already stored coefficient at \(P_1(q_{B_1(\square_0)}U_z),J_{\rm phys}\) is independent of z for every earlier source wall on its defined chart. This includes its exact normalized old integral and all of its fixed projection/contact partners on that same graph, regardless of their dependence on the earlier source walls. The prescribed fine exterior of this window was checked, not replaced by a periodic input. This identifies any exterior term that uses this same stored graph. A raw independently extended p.377 member with another defining record is not identified with it by this argument.

The proper term in (1.59) has the form \(E_j(X,U_j(B_j(\square_0),{\mathsf v}_B))-E_j(X,U_j(B_j(\square_0),{\mathsf v}_0))\), with identical finest trace and \({\mathsf v}_{B=0}={\mathsf v}_0\) on every row. It vanishes identically at B=0 for any preceding source walls after the same old coefficient is inserted in both slots, even if its individual stored value is nonzero. The remaining deterministic \(E_j(X,U_k^0)\) in (1.59) is a different term and has not acquired the window by this subtraction. Moreover the localization domains \(Y_0\) obtained from the bracket include \(\square_0\), as printed on p.372. A term read on this window across the outer face includes the adjacent current cube; it cannot be assigned the smallest record \(Y_1=Z\) that omits that cube. On the corresponding enlarged Q₂ record the wave lies inside the added positive-target region, where the actual first-join data do not change, as in §4.46. Consequently this sort of original pinned window supplies no forbidden wave coefficient in (9.1). Examples retaining fine boundary values actually touched by the wave, such as §§4.59–4.63, have different complete determining rows. ∎

### 4.65 Dense zero-one preparation walls on a harmonic pinned window

**Proposition 4.77 (P, normalized compact first-source coefficient at the binary vertices).** On the pinned first-step window of Proposition4.71 allow an arbitrary set of eligible inner-source wall cells to take value zero, all remaining cells taking value one. For the complete first fluctuation mark on this window the unprojected wave-quadratic coefficient is exactly its all-one value, including for crossing and pivotal stars. The complete compact fluctuation kernel is used in this statement. Assume the AL20 last-box margin on the relevant seed vertices, as in Proposition4.72; for an interior window with the indicated one-step mesh ratios it is a fixed comparison of widths, independent of the number of closed cells.

**Proof.** Denote the closed cells by Z₀. At the flat orbit the linear source is \(-d_D^*(w_s da)\). Because the true harmonic trial has zero unweighted dynamic current, this vector equals the negative of the corresponding difference with unweighted plaquettes. Every component of the difference lies within one weak plaquette-carrier width of a member of Z₀. A nonzero last inverse or residual applied to that component reads it through the cutoff \(\chi_z\); its complete original box therefore intersects that closed cell. Every such AL22 walk has weight zero by AL23, irrespective of the states of other cells. Hence
\[
 \Pi_b\Gamma_s\iota_1 d_D^*(w_s da)=0,\qquad T_s a=a              \tag{4.125}
\]
as a raw tangent with the unchanged prescribed exterior. This identity is stronger than equality of the reprojected target. It holds simultaneously for adjacent closed cells and for a densely closed pattern. The original protected boundary rows and their weak variations have all been included.

At z=0 each repaired background on this finite graph is the identity, and (4.125) proves exact equality of its first tangent with the physical one, including its physical-current tangent. Form \(\Phi_0\) with the actual constrained fluctuation B, solve its prescribed pivots, and use the protected bare outer repair. For every B the two source functions and their first z derivatives coincide. In the second difference only the derivative of this same first-step function in deterministic second external field/current directions remains. At the flat base its expectation is an invariant linear functional of each colored external slot and is zero. The quotient derivative has no additional score: its value difference and first source derivative difference already vanish at each B, as shown explicitly in (4.122). This proves the assertion for each literal H term, its true pointed test offsets and any fixed gauge-invariant lower/contact representative that shares this graph and source prescription. The O(τr^P) error on the actual joined tangent is covered by the finite-chart comparison in §4.60. The equality of this *unprojected* Hessian on every harmonic tangent can also test its constant-curvature marginal directions using affine harmonic potentials with their induced true boundary data. Determining the complete projection on nonharmonic coarse directions and its separately filed EOM/contact coefficients requires more than this equality on harmonic directions.

For completeness this binary identity gives a bound for intermediate complex walls on the same short-window class. Let \(F\) be the set of genuinely fractional (neither zero nor one) old wall coordinates. On a smaller common original polydisc connect each such coordinate to a closest binary endpoint along a straight segment, leaving all other cells fixed. The holomorphic finite-window norm and the field Hessian estimate of §4.60 give, by one-variable Cauchy and telescoping, a total change of the unprojected normalized wave-quadratic test sum bounded by
\[
 C g_1^{-C}\tau^2 r^2
   \sum_{i\in F\ {\rm meeting\ tested\ windows}}
             \min(|s_i|,|1-s_i|),                              \tag{4.126}
\]
relative to the binary endpoint sum. Each specified cell reaches only the bounded number of old windows checked in Corollary4.73. The tiny finite-window error from replacing the joined tangent by its harmonic comparison is summed separately using P with that same original buffer. The number of exactly closed cells does not occur in (4.126). If the displayed weighted count is at most \(r^{-2+s+\epsilon}\), this part has the desired scalar order even when the closed cells themselves cover the whole sheet. There is no interpolation from the binary points to the unrestricted complex polydisc without this or another estimate: \(\Gamma_s\) and \(f_s\) have once-per-union weights individually, while their product in MC12 can contain a square of a given wall parameter. The relative-frame trace (4.123) is an explicit manifestation of this distinction. ∎

### 4.66 The exact two-cell bound at general complex source walls

**Proposition 4.78 (P, classical coarse target defect on the complete pinned one-step graph).** In the fixed harmonic setting with the last-box footprint margin, let \(s_i\) be *all* the original inner-source walls in their allowed polydisc. Write \(D(s)=Q(T_sa-a)\) for the reduced coarse target defect and \(\epsilon_i=|s_i(1-s_i)|\). There are constants c,C and a fixed old box width D such that for a coarse row y
\[
 |D(s)_y|\le C\sum_{i\ne j}\epsilon_i |1-s_j|\,
    {\bf1}_{d(y,j)\le D}\,e^{-c d(i,j)}
    \sup_{p:d(p,i)\le D}|(da)_p|.                            \tag{4.127}
\]
Distances here are in original first-window box units; the constants retain the fixed cell and inverse-box sizes. A cell set empty in the supremum contributes zero. The full raw target can also include the fine-to-coarse site frame. Equation (4.127) is the defect *after physical endpoint/gauge identification*, so it does not delete that frame from (4.123).

**Proof.** Use (4.121) on \(f_s-f_1\), since \(f_1=0\). The plaquette p contributes its actual incidence transpose at some dynamic bond b with scalar factor \(w_s(C_p)-1\); here \(C_p\) denotes its complete weak vertex carrier. If the residual tail \(T_\omega\) acts nontrivially on that component, its last operator reads b through its true input cutoff. Hence every wall meeting \(C_p\) also meets the complete tail union, by the same finite margin as in Proposition4.72. The empty-tail term has zero coarse row on a first-force input, so this assertion needs only nonempty tails. In the telescoping bracket in (4.121) factor out \(w_s(X_\omega)\). The remaining multiplier is the product over walls of the head box absent from the tail, minus one. Expand each of the two differences of products by an ordered telescoping sum. Any terminal term selects a wall i of \(C_p\) and contains \(s_i-1\); its factor \(s_i\) is already present in \(w_s(X_\omega)\). Every head term instead selects j absent from that tail and contains \(s_j-1\). The two selected walls are necessarily distinct. No reciprocal wall parameter has been used, so zeros are legitimate throughout. The coarse row of \(I_z\) is supported in the head box meeting j. Each tail with nonzero matrix product connects this box to the seed carrier near i. The original Neumann majorant AL22–24, with a part of its length exponent retained, bounds the sum of these walks by \(Ce^{-c d(i,j)}\). The additional ordered products only use the fixed numbers of wall cells in a single head or seed; they cost a uniform constant on the already chosen polydisc. The number of local plaquettes in a fixed first-scale seed neighborhood is bounded in this one-step geometry. This proves the estimate, including the pinned Dirichlet stars in \(d_D^*\). ∎

For the actual harmonic sheet, \(|da|\le C r^2\tau\) at its maximal layer, with inward exponential decay; the finitely many exterior harmonic layers have fixed constants. Applying the actual finite-window minimizing lift to (4.127) gives a classical quadratic response on the bounded-window family with normalized absolute difference at most
\[
 Cg_1^{-2}\tau^2r^2\sum_{i\ne j}
           \epsilon_i|1-s_j|e^{-c d(i,j)}                       \tag{4.128}
\]
where pairs can be restricted to the tested window neighborhoods and their old incidence weights. Indeed each physical rooted quadratic term is bilinear in the unperturbed tangent and its lifted defect, plus a defect square. The defect has uniform supremum \(C r^2\tau\) by (4.127) and four-dimensional summability, and the output anchors of the lift have bounded row and column sums on these bounded first graphs. Multiply by the dimension-six \(r^{-2}\) and sum the actual bounded number of roots per pair neighborhood. If desired the normal profile can be retained in the terminal supremum of (4.127). Equation (4.128) controls the classical gauge-invariant near-window part with its fixed compact projected representatives. It gives the desired scalar order on complex configurations where the accumulated pair weight, including its support, is small enough; a densely repeated adjacent pattern may have order \(r^{-3}\) such pairs.
