The original single-wall quantum frame term remains (4.123). None of these estimates establishes an absolute norm for arbitrary simultaneously weighted marked coefficients.

### 4.67 Dense periodic walls on one common near-window at every intermediate size

**Proposition 4.79 (P, signed or absolute tested quadratic coefficient on the original common near-window).** Use the Q₂ wave and admissible native spacing margins of §§4.46,4.55, at birth j=1. Choose a genuinely periodic array of neighboring intermediate wall values on the homogeneous old wall grid. For example give row indices congruent to zero modulo L in the tangential second direction value 1/3 and all others value 2/3. Restore to one every original protected wall, with its own required enlarged stencil. With the original compatible periodic tiling, an L-cell period fits on the fixed torus for all sufficiently early cutoff levels of (4.113); alternatively use the actual interior restriction of that prescribed array on a finite window. The field phase has its original complete raw L-period. No independent block gauge at different root translations is inserted.

Take one of the actual near determining windows \(F=B_1(\square_0)\), including all its prescribed fine exterior and first-layer collar, from (3.18)–(3.24) or (1.59), at any intermediate localization scale n between 1 and e. Write m for the side of the original M-cube at scale n in *birth-one units*. Then \(1\lesssim m\lesssim r^{-1}\), where fixed M factors are retained. Restrict to the original near case, \(X\subset\square^{\sim2},\ \square_0=\square^{\sim5}\). All tested roots have the same F. Test the stored first scalar and its finite bounded physical lower/pointed/contact projections at \(W_F=P_Fq_F\widetilde\Gamma_Y\) and its physical current, with old original analytic amplitude \(A_1\) and the actual old absolute carrier weight. On the strict tangential plateau from §4.46 the tested z² coefficient, after the dimension-six normalization, satisfies
\[
 \sum_{X,\lambda\ {\rm on\ this\ fixed\ near\ filing}}
    r^{-2}\big|[z^2]R_{1,X,\lambda}(W_F,J_{\rm phys}(W_F);s)\big|
 \le C A_1 g_1^{-C}\tau^2 r^2,                               \tag{4.129}
\]
with bounded smooth test and its fixed shifts included here. The sum is over the original carriers within this single window, with their actual source anchors λ and the original absolute norm of their stored scalar/Hessian on their admitting birth chart; one can also take an ordered signed sum of a completed row before the modulus. This is a tested coefficient result, not a bound on arbitrary analytic-norm rows. It uses the old chart where its stored coefficient exists, not a marked transport estimate on a changed mixed domain. The proof works for every assignment of walls on the stored smaller common polydisc, hence for the indicated dense pattern.

**Screening on F.** By the minimal inward recursion CMP119 (2.13), the bare pinned strip of a one-step window has width at most a fixed number of first spacings. Hence every root in \(\square^{\sim2}\) has distance at least \(c m-C_L\) in first units to the *fine* pinned strip of F. In particular a root within a fixed number of fine layers of the true returning face is not thereby within that distance of the old window's fine boundary. If m is bounded use the finite-window estimate of §4.60. Otherwise put \(t=\min(c_0m,D(1+e))\), with \(c_0\) small enough that every t-ball in a short enlargement of the tested region has only true first-average rows of F. The outer input \(U_z\) has exactly trivial first means. For the small-Y input, in this ball its first-mean tangent differs from a pure gradient in coarse curl by \(C\tau r^P\) for any prescribed P with the original comparison margins, by §4.46 and the true restored endpoint words. This comparison is uniform on a tangential plateau strictly inside the envelope. The ball width in entry units is \(t r\le D(1+e)r\). The fields in every near cube of side \(O(1)\) in entry units are inside the annulus of §4.40, away from the actual inner rough component by order \(M R_e\); the annulus comparison and (4.109) bound their fine-angle tangents on the whole required region by \(C r^2\tau\) in a common representative. The prescribed exterior continuation has the same bound. Choose the original Q₂ buffer margin to absorb its exponentially small cut error. This last global *supremum* matters for an intermediate m and cannot be replaced by an arbitrarily large inverse power of r.

At each short tested region remove the coarse node gradient by the radial tree on the t-ball, extended by zero beyond it. The innermost half-ball then has coarse link norm \(C t\tau r^P\) by the strip identity, while all remote data are bounded by \(C(1+t)r^2\tau\). The transformation can be fixed on the prescribed fine exterior outside this ball. Applying the actual flat one-step constrained response (CMP102 (190), or its zero-target Dirichlet augmented inverse) gives for the invariant local link tangent in a common representative and its first current tangent on the old tested star the bound
\[
 C(1+t)^A\tau\bigl(r^P+r^2 e^{-c t}\bigr).                  \tag{4.130}
\]
The local gauge is chosen for one root with all its read slots transformed together, and then the physical scalar is evaluated; pure tangent gradients at the flat orbit drop from its Hessian by gauge invariance. The near/remote split in (4.130) uses a bounded row-sum exponential on a one-step hierarchy, whose two possible mesh sizes have fixed ratio L. Finite old current differences cost a fixed L factor. For the literal exterior \(U_z\) only the second term is needed. On the large Y covering the wave, its joined target is unchanged on the perturbation, as in §4.46. Thus the bound is also valid for the original corresponding proper difference when both arguments are taken on their own identified record. The exact (1.59) zero-B difference remains zero.

**Old grades and counting.** A first-source carrier with all its native read slots and required fixed enlargements inside the half-ball is evaluated on its own old chart. Cauchy there at flat input, where all first color derivatives vanish, bounds its quadratic coefficient by its original stored amplitude times the square of (4.130), with its original exponential carrier weight. Fixed lower projections, their coefficient derivatives, pointed test offsets, current stencils and the normalization of the already integrated old first mark are evaluated as part of that stored scalar. The cubic and path-area physical terms have no direct ordinary z² jet at the flat graph; their previously formed lower descendants are retained in this bound. This Cauchy step is valid for the actual stored functions on the original strictly smaller disc; it does not integrate a new Wilson law on F in place of a coefficient formed elsewhere. Equivalently on the first homogeneous chart the old compact conditional derivative, its normalizer and absolute first-carrier weights are those of §§4.36–4.41. For a long carrier requiring old slots farther than t/2 from its root, keep half of its original exponential support norm to gain \(e^{-c t}\); the crude entire-old-window amplitude there is at most a fixed polynomial in \(1+t\) times \(r^2\tau\) after the same local gauge. This gives the squared estimate with a reduced c. A crude absolute two-anchor conversion on a bounded old carrier costs at most a fixed power of its size; its original exponential moment absorbs it. This argument applies to stored lower representatives with that same genuine old chart or to their own separately identified rows with this amplitude. No norm for an independently unadmitted tail is inferred.

There are at most \(C m^4\) old root homes in the single original \(\square^{\sim2}\); the old root and source anchor weights are those before this sum. Multiply by \(r^{-2}\). If \(t=c_0 m\), every fixed polynomial of m from counting and from (4.130) is bounded against \(e^{-c m}\). The short term with \(r^P\) is then negligible since \(m\lesssim e\). If \(t=D(1+e)\), we have \(m^4\lesssim r^{-4}\); choose D and P with the original spacing margins so that the exponential or \(r^P\) pays this number and the normalization. This proves (4.129). It makes no assumption that a signed per-period cancellation survives an absolute anchor norm. For a translation average over a fixed common F, the dangerous coefficient normalized by \(g_1^{-2}\tau^2/r\) tends to zero on (4.113). The estimate remains true if absolute root coefficients are taken after their original pointed/lower filing. It is screening of the actual recorded fine pins by the old first reconstruction, not a symmetry of two adjacent fractional cells. ∎

### 4.68 What the period sum measures for independently translated old windows

**Proposition 4.80 (P, phase and old-volume factors).** Use a bounded class of original near-windows of one fixed birth-scale size, translated on the unmodified old lattice along the true flat face in §4.46. For the dense two-value pattern in §4.67 take the entire old periodic list of phases: source root, period of the wall tiling, original pivot orbit, pointed offsets and the transverse harmonic wave. The chosen protected collar is translated with its marked root; this does not replace the coarse test by a sharp slice. Restrict the test to a strict tangential plateau, far from actual corners. There is no symmetry averaging of the whole nonsymmetric history. The joined input tangents on its bounded windows agree to arbitrary prescribed r-power with the periodic harmonic/prescribed inputs used here. For this statement retain each window's originally defined action and marked function. If a source is already integrated, its period coefficients are computed by differentiating its stored function, not by imposing a new independent finite-volume Wilson kernel.

Let \(b_{n,h}\) be the true signed coefficient on bounded window/phase h, at normal fine layer n, for *unit* old test amplitude, after the actual MT projection and any proper subtraction in the particular row being tested. The normal index retains the offset of its pointed fine test; one can keep the finitely many distinct offset species separate. It includes relative-frame terms and the derivatives of the normalized joint old kernel. Let \(\bar b_n\) be the mean over the original tangential period, with the original root multiplicity and units. For smooth compact test f, summing the translated rows gives
\[
 \tau^2\left[r^{-1}B_0 \int f(0,y)\,dy
        + B_1\int \partial_1 f(0,y)\,dy+O(r C_g)\right],
 \quad B_0=\sum_n\bar b_n,\quad B_1=\sum_n(n+\delta_n)\bar b_n . \tag{4.131}
\]
The fixed L conversion between fine and old units can equivalently be absorbed into n, the offsets and the phase weights. For several offsets the moment in \(B_1\) is taken before summing their species. Here \(C_g\) can cost a fixed inverse power of \(g_1\). Equation (4.131) concerns a signed scalar on the declared different-window filing. In an absolute source-anchor row, first perform all cancellations that define that same anchored coefficient and then take its modulus; the corresponding zeroth periodic mean uses the moduli of those completed anchor coefficients. One cannot replace that mean by the modulus of a signed average over different anchors or different operations.

**Proof.** Write the sum first over congruence classes modulo the fixed tangential period. The tangential Riemann sum of each fixed translate of a smooth test is its tangential integral times the density of that class, with error of arbitrarily high order in r by Fourier summation (or repeated differences on its compactly supported smooth extension). The inward harmonic wave gives exponential decay in n after a fixed old halo on every bounded window; the uniform finite-chart costs in the unit-amplitude coefficient are at most inverse-polynomial in g. The actual joined-tangent comparison error, including the r⁻² engineering and old-root count, is negligible on the chosen margins. Two old link tangents contribute \(\tau^2r^4\), the dimension-six normalization is \(r^{-2}\), and the tangential cells contribute \(r^{-3}\). Taylor-expand the remaining normal dependence of f to order one and sum the second-order remainder using its finite second n-moment. Offsets tangential to the face integrate to zero at first order for this smooth compact test. This proves the formula. Thus any actual nonzero \(B_0\) of inverse-polynomial size in the coupling would violate that *different-window* positive-age row on the subsequence (4.113). Even a vanishing signed \(B_0\) would require the normal moment or its signed contact partner at the next order. The present proof does not assume either coefficient is nonzero.

**Which coefficients cancel.** For a literally matched bounded small-Y proper term the stored first mark and its p.377 subtractand have the same complete old data to the accuracy of §4.60. Their \(\bar b_n\)'s therefore cancel on the plateau for every periodic pattern, including the first two normal moments, before an absolute norm is taken on that proper row. For a large-record proper difference, the positive old term sees no wave, so its period coefficient is minus that of its own exterior partner. On a fixed graph where this exterior partner is reinstated with the same coefficient their signed sum is zero. This algebra does not identify the large and small windows, or replace a later normalized label probability by one. For a newly formed near proper fluctuation \(F_s(B)-F_s(0)\) (XP19/RG23), the entire order \(g_1^{-2}\) classical value in \(B_0\) cancels at formation even on an independently translated old graph: the two terms use exactly the same old pinning and source walls at zero fluctuation. The deterministic tag outside that difference has the distinct common determining record, treated in §4.67. A lower projection is a fixed linear operation on the complete kernel; its coefficient variations belong to this same difference, not to a frozen unprojected classical square.

For clarity the first quantum test on one such physical first fluctuation is also explicit. Section4.74 specifies the actual integration graph for the globally clean Q₂ birth: in its stored output these traces are first taken on the original whole conditional kernel and then filed by (4.140), not independently normalized on each readout window. In the genuine finite old chart write its density after extracting its background value as a centered normalized Gaussian expectation with precision \(K(q)\), including pivot and gauge terms, times \(1+g_0 V_1(q,B)+O(g_0^2(1+|B|)^C)\) on Gaussian integrable contours; here \(V_1\) is the log-Jacobian's linear and the action's cubic first correction with their original signs. The mean output from the proper difference at leading order is
\[
 {\tfrac12}\operatorname{Tr}\{K(q)^{-1}D_B^2 H_s(q,0)\}
 +\operatorname{Cov}_{K(q)}(D_BH_s(q,0)[B],V_1(q,B)).       \tag{4.132}
\]
The derivatives are in a physical fluctuation before the rescaling by g; the original Wilson normalization of H multiplies the formula. Expand both the numerator and its own denominator, using the oddness of the first Gaussian moment. On the fixed starred polylog cutoff the Taylor error is o(1) also after two genuine old field derivatives on a slightly smaller physical chart; the removed Gaussian tail is exponentially small in the squared cutoff. This verifies (4.132) at the physical compact saddle and includes the proper mean score. Its z² coefficient is to be projected on the actual coarse field germs before evaluating the wave; e.g. (4.123)–(4.124) isolate one of its relative-frame terms. There is no general implication from the first (classical) cancellation that the projected \(B_0\) of (4.132) vanishes. A subtraction of \(E(0;b_z)\) as a newly chosen vacuum term for each changed fine exterior would modify the stored projector: MT22 scales the exterior too and the old identity value is at the original flat record.

### 4.69 Distance to a genuine predecessor pin at a clean first touch

**Lemma 4.81 (P/L, a geometric restriction on the common determining graph).** Let a clean old carrier first meet the large region at e. Suppose a genuine old pinned set S is still in the predecessor \(Z_{e-2}\), and the immediately following transformation is the ordinary CMP119 step with no healing of its corresponding enlarged component. Then for old support points remaining outside \(Z_{e-1}\), their distance to S in birth-j units is at least
\[
 c M R_{e-2} L^{e-2-j}-C_{\rm stencil}.                    \tag{4.133}
\]
Here the last fixed allowance is for the actual complete anchored star in birth units; for a long carrier one must use its true diameter before making the deduction. To see this, CMP119 pp.265–266, before (3.3) and in (3.5), excludes the union containing the four-layer enlargement of \(Z_{e-2}\) from the next small-field region. It also takes the cover by the actual next cubes and an extra layer; (3.20) retains this inherited exclusion in \(Z_{e-1}\). A layer of the predecessor partition has physical side \(M R_{e-2}\ell_{e-2}\). Thus a fixed fraction of even one original layer already separates S from the bare support outside the enlarged region. Rescale to birth units and deduct the actual star allowance. A merger adding more occupied cubes does not decrease this distance; a healing removing them needs its own argument.

This excludes a different proposed way of repeating a nonzero fixed-window period moment on a *single* old graph: a genuinely retained fine half-space a few old spacings from every source root cannot have the displayed ordinary unhealed ancestry and simultaneously avoid touching the source before a much later e. In the Q₂ example the old first global graph is completely clean and has no such genuine fine boundary in the first place. For an artificial old near-window the relevant distance is instead the verified \(c m-C\) buffer in §4.67. A healed and refiled earlier coefficient or a newly joined exterior row is not assigned the continuous ancestry of (4.133).

### 4.70 The full complex Q₂ curve on a common window, including the nonlinear remainder

**Proposition 4.82 (P/L, same history and record margins as §§4.40,4.46,4.67).** On the original smaller complex \(\zeta\)-disc of the Q₂ outer wave, the common-window estimate (4.129) holds also for the field-dependent scalar difference itself, uniformly in the dense old wall pattern. The common physical old readout and its current have a strict ordinary birth-chart margin. The statement covers the stored scalar with its declared bounded physical old radius and canonical normalization; it does not prove every differentiated row.

**Proof.** Use the annular reference of Lemma4.50, with *identity* data on the inner artificial cut, true outer fine targets \(U_\zeta\), and identity targets at every positive level. Its only nonzero target logarithms are on the finest entries and their prescribed exterior. They are analytic old compact words of a commuting field, of size at most \(C r^2\tau |\zeta|\); the complete first and higher averages have their exact null value after the original endpoint frames are restored. Thus in the **reference variational problem alone** one can use the flat reference in CMP102 (7),(172) and take a disc \(|\zeta|<c_0/(r^2\tau)\) with c₀ fixed small. If an exterior star is encoded directly as a fixed fine boundary link it satisfies the same native bound, on its complete star. This fits the original small target ball, the fixed native layer-spacing/count assumption, and the reference extended group logarithm branches. CMP102 (173)–(180),(190) then give a holomorphic relative saddle with uniformly bounded fine link angles there. One may equivalently use the weighted contraction of §4.14 without any extra scale weight about its identity reference. This auxiliary extension does not change the original simultaneous domain of the actual R integral.

In this reference the data lie in a fixed commuting subgroup; uniqueness and simultaneous constant conjugation allow a Cartan representative. On its small native branch each first-coarse plaquette or its holomorphic flux is a bounded finite-support function of this saddle, with first-step length ratio L fixed. Its gauge-insensitive linearization is the curl of the true first raw average. Cauchy from the just established reference disc bounds the terms of order at least two by \(C r^4\tau^2\) throughout the original bounded \(\zeta\)-disc. If needed take the local plaquette flux in a common block frame; in the Cartan representative conjugation by its site phase does not alter it. By §§4.46,4.67 the reference linearization in every t-ball on the plateau differs from that of the null-average harmonic field by \(C\tau r^P\). The matched comparison (4.83) on the *actual* initial disc adds at most a fixed inverse mesh/coupling power times \(e^{-cMR_e}\); choose the original allowed buffer to bound this by \(C\tau r^P\). This proves the bound \(C(\tau r^P+\tau^2 r^4)\) for the actual coarse flux on these balls, while (4.109) supplies the global old-angle bound \(C r^2\tau\) in the exterior near cubes required here. The actual chosen zero-fluctuation compact nodal Q₂ core can be put in the common Cartan frame; no continuation of its original selectors onto the enlarged reference disc was made.

Remove the coarse gradient in each t-ball by the exact tree gauge. In the commuting logarithm chart this is the strip sum of the coarse flux. A cutoff to identity outside the ball costs a fixed polynomial in t times \(r^2\tau\). Along the real or complex radial segment from identity to these actual small first target logarithms the original one-step inverse/density bound (190) is uniform. Integrating it gives in the relevant old inner neighborhoods, for the physical old field in a common representative and its Wilson current,
\[
 C(1+t)^A\left[\tau r^P+\tau^2r^4+\tau r^2 e^{-ct}\right].       \tag{4.134}
\]
The bounds on the complete F without the improved local cancellation are \(C r^2\tau\) in old units in an allowed small gauge, since all original target/exterior data on this wave are inside that uniform one-step ball. Only the local tree taper used for (4.134) adds its t-polynomial. Finite Wilson curls and currents cost a fixed power of L on this first chart. This is also a direct spare physical radius test for the pair on F, for g weak and the stated clean birth radius. It does not allow a gauge-varying derivative to ignore an inherited raw frame.

Each gauge-invariant scalar source row has zero first color jet at identity. On its actual smaller unvaried old physical disc, radial Cauchy in the old link/current slots bounds its difference from the flat value by its existing old amplitude times the square of (4.134); long carriers receive the original exponential support estimate as in §4.67. This includes the old normalization and the fixed finite lower projections on their stated charts. With \(m\lesssim r^{-1}\) there are at most \(C r^{-4}\) old roots in F. The new nonlinear term in the squared estimate therefore costs at most \(C g_1^{-C}(1+e)^C\tau^4 r^2\) after the \(r^{-2}\) dimension-six conversion. The linear screened and small comparison terms have the bound of (4.129). Since \(e=2+R_2\asymp (\log x_2)^\nu\), the displayed age/coupling comparisons are uniform on (4.113). Thus the actual scalar difference throughout this full outer disc is at most \(C A_1 g_1^{-C}\tau^2 r^2\), also after the indicated absolute anchor sum on the one record. In particular it is below the saved clean positive power \(r^s\), \(0<s<2\), on the trajectory in question. This proves the assertion without turning any bound on a phase average into an absolute coefficient estimate. ∎

### 4.71 Independent walls on two primitive labels: the positive frame term

**Proposition 4.83 (P, pre-contact connected two-source coefficient on the same actual kernel).** In (4.123) take two independent copies of the compact H source with independent wall parameters \(u_1,u_2\), sharing the same actual fluctuation and the same harmonic input. Differentiate the normalized log first in the two physical source strengths. Since the old source exponential is linear in each primitive and the unmarked physical density is independent of \(u_i\), this coefficient is \(\operatorname{Cov}_z(m_1(z,u_1,A),m_2(z,u_2,A))\). Taking one wall derivative of each and the first possible wave coefficient gives
\[
 [z^2]\partial_{u_1}\partial_{u_2}
     \operatorname{Cov}_z(m_1,m_2)\big|_{u_i=0}
    =\operatorname{Cov}_0(\Theta_1 m_{1,0},\Theta_2 m_{2,0}).  \tag{4.135}
\]
Indeed each marked wall derivative vanishes pointwise at z=0 and has first z derivative \(\Theta_i m_{i,0}\) by (4.123)'s actual frame covariance. Therefore all derivatives of the varying normalizer or unmarked weight at this order multiply a vanishing value; their subtraction at z=0 is the actual covariance on the right. No term proportional to \(\Theta_i^2m_i\) occurs when the two wall coordinates belong to different labels. For equal marks, same single-cell direction and real harmonic input this quantity is a true nonnegative conditional variance. Subtracting a deterministic one-source coefficient in either argument does not affect it.

With \(D_\theta\) the block diagonal skew matrix of adjoint rotations on the true independent starred coordinates, and the real positive \(C_*\) and symmetric \(K_*\) of (4.124), its fixed-window Gaussian principal is
\[
 2\,\big\|C_*^{1/2}[K_*\otimes 1,D_\theta]C_*^{1/2}\big\|_{\rm HS}^2 , \tag{4.136}
\]
where the covariance includes its identity in color. This follows by differentiating the vacuum quadratic under starred rotations and using the exact centered Gaussian fourth moment, with the same compact finite-chart error as in (4.124). For a crossing pair of independent indices i,j, its matrix block is \((K_*)_{ij}\operatorname{ad}_{\theta_j-\theta_i}\). The quantity is positive if and only if some nonzero coupling of the actual H matrix connects distinct induced generators; the strictly positive covariance and simplicity of \(\mathfrak{su}(N)\) give both directions of this criterion. The H matrix can contain such cross-block entries: choose a nonpivot matching star across a coarse face normal to ν, transverse bond direction µ, and take its two parallel µ-links at offsets \(\pm\hat\nu\). In the second-difference matching Hessian both enter the same ν force; its squared coefficient has weight \(1-1/3\). When the *entire* star avoids all pivots this survives restriction to the starred variables. For sufficiently large L one can choose stars on a coarse interface away from the bounded number of pivot stencils. The nonconstant generator still has to be induced by the actual \(\rho G f_\Delta\) field on this window; an arbitrary rotation cannot be substituted.

This exposes a second, positive diagnostic on the dense pattern. It does not transfer a positive-age first-scalar coefficient to a second-source contact: the separately born joint covariance and any prescribed two-label counterterm have their own grade and source degrees. If using many translated old windows, the denominator and the contact projections must still be checked on each actual state in the indicated order. In particular (4.135) is not a lower bound for the stable scalar row of (9.1).

### 4.72 A modulated face without a constant tangential plateau

**Proposition 4.84 (P/L, scalar on the actual Q₂ common near-windows).** The previous nonlinear result extends to the transverse transition of the Q₂ probe, where the old boundary wave has a slowly varying envelope. Use the original commuting first-period cosine and the block-constant tangential samples of a fixed Lipschitz envelope with finite support as in §4.46. An old cell sees a change of envelope at most \(C r\). Retain the fixed number of exterior harmonic layers across the face, so no free Wilson plaquette in the annular problem meets the abrupt exterior normal cutoff. The inward cutoff at fixed entry distance is exponentially small. For any common near-window in §4.67, and any bounded test supported also on that transition, the absolute tested scalar difference throughout the original complex curve and dense source-wall family is at most
\[
 C A_1g_1^{-C}\tau^2 r .                                    \tag{4.137}
\]
This keeps the usual first stable exponent \(0<s<1\) on (4.113). It avoids the constant-envelope approximation on an expanding old neighborhood.

**Proof.** Write n for the fine-layer distance inward from the chosen outer face, in units of the fine lattice spacing η; first steps and fine steps differ by fixed L. The proposed field a has the exact null first mean because each transverse complete first block has one cosine period with the same sampled envelope. On any dynamic fine link of the reference box the old *dimensionless* Maxwell defect obeys
\[
 |d^* d a|\le C r^3\tau e^{-\lambda n}+O(r^P\tau).          \tag{4.138}
\]
Indeed the constant-envelope differences cancel by the harmonic dispersion relation. Every uncancelled term carries at least one shifted envelope difference, bounded by Cr, times the actual old angle of order \(r^2\tau\); the harmless fixed shifts change the exponential by constants. This estimate includes the other vector components of the Maxwell current from a varying transverse cutoff. The original starred fine exterior sites contribute only through their actual incident dynamic stencils. The known block-gradient restoring the raw means has zero curl.

We give the uniform mesh conversion that is needed at this transition. The physical force entering the flat zero-target inverse is \(\eta^{-3} d^* d a\). The first/other targets of a agree with those of the annular reference, except for its exponentially tiny inner-cut values. At a native cell z with nearest fine layer \(n_z\), minimal retreat of the original domains gives \(\sigma_z/\eta\le C(1+n_z)\); the same bound with a fixed enlargement holds on complete native stencils. Hence its actual native force \(\sigma_z^3 \|J\|_{R_z}\) is at most \(C r^3\tau(1+n_z)^3 e^{-c n_z}\), plus an arbitrarily small cut term. This uses the original weighted zero-target Green bound of §4.14, namely that the native potential and first difference at y are bounded by the actual kernel sum of these normalized forces with exponential decay in the native path metric. On the flat admissible record the zero-target and gauge projection use the same finite products and native density sums in CMP99 (3.132)–(3.138),(3.148)–(3.153); no unnormalized count of all fine bonds is inserted. Converting back to a fine link angle multiplies by \(\eta/\sigma_y\le1\).

The resulting fine-angle error at layer n is bounded by \(C r^3\tau (1+n)^{-p}\), for a fixed p>1 on the same sufficiently spaced geometry. To verify the last spatial weight, sources with \(n_z\ge\sqrt n\) already have the required decay. A path from the other cells to y rises from native index \(h_z\le C+\log_L(1+\sqrt n)\) to its largest index k and pays at least the larger of \(c nL^{-k}\) and \(c S_0(k-h_z-2)_+\). If \(k<\tfrac34\log_L n\) use the first bound, and otherwise use the second. A portion of the exponential from the genuine native inverse therefore supplies the power, and the remaining portion sums by the checked native density estimate. The scope is the bounded face patch away from the other returning corners. Error terms from the inner cut and from the actual versus reference joined graph are handled as in §§4.46,4.70 with the original spacing margins.

The linear coarse wave from the reference therefore has gauge-invariant first-mean curls \(O(r^3\tau (1+n)^{-p}+\tau r^P)\) throughout the whole sampled envelope. The nonlinear part is at most \(C\tau^2 r^4\) by the enlarged *reference* Cauchy disc of §4.70. Apply the old first-scale reconstruction on the common F. Its near/remote split, tree gauge and original physical current estimates now give the version of (4.134) with the additional term \(C(1+t)^A r^3\tau (1+n)^{-p}\). For the local t-neighborhood use \((1+n')^{-p}\le(1+t)^p(1+n)^{-p}\), so no field derivative estimate depends on the tangential distance to the envelope transition. The square of the new term is summed over at most \(C r^{-3}\) fine roots per normal layer and the normal weight is summable. After normalization it costs \(C g_1^{-C}(1+e)^C\tau^2r\); the earlier terms satisfy their \(r^2\) bound. Carrier enlargements cost their actual polynomial moments and remote terms use their existing exponential bounds. This proves (4.137) uniformly for the actual complex old source walls. It does not identify contributions from independently translated windows with this common reconstruction. ∎

**Corollary 4.85 (P, dense-center wall derivatives on the modulated readout).** Take any fixed finite number b of differentiations in the original old auxiliary walls on the common record in (4.137), and any fixed number of derivatives in the wave amplitude on a smaller original disc. The same estimate holds with a constant depending on the numbers of derivatives and spare radii. For a single free old wall label, its absolute sum over all original old cells obeys the same bound: Cauchy along an arbitrary simultaneous sup-bounded direction, followed by phase choice in the dual of the wall sup norm, sums the one free index. For multiple free old-wall indices, apply coefficient Cauchy separately on each already stored member and count only cells in its *actual* old carrier and its complete slots. At this fixed first mesh each carrier's count is at most a fixed polynomial in its original size, and the pre-existing spare exponential moments in the first-source norm pay any fixed finite power of that count. If only a directional analytic bound with no such carrier moments is available, the multiple absolute-index conclusion does not follow. These are derivatives of the given auxiliary walls and the admitted wave family; they do not replace independent physical currents on unverified mixed domains.

### 4.73 The true matching matrix couples adjacent retained colors

**Lemma 4.86 (P, interior coarse interfaces at sufficiently large allowed L).** For the compact first H summed on a patch with its original untapered positive test on that patch, the commutator in (4.136) detects any difference of the induced generators across a coarse interface whose complete stars lie there. This conclusion allows the actual constraint's solved pivots and does not infer such a generator from an arbitrary local test.

**Proof.** Fix a face normal to ν and a transverse µ. Choose parallel fine µ bonds separated by exactly two fine ν steps, with the same three other coordinates and with one in each of the two neighboring determining blocks. In the bare H quadratic form the off-diagonal pairing of these two components is a fixed positive multiple of \(1-1/3\): both occur in the *same* centered ν matching force with shift coefficients of equal sign. No other center has these two normal offsets in one term. In particular, in another bond direction the relevant mixed first differences span at most one ν step. For other tangential positions the cross pairing between the two chosen layers is zero; spanning two normal steps forces the remaining coordinates to match. This gives a positive diagonal cross matrix on the common array of tangential positions across the face. The physical Wilson factor and the quadratic-form polarization convention multiply it by a fixed positive constant.

Choose many such tangential positions away from other block faces and discard any selected pivot itself. In the fine layer of each block only a fixed number of coarse rows can see these variables: every flat one-step averaging path is in its own two first blocks. The number of such neighboring coarse rows is bounded by a dimension constant independent of L. Thus for sufficiently large L there exists a nonzero real array v on these tangential positions that, separately inserted in either block, has zero in all its actual coarse rows. Normalize v in the starred-coordinate Euclidean norm. Taking the two trial arrays as independent starred variations, their original pivot reconstruction therefore has no extra entries. Their cross quadratic pairing is the just computed fixed positive constant. This prevents cancellation by other pivots even when the full matching sum reaches them. The two trials see distinct constant generator matrices on their respective blocks. Testing \([K_*\otimes1,D_\theta]\) on them gives that same nonzero pairing times \(\operatorname{ad}_{\theta_{y'}-\theta_y}\) in color. Since the adjoint kernel is trivial on the Lie algebra, this is nonzero for a nonconstant pair. The fixed compact finite-window covariance is positive definite, so its insertion on either side cannot turn it into zero. Conversely a constant generator commutes with the color-diagonal quadratic form. This proves the stated detection. It is an actual sign test for the pre-contact joint second-source coefficient. It does not evaluate the old relative gauge \(\theta\) or establish that this contact carries the first scalar saved age. ∎

### 4.74 Membership of one translated quantum proper file

**Proposition 4.87 (P/L, original first homogeneous birth in the Q₂ history).** The near proper insertion is a genuine vertex of the recursion. The independently normalized period sum in (4.131)–(4.132), if its integration bodies are the individual near determining windows, is not the corresponding stored coefficient of this birth. Its readout windows occur before the original global conditional integration; its actual offspring are the individually localized connected coefficients described below. This is a formula-specific distinction, not a declaration that every boundary term vanishes.

**One file before and after its integral.** Fix a fine primitive root b and its original near cube F, including its genuine pins and the complete MT21 collars. On the first homogeneous branch \(\Omega_1=T\), denote its near source readout by \(m_{b,F}(B;U,J,s)\). Its proper part is \(d_{b,F}=m_{b,F}(B)-m_{b,F}(0)\), coefficientwise also after a derivative in the original source-only walls (HI9–HI12; XP15–18). The mass-normalized coefficient *before cluster filing* is
\[
 {\int_{\mathcal B_T}d_{b,F}(B;U,J,s)\,w_T(B;U,J)dB
       \over \int_{\mathcal B_T}w_T(B;U,J)dB}.               \tag{4.139}
\]
Here \(\mathcal B_T\) comprises the original independent starred variables of the whole first step (CMP119 (1.13)–(1.18)); its cutoff, Jacobian, source-zero weight and any nontrivial selected restrictions are those of that history. F labels the near determination in the *numerator test*, not the set of variables in either integral. For a true different mixed birth replace T by the original selected transformation domain, never by a near cube solely because that cube appears inside m. The degree-one XP40 term is exactly this first cumulant. At degree two it is joined by the cumulant of two proper labels and any already present two-label row. No such joint source coefficient is generated by the one-label trace alone.

For the actual connected filing let \(H_0(Z)\) be the unmarked polymers of CMP116 (2.9)–(2.14) and \(H'_{b,F}(Z)\) their marked derivative from this one proper vertex. The unprojected stored coefficient assigned to X is
\[
 Q_{1,X;b,F}=\sum_{n\ge1}{1\over n!}
  \sum_{\cup_j Z_j=X}p^T(Z_1,\ldots,Z_n)
  \sum_{i=1}^n H'_{b,F}(Z_i)\prod_{j\ne i} H_0(Z_j).        \tag{4.140}
\]
This is the derivative of the literal truncated-graph expansion CMP116 (2.12),(2.13); the marked connected coordinates retain all original read footprints and source anchors at this order. Its weak-kernel integrals use the localized covariance and precision in (2.14), including their original factors. Summing (4.140) over all X gives (4.139). The normalized differentiation of (4.139) under an external variation is \(E(d')+\operatorname{Cov}(d,\partial\log w_T)\). At weak coupling (4.132) uses the actual global \(K_T^{-1}\) and \(V_{1,T}\), with derivatives of the genuine F *readout*. To obtain the coefficient of one assigned X, localize that trace and score by (4.140), rather than measuring either in a new isolated experiment. When the localization creates a factorized integration on some islands, it retains its own actual support, covariance and denominator from that expansion.

One can verify the normalization distinction in the model itself. At flat data the true Gaussian precision on independent fluctuations is the restriction of the Wilson Hessian plus the original positive soft axial form. Take a straight interior coarse face of F. For large allowed L take two layers of transverse µ-links separated by *one* fine normal step across this face, and take the same real tangential array in each layer, vanishing at every pivot and solving all actual coarse constraints separately in each block. Existence is the dimension argument of Lemma4.86. The Wilson quadratic cross pairing between the two is a negative constant times the squared norm of that array, by the unique plaquettes through this face. The soft axial terms are confined to the individual first blocks and cannot cancel this pairing. In the full zero-target space use the Euclidean decomposition into the subspace S of variations supported in F with zero exterior, and its orthogonal complement. The displayed outside trial is orthogonal to S. Thus \(K_{S,S^\perp}\ne0\), and the global covariance on these coordinates is
\[
 (K_{SS}-K_{S,S^\perp}K_{S^\perp,S^\perp}^{-1}K_{S^\perp,S})^{-1},     \tag{4.141}
\]
strictly different as a matrix from the zero-exterior covariance \(K_{SS}^{-1}\). This does not give a sign for a specific H trace, but rules out identifying the two coefficient recipes by the original constraints. The argument does not need the boundary pivots of the two formulations to coincide.

**Physical graph, exterior and projection.** The identity for the analytic extension of the stored proper coefficient on its physical birth graph is the original CMP109 (1.1),(1.7)–(1.9), with \(U_1(V_1)\) the minimum on the first periodic graph in this globally clean history. Formula (4.140) defines a function of the old pair on X; it has not converted the external first critical graph into the inner F used in its integrand. In the next actual R localization, LFRII p.373 (1.61) files the function by X and every component it meets, not by the point b alone. In the Q₂ wave, a near proper contribution whose complete determining carrier straddles the face is read on the enlarged record. A smaller filed X wholly on the inner side has its own \(Y_1=Z\). The exact finite projection of the **connected coefficient** sends \(Q_{1,X}\) into its stable and lower/current/contact families; MT37/38 combines the actual pointed and covariant area contributions before taking absolute moments. It does not turn (4.140) into one unprojected local trace. The p.377 subtractand uses this same projected stored function of the exterior in its own record, and the corresponding exterior action receives it once.

There is an exact formulation even for a sum over translated files. Restrict the original polymer gas to allowed connected supports \(Z_i\subset A\) and denote its partition by \(\mathfrak Z_A\). With the original fixed compatibility relation,
\[
 \sum_{X\subset A} Q_{1,X;b,F}
 = \partial_\lambda\log\mathfrak Z_A(\lambda)\big|_0 .       \tag{4.142}
\]
Indeed \(\cup_i Z_i\subset A\) if and only if all its members are in A, so the logarithm of the restricted gas uses exactly the summands of (4.140). It includes its own normalizing subtraction. The class touching both sides is obtained by subtraction of restricted logs (and the correct exterior if required). This is a genuine sum in the localized first-order R calculation at a common external argument. Its individual \(Q_{1,X}\)'s, after projection on their proper original graph, are members of the stored packet. Its signed sum cannot be substituted for a weighted absolute anchor norm. In particular (4.131) with independently chosen normalized F-laws is absent from (9.1) for this file. A period expression with the **restricted polymer gas** at the correct arguments could occur in an actual summed R branch; on the present Q₂ complex curve the field-dependent scalar with the global first physical readout is screened as in §§4.58,4.70,4.72. To apply that argument to the full first torus take \(t=D(1+e)\); count only the \(O(r^{-4})\) original homes of the compact test, and use the exponential first-carrier tail outside that distance. Remote core inputs on this fixed Q₂ record remain in the previously admitted positive layer; even their crude inverse mesh cost is paid by the old-scale exponential at distance \(\gtrsim MR_e/r\). Thus the per-period coefficient at forbidden scale is zero for the stated scalar test on this actual birth. This conclusion does not settle a different genuinely pinned mixed birth, or make (4.142) a formula for a normalized R-body quotient with a different denominator. ∎

### 4.75 Two genuine returns and the actual radius of the first R-origin

**Proposition 4.88 (P/L, nonempty second mixed return and protected crossing geometry).** On the weak fixed-terminal subsequence of (4.113), first make the single Q₂ failure at A, then take its native ordinary waits and the healed branch of its first eligible R at \(j=2+R_2\). No other component is live there. A tagged complete compact H in this first component gives the original additive R-origin source coefficients in the first logarithm (1.98), with their actual supporting X's. At an interior real weak configuration the original component weight is positive, and a compact matching square on an independently variable matched stencil is positive on an open subset of the original integral. This describes a genuine tagged sector even if an individual projected X-file or its constant-subtracted value vanishes. Retain its actual source amplitude \(a_{j,X}\) and any earned earlier age separately. The background defining (1.98)–(1.100) on this no-still-live branch is the genuinely healed scale-j graph, with only jth-level links on the whole torus. Its member space is CMP119 (2.28),(2.34)–(2.39); it has **shrinking** radii
\[
 \mu_j=\min(\alpha_{0,j},\alpha_{1,j}),\quad
 \alpha_{i,j}=c_i g_j(\log g_j^{-2})^{q_i},\qquad
 p_{\rm old}=p_0(g_j).                                      \tag{4.143}
\]
The printed (1.100) rare amplitude is \(e^{-p_{\rm old}}e^{-\kappa d_j(X)}\) for the regular unmarked R-origin. In the corresponding bounded marked sector one keeps its actual scalar cap times this one earned reserve as in §§4.39,4.50 and its source derivatives. A clean fixed physical radius is not assigned to it.

At \(b=j+1\) make a second single Q failure at a new seed B. Use the real compact pure nodal spike in the j-lattice with angle strictly between the failure threshold and the allowed soft axial ceiling, identity on b-nodes. Lift this gauge block-constantly into all earlier grids. It is supported far from A. This is exactly the Q construction of §4.42. Before b the extension changes no earlier plaquette, all earlier stars in its own component are ordinary, and both targets and selectors at A have their original values. The two seed neighborhoods are disjoint even after the first return of A. Thus the initial Q₂ selected integral at A, its real healed X branch, and the new strict Q failure at B occur in the same original history. In the following ordinary steps take the newly inspected data flat outside B's inherited component and no new failures. It first becomes eligible at \(e=b+R_b\), once its original size and waiting tests hold. The original spacings at j,b,e can all have the same R by the rounding argument of (4.113); \(e-j=R+1\), \(j=R+2\), and \(e=2R+3\ll K\).

Put A at the second component's outer regular band on the face of \(I=Z_e^{\sim-2}\) from §4.16. More precisely choose the aligned B center so its final I-face contains a chosen old grid plane, place the earlier A rectangle across this plane away from all corners, and keep its true marked carrier within a fixed multiple of its original \(MR_j\) diameter. The translations are allowed on the actual nested grids: translate the complete B construction by an e-scale lattice multiple of its native M/R units, then independently choose A around a point on that plane at one of the compatible earlier block centers. Its old size in e units is \(O(MR_j r)\), \(r=L^{j-e}\), and the new component's old B seed is a distance of order \(M R_e\) in e units from the tested region. There is room on the fixed torus because \(K-e\to\infty\). At the preceding ordinary steps A avoids the covered B component; the final original ten-layer enlargement first meets its carrier. As in §4.16, the complete starred/old stencil is in \(\Omega_e\) on that band, a fixed positive e-unit distance from \(\partial Y_1\), while its carrier intersects the new rough component Z and can cross the artificial inner seam. This is a second mixed R applied to a previously integrated R-origin, not two tests in the same first component. ∎

### 4.76 Spare birth units at the second true mixed R

**Proposition 4.89 (P/L, the protected second-return family of Proposition4.88).** On the stated two-seed history fix the original commuting nodal representatives and zero new fluctuations on the tested branch. Let the outer real/complex data be the actual small global top physical field on the e-torus (a small Cartan deformation supported near A in top coordinates suffices), with its physical current and the matched native fine exterior. At the unweakened second R endpoint the following old-radius comparison holds. It also holds on original scalar preparation walls of (1.61)–(1.65) for which the prescribed exterior is that same top field, the component targets are on the stated commuting selector support, and the tested patch is the true top/next-to-top protected overlap of §4.16. All original source-only walls in the old member chart may vary. This is a class of genuine second returns, not a claim about an arbitrary mixed exterior gauge.

Let \(\tau_e=C(\alpha_{0,e}+\alpha_{1,e})\) be the actual top member ceiling, \(d_X=1+\operatorname{diam}_j X\le C M R_j\), and \(\eta_j=L^{-j}\) the *fine* mesh in birth-j units. With fixed geometric constants and P, put
\[
 E_{j,e}= C(1+r^{-1})^P\eta_j^{-P} e^{-c/r}
                 g_j(1+\log x_j)^P,\qquad
 h_{j,e}= C d_X^P(r^2\tau_e+E_{j,e}).                       \tag{4.144}
\]
On the complete actual old read support of the identified R-origin X, the pair \((W,J_{\rm phys}(W))\) fits the native old tests with old-size field/first-derivative and curvature bounded by \(h_{j,e}\) and old current bounded by \(C(r^3\tau_e+E_{j,e})\). In particular each scalar inequality of CMP119 (2.34)–(2.39), in its prescribed units, has spare at least a fixed fraction of its *actual* radius (4.143). For example, in the normalized native old physical core the remaining margin is at least
\[
 c_*\mu_j-h_{j,e}\ \ge\ \tfrac12 c_*\mu_j                     \tag{4.145}
\]
for all sufficiently weak trajectories in the stated subsequence. Here \(c_*\) is a fixed stricter fraction of the original old chart, allowing its fixed geometry factors. This is not a free fine-link sup ball of that radius: the original curvature, reconstruction, current and relative first-order tests all retain their original mesh and support. A physical incoming coarse direction is measured on its true graph, and independent J may be varied in its own (2.36) units.

**Genuine endpoint.** Before its second localization, the actual field in (1.50) is the stationary mixed Wilson minimum on the original global \(B''_e\). All genuinely retained rows now have depth at least j: the first component has healed, and the new Q at j+1 introduced only j links and later descendants. Thus every global \(M^j\)-preserving variation preserves all genuine rows of this actual graph, including its coarse node frames. The original older homogeneous j-minimum at these first-j data is the very same orbit by uniqueness; this is HI3 on the actual common action and fixed exterior, not a proof via different window endpoints. In the protected band the shape necessarily lies in the top part of \(\Omega_e\). The (1.66) curvature row and the *fourth*, reconstructed-current row with \(p=m=e\), give (4.38), including the actual physical-current stars. The regular patch construction of §4.25 supplies a holomorphic gauge with \(\|A\|_{C^{1,\gamma}}\le C\tau_e\) in e units on its fixed smaller patch. In birth units the curvature and its first-order potential derivative are therefore \(C r^2\tau_e\); the physical current is \(C r^3\tau_e\). In this commuting patch subtract the potential at one root by a common site gauge. Its values across the complete old source have size \(C d_X r^2\tau_e\). All original endpoint data are transformed together. At this endpoint the comparison error in (4.144) is unnecessary.

**Actual localizations on the protected overlap.** The outermost function of (1.61) is \(U(B''_e(Y_1),\cdot)\); the earlier preparation parameters modify its specified targets. The second and third joins and their actual block frames precede this last minimum. For the chosen Y and X the whole enlarged contractible site patch lies in the top one or two artificial layers of this final graph, by §4.16; thus all its genuine/determining rows meeting an even smaller cube are at depth \(e-1\) or e, above j. On this patch (1.65),(1.66) and the physical-current argument just used provide (4.55) also for the prepared field \(\Gamma\), on its original domain. On the whole recorded field, including the supplied homogeneous exterior, every actual positive member layer has original index at least j. The old-unit bound (4.90) for its true raw first-j targets gives \(u=C g_j(1+\log x_j)^P=o(1)\): sum the factors \(L^{-(n-j)}\) over positive n and keep \(L_0^{2N_0}\le C(1+N_0)^{\beta_0}R_e\). In a complete j-block the real coarse plaquettes and the complex raw perturbation have that bound by the original prefix estimate (4.33); these are precisely the small tests (7),(172) for the global first-j problem. This uses the chosen post-healing graph and its real native comparator. No such test follows for an exposed level-zero exterior on an arbitrary history. The global old physical readout W exists; its real curvature and its small complex relative field have the top-old-unit bounds \(C u\) by CMP102 Theorem1,(9)–(10),(172)–(180).

To obtain the *smaller* bound at A, take an aligned old-comparison cube O about A, with a fixed small positive side in e units, contained with the full stars in the top overlap just described. Form its minimal j-level determining graph with its actual finer collar. Its two sets of target data are induced by W and \(\Gamma\); in its top-j interior they agree exactly since \(M^jW=M^j\Gamma\). Each is the corresponding local minimum. For W extend its local variations by zero to the global j-graph. For \(\Gamma\), such variations preserve all rows of (1.61) they meet: each is a genuine higher-depth mean of the unchanged full j-data and exterior on O. This is the exact localized HI1 test, before any estimate. The two true collars, including all finer raw rows, now need to be compared only at a distance at least \(c/r\) in old first-j units from X.

One can make this comparison without postulating the derivative of an arbitrary inverse axial frame. Use the commuting family specified above; equivariance under the constant maximal torus and uniqueness give Cartan representatives on it. Apply the common e-unit regular chart on O to fix the same j-node frames in its interior. In each j-block use a based tree for the two potentials to flatten their internal paths. The genuine j-mean links are unchanged by a fine gauge fixing these nodes. The strip identity and the real/complex curvature bound in old units bound each internal fine-bond angle by \(C\eta_j u\). A crossing between two first-j blocks may additionally carry its true j-link of size \(C r\tau_e+C u\) in this patch. Every h-prefix on a finer collar row spans only a bounded number of j-blocks, and its length inside any one block is at most \(C/\eta_j\). Thus all of these collar *angles* are \(O(u+r\tau_e)\), with the same prescribed exterior and endpoint gauges. On the commuting branch endpoint conjugations do not amplify this estimate. The interior j-targets agree throughout. Interpolate the actual remaining collar angles about the flat reference in CMP102 (172); its uniform small absolute target radius includes this interpolation. Complete frozen exterior bonds are part of the finest rows/trace, and any action derivative they contribute is supported on the incident boundary stars. The native derivative densities (190), or the corresponding augmented boundary-force inverse of §4.14, give an exponential \(e^{-c/r}\), since all native cell sizes of this **old** j-record are at most one. Even counting all fine collar rows by the crudest volume bound, losing fixed inverse fine mesh powers for fine link/current conversions and a polynomial in the diameter of O, gives (4.144). For a local gauge-invariant physical field use the relative solution and restore the same prescribed coarse frame; on the Cartan variables any remaining site-gradient does not alter the curls/current. A local derivative of an arbitrary raw gauge function (106) has not been used. On this contractible patch its potential can be fixed by the same tree/regular chart as in §4.25; the exponential error can absorb the fixed powers of \(\eta_j^{-1}\) in this last local chart. This proves the comparison for the declared native localization walls.

**Every earlier native row.** The old action graph is j-homogeneous away from its original finite source collar. On the actual restricted p-windows in CMP119, \(p\le j\), the p-reconstruction of W with its prescribed trace is W itself up to the prescribed gauge. A variation preserving its full p-data, including boundary children and prefixes, preserves the global j-targets; the admitted old orbit is critical and unique there. Thus (2.35) on the j-layer is the genuine fine plaquette bound \(C h_{j,e}\eta_j^2\). In (2.37) the p-unit current is at most \(C(r^3\tau_e+E_{j,e})(L^{p-j})^3\), smaller than \(\alpha_{0,j}(L^{p-j})^2\). The independent J test (2.36) here uses the true physical j-current; it also has a separate complex J-disc of radius \(c_*\alpha_{0,j}\) after paying this value. The compact real field, relative potential and its first derivative in the common local chart verify (2.34),(2.38),(2.39) with \(h_{j,e}\). The old relative analytic core, including its actual reconstructed-current tests, consequently has the margin (4.145). Numerically \(g_e/g_j=1+O(R/x_j)\), \(d_X\le CMR_j\), and \(r=L^{-R-1}\), so every finite logarithmic factor in (4.144) times \(r^2\) is smaller than the specified fraction of \(\mu_j\); \(\eta_j^{-P}=L^{Pj}\) costs only \(e^{O(R)}\), dominated by \(e^{-c/r}\).

For a constant-subtracted gauge-invariant scalar R-origin whose old amplitude is \(a_{j,X}\), the actual old core and the zero adjoint-invariant first derivative give, on the same declared physical/canonical source chart,
\[
 r^{-2}|R_{j,X}(W,J_{\rm phys})-R_{j,X}(1,0)|
 \le C a_{j,X} r^{-2}(h_{j,e}/\mu_j)^2 .                  \tag{4.146}
\]
Here is the required radial *disc*, since mere segment inclusion would not justify the two powers. In the centered commuting chart write \(W=\exp(i\eta_j A)\), set \(F_z=\exp(i\eta_j z A)\), and take independent current \(zJ_{\rm phys}(W)\). All slots, including the fixed fine exterior read by X, follow this same path. On the whole required enlargement \(\|A\|+\|\nabla A\|\le h_{j,e}\). Also \(\|d^*d A\|\le C h_{j,e}\) on the actual action stars: in the Cartan Wilson-force identity replacing the plaquette sine by its linear curl costs at most \(C\eta_j^3\|F\|_\infty^3\), by the elementary fixed-stencil inverse bound; the actual physical j-current was already bounded above. For \(|z|\le c\mu_j/h_{j,e}\) this entire uncritical trial therefore satisfies the three old-unit weak reference tests (4.40) with ceiling \(C c\mu_j\), including first difference and physical divergence. In each original local p-reconstruction use this trial and its actual exterior as the zero-target reference exactly as in (4.41)–(4.42). The native force has size at most \(C c\mu_j\sigma^3\); the weighted zero-target contraction and its physical angle-trial multiplier bound on the original spacing give its curvature \(C c\mu_j\eta_j^2\) and physical p-current \(C c\mu_j(L^{p-j})^3\), respectively. The count and spacing test is the same fixed power-three test used there, and the p-graph has no native mesh exceeding one in birth units. This checks (2.35),(2.37) on the entire radial disc, not just on the W endpoint. The field itself gives (2.34),(2.38),(2.39), with an old compact real comparator on the actual patch; commutativity controls the original raw means. Taking c fixed small puts all tests strictly inside their old radii. The constant/linear terms at the flat point are subtracted/zero; Schwarz on this particular disc gives the square in (4.146). A holomorphic matrix element of an independent physical response is only covered if its own lowered grade and old anchors have also been retained. With the physical degree-two old cap \(a_{j,X}\le A_{j,X}(\mu_j/g_j)^2\) this gives \(C A_{j,X}d_X^{2P}r^2(\tau_e/g_j)^2\), preserving its old coupling ratio and this second waiting age; an older earned coefficient included in \(A_{j,X}\) is not reopened. For the prescribed small physical exterior one can take \(\tau_e\) at its actual cutoff or write it as \(g_e\) times its canonical envelope and keep that factor. No first or second R rare factor was multiplied into (4.145), and no general old-anchor MT36 inequality follows from this scalar estimate alone. ∎

### 4.77 The actual outer face of the second component

**Proposition 4.90 (P/L, an off-endpoint sharp-edge second return).** Translate A instead to the true outer face of the second returning Z, with its old bounded-diameter-in-\(MR_j\) carrier meeting both sides and its test away from corners. Keep the first healed history and the distinct genuinely rough B seed of §4.75. Use the admitted empty second preparation from §4.33 with the original bare-frequency Cartan wave near A and the same real compact nodal interior. Now the fine spacing in entry units is \(\eta_e=L^{-e}=r\eta_j\), where \(\eta_j=L^{-j}=r/L\) on the specified plateau. All earlier *source-only* walls may take their original complex values. On the full bounded outer \(\zeta\)-disc of this input the first R-origin has the sharp second-seam old margin
\[
 h^{\rm edge}_{j,e}= C d_X^P\,[\eta_j r^2\tau+E_{j,e}+L^j\eta_e^{-P}e^{-cMR_e}\tau_e],
 \qquad c_*\mu_j-h^{\rm edge}_{j,e}\ge c_*\mu_j/2 .          \tag{4.147}
\]
Choose the original M and spacing in their original order with the fixed extra slack for \(e=2R+3\) and the indicated finite mesh powers. This condition does not reselect M as the ultraviolet cutoff changes.

**Proof.** At this stage the actual inner cut in §4.40 is still in the top ordinary ring: the newly failed Q-component is the only component of the second R and follows the same ten/eight-layer ordinary cover. The independent U of the test has its first raw mean I, including both endpoint transports. Put its fine angle equal to \(C\eta_e^2\tau\zeta\) times the same bounded harmonic profile and complete transverse-period envelope used in §§4.33,4.46. All higher raw target means are I. The member check uses the actual top n=e fine plaquette ceiling \(C\tau\eta_e^2\), the p-current ceiling \(C\tau(L^{p-e})^2\), and the actual regionally reconstructed p-fields, as in §4.33. On the true small Y annulus use its flat artificial-cut reference. Proposition9 (190) and its native density sum on this reference give fine link angles bounded by \(C\eta_e^2\tau|\zeta|\) in the weak common representative over the current-size near region; the actual inner-cut error is \(C\eta_e^{-P}e^{-cMR_e}\tau_e\) with fixed polynomial factors absorbed into P or the original buffer. The positive targets of the large Y are unchanged on the supported wave. On the prescribed exterior use the explicit fine field. This is a statement on the original empty preparation, without extending the native R-operation to a wrong contour. The original complex ζ-disc and Cartan selector point remain the same as at the first such calculation.

For any actual j-mean in this region, its complete geometric path extent in fine spacings is at most \(C_L L^j\). On these commuting small links the normalized straight mean and the accumulated endpoint gauges have total response at most \(C_L L^j\) times the fine angle. Explicitly the straight averages are convex normalized sums of parallel segments; each previous block-endpoint gradient is restricted to the next nodes by gauge covariance, and the lengths of its remaining prefixes sum to \(C_L\sum_{h<j}L^h\le C_L L^j\). This is the group/endpoint statement (4.33) on the commuting branch. Thus the actual old top-coarse link logarithms throughout the patch have size at most
\[
 C\,[\eta_j r^2\tau+L^j\eta_e^{-P}e^{-cMR_e}\tau_e].      \tag{4.148}
\]
There is no approximation by reopened bare variables in this argument: the left hand side consists of the genuine first-j determining data of the earlier healed R-origin.

Take an auxiliary old homogeneous top target agreeing with these actual j-data throughout a fixed e-unit neighborhood of the whole X and equal to identity outside a slightly larger such neighborhood, on its original periodic j-graph. This is a comparator only. The fixed ratio estimates (172)–(180),(190) on a globally small top-old target bound its local old (C^1\) potential, physical Wilson curvature and current by a constant times (4.148); the (D^*D\) row at the flat reference and the finite-plaquette Wilson expansion control the current, or equivalently use the actual homogeneous adjoint/multiplier estimate of §4.12. This estimate is in top-old physical units even though its fine mesh is \(\eta_j\). The real/complex actual global old solution may have a rougher target near B. Its uniform old-unit small bound is \(u\) from (4.90), since every genuine layer there is positive of depth at least j. Restrict it and the comparator to the same nested old cube about A. Their interior j-data agree, and the actual finer collar data (including the prescribed traces) are weak after the common j-node tree construction of §4.76. Formula (190) from that old collar again costs at most \(E_{j,e}\) at X. This comparison uses the true old global orbit and has not replaced it by a periodic extension of an individual source carrier. Being Cartan, all gradient corrections can be made in a common local old chart for the entire finite star. This proves (4.147), including the old curvature/current and absolute lower tests; all p-windows are again true subminima of the resulting old W. The radial-disc argument following (4.146) applies with this edge size. The large-record wave response is zero where all its positive data and exterior are unchanged.

For one such old home the engineering-scaled scalar costs at most \(C a_{j,X}r^{-2}(h^{\rm edge}_{j,e}/\mu_j)^2\). The cutoff error can be made smaller than any prescribed fixed r-power in this two-interval history. Even the conservative count of \(O(r^{-3})\) old homes on a bounded current face gives an extra factor \(\eta_j^2/r=r/L^2\) from the first term of (4.147), times the actual old radius, test and logarithmic factors. This last count is an upper estimate, not an assertion that one earlier local R event produces all those homes with comparable weight. Actual born amplitudes and rare factors remain in their own ledger. The result therefore gives a second nonempty sharp-edge admission calculation at its **shrinking** R-origin radius. Higher-frequency raw frame derivatives or a wave whose wavelength is the old j-spacing rather than the bare spacing have not been inferred from this comparison. ∎

### 4.78 A second sharp return with a wavelength at the old R-origin spacing

**Proposition 4.91 (P/L, the j-period transverse probe).** In the second geometry and original empty preparation of §4.77 there is an admissible current-size transverse wave of wavelength r, with \(M^j U=I\), for which the physically reconstructed old target and current near the entire second face have the stronger local bound
\[
 C(1+\log(1/r))^C\left[r^3\tau(1+n)^{-p}+r^4\tau^2+E'_{j,e}\right],\quad p>1,       \tag{4.149}
\]
where n is the inward distance in *birth-j spacings*. \(E'_{j,e}\) consists of the original cut/comparator errors in (4.144),(4.147) with sufficient original buffer margin. The bound holds on the original fixed complex wave disc and uniformly on all prior source-only walls of the stored R member; it is not a bound for arbitrary scalar preparation at the new sharp seam. The previously earned shrinking radius (4.143) again has strict spare. After the original engineering factor and an absolute sum over a bounded current face, this new term costs at most \(C a_{j,\sup}\mu_j^{-2}(1+e)^C(\tau^2 r+\tau^4r^2)\), with the actual old root and marked carrier amplitudes. For the actual single old R component this is a conservative count, not a new multiplicity assigned to its shape.

**Smooth null mean.** Let \(x_1\) be inward normal, \(x_2\) one transverse direction and take the actual fine 3-potential of amplitude \(r\tau\), multiplying a commuting generator by an inward discrete harmonic normal exponential and \(\cos(2\pi x_2/r+\varphi)\). Its fine normal multiplier is \(\exp(-\lambda_j x_1/\eta_e)\) with \(\cosh\lambda_j=2-\cos(2\pi/L^j)\), hence \(\lambda_j/\eta_e\asymp1/r\). Start with a smooth current-size envelope in all transverse variables, supported strictly inside the adjacent added current cube and nonzero on the test. To keep an exact null first-j average without a fine jump in potential, fix a smooth nonnegative bump \(\psi\) on the open unit interval, positive and normalized in its interior. In each complete transverse j-block subtract from the enveloped cosine its *discrete average* times \(\psi((x_2-a_k)/r)\) divided by the discrete average of this bump there, where \(a_k\) is that block's left endpoint. Do so for each fixed other coordinate. Its denominator is uniformly positive for the actual fine mesh \(L^{-j}\); the numerator is \(O(r)\) since a bare cosine has zero discrete mean on each complete block. The same bound holds for fixed transverse derivatives of that numerator in the other coordinates. The correction vanishes smoothly near each block boundary. Its first and second finite derivatives in x₂ are at most \(C\) and \(C/r\). Including the physical potential amplitude \(r\tau\) this creates bounded current \(C\tau\) rather than a fine-mesh divergence. The exact segment and transverse block averages in the true level-j straight row are zero. Undo the remaining raw node gradient by its actual coarse node gauge. A smooth fine representative may be used for this operation: the needed coarse site phases are \(O(r^2\tau)\) by the actual prefix lengths, and a partition of scale r interpolating their nodal values has first, second and third differences bounded by \(C r^{2-l}\tau\) for order l. Coarse gauge covariance makes its extension immaterial to the target, and this choice preserves the physical/complex first-derivative bounds. Thus \(M^h U=I\) for every \(h\ge j\). The inward cutoff is at fixed positive entry distance. Continue the exact harmonic profile a fixed multiple of r into the exterior, enough to include all complete smaller-prefix and action stencils, and cut it smoothly there on scale r. The possibly large fixed harmonic growth on these finitely many old layers is paid by reducing the fixed wave prefactor.

**Native input tests.** The fine curvature is \(O(\tau)\) in e units and the potential/first derivative are \(O(r\tau),O(\tau)\). The exterior current row can be set to zero. On the original small Y every *dynamic* incident star is in the harmonic extension, so the physical force is \(O(\tau(1+n)^C e^{-c n})\) plus the inward cutoff error. This follows by taking each actual difference: the unmodulated normal/cosine pair has zero Maxwell force; each product difference with the smooth envelope or its mean correction costs at most \(C\tau\). The compact commuting Wilson sine changes this bound by harmless higher powers, since \(\eta_e\le r\). On the added exterior cutoff in the larger record the force can be \(C\tau/r\). For a reconstructed test at \(p<j\), its maximal mesh is \(s_p=L^{p-e}\le r\). Use the actual trial with its induced targets and fixed exterior as a zero-target reference, exactly as in (4.41)–(4.42). Its normalized forcing \(\sigma^3 J\) is at most \(C\sigma^3\tau/r\). The original weighted contraction then gives physical curvature at most \(C[\tau+\tau s_p/r]\le C\tau\) and physical current at most \(C\tau/r\). In p units the latter is \(C\tau s_p^3/r\le C\tau s_p^2\), as required by (2.37). On small Y and \(p\ge j\) use the better dynamic force ceiling \(C\tau\). On large \(Y^+\), for \(p\ge j\) the complete genuinely fine strips near its outer boundary miss the wave, every other raw positive-depth target there is I, and its p-reconstruction is flat. This verifies the (2.34)–(2.39) member tests on the actual two determining domains after the fixed prefactor is chosen. A blockwise discontinuous envelope or a discontinuous complex node gauge would not give this verification.

**Weighted reference response.** For a row of native scale \(\sigma_h=L^{h-e}\le r\) in the annulus of §4.40, every actual boundary target word has magnitude \(C\sigma_h r\tau|\zeta|\) by its full commuting raw prefix length including node frames. Above j these targets are I. Middle e-rows and artificial inner cut in the reference are I. Put \(w_y=\max(1,r/\sigma_y)\). Its ratios across native layers cost only the first power of their scale ratio, absorbed by the same spacing/metric budget as in (4.34)–(4.36). Thus the boundary data have weighted size \(C r^2\tau|\zeta|\). Integrate (190) in the true small native target tube, applying the ratio before the native density sum. On \(|\zeta|\le c/(r^2\tau)\) the reference fine physical potential is at most \(C w_y^{-1}\sigma_y^{-1}r^2\tau|\zeta|\le C r\tau|\zeta|\); equivalently use the corresponding weighted zero-target contraction. This is a disc for the reference only. The whole j-raw average, including all endpoint phases, consequently stays in a fixed small branch on that disc, by the total old physical prefix length \(C r\) of (4.33). Its nonlinear remainder on the original bounded disc is \(C r^4\tau^2\) by Cauchy. This estimate uses the weight also on the fine layers; merely counting \(L^j\) bounded fine angles on the enlarged disc would give a false radius.

For the linear term the original harmonic trial and the reference have identical complete outer and positive rows, including the h<j ones; their inner-cut mismatch is exponentially small. Their remaining difference solves the true flat *zero-target* equation driven by the displayed envelope force. If \(n_z\) is the inward distance in units r, the original layer retreat gives \(\sigma_z\le C r(1+n_z)\). Hence \(w_z\sigma_z^3|J_z|\le C r^3\tau(1+n_z)^C e^{-c n_z}\). As in §4.72 reserve part of the native metric rate for this normal profile. From \(n_z<\sqrt n\) a path to y either pays \(c n L^{j-k}\) at its largest layer k or \(c S_0(k-j-\log_L(1+\sqrt n)-C)_+\) for ascending through layers. With the original spacing this leaves \((1+n)^{-p}\); sources at larger \(n_z\) already have this decay. The weighted potential correction is therefore at most \(C r^3\tau(1+n)^{-p}\) in its normalized norm. Divide by \(w_y\sigma_y\ge r\) to bound physical potential, and integrate for the true j-average across a length \(C r\). Since the trial's j-raw mean is exactly I, this gives the first term of (4.149). The actual annular cut comparison and the local/global old comparison contribute the stated \(E'_{j,e}\); as e is only \(2R+3\), the original \(MR_e\) buffer pays their finite inverse-mesh losses. On the old homogeneous graph its regular fine curvature/current follow by (190) in old physical units as in §4.77. Old-scale exponential convolution preserves the normal weight up to a fixed polynomial in the chosen comparison radius. Finally sum at most \(C r^{-3}\) old homes per normal layer for the squared \(r^3\) term and \(C r^{-4}\) homes for the squared \(r^4\) term, with the original \(r^{-2}\) normalization. This proves the claimed scalar estimate and old-margin comparison. ∎

### 4.79 The physical marked quotient at the second return

**Proposition 4.92 (P/L, sector of the actual undivided physical second law).** At the unweakened second physical endpoint on the protected band of §4.76, the old-radius estimate applies to the full real selector support of the specified *second* B ancestry, not just its commuting representative. Let \(M_X= r^{-2}(R_{j,X}(W,J_{\rm phys}(W))-R_{j,X}(1,0))\) be the stored constant-subtracted scalar tag, or the corresponding difference of two actually admitted old slots in this sector. Let \(K_{j,e;X}\) denote the right side of (4.146), doubled for the latter difference. Condition on the physical small exterior, the identified old X-file and its original j-level action; the second-resolved ancestor is not averaged a second time inside the new body. In the second full real unexpanded return gas with its own unmarked normalizer,
\[
 |E[1_{X_B\ {\rm present}}1_{\mathfrak h_B} M_X]|
       \le C e^{-(1+\delta)p_0(g_e)} K_{j,e;X} .              \tag{4.150}
\]
Here \(X_B\) is the second body's fixed eligible rectangle/home, and \(\mathfrak h_B\) selects its actual one-Q-at-j+1 ancestry inside that body. The first component A has already been integrated in the stored birth. There is no indicator reopening its microscopic fields. There is no separate lower bound for the mass of this restricted subhistory.

**Proof.** At the unweakened point the raw determining data of every second-B history in this specified sector have depth at least j globally, regardless of the values of its real internal variables. On the actual selector they satisfy the positive native estimates. Consequently the global stationary field already solves the old j-problem, and the protected regular band estimates (4.38),(4.55) give the same j-unit field, curvature and Wilson-current bounds for each allowed internal configuration, with the gauge transformations on the old source and its fine boundary made together. A common local smooth gauge can be centered at a root: remove the value of its potential there by a site exponential on the patch and extend on a larger collar. The Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff commutator error on a patch of old diameter \(d_X\) is bounded by \(C d_X^P r^2\tau_e\) because the constant connection in old units is \(O(r\tau_e)\); its curvature contributes its square. The old radial test in (4.146) remains valid here without commutativity. In detail the finite plaquette expansion on a bounded (C^1\) connection is \(F=dA+[A,A]+\eta_j L(A,\nabla A)\) with local uniformly bounded analytic coefficients; differentiating its last term by a single lattice difference costs its displayed \(\eta_j\). Thus \(d^*d A\) is controlled by the true current, \(\|A\|+\|\nabla A\|\), and their quadratic products as in (4.40).
