On the (zA\) disc of §4.76 the covariant Wilson current of the trial is bounded by \(C c\mu_j+C(c\mu_j)^2\) on the full required stars. The original weighted zero-target argument then checks every p-reconstruction. This yields the actual uniform \(K_{j,e;X}\) on the physical histories entering the numerator. No independent complex wall estimate on all mixed frames is inferred by extending that real-support conclusion.

Before exponentiation the second R measure at this exterior is the true positive integral/sum in (1.72). Its probability of the specified body is bounded by \(C e^{-3p_0(g_e)/2}\) via its actual presence tag and the signed exclusion-ratio estimate (4.97), with the original small unmarked margins. Restricting its real integration to \(\mathfrak h_B\) only lowers this probability. Multiply the bound by the already normalized old scalar cap; this gives (4.150) with surplus. Equivalently on a single positive body T one has \(|T(1_{\mathfrak h_B}M_X)/T1|\le K_{j,e;X}T(1_{\mathfrak h_B})/T1\); it is the *full* R denominator that yields the separate inclusive body probability above. Confusing the two ratios would lose the conclusion.

The corresponding fresh covariance with the literal inherited second-body \(V(X_B^-)\), extended by zero where the body is absent, retains one B-bank. LFRII (1.69) bounds its absolute size by its original geometric count; even counting every fine cell in this fixed rectangle throughout its e levels costs at most \(C(1+MR_e)^C L^{C e}\) on this two-interval subsequence. Thus both the product mean and the product of separate means in the covariance are bounded by this number times (4.150); since \(e=2R+3\), the actual surplus absorbs it and leaves \(e^{-p_0(g_e)}K_{j,e;X}\). Earlier R amplitudes and their attained source age stay inside \(K_{j,e;X}\). This is a real second normalized marked contribution on a genuine new mixed history, not a normalized estimate on every independently localized complex R branch. ∎

### 4.80 Old-current anchors and a finite two-interval reserve

**Corollary 4.93 (P, second-return current rows with their units displayed).** On the admitted old R-origin pair of §§4.76–4.78 the partial derivative with respect to its independent old current, evaluated at the true physical current, has the absolute bound
\[
 \sum_{b,A}\left|\partial_{J_b^A}R_{j,X}(W,J_{\rm phys}(W))\right|
       \le C a_{j,X}\,{h\over\mu_j\alpha_{0,j}},             \tag{4.151}
\]
where h is the corresponding old size from (4.144),(4.147) or (4.149), including the specified original star enlargements. The basis and all bonds are in old units. Choose the actual radial test \(F_z=\exp(i\eta_j zA)\), \(J_{z,t}=zJ_{\rm phys}(W)+tH\) with arbitrary \(\|H\|_\infty\le1\) supported on X. The earlier p-reconstructions depend on \(F_z\) alone, while (2.36) gives the independent current disc \(|t|\le c\alpha_{0,j}\). Their joint physical domain contains the earlier \(|z|\le c\mu_j/h\) disc after reducing c. Cauchy in t followed by Schwarz in z uses the zero of every invariant one-current linear form at the flat state. Finally choose the phases of the components of H in the finite old fine array before taking its supremum. This proves (4.151) without a power of \(\eta_j^{-1}\) in the single free anchor index, even though the fine mesh in old units tends to zero. It is the independent partial entering the physical chain rule, not the full derivative through W.

For the protected commuting preparation branch of §4.76 one can instead perturb the **incoming e-current**, leaving U and its real selectors fixed, in any original monitored Cartan color direction of a fine bond in the complete joined input domain Y. Take its smaller analytic radius \(\delta=c g_e^2\). The sup condition (2.36) is then strict also at the finer positive layers because their \((L^{n-e})^{-3}\) factor only enlarges the admissible dimensionless current. On the positive j-to-e interval their coupling ratios are bounded, and the actual physical background J at this restricted center was chosen within the stated margin. The other domain conditions are field and reconstructed-current conditions and do not depend on independent J. The actual marked scalar bound \(K_{j,e;X}\) is uniform on this incoming disc; the weights and third-join defining variables may change with J and are retained by that uniform bound. Cauchy on arbitrary simultaneous bounded directions therefore gives an absolute *one* incoming Cartan-current anchor row \(C\delta^{-1}K_{j,e;X}\). If a finite number m of such independently free old-wall/current indices is needed on a given fixed join, one conservative coefficientwise estimate on an actual joined determining domain of at most \(C M R_e\) current diameter is
\[
 C_m \delta^{-m}(1+MR_e)^{C_m} L^{4me}e^{C_m R_e}
                       K_{j,e;X}.                             \tag{4.152}
\]
Here the native e-unit anchor weight on this domain has explicitly been included. All variables outside the input domain of the chosen localized terms stay external; no count of all bonds of the reference torus belongs here. The estimate only claims those current directions for which §4.76 proves the full induced small old pair, and not an unrestricted noncommuting axial differential. The crude many-index cost in (4.152) is specific to \(e=2R+3\). For the fixed first-return home marked by one actual distinguished body, the unspent first (1.95) surplus of §4.39 pays this combined \(\exp[O_m(R)] g_e^{-2m}\) cost once while retaining the usual \(e^{-p_0(g_j)}\) if that is how its original marked home was allocated. Alternatively inside the real second-body estimate the new (4.150) surplus pays the corresponding pointwise derivative bound. An unspecified norm with no identified extra reserve cannot spend it. In particular there is no such conclusion by counting microscopic indices on a general history with e much larger than its available waiting bank. ∎

### 4.81 Consecutive R operations with true finer pins in the old graph

**Proposition 4.94 (P/L for the geometry and variational implication; C for the varying-exterior physical disc).** There is also a consecutive-return example with \(e=j+1\), in which the old global R-origin graph itself is mixed when the tested coefficient is born. Use the constant-R interval of (4.113). Make the real b=1 plaquette seed A of §4.16 and the strict b=2 nodal Q seed C of §4.42 on disjoint neighborhoods. Take the two genuine ordinary waiting ancestries. At \(j=1+R\) A becomes eligible and heals while C is one wait short. At \(e=2+R\) C is eligible. Choose L sufficiently large and the origins so that A's regular source carrier \(X_A\), of old diameter \(d_X\le C MR_j\), lies in the top ordinary band between the true C component and the e-scale exterior, for example about its 9th layer in the ten-layer cover. This can be done with positive M-cell clearance: its e-width is at most \(C MR_j/L\); C's prior and new covers have widths of different scales. At j the complete short A carrier is disjoint from the ineligible C and its boundary support. Both seeds are legal together by their explicit compact stencils and the block-constant lift on the C side. Thus LFRII (1.99),(1.100) assigns this marked X-file the *regular R* class while the old background graph still includes C's genuinely retained finer V rows. There are also boundary files reaching C already at j; they are not relabeled regular by this construction.

Choose the actual physical endpoint of the e-operation, (B=0) in (1.50), with C's real compatible pure-gauge data and zero intervening fluctuations. Vary the genuine top e-links near A by a complex Cartan array of uniform size \(\delta=c g_e^2\), with support in this top band and vanishing on a full top neighborhood of C's integrated cut. Keep the earlier fine C variables fixed. Assume on that disc that the literal axial–Landau output of (1.85)–(1.87) on the released inner component has e-unit potential change at most \(C(1+R_e)^P\delta\) in its prescribed representation, including its complete raw h-means and compensating endpoint phases. Under this extra input, throughout a smaller fixed multiple of that disc, the last R-origin pair on \(X_A\) has old-unit native size at most
\[
 h^{\rm mixed}_{j,e}\le C(1+d_X+R_e)^P L^{j-e}\delta,
 \qquad c_*\mu_j-h^{\rm mixed}_{j,e}\ge c_*\mu_j/2 .          \tag{4.153}
\]
The comparison is at the physical preparation vertex, with all previous source-only walls allowed on the admitted old chart. It keeps the true finer pins in C; no assertion that its old graph equals the new global graph is needed.

**Proof.** At zero the data after A's healing have an actual flat orbit modulo C's compact nodal spike. The new second R physical graph at C and the *previous* old mixed graph agree with their own true pure-gauge targets at this reference. The previously integrated nonflat A plaquette has disappeared into the old coefficient; it is not a new determining row. Perturb only the top coarse variables near A. The innermost V″ rows in (1.50) remain the selected fixed variables. The h-mean targets there come from the actual axial–Landau representative \(U_0^{(AL)}\); its designation is a gauge choice, independent of setting old fluctuations to zero. The following released critical-equation estimate addresses the top data. The estimate for their complete physical axial–Landau representative is the separate hypothesis just stated. U₀ is the genuine LFRI (1.79) background obtained by minimizing in the e-unit rectangle Λ with the top exterior held fixed. Its released Λ-coordinates have the positive Hessian and fixed-size inverse of LFRII (1.7)–(1.9); the action has no inverse coupling in that critical equation. The map from all prescribed remaining top data to the force on Λ is locally Lipschitz with a uniform constant up to a polynomial in the number of e-cubes in Z. At the flat base its derivative is the bilinear Wilson form on two actual top-target responses. Their e-unit first-derivative bounds follow by native density summation in (190). For nearby small data use the same analytic variational expansion (172)–(180), the fixed number of released top coordinates and the bounded inverse in the contraction on p.359. The possible geometric polynomial is \((1+R_e)^P\), and \(\delta R_e^P\) is uniformly weak. To see why a fine power is not needed in the action differentiation, on a fixed native analytic top-target disc the first response to a top datum and its e-unit differences are bounded, with the exponential native kernel. Where Z has a refined boundary, give this top datum the extra native weight \(\sigma^{-2}\) of §4.15: it is at scale one, the admissible fixed spacing absorbs two scale powers, and there is zero forcing at the fine boundary. The resulting fine physical derivative bound is uniform (its native first-difference row has \(\sigma^2\)). Native density sums of the Wilson curl form, or the analytic Wilson action with its genuine \(\eta_e^4\) volume per plaquette, cost at most a fixed power of the e-unit Z-volume. The second derivative bound needed for the finite Λ implicit step follows on a smaller genuine analytic ball by Cauchy. This is the physical finite-rectangle lemma of p.359, not an assumption of independent current analyticity for a source ratio.

Accordingly the full top target change in \(U_0\), including the minimizing Λ values, is at most \(b_* = C(1+R_e)^P\delta\). In the compatible Landau response the one-scale weight on (190) controls the physical potential change. Passing through the specific two gauge steps in (1.83)–(1.87), with the actual prescribed traces, requires the stated axial–Landau hypothesis on this varying-exterior family. The auxiliary fine boundary rows of its minimal window are unchanged since the perturbation is away from that boundary; a genuine top row has its original weight one. Hence each actual h-target from \(M^\bullet U_0\) in (1.50) changes by at most \(C L^{h-e} b_*\), by the complete commuting prefix length. In the part of \(\Lambda^c\) that remains top the prescribed target change is \(O(b_*)\), and all surviving finer fixed V″ rows there are unchanged. Apply the old/native density estimate now on the *new* mixed \(B''_e\) with weight \(w_h=L^{e-h}\): the weighted data just proved have supremum \(C b_*\). Include all actual exterior and zero-target restrictions. The first derivative bound on this source-free variational problem implies a fine Cartan link change of at most \(C\eta_e b_*\) globally in a physical relative representative. Cartan compatibility permits using this representative, with all endpoint node phases, in the next genuine raw means. A real compact initial pure gauge is transferred together with its entire frame. The quantifiers here are only for the stated fixed pure-gauge remaining C data and physical complex top disc.

Now replay the actual **old** j-hierarchy, including every original finer C row of depth h. Each h-link changes by at most \(C L^{h-e}b_*\); this includes h=0 if such an exterior row is prescribed. This follows from its own true raw j/h path length and inherited endpoint phases, not from identifying the two hierarchies. Its size with the corresponding old-native weight \(L^{j-h}\) is at most \(C L^{j-e} b_*\). The original (7),(172) give the old branch about the actual mixed real orbit. One more weighted response summation on the old graph gives an old-unit potential change bounded by \(C L^{j-e} b_*\). Near \(X_A\) the active old layers are genuinely at top j: its whole old halo avoids the old C rough rectangle. The first-difference and Wilson-current rows there have the same bound on their own complete stars, by the local regularity (9),(10),(190) or the actual physical constrained multiplier calculation of §4.12. For the latter one may use the local gauge-invariant scalar/current row; the direction is Cartan and no derivative of the raw axial inverse between unrelated gauges is inferred. A centered local gauge and the whole finite source halo cost only another fixed polynomial in d_X. The p-subminima for the earlier native member tests reproduce W on those stars: their variations fix all intersected *old* j-rows and the actual fine trace; they do not alter the distant C constraints. The conversion and radial verification of §4.76 therefore apply with \(h=h^{\rm mixed}_{j,e}\). Since \(g_e/g_j=1+O(1/x_j)\), \(R_e\) is logarithmic in \(x_j\), and \(g_e^2(1+R_e)^P/\mu_j\to0\), this is a strict old-radius margin, independent of the microscopic mesh and of the actual chosen top direction inside the specified support. It also bounds the already integrated stable scalar by \(C a_{j,X}(h^{\rm mixed}_{j,e}/\mu_j)^2\) with the retained original normalization. This last gauge-invariant result is separate from any all-history varying-bath estimate. ∎

### 4.82 A whole-torus boundary carrier on the consecutive-return graph

**Proposition 4.95 (P/L for the member implication, C on the varying-exterior fixture of §4.81).** In §4.81 take the maximal connected localization domain \(X=T\) of the old j-partition on a divisible torus. It meets the still-live C region, so the applicable class is CMP119 (2.40)–(2.42). The assertion bounds every filed coefficient of this support without requiring a particular spanning Ursell coefficient to be nonzero. Keep its real fluctuations at their selected zero values. In old j-units let \(\sigma_n=L^{n-j}\), \(b=C(1+R_e)^P L^{j-e}\delta\), and \(\mu_{\min}=\min_{1\le n\le j}\{\alpha_{0,n},\alpha_{1,n}\}\). At the physical vertex on the real Cartan target ball of §4.81, the native inequalities (2.34)–(2.39) have at least \(c_*\mu_{\min}/2\) spare radius in their respective dimensionless units. Their invariant values extend to the complex physical disc with the same bounds in the compatible representative, with the prescribed endpoint frames carried together.

**Proof.** The replay of §4.81 has raw h-angle changes bounded by \(b\sigma_h\) on the entire old mixed graph. Write \(A\) for the Cartan potential relative to the retained pure-gauge orbit. The old weighted inverse and contraction used in (4.153) bound its native potential and first difference. Thus on every genuine n-layer
\[
 \sigma_n|A|+\sigma_n^2|\nabla A|
    +\sigma_n^2\eta_j^{-2}|1-W(\partial p)|
    +\sigma_n^3|J_j(W)|\le C b .                                      \tag{4.154}
\]
The third term follows from the complete fine plaquette word. For the last one, test the stationary equation with a native unit angle trial at s. Curvature in a cell of length \(\sigma_y\) is \(Cb/\sigma_y^2\); its trial derivative is \(C(\sigma_y\sigma_s)^{-1}e^{-c d(y,s)}\). After its cell volume \(\sigma_y^4\), (4.27)–(4.32) yield \(\sigma_s^3|\lambda_s|\le Cb\sum_y(\sigma_y/\sigma_s)e^{-c d(y,s)}\le Cb\). The path transpose is bounded in its physical pairing and only neighboring old scales meet a fine bond. The genuine endpoint correction vanishes against the complete multiplier. For a fixed bare edge the incident scales are at most \(C_L\eta_j\), so the fine plaquette difference gives the bound there as well.

The earlier readout in CMP119 p.261 uses \(B_p(X)\cup\{\Gamma_i\}_{i<p}\). At \(X=T\) the p-record has no new fine exterior. A variation preserving it fixes all original \(\Gamma_i\) below p and the p-means on the remaining p-region. For any old h-row with \(h>p\), its p-descendants and endpoint blocks stay in that p-region: their total reach is at most \(C_L\sigma_h\) by the raw prefix-length sum, whereas the ordinary buffer separating \(\Omega_h\) from \(\Omega_p^c\) contains the intervening \(h-1\) buffer. Use the original spacing with \(RM_1/L>C_L\) as in (4.23). Thus this variation preserves every old h-row by composition. Rows at h=p are imposed directly. Hence W is already critical on each restricted p-fiber, and uniqueness of the weak orbit gives its p-reconstruction. Analytic continuation yields the same invariant identities in the physical complex representative.

In (2.35) the curvature ceiling has scale \(L^{-2p}(L^{n-p})^{-2}=L^{-2n}=\eta_j^2\sigma_n^{-2}\). In p-units the current is \(\sigma_p^3 J_j(W)\). From the printed (2.37) its ceiling, before the fixed fractional margin, is
\[
 \alpha_{0,n}L^{n-\min(p,n)}(\sigma_p/\sigma_n)^3.                    \tag{4.155}
\]
The extra factor is at least one. Equation (4.154) verifies these two tests for every p, and gives the independent (2.36) radius and the relative tests (2.38)–(2.39) about the compact comparator. At real targets all necessary gauge changes are compact. At complex targets the bounds apply to the compatible representative; an invariant old scalar agrees with the physical scalar on the real ball and hence on their common complex continuation. This does not differentiate an arbitrary raw axial inverse. On this consecutive plateau \(g_n/g_j=1+O(j/x_j)\) uniformly and (2.28) gives \(b/\mu_{\min}\to0\). This proves the margin. The amplitude uses the actual boundary formula (1.99); in this particular j-operation its omitted set is empty, so the tree metric there is the full one. ∎

### 4.83 A local mixed boundary file with all of its earlier readouts

**Proposition 4.96 (P/L for the weighted native implication, C on the varying-exterior fixture of §4.81).** In §4.81 take a rectangular old j-carrier \(X\) enclosing A and C and the entire surviving old lower-layer region, with a ring of at least \(10d M_1\) top j-units between that region and \(\partial X\). Such rectangles are unions of old localization cubes, of size \(O(LMR_e+MR_j)\) in j-units, and fit in the selected torus. They satisfy both intersection conditions of CMP119 (2.41). For a boundary coefficient filed on such an \(X\) keep its original mixed cap (1.99),(2.42), all finer rows and its selected fluctuations. Under the fixed native spacing test (4.156), the full earlier native tests have margin at least \(c_*\mu_{\min}/2\) on the physical commuting family, with the representative convention of Proposition4.95. This bounds each designated file without assuming a spanning coefficient to be nonzero.

**Proof.** Write \(\sigma_y=L^{n(y)-j}\) on the old genuine n-layer and take the top value one at the outer face. Let \(t_y\) be the native mesh of the actual capped p-record \(B_p(X)\cup\{\Gamma_i\}_{i<p}\), measured in j-units. In the interior it retains the genuine layers below p and uses scale \(\sigma_p\) on the remaining cells. The minimal determining set near the outer face refines to the bare exterior, as in (2.13)–(2.15). Its refinement is within \(2d M_1\sigma_p\) of the face, where \(\sigma=1\) by construction. Consequently \(t_y\le C_L\sigma_y\) on every cell and incident star, including at either type of junction. Write \(d_p\) for the p-distance and \(w_y=(\sigma_y/t_y)^3\). The layer separation gives \(|\log(t_y/t_z)|\le C\log L+c_p d_p(y,z)\), \(c_p=C\log L/b_{\rm sep}\). The corresponding estimate for \(\sigma\) initially uses the old distance; this is at most \(C_L(1+d_p)\) by the mesh comparison along a p-path. In the aligned interior \(t_y=\min(\sigma_y,\sigma_p)\); in the outer collar \(\sigma\) is constant. For the fixed localized inverse rate \(c\) used in §§4.12–4.17 the required test is
\[
 \sup_z\sum_y e^{-c d_p(y,z)}
 \max\left\{w_y/w_z,w_z/w_y,(t_y/t_z)^4(\sigma_z/\sigma_y)^3\right\}\le C. \tag{4.156}
\]
The preceding layer inequalities and the slower-rate native counting theorem give this whenever the combined scale exponents fit in the spare difference of rates. This condition concerns the once-chosen original spacing, not a change to any individual collar.

Use \(F=W\) as the reference, with its actual trace, for the p-reconstruction at \(M^\bullet W\). Its relative target is zero on every row including the synthetic collar. By (4.154) its force is at most \(Cb/\sigma_y^3\). Its native curvature and first-order sizes in the p-window are small by \(t_y\le C_L\sigma_y\). The forcing in the true zero-target stationary equation therefore satisfies \(t_y^3|J(F)|\le Cb(t_y/\sigma_y)^3\). Apply the contraction of §§4.14–4.17 with weight \(w_y\). The first two ratios in (4.156) control the weighted inverse and nonlinear partition sum (189); the derivative of the nonlinear term retains its factor \(C\epsilon\) on the original native-small ball. For the relative p-potential \(Y\) this gives
\[
 t_y|Y|+t_y^2|\nabla_FY|\le Cb(t_y/\sigma_y)^3,\qquad
 |{\cal F}(F^Y)-{\cal F}(F)|\le Cb\,t_y/\sigma_y^3,                   \tag{4.157}
\]
where \({\cal F}\) is the transported Wilson plaquette in curvature units. The second inequality follows from its original finest word. The total p-curvature in j-units is bounded by \(Cb/\sigma_y^2\).

Use the p-window's genuine unit-angle trial \(K_z\). In \(DS(F^Y)[K_z]\) the term \(DS(F)[K_z]\) costs
\(Cb\sum_y t_y^4\sigma_y^{-3}t_z^{-1}e^{-c d_p(y,z)}\le Cb\,t_z^3/\sigma_z^3\)
by (4.156); all action stars of an unfixed bond are included. In the difference of the two Wilson variations the curvature difference \(Cb t_y/\sigma_y^3\) multiplies the trial derivative bounded by \(C(t_y t_z)^{-1}e^{-c d_p(y,z)}\). The transport variation multiplying the original curvature costs at most \(C b^2 t_y^2/(\sigma_y^5t_z)\) times that same exponential. Both bounds sum by (4.156) with the original \(t_y^4\) measures. Thus \(|\lambda_z|\le Cb/\sigma_z^3\). The path transpose meets only boundedly many neighboring cells of comparable old mesh; its full endpoint term cancels by (4.32). At a fixed bare edge difference the incident curvature change from (4.157) with the factor \(\eta_j^{-1}\); its incident \(t_y\) are at most \(C_L\eta_j\). This gives the same \(Cb/\sigma_y^3\) for the p-current in j-units. Converting to the actual p-units yields precisely (4.155). The other native tests concern F itself. This proves the local member margin without extending a variation across the genuine synthetic boundary.

Finally let \(F_\lambda=\exp(i\eta_j\lambda A)F_0\) in the compatible commuting chart about the compact pure gauge \(F_0\), and take independent \(J_\lambda=\lambda J_j(W)\). Each p-record uses its own raw means and the trace of this same \(F_\lambda\). For \(|\lambda|\le c_*\mu_{\min}/b\) its curvature and relative potential have (4.154) with \(C|\lambda|b\) in place of \(Cb\). So does its uncritical current: differencing the cubic sine remainder costs at most \(C\eta_j^3(|\lambda|b/\sigma_y^2)^3\) in j-current units. Its dimensionless value is \(O((|\lambda|b)^3)\) since \(\eta_j\le\sigma_y\). The same weighted zero-target correction verifies every p-test throughout the enlarged radial disc. For a gauge-invariant constant-subtracted scalar at zero fluctuation, global diagonal color invariance kills its first jet at the compact flat orbit. With its actual old analytic cap \(a_{j,X}\), Schwarz gives
\[
 |B_{j,X}(W,J_j(W),0)-B_{j,X}(F_0,0,0)|
             \le C a_{j,X}(b/\mu_{\min})^2 .                           \tag{4.158}
\]
Rows carrying independent lower, linear or contact contributions must keep their separate grades. This estimate preserves the mixed boundary cap and uses the actual old normalization. ∎

### 4.84 Absolute old independent-current anchors across the surviving fine component

**Corollary 4.97 (P for the current-row implication, C on the varying-exterior fixture of §4.81).** In Proposition4.96 fix an invariant old scalar with its given analytic cap \(a_{j,X}\). In a fixed orthonormal color basis, let n(b) denote the actual old layer of each independent current bond b, and let \(\partial_{b,A}\) differentiate its physical j-unit component. The preceding local boundary margin yields the dimensionless absolute row
\[
 \sum_{b,A}\alpha_{0,n(b)}\sigma_{n(b)}^{-3}
      |\partial_{b,A} B_{j,X}(W,J_j(W),0)|
      \le C a_{j,X}\,b_*/\mu_{\min},                                  \tag{4.159}
\]
where \(b_*=C(1+R_e)^P L^{j-e}\delta\) is the input size called b in §4.82. It does not count the number of fine old sites inside C. The stronger marked anchor weights of a specific record remain its own requirement.

**Proof.** Along the entire radial disc of (4.158) take the *independent* perturbation \(J_\lambda+sH\), with \(H_{b,A}=\alpha_{0,n(b)}\sigma_{n(b)}^{-3}u_{b,A}\) and \(\sup|u_{b,A}|\le1\). The printed (2.36) retains radius \(|s|\le c_N\), since (4.154)–(4.157) consume at most half its fractional ceiling. This perturbation makes no change to (2.37), whose current is reconstructed from the field. Cauchy gives a uniform cap \(C a_{j,X}\) for the first s derivative on that radial disc, for every simultaneous phase choice u. It vanishes at \(\lambda=0\) by diagonal color invariance, after the one common compact gauge of the flat orbit. Schwarz at the physical point \(\lambda=1\) gives the factor \(b_*/\mu_{\min}\). Taking the phases to maximize the complex scalar gives exactly the sum in (4.159). The finite basis size costs only a constant. No factor of the torus volume, count of current sites, or inverse rare mass is introduced. For an old scalar already analytic and bounded in a *specified* independent source disc, Cauchy in that source may be performed on the same product region, with precisely its source-radius cost; this does not prove that another marked history has such a disc. ∎

### 4.85 The released representative and a genuinely nonzero innermost wall

**Source correction (L).** In (1.50),(1.53),(1.61) \(U_0^{(AL)}\) is the axial–Landau representative of the released minimum (1.79), independent of choosing the old fluctuation variables to be zero. LFR I p.197 first fixes the axial gauge of \(M^e(U_0)=V_\Lambda\) in the e-block rectangle. Next it constructs the axial fine representative of \(U_0\), replays its true data \(M^\bullet U_0\) in (1.85), and takes their Landau solution about the identity in (1.86),(1.87). The accompanying transformation acts also on the inner retained variables and their boundary frames. The compatible response of the released critical equation bounds the physical orbit in §§4.81–4.84. The comparison of the raw data of this axial–Landau representative on the whole varying-exterior disc is an extra input there. The first homogeneous quantum membership argument of §4.74 uses none of these released coordinates.

There is a useful *different perturbation* on the same two-seed geometry. Set the top exterior of the second released rectangle \(\Lambda\) identically equal to one, keep the compact C spike and earlier selected fluctuations as in §4.81, and vary a new top return variable \(V'_e\) on an internal nontree link of \(\Lambda\). Take the variation Cartan and of modulus \(0<|v|\le c g_e^2\) in the original weak selection chart. Such an internal off-tree bond exists in a rectangular component containing a plaquette; take a component with this strict clearance. At the input use the entire one-link word, so it can be a genuinely curved perturbation. Its fixed axial tree values remain one. The released top minimizer is formed before this new coordinate is inserted. With the flat exterior it is exactly the flat orbit: its action is zero, the weak minimizer is unique, and both prescribed axial and Landau representatives about one are the identity. Thus \(V_\Lambda^{(A)}=U_0^{(AL)}=1\) in this fixture. The lower C spike lies in a genuine integrated old coordinate; transform its complete compact frame with it when using the flat comparison, leaving the reintroduced top link unaffected. No selector is dropped in this pointwise assertion.

By LFRII p.374, (1.63)–(1.64), the actual \(H_e\) now has the nonzero source \(-\log V'_e/i\) on that very link. A preparation wall of this function is eligible outside \(\Lambda\). The small input on the synthetic skin of \(B_e(Y_3)\) is the product of the based frame at its two endpoints, the averaged \(H_e(s)\) word and \(M^\bullet U_e\), in the order of (1.63). Its interior inputs on \(\Lambda\) are instead \(V_\Lambda^{(A)}=1\). Setting \(V'_e=1\) kills this marked perturbation. At that point the pure C data, with all frames carried simultaneously, determine the same flat orbit in the compared regions. The perturbation is not the \(\Lambda^c\) variation of (4.153). In particular the AL issue there cannot be removed by setting fluctuations to zero.

**Geometry.** Choose the e-partition supports \(Y_i\) enclosing the full returned component, as in (1.61)–(1.63), with their exterior faces in the ordinary e-layer. Restrict to the records with one rectangle and the one-rectangle buffers of §§4.11,4.16. The actual distance from \(\Lambda\) to any synthetic skin then used in this comparison is at least \(D=c MR_e-CM_1\) in e-units. This is the distance from the *source* to the skin, not a distance required of each marked root. A skin reaching the released region itself is outside this assertion.

### 4.86 What the based-frame variation is at the flat Cartan tangent

**Lemma 4.98 (P/L, original one-step averaged-frame variation).** For the flat Cartan tangent write \(P\phi=L^{-4}\sum_{x\in B(y)}\phi(x)\). CMP99, *Propagators in a background field*, (3.18),(3.19),(3.113), gives this as the infinitesimal gauge average. In the commuting logarithmic branch the explicit gauge average displayed below (3.113) is the exponential of this arithmetic mean, since conjugation by the reference Cartan words is the identity on this subalgebra. The group mean of the shortest contours of CMP109 (0.8),(0.11) has at identity the uniform path average; for the particular admissible mean (0.10) this identity is exact on a commuting logarithmic chart.

At one axial level let \(c(x)\) be the signed sum of the actual relative tangent along its normalized axial path from y to x, or the corresponding average over its prescribed shortest paths. For the sign convention \(A^u=A-d\phi\), the restored axial path condition is \(\phi(x)-\phi(y)=c(x)\). If \(P\phi=0\), the unique phase is
\[
 \phi(x)=c(x)-L^{-4}\sum_{x'\in B(y)}c(x'),\qquad
 |\phi|_\infty\le 2dL\,\max_{\rm block}|\dot A_{\rm angle}|.          \tag{4.160}
\]
Zero at the coarse site would give a different phase. On the homogeneous iterated centered axial tangent (CMP95 (1.16)–(1.20)), the identity at successive levels is
\[
 (P^\ell\phi)(x)=(P^{\ell+1}\phi)(y)
        +c_\ell(x)-L^{-4}\sum_{x'} c_\ell(x'),                         \tag{4.161}
\]
where \(c_\ell\) uses the actual \(\ell\)-averaged field and that level's contours. This follows by applying the one-step relation to \(Q^\ell A\), using \(Q^\ell d\phi=d(P^\ell\phi)\) in its own scaled units. Summing from \(\ell=0\) to the native top at which \(P^h\phi=0\) gives the unique linear phase at every site. Centered averages have physical sup norm at most one, so \(|\phi|\le C\sum_{\ell<h}\sigma_\ell\|A\|=O(\sigma_h\|A\|)\) in a homogeneous physical block. At the raw rather than centered link readout restore the original endpoint prefixes. A deliberately crude bound for their sum is \(C_L^h\|A\|_{\rm local}\): each path substitution has boundedly many segments in a fixed L-block, and repeated physical lengths are at most \(C_L^h\). No determinant of dimension \(L^{4h}\) enters this bound.

Consequently the derivative of the actual skin product in (1.63) at the flat point includes, in addition to the two field derivatives,
\[
 \dot m_{\rm skin}(b)=\dot M^\bullet U_e(b)
       +\widetilde Q_b(\eta_e\dot H_e(s))
       +\dot\phi_s(b_+)-\dot\phi_s(b_-).                              \tag{4.162}
\]
This sign follows by differentiating the original \(u_-^{-1}(\cdots)u_+\). A common site value for each shared endpoint is essential. Formula (4.160) gives its actual one-step contribution at that level; (4.161) gives the homogeneous iterated tangent under its displayed gauge conditions. The individual endpoint values need not be zero. This argument does not identify an unknown mixed relative AX section with CMP95's global centered recursion. CMP102 (124) specifies \(R_0^\ell u=1\), but its full relative axial conditions refer separately to [6](1.19); CMP109 pp.265–266 refers the inverse to CMP98 (106). Local analyticity alone does not give a depth-uniform bound for that nonlinear mixed section.

**First skin estimate.** On the flat, post-healing homogeneous e-graph of (1.63), the unweakened first response to the reinstated link has exponential off-source decay by CMP102 (190). Its homogeneous weakened first Green response has the same off-source bound with a smaller exponent as long as the original walk majorant converges at the required simultaneous wall radii: in the original localized walk, every contributing chain connects the forcing link to the observed block, and each cell's factor occurs at most once per chain's union. The complete walk estimate, rather than the unweakened kernel in isolation, is required here. For a single wall in its original bounded complex disc, each walk is multiplied by at most \(\max(1,|s|)\). Thus its absolute already convergent unweakened *walk majorant* still controls it with the same spatial exponential after a fixed decrease. For a first-order term from that one homogeneous wall, (4.160)–(4.162) bound the tangent skin field by \(C_L^e e^{-cD}\|\dot v\|\), with an additional fixed power of the inverse fine mesh permissible for finite spatial differences. The presence of the endpoint term does not remove the spatial exponential. This is an estimate for the genuine innermost tangent and its original nonzero seed. Extension through (1.62) and (1.61) to an arbitrary nonlinear mixed wall, including their bases and relative AX sections, is a further claim.

### 4.87 Both free indices on the enclosing actual mixed file

**Printed metric.** In (1.67) the intermediate localizations use the region Z shown there. The *final* (1.99), printed p.390, has \(d_{j,\bigcup Y_i}(X)\), with gain \(1+\beta/2\), and \(c_1=e^{-p_j}\) if X misses those newly still-live bodies. A first j-return with A healed and C ineligible has no such \(Y_i\), hence its printed (1.99) metric is the full \(d_j\) even for a file classified B because it meets C. Replacing that metric in an upper bound by \(d_{j,Z}\) with a larger omitted Z gives a valid weaker estimate; the enclosing-rectangle computations below sometimes make that weakening. It must not be read as the exact printed metric or as evidence that every such file has genuinely short metric.

**Proposition 4.99 (P/L, two absolute indices paid from the actual carrier on this geometry).** In the consecutive geometry of §4.81 choose the enclosing rectangular old file of §4.83 with a full ordinary subcube of side at least \(cLMR_j\). This can be done by taking one more e-partition width on the A side; the torus is much larger. The original j-partition includes this rectangular carrier. Suppose its old B-file has the convergent first-return marked bound \(K e^{-(1+\epsilon)\kappa d_{j,Z}(X)}\) (a permissible weakening of the printed metric in this case) on its own native chart, where \(\epsilon>0\) is a fixed fraction of the spare in LFRII (1.97),(1.99). In particular one can use the actual first-return mark on a complete primitive \(H\) stencil inside the b=1 A component. Its links are retained and integrated compact coordinates throughout the ordinary waits up to the return. Its initial one- or two-source cap is \(C(1+g_0^{-2})^2\) for specified local homes, as in Theorem3.11. Inserting bounded-source exponentials in the distinguished A-operation before expansion differentiates the true bracket (1.72),(1.98). Its logarithm supplies the normalization/contact subtraction. On \(|\eta|\le c/(1+g_0^{-2})\) the additional factor and its absolute majorant are bounded by \(e^c\); they multiply a distinguished occupied-body contribution and do not alter the placement sum. The estimates (1.95)–(1.99) with a strictly smaller spare exponential thus give the stated marked cap for this carrying subcase. This reasoning does not turn a previously integrated arbitrary marked file into a compact stencil.

Give the independent current coordinates their actual dimensionless radii \(h_b=\alpha_{0,n(b)}\sigma_{n(b)}^{-3}\) times a fixed spare fraction. If an external index is instead an original compact source home, give it the actual old auxiliary source radius. On a simultaneous product region preserving these radii, for any two sets of such indices \(u,v\), the *entrywise absolute sum*, with all finite polynomial offset moments required by the MT37/40 rows and full old carrier weight, obeys
\[
 e^{\kappa d_j(X)}\sum_{u,v} (1+\operatorname{off}_{u,v})^m
        h_u h_v\,|\partial_u\partial_v B_{j,X}|
       \le C_m K\,\exp[-c_\epsilon R_j^4].                         \tag{4.163}
\]
Here the offset is in the original units of the specified record; it can even be measured in the finest lattice units in this bound. Coincident indices and the independent lower/contact representatives, when taken with their own caps, have their usual factorial or finite projection factors. An additional exponential weight for attaching a fixed number of homes measured in j-units costs at most \(\exp(C LR_j)\) on this file. Formula (4.163) does not assign an exponential in the old *bare-site distance* through a collapsed mixed component. No such weight follows from (1.99).

**Proof.** By (1.67) the relative tree must meet each old M-cube of \(X\setminus Z\). On the ordinary subcube choose one out of every three cubes in each coordinate; these disjoint closed cubes have separations at least M. A contained connected tree meeting all of them has length at least \(cM(LR_j)^4\), as follows by packing disjoint radius-\(M/4\) portions of the tree. Thus \(d_{j,Z}(X)\ge c(LR_j)^4\). Conversely extending its tree to meet all omitted cubes costs \(C\#\{M\text{-cubes of } X\cap Z^+\}\). There are only the two original small A,C rectangles in the first j-operation, each of side at most \(CMR_j\) in j-units. Boundary allowances add at most \(C R_j^4\) cubes. Therefore \(d_j(X)-d_{j,Z}(X)\le C R_j^4\). Choose the fixed large L of this two-seed construction with \(C<c\epsilon L^4/4\), keeping the original prescribed spacing. Alternatively use the native relative weight itself, which requires no comparison with the full tree; for a fixed L one can enlarge the rectangular X by a fixed factor to achieve the same fraction. This choice selects an actual original carrier, rather than altering the cutoff or a chart.

At most \(C(1+LR_j)^4 L^{4j}\) fine bond/color positions lie in the full old X with its fixed enlargements. Each offset in its literal units is at most \(C(1+LR_j)L^j\). Apply the two-variable Cauchy formula on its *actual* native product region to every ordered entry, using two independent variables also for two sources in the original gas. The expense from their cardinalities, moments and any fixed old-age or coupling powers is at most \(\exp(C_m[j+\log x_j+\log(1+R_j)])\), where \(x_j=g_j^{-2}\). The earlier pre-entry age is no larger than j along this history. Since \(j=1+R_j\) on the plateau and \(R_j\gtrsim(\log x_j)^2\), the spare \(c_\epsilon R_j^4\) pays it after the displayed full old weight. This proves (4.163) with both free indices, preserving their actual derivative radii. In particular a shrinking raw input disc must still be divided out in its own units. Compact projections with finitely many specified source moments cost only the stated polynomial. A directional \(\ell^\infty\) test alone would not imply the absolute matrix sum.

At zero physical perturbation on the flat-exterior fixture of §4.85 the entire old mixed product region for the independent current/source variables is genuine: its base fine field is the compact pure gauge, the p-minima there agree with it, and \((2.36)\) is independent of these reconstructed currents. Thus there is an actual two-index old source/current estimate there, including contacts of the first-return marked gas. The same implication works on the compatible physical comparison of §§4.81–4.84 if its missing AL input is verified, and on any further native disc where both the composed physical re-readout and the marked scalar are indeed analytic. The differentiations of \(\Gamma_s\) with respect to *two incoming physical* variables require the corresponding physical input disc or two anchor-summable reconstruction kernels. They do not follow just from the independent-coordinate estimate (4.163).

### 4.88 Physical current after a small spliced target

**Lemma 4.100 (P, coarse tolerance for the actual reconstructed current on the consecutive graph).** Work in j-units on the old mixed hierarchy of §§4.81–4.83, with the original fixed spacing. Suppose its actual raw h-angle changes from the compact pure-gauge baseline, including prescribed fine trace, are bounded by \(b'\ll1\) on each complete old row. No extra factor \(\sigma_h\) is required for this estimate. On the commuting compatible branch the stationary solution obeys
\[
 \sigma_n^2 |{\cal F}_j(W)|+\sigma_n^3|J_j(W)|\le C b' ,
 \qquad
 \sigma_n^3 \sigma_p^{-3}|J_p(U_{p,X}(M^\bullet W))|\le C b'.       \tag{4.164}
\]
The last bound is for the enclosing mixed local file and (4.156). For the first bound apply (172),(190) to the genuine old hierarchy with its *unweighted* native norm, giving \(\sigma_n|A|+\sigma_n^2|\nabla A|\le Cb'\). Test its full Wilson derivative against the actual compatible unit-angle trials and restore the raw transpose using (4.32), exactly as in (4.154). This is a bound for the action current of the resulting minimum, not a Cauchy variation of an independent J. For the second bound take W with its true trace as the reference of every p-window and use (4.156)–(4.157); its relative target defect is zero. The printed (2.37) has the additional harmless factor in (4.155). Differentiating along a physical input on a smaller genuine holomorphic disc costs its own radius, by Cauchy applied to this current.

In particular even a loss \(L^{C e}\) from finite mesh conversions on the nested skins can be tolerated here **if** each such loss multiplies the actual transmission \(e^{-cD}\) from the localized nonzero source. On the consecutive plateau \(e=2+R_2\) one needs \(cD> C e\log L+C'\log(1+R_e)\), with constants for the finite number of current/source derivatives; the native spacing and M must meet this fixed test once. Under this inequality \(b'=C L^{Ce}(1+R_e)^{C'}e^{-cD}\delta\ll\mu_{\min}\) for the explicit \(\delta=c_0g_e^2\). Then (4.164), (4.157) and the radial test of §4.83 give the strict old spare margin and (4.163) pays the full finite two-index count. This is a propagation estimate, not a claim that an absent endpoint decay can be replaced by \(e^{-cD}\).

**Remaining intermediate operator.** For the flat-exterior reinstatement the term to be bounded after (4.162) is the actual current of the split \(U_{1,2}\) in (1.54) projected by \(G_e''\), the h-mean term of the released representative, and the two new frame values at each of the skin rows of (1.62),(1.61). The released representative itself is exactly flat in this fixture. The earlier prepared \(U_{e,\Lambda}(s)\) need not be flat on the next skin. The first homogeneous tangent (4.162) and the absolute double-index cost (4.163) do not settle its nonlinear mixed-frame transformation or its complete incoming physical Hessian. No counterexample to the named I-2 coefficient is obtained.

### 4.89 A matrix Schwarz estimate on the original member range

**Lemma 4.101 (P).** If a holomorphic matrix \(F(z)\) on \(|z|<1\) has \(F(0)\) unitary and \(\|F(z)\|_{\rm op}\le e^m\), \(0\le m\le1\), then
\[
 \|F(z)-F(0)\|_{\rm op}\le C m |z|,\qquad |z|\le1/2 .                \tag{4.165}
\]
Indeed set \(r=e^{-m}\), \(Z=rF(0)^*F\) and \(\Phi=(Z-rI)(I-rZ)^{-1}\). The identity
\(I-\Phi^*\Phi=(1-r^2)(I-rZ)^{-*}(I-Z^*Z)(I-rZ)^{-1}\)
shows \(\|\Phi\|\le1\); its scalar matrix elements and \(\Phi(0)=0\) give \(\|\Phi(z)\|\le |z|\). Now \(\|Z-rI\|\le |z|[1-r^2+r\|Z-rI\|]\). The case \(m=0\) follows by a limit. The estimate applies on every complex line of a polydisc. In particular a compact factor in an allowed member factorization can depend arbitrarily on the evaluation point. It need not be chosen holomorphically.

**Lemma 4.102 (P, an actual top input family).** At the flat retained exterior of §4.85 there is an original physical input ball with radius \(\rho=cg_e\) for arbitrary commuting upper link angles \(v\) supported on the internal free links of \(\Lambda\). On this ball \(M^e U(v)=\exp(iv)\) there and equals the identity on all other upper links, and the released AL minimum is flat. The input is holomorphic on the ball. Its fine potential and its first two physical differences about the identity are bounded by \(C\|v\|_\infty\) in e-units. This is a larger *input* ball used for the estimate; the conclusion below uses \(\delta=c_0g_e^2\).

Here is an explicit construction on the fine torus. For each oriented unit link \(y,y+\widehat\mu\), place a smooth \(\mu\)-potential supported within a fixed small width \(\epsilon\) of its common midface and in a fixed transverse subcube of that face. Normalize the discrete longitudinal integral and transverse average to one (bounded factors for all \(\eta_e\ll\epsilon\)). For the centered \(Q^e\) of (1.18), the diagonal coefficient differs from one by \(O(\epsilon+\eta_e)\): a translated unit segment through the block crosses the whole bump except when its initial longitudinal coordinate is within \(\epsilon+\eta_e\) of a face. Only the two neighboring longitudinal bonds with the same transverse block acquire off-diagonal coefficients, with total \(O(\epsilon+\eta_e)\). Different orientations do not mix in this centered operator. Its coefficient matrix thus has a bounded inverse on \(\ell^\infty\) by Neumann expansion, with constants independent of torus size and depth. Superpose the resulting bumps with these inverse coefficients. Their overlap is bounded and the first two spatial derivatives cost only fixed powers of \(\epsilon^{-1}\).

For the Cartan raw mean, at one step the averaged endpoint prefixes add a site gradient to that centered mean by (0.4) or (0.10)–(0.12) of CMP109. Iterating, the difference from \(Q^e\) is a top node gradient \(dP\); the phase is the sum of the endpoint-prefix averages of \(Q^\ell A\), evaluated at the successive nodes. To see this, use the exact *node-covariance* of the raw next mean on the previous gradient, so the previous phase is restricted to the next nodes, not sent around every new path. Each new average has absolute angle at most \(C\sigma_{\ell+1}\|A\|_\infty\) (\(\sigma_{\ell+1}\) here in e-units). Their sum is bounded by \(C_L\|A\|_\infty\). Interpolate \(P\) to a smooth site function on the fine grid by unit-scale bumps equal to one at their respective nodes and zero at every other node. Subtract its lattice gradient from the trial potential. The true nodal covariance removes exactly \(dP\) and the new trial has the same uniform first two derivative bounds. Thus its **original** upper data, including their outside values and the axial tree, are exactly those prescribed. The fine trial need not have compact spatial support in \(\Lambda\); it is its upper *exterior* which is held flat.

The fine current and plaquettes of this trial are \(O(\|v\|_\infty)\) in e-units. On each of its native p-windows use the trial itself with zero target defect and its actual exterior. Equations (4.41)–(4.42) give the same physical curvature and current bounds down to its synthetic fine collar under the fixed spacing test. The conditions (2.34)–(2.39) for the input at the e-layer therefore hold on \(\|v\|_\infty<\rho\), since all native ceilings on this plateau are at least a fixed multiple of \(g_e\). An independent current on the same new top chart of size \(c g_e\) in e-units is also allowed. In particular complexification here is performed on an actual unmarked member chart, not by extending a real conditional probability estimate.

**Proposition 4.103 (P/L on the native member domain, a finite consecutive-return sector).** Fix the pure C fixture, the enclosing old X, and a determining term of (1.61)–(1.63) whose \(Y_i\) contain C, whose outer faces are in the regular top band, and whose exterior continuation uses the prescribed trial there. Take \(s\) in the localization range of the actual unmarked inclusion (1.65). In particular this gives the comparison at genuine fractional localization values, not just at their binary vertices. The standard complex single-wall test uses the convergent original localization radius; CMP116 (1.7),(1.10)–(1.11) bound the homogeneous H-walk at its complex radius. Any extra auxiliary wall domain beyond the unmarked native inclusion must establish that same range first. For \(\|v\|_\infty\le\delta\) the actual completed raw h-mean on every old row differs from the old compact baseline, in angle, by at most
\[
 b'= C(1+R_e)^P \bar\alpha\,\delta/\rho,\qquad
 \bar\alpha=\max_{i,n\le e}\alpha_{i,n}.                               \tag{4.166}
\]
Its constants do not count the finer C sites. The weaker, unweighted old variational estimate (4.164) then suffices for the actual stationary current. The compatible old scalar and local p-tests have spare radius at least \(c_*\mu_{\min}/2\). For the Cartan parameters the current bound applies to the actual readout, since conjugation by its Cartan frame acts trivially on the current. Gauge-sensitive noncommuting frame derivatives are distinguished below.

**The base and the member mesh.** At \(v=0\) the released orbit, \(V'_e\) and its soft upper frame are identity. The old pure nodal C gauge equals one outside its interior and at higher nodes. Use that one compact transformation, with all its retained endpoint data, to compare the background to the flat orbit. Both sides of the split (1.53) have flat action and identical flat data at their seam; on that seam all determining blocks see the identity. The original relative fluctuations (1.51),(1.54),(1.57),(1.58) have zero seed there and at the all-identity relative base. Consequently the output is the same compact flat orbit for every such localization, with its prescribed C data. This is a comparison of the carrying field on the fixed record, and does not redraw its earlier selectors.

Write \(k=e,\ k_0=e-N_0\). Printed LFRI (1.10)–(1.13) give \(\Omega_m''=\Omega_m\) outside the affected component, and \(\Omega_m''\supseteq\Omega_m\) inside it for \(m\le k_0\): below the first affected layer the domains are equal, and at the intermediate affected layers \(Z''_m=(\Omega_m^{\sim5})^c\cap Z\). In the exceptional top \(N_0\) layers the smallest scale is \(L^{-N_0}\) in e-units, whose inverse is at most a fixed polynomial in \(R_e\) by LFRI p.192. The single ordinary step from j to e leaves the earlier true strata through j as they were at the A-healing birth; on its old top part it can add the new e-layer. There is no newly retained bare component at C: the Q₂ seed first retained first-coarse variables there. Hence every fine link meeting an old h-row's full descendant blocks has controlling physical scale at least \(c L^{h-e}/(1+R_e)^P\) in the original output inequalities (1.64)–(1.69). For the old row this uses the prefix support bound and one-neighbor scale comparison (4.23), with the same fixed spacing. In (1.66) the controlling scale is the final stage e where this improves the bound; the explicit \(L_0^{2N_0}\) costs only another power of \(R_e\). Importantly these printed output inequalities are on the indicated **genuine** \(\Omega_m,\Omega_m''\) layers intersected with \(Y_1\). A synthetic p-cut is part of the determining problem, but is not a replacement for the scale in its amplitude ceiling. A word straddling \(Y_1\) uses its full prescribed exterior; on the specified extension this is the smooth trial at the same or a smaller bound.

**All endpoint phases in the actual output.** In (1.65) the output at a fine link admits the factorization \(\Gamma_b=\exp(i\eta_e A'_b)\bar U_b\) with \(\bar U_b\) compact. On the *whole* input disc of Lemma4.102, (1.64)–(1.69) and the preceding comparison bound its operator norm by \(\exp(m_b)\) with \(m_b\le C\eta_e(1+R_e)^P\bar\alpha/L^{h-e}\). No holomorphic choice of \(\bar U_b\) is necessary. Apply (4.165) to the actual matrix \(\Gamma_b\) along each complex input line through the flat compact base. In the original j-units the improvement is
\[
 \|\Gamma_b(v)-\Gamma_b(0)\|_{\rm op}
       \le C(\eta_j/\sigma_h)(1+R_e)^P\bar\alpha\,\delta/\rho.        \tag{4.167}
\]
It is the product *with* the frame in (1.61), not a Landau-gauge bound substituted for that product. The same argument allows additional independent fine current variations on their genuine small product disc and does not assume the matrices commute. On the old endpoint blocks transport once by the compact base. The true non-Abelian raw-mean estimate (4.33), with this flat comparator and complete prefixes, gives (4.166) uniformly in h. On the Cartan line it also follows directly from the centered/prefix formula in Lemma4.102. The compact node frame at the other endpoint is retained in both estimates.

Now \(b'/\mu_{\min}\le C (1+R_e)^P(\bar\alpha/\mu_{\min})g_e\to0\), as the radius ratios on this weak consecutive plateau are powers of \(\log x_e\). The old true constraints admit the native solution about the compact orbit by CMP102 (172),(190). The Wilson multiplier estimate (4.164) gives its actual old current on every fine C layer; (4.157) gives all earlier p-readouts of the enclosing file with their true exterior. This proves the asserted physical Cartan radius. The proof bounds the complete based output without inferring a bound for a separately removed site phase from compactness on real data. Equation (4.162) is the explicit first variation of that phase difference. For arbitrary gauge-varying observables, or for an individual unpaired nonlinear inverse site value, (4.167) is not that additional estimate. ∎

### 4.90 Repaired total-output conclusion and the off-base absolute physical tensor

**Corollary 4.104 (P/L in the stated finite sector).** The total-output proof applies also to the top-exterior Cartan perturbation of §4.81 supported near A. Use the same smooth right inverse of Lemma4.102, now on those prescribed upper links. At zero the native output again has the compact pure-gauge value. The new released representative varies; it cannot be set to the identity. But every \(M^\bullet(U_0^{(AL)})\) appearing as an imposed inner row in (1.61) is the imposed raw row of the resulting native solution itself. Estimate (4.167) on that row's whole descendant blocks and then (4.33) bound that actual imposed value including its prescribed endpoint transports. This establishes the weaker unweighted \(b'\) control of all original raw data sufficient for the paired curvature/current and scalar bounds, without proving a physical-potential bound for \(U_0^{(AL)}\) at an arbitrary point. At \(e=j+1\) the fixed \(L^{j-e}=L^{-1}\) in (4.153) can be kept by changing the fixed L-dependent constant. Thus the total compatible physical conclusions of (4.153)–(4.159) follow on this admissible native input family even though the earlier separated argument in §4.81 remains conditional. The distinction matters under a further waiting age.

For the specified old marked boundary file take the nonzero reinstated \(v\) of §4.85 in a smaller \(\delta\)-ball. In its original localized native chart allow the independent e-current on every fine bond of \(Y_4\) and arbitrary generator components, initially of size at most \(c\delta\) in e-units. One or two other genuine analytic scalar source coordinates of the compact A birth retain their own previously established radii. Take bounded original localization domains with the full \(Y_4\) contained in \(C_LMR_e\) e-units; no external variable outside this specified carrying record is differentiated in this statement. Extend the current to the larger auxiliary polydisc of size \(\rho=cg_e\). It changes only the independent input test and preserves (2.36); all p-reconstruction tests of the smooth trial are unchanged. The whole matrix Schwarz argument applies on these larger products by complex lines, even though a varied current need not commute with v. On the smaller products it bounds the true old raw data as before. The old compatible solution and multiplier are those of the **same** Wilson action. For a noncommuting probe use the physical trial and transpose (4.27)–(4.32) in the one small relative chart about the compact base. The first two native bounds and the curvature bound of (4.154) follow from (19),(190) with the original relative targets; (4.29),(4.32) give its actual current. The proof of (4.157) for the subsequent p-window uses the covariant derivatives and the full transport-difference term already displayed, and therefore gives its previous bound also with such probes. For an invariant scalar and zero retained old fluctuations the compatible representative gives the physical analytic continuation: on the real parameter and real-wall chart all the restoring gauges are compact; then continue the identity of the physical germs in the prescribed analytic variables. This argument avoids claiming bounds for the derivatives of an arbitrary raw complex site gauge.

The scalar composite on this product is bounded by the old native amplitude \(K e^{-(1+\epsilon)\kappa d_{j,Z}(X)}\). Apply entrywise Cauchy with independent parameters of radii \(c\delta\) for *incoming* e-current slots, including their effect on both \(W\) and \(J_j(W)\). For an incoming first/second source slot actually present in the old marked gas use its original own source-radius cost instead. Each original local coordinate sum over \(Y_4\) costs at most \(C(1+R_e)^4 L^{4e}\) choices before offset weights. Equation (4.163) then proves the weighted absolute two-index bound, with the two actual derivative-radius costs, for this composed physical scalar at the nonzero reinstatement. In particular this operation differentiates the physical current of the constrained readout, and does not hold it fixed at an independent slot. At fixed finite grade the factors \(\delta^{-2}\), the change between physical/canonical units, the original engineering normalization, and every polynomial offset moment are all dominated by the spare \(cR_j^4\) on this one consecutive rectangular file. The first-return log supplies its original numerator/denominator contact before the derivatives are taken. This is a bound on the indicated individually filed coefficient; the new T′-integral still runs over other retained configurations.

For clarity, this result concerns the native outer chart on the explicit upper lift, every chosen home in the indicated \(Y_4\), and the actual member localization range. It does not claim an independent exponential in a bare-site distance across the rough component, analyticity of unrelated external physical frames, or the full general second-R quotient. The named positive old age and lowered grades are maintained on this file using its available geometric reserve. At a short carrier with no ordinary subcube that reserve need not exist. In that case the two physical indices must instead be attached by summable mixed response kernels before taking absolute values.

### 4.91 One more true mixed transition with the same old source file

**Proposition 4.105 (P/L for the specified inherited summand).** Work on a weak plateau with \(R_1=R_2=R_3=R\). Use the b=1 plaquette seed A, and the b=2 and b=3 compact nodal Q seeds C and D from §4.42, on disjoint enlarged neighborhoods. Their initial local words and lifted nodal gauges have disjoint support, and the latter are identity at all nodes beyond their birth. Thus the old regular selections on the other seeds are preserved in the construction of §§4.16,4.42; take ordinary waits on the three separate components. They become eligible successively at \(j=1+R\), \(j+1\), and \(e=j+2\). Put A and D within \(O(L^2MR)\) old j-units, with A in the top regular neighborhood of D's eventual returning component. Put C sufficiently far from both throughout those waits that the entire enclosing rectangular old X and its original old readout enlargements avoid C's R-operation area at \(j+1\). The separation is a fixed multiple of \(L^2MR\) old units and fits the weak reference torus.

At j, A heals, while C and D both have genuinely finer retained coordinates. Take the first-return boundary coefficient \(B_{j,X}\) carrying the literal compact A mark and reaching D. It has the cap and old mixed metric of Proposition4.99. At \(j+1\) the C operation treats this X as disjoint from its returned component. LFRII (1.59)–(1.60) on pp.372–373 keep a term equal to the original summand evaluated at the new determining background and produce other terms whose localization reaches the returned region. Track the former, including its original source sign and grade. Its vacuum difference, if present, is taken on the same old file. None of the proper terms reaching C is discarded from the physical partition; they are different filed summands. This construction extends the calculation of *one exact inherited summand*, not of the entire middle marked integral. Choose C's healed branch and the ordinary remaining D data as above.

At e the D component becomes eligible. Vary the reinstated top field in its own released rectangle with the top exterior flat, or use the small admissible top variation near A of §4.90. The older C data are now absent from the true new determining graph. On replay to the j-graph the old C h-rows are the **re-averaged** data of \(\Gamma_s\); they are not restored to their earlier numerical spike. Their current physical scale cannot be worse than the one used in (4.167): outside the D component C's healed region is regular at top; near D the ordinary lower strata have persisted, and the exceptional last \(N_0\) scales cost only \(L^{N_0}\). The old j-top region differs from e by at most two steps. In particular \(\Omega_m''\supseteq\Omega_m^{\rm old}\) below \(e-N_0\) wherever the comparison could otherwise cross more than one native layer, by (1.11),(1.12) and the separated choices. The unmarked range proof of (4.167), the p-current calculation (4.164), and the two-index bound (4.163) now apply with \(r=L^{-2}\). The actual upper extension and the Cartan/compatible-frame distinctions are those of §§4.89–4.90. In the metric comparison the three old Z rectangles cost at most \(C R^4\) cubes; choose the original enclosing old support with its \(c L^2 R\)-side ordinary subcube to retain the spare. No new rare reserve has been assigned to the old A coefficient at the intermediate C healing. This establishes that one intervening genuine R-step can be crossed without restarting the old birth clock. It does not bound the independently generated terms connecting the mark to C or an arbitrary number of such intervening sums. ∎

**Separate phase and remaining scope.** On a skin row at actual mesh h, the first-order phase in (4.162) contributes \(L^{j-h}[\dot\phi_s(b_+)-\dot\phi_s(b_-)]\) to the physical potential in old j-units. In the homogeneous tangent comparison of §4.86 its sup is at most \(C L^{j-h} C_L^e e^{-cD}\|\dot v\|\), and its h-dimensionless size lacks the factor \(L^{j-h}\). Equations (4.165)–(4.167) bound the complete, nonlinear spliced row on the original member range. An estimate for the separate nonlinear inverse gauge on a general mixed block, or its incoming tensor before multiplication by the other factors of (1.61), still requires the relative AX dictionary or an equivalent norm theorem. For a noncompact base value use the relative increment (4.171) and check the absolute margin at that base separately. These are real limitations on extending this sector; the present coefficients supply no violation of (9.1).

### 4.92 The full reinstatement radius at the fixed logarithmic hierarchy

**Proposition 4.106 (P/L, sharp native scale comparison on this record).** The factor \((1+R_e)^P\) in (4.166) can be removed on the consecutive component shapes just treated, with the same genuine p-window spacing test. The auxiliary source radius can be taken to be \(\rho=c\mu_{\min}\). At the fixed equal-exponent choice \(q_0=q_1=q>p_0\) of LM31–LM34a the estimate therefore covers the entire original upper reinstatement size \(M_0\epsilon_e\), not only the \(c g_e^2\) subdisc. The assertion concerns this reinstatement and the stated incoming probes, with all earlier retained variables as specified. The full independent member disc for every earlier rough coordinate is a different quantifier.

**Proof of the scale comparison.** Write \(m\) for the ordinary e-history layer at a fine link of an old h-word (\(m=e\) includes the top). On its complete descendant and prefix blocks \(m\ge h-1\) by the original lower-layer buffer and (4.23). This remains so after the intervening separated C healing of §4.91, which removes C's genuine finer pins while changing no lower D rows. Take \(k_0=e-N_0\); the last preliminary stage in LFRI p.190 has index e. When the point belongs to \(\Omega_{k_0+1}\), (1.66) bounds the \(A'\) row on the old h-word, in its own angle units, by
\[
 C\,L^{h-e}L_0^{2(e-m)}\alpha_{1,e}
       \le C L (L_0^2/L)^{e-m}\alpha_{1,e}\le C_L\bar\alpha.         \tag{4.168}
\]
Here \(L_0^2\le L/3\) is the actual fixed requirement on p.191. On the complementary region \(h\le k_0+1\). By (1.11),(1.12), a controlling \(m'\le k_0\) of (1.64) there must satisfy \(m'\ge h-1\). It contributes at most \(L\bar\alpha\). For a controlling \(m'>k_0\), including the exceptional final piece, one has instead
\[
 L^{h-m'}L_0^{2(m'-k_0)}\alpha_{1,m'}
        =L^{h-k_0}(L_0^2/L)^{m'-k_0}\alpha_{1,m'}
        \le L\bar\alpha .                                             \tag{4.169}
\]
These are estimates for the imaginary perturbation of a fine link after multiplication by the length of an h-word. They use the two different original member tests, instead of maximizing their \(L_0\) factors independently. At a boundary fine bond use its incident native cells; one extra neighboring scale costs only a fixed power of L. Thus in (4.167) and (4.166) the only field-amplitude constant needed is \(C_L\bar\alpha\). The old current estimate in (4.164) and all p-tests in (4.156)–(4.157) are already independent of the size of X. The native local gauges in (2.38) are chosen on their own fixed \(CM\sigma_n\) cubes, not on a tree of diameter \(MR_j\).

**Larger disc and all top color directions.** The proof of Lemma4.102 needs the physical trial, its current, and its p-reconstructions to be bounded by a fixed small fraction of every applicable analytic radius. It works for \(\rho=c\mu_{\min}\) with a fixed c from those inequalities. The calculation of the linear right inverse at zero holds in every Lie algebra direction and has bounded fine spatial derivatives through order two. Write \(E\) for that linear lift and \(T(w)=\log(M^e\exp(i\eta_e E w))/i\) with the original raw words. At the identity \(DT=I\) in the physical unit-link normalization. On a fixed absolute complex parameter ball \(T\) is uniformly bounded independently of the number of links and of e: (4.33) applies at the flat comparator to these smooth small physical potentials, with every original contour retained. Cauchy on complex lines in the sup norm and polarization give \(\|T(w)-w\|\le C\|w\|^2\) and \(\|D(T-I)(w)\|\le C\|w\|\) on a smaller fixed ball. The iteration \(w=v-(T-I)(w)\) is a contraction there for all sufficiently small matrix-algebra arrays v and gives a holomorphic solution on their full product ball. The smooth lift \(\exp(i\eta_e E w(v))\) therefore prescribes the exact upper links, also for noncommuting v, with the same physical weak bounds. Outside the specified upper support its raw upper links are exactly identity. This construction keeps the original normalized gauge tree and does not perturb any physical selector. The auxiliary holomorphic enlargement uses the unmarked native extended function at \((U(v),J)\); the actual compact integration over a real \(V'_e\) is never extended past its selector.

It follows on \(\|v\|\le M_0\epsilon_e\) that the consumed old dimensionless radius obeys
\[
 b_{\rm full}\le C_L\bar\alpha\,M_0\epsilon_e/\mu_{\min},
 \qquad
 \frac{b_{\rm full}}{\mu_{\min}}
   \le C_L M_0\,\frac{\bar\alpha\,\epsilon_e}{\mu_{\min}^2}
   =O((\log x_e)^{p_0-q})\longrightarrow0 .                           \tag{4.170}
\]
The last equality uses LM34a's fixed equal exponents and the consecutive plateau; for other fixed exponents keep the preceding explicit ratio. The e-current probes can have the same total sup radius or a fixed smaller fraction of it, and the product used for two-variable Cauchy is obtained by a fixed enlargement of \(M_0\) within the auxiliary analytic chart. Thus the old current is bounded by \(Cb_{\rm full}\sigma_n^{-3}\) on every n-layer, with the genuine earlier p-current given by (4.164). The invariant scalar two-index estimate of Corollary4.104 holds at every point of this complete reinsertion range in its specified outer chart. Real noncommuting reinstatement is included, since its compact gauges preserve physical norms. For complex noncommuting inputs we control the compatible physical scalar and current as stated above. This proof keeps every prior cut and gauge in the *total* output; it does not yield a norm for a separately exposed nonlinear relative site phase. ∎

### 4.93 Increments at a nonreal base and repeated use

**Corollary 4.107 (P, matrix increment without a compact base hypothesis).** Suppose the actual unmarked member holds on a common input disc, with the two singular-value bounds \(e^{-m_b}\le s_{\min}(F_b(z))\le\|F_b(z)\|\le e^{m_b}\) at each fine link; the compact-times-complex member factorization provides both bounds. The base at zero may itself be nonreal. Apply (4.165) to \(F_b(0)^{-1}F_b(z)\), whose value at zero is identity and whose uniform norm is at most \(e^{2m_b}\). This gives
\[
 \|F_b(z)-F_b(0)\|\le C m_b |z|/\rho,\qquad |z|\le\rho/2,             \tag{4.171}
\]
in the fixed original matrix frame. No real positivity or scalar log is needed. This is a bound on the increment from the *actual* base. To prove full admission, that base must still have a verified old spare margin, and its complete old row stencils must be in a common local analytic chart to use the raw mean and constrained response. A history already holomorphic on a single common original disc can be evaluated at its last member in (4.171); one does not multiply a scale constant for every preceding transition. This is not a construction of a common disc for rough histories where it has not been proved. ∎

### 4.94 The nonlinear based contribution on an actual exposed skin row

**Proposition 4.108 (P/L, compensated frame on the original single-wall disc).** Choose the allowed localization summands with \(Y'_2=Y'_3=\varnothing\), \(Y_1=Y_2=Y_3=Y\). Take the enclosing X sufficiently large that the synthetic skin is at top e on its full endpoint-block enlargements and is separated by a closed band of eligible localization cubes from the sources in (1.55),(1.57). Such empty earlier enlargement terms are included in the original expansion (cf. p.376 before (1.67)–(1.68)). Close the corresponding earlier unprotected walls in that band, and vary a genuine last, innermost \(H_e\) localization wall along the chosen \(Y'_4\) as in (1.63). This selects an original determining term; other terms of the physical sum are not subtracted from it for this test.

On the skin the first two relative responses \(H''_e,H_{B_1}\) now vanish *exactly*. Indeed the \(H_{B_1}\) seed is supported on \(Z''_e\) by (1.57); (1.55) puts both current seeds of \(H''_e\) near the inner region after the exact \(Q^*\)-projection. Take the closed band outside these support halos. A kernel chain reaching the skin must cross the band, so has a zero wall factor. Equivalently, on each disconnected exterior component the localized zero-input equation has zero solution by the original small-ball contraction. This is the component rule used in CMP116 (1.9),(1.10). Enlarge the band so this vanishing holds on the whole determining block associated with the skin endpoint. By the block-locality stated before LFRII (1.62), each of those two restoring frames is the identity on that block. Thus on every row actually assigned to the common synthetic skin the final \(\Gamma_s\) has precisely the framed target of (1.63); the two preceding readouts transmit this same raw prescribed datum. Using the same Y is important: distinct translated windows do not have this identity.

Let \(S_s\) be that nonlinear raw row, and \(T_s\) the identical right-hand word of (1.63) with the *two indicated frame factors* set to identity. Keep the original averaged response and the background field in \(T_s\). At the flat baseline both rows equal one on this top skin. In e-unit physical length write \(t_h=L^{h-e}\) for the synthetic row. The \(\Omega_e\) member at the skin uses \(m=e\) in (1.66), with no \(L_0\) loss. From (4.165)–(4.167) and the true raw mean on the full row,
\[
 \|S_s-I\|\le C t_h(\bar\alpha/\mu_{\min})\,\|v\|.                     \tag{4.172}
\]
The fine input of Lemma4.102 gives \(\|M^\bullet U-I\|\le Ct_h\|v\|\) there. The homogeneous \(H_e(s)\) on the post-heal graph has source \(-\log V'_e/i\). Its fixed-radius weakened variational equation, with the original walk bound (CMP116 (1.7),(1.11)–(1.14)), gives a bounded physical potential on the auxiliary native input ball, of size \(C\bar\alpha\); it vanishes at the zero seed and zero independent current. Schwarz on the same input lines bounds its value by \(C(\bar\alpha/\mu_{\min})\|v\|\). Its relative averaged word on this top block satisfies the same bound with \(t_h\), by (4.33) in the smooth upper background and complete prefix lengths. Hence \(\|T_s-I\|\le Ct_h(\bar\alpha/\mu_{\min})\|v\|\). These estimates hold on the original homogeneous single-wall analytic range, with the earlier cuts just specified. On \(\|v\|\le M_0\epsilon_e\), Cauchy on the remaining fixed fraction of the auxiliary disc gives, in the original birth units,
\[
 L^{j-h}\|D_v(S_s-T_s)\|_{\infty\to{\rm op}}
       \le C L^{j-e}\bar\alpha/\mu_{\min},\qquad
 L^{j-h}\|D_v^2(S_s-T_s)\|_{\infty,\infty\to{\rm op}}
       \le C L^{j-e}\bar\alpha/\mu_{\min}^{2}.                        \tag{4.173}
\]
This is a derivative of the actual row change due to its original based axial gauge, evaluated with the other nonlinear factors still in the row. On the commuting subfamily \(S_sT_s^{-1}=u(b_-)^{-1}u(b_+)\) exactly, so the same bounds give the derivatives of the *logarithm of the genuine site-phase difference*. On noncommuting parameters (4.173) gives the compensated matrix contribution; the absolute two-index sum for the filed invariant scalar with such variations uses (4.163), not the directional estimate alone.

**A nonzero spliced datum.** There is an allowed independent outer trial on which the synthetic target itself is nonzero, as distinct from the internal nonzero seed in §4.85. Take a fine link of a complete bare skin plaquette, with all its edges in the common assigned skin, and add a Cartan angle \(\eta_e^3 t\) there. The normalized potential has first two differences \(O(t)\). Its raw e-mean is a small array of size at most \(C|t|\eta_e^2\) by (4.33) (the derivative bound is needed only at zero for this test). Cancel that e-mean with the smooth right inverse. To see that its correction cannot cancel the fine plaquette coefficient, the smooth correction has second spatial differences \(O(|t|\eta_e^2)\), hence contributes only \(O(|t|\eta_e^4)\) to a fine plaquette. On the commuting logarithmic branch both raw averages are additive, so this is an exactly null-upper input. Take independent \(J=0\). At \(V'_e=1\) the relative \(H_e\) at zero argument and zero independent current vanishes (the zero solution of its homogeneous zero-seed contraction). Its skin data are therefore those of this input, with a nonzero plaquette for weak mesh. The fine pulse and its actual exterior trace can be chosen in the available top collar, continuing the smooth correction outside Y if a complete boundary word requires it. The native p-checks hold with the same \(O(t)\) physical current/curvature estimates as in Lemma4.102. Now add a sufficiently small nonzero reinstated field on a nontree link in \(\Lambda\). Holomorphic continuity preserves the nonzero spliced plaquette at any fixed genuine interior single-wall value, while (1.64) also has a nonzero internal source. This example is on the independent outer member chart. It does not assert a lower bound for a projected H-activity, or that a particular coefficient changes sign when s changes. ∎

### 4.95 Consequence for the protected real unprocessed T comparison

**Lemma 4.109 (P/L, one current-block compact rotation).** In the early unprocessed T prefix, let two real constrained Wilson fields have the same \((k+1)\)-target on a protected ordinary cube and the complete raw-prefix enlargements of its interior. Let every tested current block have distance b in upper units from its synthetic collar, and let both fields obey the true regional regularity conditions there. Suppose the common upper field in a single box gauge has small links \(C b\epsilon\), below the absolute local target chart radius. A reflected homogeneous comparison is an example only if it has these same complete upper rows. Then on the tested k-blocks \(V^{(k)}=M^k U_{k+1}\) of (3.10) the two true fields are within
\[
 C(1+b)^P L^{Ck}e^{-cb}                                             \tag{4.174}
\]
after a compact rotation which can be taken constant on each current block, with another fixed L-dependent factor. There is no determinant of the much larger global variational problems in this estimate.
