**Proof.** Localize both physical critical points on the same minimal \((k+1)\)-record, using its true finer collar and its fixed fine trace. Its compatible cubical variations preserve every original global row they meet in the protected part and vanish at the outer seam, so both restrictions are the actual local minima. Put the identical upper data on the box in one gauge. Within each upper block put the two fine potentials in their compact based trees preserving that block's top node. For any finer collar word, internal fine paths have total variation \(C\epsilon\) by the strip bound for weak plaquettes; crossing into the next block can additionally cost the actual shared upper link and this same curvature bound. Each collar word meets only boundedly many such blocks. Hence the distinct complete collar targets are in a common absolute small ball. The upper interior targets agree exactly. Interpolate the collar targets on this one localized graph and apply CMP102 (172),(190). Fine current differences obtained by two further finite differences or compact transport comparisons, and even the crude number of collar input sites, cost at most \((1+b)^P L^{Ck}\). The native distance to the center is at least \(cb\). On a smaller interior cube use the common compatible physical representatives and apply the original \(M^k\), including both endpoint paths and all complete current-block contour stars; a crude \(C_L^k\) loss is included in (4.174). Gauge covariance then gives a compact \(\gamma\) relating the original k-link representatives to this accuracy.

At the real physical argument CMP119 (3.10) fixes each based terminal \(V^{(k)}(y,x)\) to identity. Its averaged contour transforms covariantly under \(\gamma\); it is Lipschitz on this fixed one-step weak chart, with only \(C_L\) short path lengths. Closeness of the transformed and untransformed fields therefore gives \(\|\gamma(y)\gamma(x)^{-1}-I\|\le C_L\) times (4.174) for all x in the current block of y. Replacing \(\gamma(x)\) by \(\gamma(y)\) on its block preserves (4.174) after a fixed factor. This proves the real rotation estimate for these background factors. It does not differentiate the full conditional T-density or its selector, nor an unpaired inverse gauge. ∎

### 4.96 A two-index estimate from localized first variations

**Lemma 4.110 (P).** Let \(r,s\) index actual native cells with distance \(d\), let \(i\in r,l\in s\) be the individual color/link parameters incident there, and measure their derivatives \(D_i,D_l\) in their prescribed radii. On genuine simultaneous product neighborhoods of a tested point suppose the scalar (or fixed matrix component) \(F_y\) is holomorphic and
\[
 |D_iF_y|\le k_y(i),\qquad
 \sum_{i\in r}k_y(i)\le K e^{-c d(y,r)}.                             \tag{4.175}
\]
The *same individual* majorants must work throughout a fixed spare fraction \(\theta\) of the product region. Then
\[
 \sum_{i\in r,l\in s}|D_lD_i F_y|
 \le C\theta^{-1} K
       e^{-c[d(y,r)+d(y,s)]/2}.                                      \tag{4.176}
\]
For fixed i choose simultaneous phases of all \(l\in s\) to attain the absolute sum of that row. Apply Cauchy in this one direction to \(D_iF_y\); its bound on the contour is \(k_y(i)\). Choose phases afresh for each i before summing. This yields \(C\theta^{-1}K e^{-cd(y,r)}\). Symmetry of the analytic Hessian gives the same estimate with s in place of r; take their geometric mean. The proof permits different source/current species with the respective radii and the geometric mean of their first-row caps. A finite enlargement of the stencil costs a fixed factor. The fixed native counting test at a slower rate then gives an absolutely summable tree bound, including polynomial offset moments and sufficiently small exponential attachments. There is no factor for the number of fine bonds in a native cell. A bound only on \(\sup_z\sum_i|D_iF_y(z)|\) cannot be substituted for (4.175).

**Proposition 4.111 (P/L, both free genuine target directions on an admitted mixed minimum).** On the original regular CMP102 hierarchy of (172),(190) take the existing spare physical target-angle polydisc, the fixed weighted counting test, and a common compatible relative chart satisfying (19)–(21). Let \(r,s\) be two native target bonds, including bonds at the first finer retained layer. On a slightly smaller genuine disc the variations of \(W\)'s compatible potential, curvature, and the physical Wilson current obey (4.176), with their actual output factors \(\sigma_y,\sigma_y^2,\sigma_y^3\). More explicitly the second angle derivative has the right-hand side of (4.176) divided by the applicable spare angle radius, if the first derivative in (190) is taken per unscaled angle. This is a depth-uniform bound for genuinely mixed native rows before taking absolute anchors; it includes unequal r,s and the diagonal. It does not assert the same per-coordinate majorant for an itinerant independent fine current.

Indeed (190) differentiates against the true measure \(\sigma_r^4\) multiplying its printed density \(\sigma_r^{-4}\). Thus one native angle index already satisfies (4.175), uniformly with respect to the other regular inputs on a smaller central disc. For curvature take the derivative of the complete plaquette word, summing its four transported tangents. For the action current, in a regular local frame write the background link \(e^{i\eta A}\), with \(\sigma|A|+\sigma^2|\nabla A|\le C\epsilon\). The first variation of its current is \(d^*d\dot A\) plus terms bounded by
\(C[\sigma^{-1}\sup|\nabla\dot A|+\sigma^{-2}\sup|\dot A|]\) on the complete neighboring stars. To check the scaling, expand each oriented Wilson sine word divided by \(\eta^2\): the linear part is \(dA\), the quadratic commutator has one ordinary difference after taking the divergence, and the remaining curvature terms containing \(\nabla A\) have an explicit \(\eta\). For terms where a second ordinary difference hits \(\dot A\) or A in these remainders, use \(\eta|\nabla^2 f|\le 2\sup|\nabla f|\) on the enlarged stencil. Terms where the relative exponential multiplies \(\dot A\) differ from its identity coefficient by \(O(\eta |A|)\), and the same inequality applies. The difference of conjugate plaquette frames produces the bounded \([A,\dot F]\), \([F,\dot A]\) terms. These bounds are also valid on the complex compatible tube, and conversion from covariant to ordinary derivatives of (190) costs precisely these lower rows. The leading \(d^*d\dot A\) is the covariant divergence row in (190), not a bound for each separate second difference. The result supplies (4.175) for the *reconstructed* scaled current. Now use the lemma. For a gauge-invariant scalar whose old first/second saved absolute kernels are available, compose these first and second rows by the signed chain rule before the nonnegative sums of (4.4). This identifies a part of the required short-file tensor on general admitted backgrounds. Arbitrary separate nonlinear raw axial gauges are not covered by the compatible chart assertion.

### 4.97 The incoming independent current on the unweakened critical-reference edge

**Proposition 4.112 (P/L, compressing fine currents at the literal critical-reference variational edge).** Hold the external field U fixed. In the unweakened CMP102 (174)–(175) or (179)–(180) critical-reference function used for \(H_e\) in (1.63), the independently extended fine current enters through
\[
                  \lambda=H(U)^*J.                                    \tag{4.177}
\]
In particular this statement does not apply to the noncritical projected forcing in (116),(1.54). To verify it in the source, (74),(78)–(80) write the current couplings after the averaging change of variables. The linear term \(\langle A',J\rangle\) gives \(-{\mathcal G}J\) for the general variational problem (116). In the critical-reference extension (175) that linear term has already dropped by (141) on the zero-target variations. The quadratic and higher dependence is exactly \(-\langle H[C^{(2)}(Y)+D_3(Y)],J\rangle\). The remaining terms of (80) and the equation (49) defining \(D\) depend on the field and \(H\), but not further on the independent J. In (180) the resummed quadratic operator has (3.136) of CMP99 with the very same \(H^*J\). Thus (4.177) is exact at fixed U, not a guess about the explicit affine current in a different response. Both the based restoring gauge of this edge and the ensuing raw word are functions of the resulting relative \(H_e\), so this compression retains their complete J-dependence.

Use physical pairings \(d\nu(x)=\eta^4\) on fine sites and \(d\nu(r)=\sigma_r^4\) on native sites. In (4.177) and (4.178) write H in the physical convention (3.126), so that the angular interpolation in (47) of CMP102 is \(H\,\mathrm{diag}(\sigma_r^{-1})\). In particular the current coupling is \(-\sum_r\sigma_r^4\lambda_r\sigma_r^{-1}D(Y)_r\); absorb the indicated scale only in this change of coordinates. In the convention \(QH=I\) for physically normalized targets, a right inverse of \(H^*\) is the actual compatible transpose \(Q^*\). The original positive path majorant, (3.78)–(3.81), and (4.23) bound \(Q^*\) from numbers of size \(C/\sigma_r^3\) to the analogous native fine-current norm, on the regular chart; include its color and neighbor factors. With a fixed weak noncommuting background the convergent perturbation of the straight paths multiplies this bound by a constant as in §§4.9,4.12. No inverse of a new synthetic Gram matrix is taken. Equation (3.133) of CMP99 gives the kernel of \(H(U)\) for the physical source normalization, with \(e^{-cd(r,n(x))}\sigma_r^{-4}\), up to the bounded native scale quotients covered by the spacing test. Consequently, in the native dimensionless variables \(\widehat\lambda_r=\sigma_r^3\lambda_r,\ \widehat J_x=\sigma_{n(x)}^3J_x\),
\[
 |D_{\widehat J_x}\widehat\lambda_r|
       \le C {\eta^4\over\sigma_{n(x)}^4}\,
                    e^{-c' d(r,n(x))}.                                \tag{4.178}
\]
In fact the unabsorbed ratio is \(\sigma_{n(x)}/\sigma_r\); absorb it with the fixed scale-ratio counting test. This is an individual background-uniform majorant summable over the fine links of each cell.

Here is also a local spatial derivative bound for the resulting \(H_e\) itself. Differentiate its critical equation (175) at fixed U, or (179) with the quadratic \(\lambda\)-vertex included in the nonlinear part. The new source for variation of \(\lambda_r\) is the derivative of the full \(D(Y)_r\), tested against a unit native coefficient. The complete quadratic and higher parts of (78)–(80) are retained. Estimate (73) of CMP102 bounds its density at b by \(C\epsilon\,\sigma_r^{1-4}\exp[-c d(r,n(b))]\); with the native measure and the dimensionless \(\widehat\lambda_r\) this gives a force of physical size \(C\epsilon\,\sigma_{n(b)}^{-3}e^{-c' d(r,n(b))}\). The ratio of native lengths is paid by the fixed spacing. Solve the linearized equation at the actual solution. The true zero-target Green sends a force supported on one native cell to a potential/first-difference row bounded by \(C e^{-c'd}\) in native units by CMP99 Theorem3.13, (3.148), and (3.42); (189) gives a convergent inverse for its nonlinear perturbation at a smaller exponential rate. The transformation \(I-HD'\) has the same summability by (70)–(73). Thus the compatible potential and its first native difference have first \(\widehat\lambda_r\)-derivative bounded by \(C\epsilon e^{-c''d(y,r)}\). Combining this with (4.178) yields (4.175) for the actual independent fine current at the unweakened innermost variational edge. Lemma4.110 then gives a spatially summable second fine-current row in that compatible potential or its first difference, for arbitrary weak noncommuting admitted backgrounds, without any support reserve. A current derivative divided by a different stored radius must keep that factor in the final norm. No \(\ell^1\) estimate for \(d^*d{\mathcal G}\) on arbitrary fine impulses was used.

### 4.98 A short carrier meeting the first true finer retained layer

Use the two separated A and C components before the j-return, choosing the nearest face of the C component a bounded number of old \(MR_j\) cubes from A. They remain distinct components. The old j-boundary file can comprise the A operation body, an M-cube corridor and a terminal rectangular portion hitting C. Choose its terminal face within the true \(n=j-1\) C shell, with its complete original stars clear of the deeper \(\Omega_{j-1}^c\). Such a shell has room by the original successive buffers. The shape can be enlarged by fixed M-layers at its corners to accommodate all required stars. The interior of A is regular again after its j-healing, and C's \(j-2\) or finer layers are outside this particular readset. No ordinary four-dimensional subcube of side \(cLMR_j\) is used here. As in (2.41) the B-file is classified by its actual intersection with the surviving component; the assertion is for a coefficient filed on this support and does not require every putative corridor coefficient to be nonzero.

**Proposition 4.113 (P/L, local old member on this actual finer edge).** For every \(p\le j-1\) the p-reading problem on this short carrier uses exactly the minimal \(B_p(X)\) and its genuine trace: none of the additional \(\Gamma_i,\ i<p\), have interior stencils in this X. For p=j, the top boundary part uses \(B_j(X)\), and the genuine finer part \(B_{j-1}(X)\), with (2.14) and its stated piecewise convention. The maximal nested minimal domains through level \(j-1\) are the same in both constructions: each step takes the maximal aligned union satisfying the spacing relative to the previous domain, before the final cap is chosen (CMP119 pp.256–257). The actual j-top region can only remove cells from the final \(B_j(X)\) top region when it meets the \(j-1\) shell. Thus its distance to the preceding complement cannot shrink. Equivalently one may apply (2.14) separately on the two boundary pieces, which agree on the shorter truncation at their interface. This verifies the native spacing on this particular one-layer edge. The older genuine meshes on all incident stars satisfy \(1/L\le\sigma_y\le1\) in j-units, even though the p-window has arbitrary finer synthetic meshes. Hence \(t_y\le L\sigma_y\), and the scale moment (4.156) follows from the same fixed power-three/four counting test without a growing old-layer factor. Use the old stationary \(W\) itself as the zero-defect reference in (4.157). The physical W and its earlier reconstructed currents obey (4.164) on this short carrier throughout the reinstatement sectors of §§4.89–4.92. In particular the scalar analytic cap is available also on this genuine finer boundary, rather than only for an enclosing X with its outer faces in the j-layer. This conclusion alone gives no summability over the two microscopic source indices.

**Proposition 4.114 (P on a fixed number of coarse levels from the reference scale; an arbitrary fine cutoff).** There is a nontrivial cutoff-uniform two-index sector on this short carrier. Take \(K-e=D\) fixed so the number of post-healing e-sites is bounded by a constant of the fixed reference problem. Take D sufficiently large once that the indicated small components and collars fit, and use the weak equal-\(R\) plateau for their births. The j index still tends to infinity with the ultraviolet cutoff. A, C may first fail at \(b=j-R_b,\ b+1\) after the wholly ordinary earlier steps. The compact nodal construction of §4.42 works at these birth indices, with their old fine lifts. The neighborhood of the A/C interaction at j is the same one-step (j to e) geometry just verified; it need not have \(j=1+R\). All initial ultraviolet indices and their true microscopic source coordinates remain present. The assertion transports a given certified completed old marked boundary coefficient, including the physical H if it has that stated old cap. It does not prove a new clean H induction through the long prefix.

Use an actual member term on which the critical-reference \(H_e\) of (1.63) is at its unweakened endpoint; take the full original innermost determining region if necessary. A middle preparation wall of (1.62) may vary. Cut the first (1.61) layer in an eligible band separating its true sources (1.55) from the synthetic skin, as in §4.94. Its restored word on that skin therefore transmits the previous field. On this file the external independent fine J enters the final determining rows only through (1.63). Indeed its interior entries in (1.61),(1.62) are the displayed \(V'',V_\Lambda^{(A)},M^\bullet U_0^{(AL)}\), functions of U and the retained coordinates; on the skin they are the complete transmitted words. In (1.62) the varied \(H_{B_1}\), its restoring gauge, and that stage's actual critical re-readout have the previous \(U_{e,\Lambda}\) as background and argument (LFRII pp.374–375). Thus the whole composite, including this possibly nonlinear mixed frame and the final \(J_{\rm phys}\), is a function of \(\lambda=H(U)^*J\). It is not asserted that this same factorization holds with an arbitrary independently weakened innermost propagator, or for an additional bare independent fine \(J\)-slot of a different effective action.

Let \(A_X\) be the original scalar cap of the actual signed marked file on its admitted product chart, with its own coefficient and contact subtractions. The first-return cap for a distinguished compact primitive carrying sector follows from (1.73),(1.91)–(1.99), as in §4.87; for an older completed H use its certified source coefficient. Let \(\rho_J\) be a fixed small fraction of the actual incoming e-current radius available on that chart. By (4.177) and \(H^*Q^*=I\), replace \(J\) by \(J+Q^*\zeta\). Every \(\|\zeta\|_\infty\le\rho_J/C\) is realized by an actual allowed sup-current perturbation on the homogeneous post-healing hierarchy. It therefore gives an independent analytic ball in the compressed parameter, with its *original normalized* scalar bounded by \(A_X\). This remains true for a short A/C file by Proposition4.113 and the total native output estimate (4.167)–(4.170); the complete earlier determining graph, saved old J and the signed denominator are those of the old coefficient. For \(\|v\|\le M_0\epsilon_e\), take the two Cauchy contours inside the remaining fixed fraction of the original weak input ball. Both ordered current components at points \(x,x'\) obey
\[
 \rho_J^2 |\partial_{J_x}\partial_{J_{x'}}F_X|
 \le C A_X\,\eta_e^8
       \sum_{r,s}e^{-c[d_e(r,x)+d_e(s,x')]} ,                        \tag{4.179}
\]
and the analogous estimate with one compressed kernel holds for one independent current and one genuine marked source with its own radius. In this displayed case all e-native meshes are one. The scalar \(F_X\) is the composition with the physically reconstructed current, not a carrying scalar holding that slot fixed. Its two-variable Cauchy contours differentiate **both** actual frame factors wherever they occur, their crossed terms, and the physical-current response of the old minimum; they act on the contact-subtracted analytic function. This argument neither drops a phase derivative nor takes separate absolute values of its two summands before their physical composition. In detail, differentiating the single scalar \(\widetilde F_X(\lambda)\) gives \(\sum_{r,s}\partial^2_{rs}\widetilde F_X (D_x\lambda_r)(D_{x'}\lambda_s)\); there is no second derivative of the compression because U is held fixed. Equation (4.178) then gives (4.179).

There are only \(N_D\) e-native cells. After inserting the native tree weight of any fixed attachment rate, the sum of (4.179) over *all* the fine color/bond pairs is at most \(C_D A_X\): the fine densities \(\eta_e^8\) cancel the true fine counts, and the diameter of the e-torus in e-units is bounded in terms of D. Equivalently, there is a bound \(C_D A_X\eta_e^8\rho_J^{-2}e^{-\lambda\tau_e(x,x',X)}\) for any fixed native attachment exponent on this bounded reference geometry. When \(e=j+1\) conversion to old j-units changes only fixed powers of L. All fixed moment weights in those units follow. No area of X pays this estimate; \(C_D\) is independent of the ultraviolet cutoff. It is not claimed uniform in \(D=K-e\). For arbitrary D a spatially decaying bound for the *compressed* Hessian at this mixed edge is still needed. The local part at the innermost compatible variational step is Proposition4.112. No exponential at the bare fine-site metric inside one large e-cell follows from the column density (4.178).

### 4.99 Current rank at genuinely fractional walls

**Lemma 4.115 (P, microscopic absolute norm of a finite-rank physical Hessian).** Let a complex bilinear form \(B\) on two finite families of independently normalized coordinates have matrix rank m. Then
\[
 \sum_{x,y}|B_{xy}|\le m\,\|B:\ell^\infty\longrightarrow\ell^1\| .      \tag{4.180}
\]
The operator norm on the right is the supremum over two independent phase families, not an entrywise absolute sum. In the m-dimensional range choose an Auerbach basis \(u_\ell,\phi_\ell\) for the inherited \(\ell^1\) norm. For completeness, maximize the modulus of the determinant of m unit vectors in this range; replacing its \(\ell\)-th vector by any other unit vector gives the bound one for that coordinate functional by multilinearity. The resulting vectors and coordinate functionals have norm one. Expand \(B=\sum_\ell u_\ell(\phi_\ell B)\). Each row \(\phi_\ell B\) has \(\ell^1\) norm at most \(\|B\|_{\infty\to1}\). This proves (4.180). It works unchanged for unequal index spaces, singular compressions or a complex symmetric Hessian.

**Proposition 4.116 (P/L, exact finite-dimensional current dependence of the localized post-heal first edge).** Hold U fixed, and let \({\cal W}\) be the N *active nonbinary* wall cells of the innermost critical-reference function \(H_e(s)\) on its original \(Y_4\); the other cells are fixed at their binary cut values. Use the generalized walks of CMP116 (1.5)–(1.8), hence the construction of CMP99 (3.130),(3.135)–(3.138) and Theorem3.12. At fixed U this variational function, its based gauge and its exposed raw row depend on the independent fine current J through a finite family of linear maps
\[
 \{P_\epsilon J:\epsilon\in\{0,1\}^{\cal W}\},\qquad
       P_\epsilon J=(H_\epsilon)^*J,                                \tag{4.181}
\]
with the fixed finite multiplicity of original reference interpolants if written in their different physical/angle conventions. The native index r of each \(P_\epsilon\) is on the post-healing hierarchy, not a microscopic fine bond. No invertibility for the stacked list of all these maps is needed.

To see why this also covers the *propagators* that depend on J, start with (78)–(80) and the projected quadratic correction (3.134)–(3.136). The marks J enter as \(H^*J\); all factors \(G',R,D\) flanking \(\Delta^{(2)}\) in (3.135) are J-independent at fixed U. CMP99 p.423 obtains the generalized walk for \(G_1\) by replacing the operators in its Neumann expansion (3.138) by their generalized random walks. The interpolant (3.129) uses this same propagator and its native inverse; these inverses have the original convergent expansions, as do the zero-target operators (3.139)–(3.148). Thus each J occurrence in an expanded product has its H-transpose terminal. This uses the construction of (3.138), rather than assigning an unrelated independent quadratic source to every microscopic site of a walk box. The primary J-free operators localized inside boxes remain just those of the original generalized walk.

For a binary set of walls the weight of a concatenation of subwalks is the product of their binary weights: the entire support avoids every closed cell if and only if each piece does. Hence binary evaluation is multiplicative on these absolutely convergent expanded products and Neumann series. In particular each occurrence remains \(H_\epsilon^*J\) in the binary component. If an equivalent formula leaves a global \(H^*J\) unexpanded, it adds only that single native array. For a general fractional array the weight of a **single complete walk of a propagator** is a multilinear polynomial, since every cell appears only once for the union of its supports. Its unique binary interpolation is
\[
 K(s;J)=\sum_{\epsilon\in\{0,1\}^{\cal W}}
    \left(\prod_{\epsilon_\Delta=1}s_\Delta
          \prod_{\epsilon_\Delta=0}(1-s_\Delta)\right)K(\epsilon;J). \tag{4.182}
\]
The other fixed binary walls are retained in each \(K(\epsilon;J)\). This identity holds for the original operator walks \(H,H_0,G\), before solving the nonlinear equations for \(D,A_0\). The solution itself is not claimed multilinear in s. But its input operators in (1.2)–(1.5) depend on J only through the listed arrays. Its explicit (80) term uses \(H(s)^*J\), also a linear combination of these same arrays. This proves (4.181), including for noncommuting U. Terms with incompatible backgrounds in entirely different filed graphs cannot be equated by this reasoning.

**Proposition 4.117 (P under the stated rank/bank comparison, full two-current sum on the short exposed file).** On Proposition4.113's actual short file take the common exposed skin of §4.94: the two earlier sources are separated from that skin by closed admissible bands (after (1.55)), and the interior determining rows are held at their true \(U_0^{(AL)},V''\) values. Allow arbitrary original fractional innermost walls on a determining region \(Y_4\) of diameter \(O(MR_e)\) in e-units. There are at most \(C R_e^4\) e-wall cubes. The post-healing graph is top-homogeneous in this two-component example, and the number of its native interpolation rows incident to \(Y_4\), including the fixed-size enlargements used by the walks, is at most \(C_L(1+MR_e)^4\). The finite matrices have the original color and orientation multiplicities. Cells wholly outside the closed determining support cannot occur in any of the contributing walks. The actual coefficient and its source derivatives have the same current map (4.181): on the skin the final data are precisely (1.63), and the other determining data are independent of the external fine J, by the literal original (1.61). Thus on a fixed record the rank of its fine-current Hessian is at most
\[
    m_X\le C_L(1+MR_e)^4\,2^{N},\qquad N\le C R_e^4,                \tag{4.183}
\]
irrespective of the microscopic mesh and irrespective of how close the different coarse maps are to being dependent. This applies to the physical invariant scalar composed with its actual reconstructed current. The original restoring gauge is inside this composition; at two derivatives both of its variations and all cross terms occur. For one marked source and one incoming fine current the corresponding rectangular array has the same rank bound on its current side. For two old compact source homes differentiate their own signed log including its contact; this does not replace an old physical source by an independent current.

For this A-heals calculation the visible ordinary corridor need not contain an \(R_j^4\) subcube. The full final (1.99) weight can still count that many cubes in the healed A body. Our rank argument does not use this possible gain. In this j-operation A heals and C is one wait short, so there are no *newly* still-live \(Y_i\) in (1.72) at j. Nevertheless an X meeting C is a boundary file. Printed LFRII (1.97),(1.99) give \(c_1=e^{-p_j}\) in this case, \(p_j=p_0(g_j)\). For the literal marked first-return compact A carrying sector, the distinguished fixed occupied body has spare prior to (1.95): after its diameter completion its exponent is at most \(-3p_j/2\) on the weak trajectory as in §§4.39,4.44. Use a factor \(1+zF_A\) with its actual compact H bound \(K_A\) before the Mayer operations. With two specified compact tags use independent factors. Radii \(c e^{\varepsilon p_j}/K_A\) with, for example, \(2\varepsilon<1/4\) still leave the ordinary \(e^{-p_j}\) bank plus a fixed spare for convergence on the marked body. No new placement sum is introduced for the specified A. Expand the same signed gas and take the Cauchy coefficients of its logarithm. This gives, with a fixed smaller \(\varepsilon'>0\),
\[
 A_X^{\rm mark}\le C K_A^m e^{-(1+\varepsilon')p_j}
                        e^{-(1+\varepsilon')\kappa d_{j,Z}(X)}       \tag{4.184}
\]
for the indicated one/two-source coefficients on their old chart. This uses the retained integrated stencil and the old unextracted denominator as in §4.87. The marked log contains its normalization and its actual contact; a remote mark lacking that occupied body cannot receive its bank. For an incoming previously certified coefficient with its own amplitude use that amplitude; do not manufacture an extra rare factor if its construction has no such spare.

On the full reinstatement range of (4.170) and its allowed small independent-current probe product disc, the corrected short-edge comparison of Proposition4.113 gives the native old analytic chart. Two-variable Cauchy on the *single signed scalar* gives \(\|B\|_{\infty\to1}\le C A_X^{\rm mark}\) with the two actual derivative normalizations. Now apply (4.180)–(4.183), after this signed recombination. In particular no \(L^{8j}\) factor occurs even when \(j\gg R_j\). For e-source homes in \(Y_4\), attachment lengths in the actual e or consecutive j units cost at most \(C MR_e\); all fixed offset moments in these units cost a polynomial in \(R_e\). In this A-heals case the printed metric is already full. Only when one first weakens it to a larger omitted Z and then asks to restore a full weight does the conservative completion cost \(C R_j^4\) arise. Consequently, whenever the fixed native reserve satisfies
\[
 \varepsilon' p_0(g_j) >
    C_{\rm fill}\kappa R_j^4 +(\log 2)N + C_{\rm att}MR_e
         + C_m\log(2+R_j+\text{old radius}^{-1}),                   \tag{4.185}
\]
the complete indicated weighted absolute current/source sums are bounded by
\(C K_A^m e^{-p_j} e^{-\kappa d_j(X)}\) in their proper radius units. For the relative carrier weight, or for the actual full metric of the A-heals case without an intermediate weakening, omit the first cost. For a single intermediate wall \(N=1\). The native rare-factor proof (1.80)–(1.85) already confronts \(MR_j\)-volume costs. The sufficient explicit condition here holds, for example, if the once-fixed threshold exponent gives \(p_0(g_j)/R_j^4\to\infty\); no individual history's parameters are changed. It is the actual occupied-body surplus, not a presumed \(R^4\) *ordinary support size*. The inequality gives spatial summability on the declared original short-corridor carrier and \(O(MR_e)\) determining regions, without restricting \(K-e\). It is not a pointwise universal estimate at bare-site metric through a collapsed component, a bound uniform for arbitrary \(Y_4\) or numbers of still-live bodies, or an allotment of a second reserve to the same mark.

### 4.100 A genuinely short normalized still-live file without an area or rare reserve

**Proposition 4.118 (P/L in the following original sector).** This time make A take the still-live branch at j, with C one wait short. The strict real configuration is the pair of opposite internal nontree links \(\pm2\epsilon_j\) of Proposition4.59, not an identity field declared to satisfy \(\chi_j^c\). Choose A and C separated in j-units by a fixed several \(LMR_e\)-widths, so their next e-components do not merge. Use the variant of §4.47 with no fresh exterior failure; the retained A body at the e-step is not eligible to heal again immediately. C has its true eligible branch at \(e=j+1\). The sizes and separations can be chosen on the same weak plateau, with the torus containing these rectangles.

At j use precisely the alternative (1.103)–(1.104). Inside the normalized current operation at A insert one or two physical compact marks on stencils still integrated there; a completed designated H with an already certified old cap can instead be followed with that cap. The factor \(T'_j(A)1\) of p.391 and its actual \(\chi_j^c\) are kept outside the normalized logarithm. On the original small source disc let \(m(\eta)=\widehat T_A(e^{\eta F_A})\), \(\widehat T_A=T'_j(A)/T'_j(A)1\). Equations (1.73)–(1.75) and \(|F_A|\le K_A\) give \(|m(\eta)-1|\le C|\eta|K_A\) on a smaller fixed multiple of \(K_A^{-1}\). Factor this mass once, and replace the normalized operator in the last exponential by \(\widehat T_A(e^{\eta F_A}\,\cdot)/m(\eta)\). The latter has the same bounded one-body majorant, up to a fixed factor arbitrarily close to the original one. The mass term has A support. The connecting terms retain the original normalized Mayer expansion; differentiating its log gives the contact and denominator subtraction. With two sources the same reasoning uses a product disc. Printed p.391 explicitly uses \(d_j(X\setminus A)\) in (1.93), the \(e^3\) one-body bound, and \(\beta/4\) in place of \(\beta\). Keeping a strictly smaller piece of this spare gives, for a filed connecting coefficient rooted at the compact mark,
\[
 |B_{j,X}^{\rm mark}|\le C K_A^m
             \exp[-(1+\epsilon_{\rm sp})\kappa d_j(X\setminus A)].      \tag{4.186}
\]
This is a boundary estimate in its own metric. There is no factor \(e^{-p_j}\) in (4.186); the outside mass is never counted as a reserve for the conditional coefficient.

Take a permissible X containing the A body, an ordinary corridor a bounded number of old M-cubes across, and a terminal portion in the true \(j-1\) shell of C, ending before its \(j-2\) region. Near A include the fixed localization halo needed by its determining data. At the j graph the earlier A links have been integrated and reintroduced at the top j level, exactly as in (1.104); near C the first genuinely finer old edge is still present. The p-window verification of Proposition4.113 applies. The measured size of a shortest such corridor outside A is of order \(LR_e\), up to constants for any finite halo; an outside boundary layer on all faces of A is not needed for the thin corridor term. In particular the proof below uses only the lower bound \(d_j(X\setminus A)\ge cLR_e\) and no four-dimensional subcube. As throughout these carrier estimates, the assertion bounds any term actually filed on the admissible support without claiming it has a nonzero numerical value for every exterior.

**Actual upper family and its old margin.** After the C return the new graph has top level e in the ordinary component and level j at the newly retained A data. In j-grid variables put the two admissible opposite A links in the interior of A. Near C lift its specified internal e-links by the exact face-constant one-step section of CMP119 (1.3): only links between consecutive e-blocks have the upper value, all interior links are identity. Its actual coarse mean is the specified e-link because every straight row crosses the same face, while its endpoint prefix links are identity. The A and C prescriptions have disjoint full upper stars. Take the smooth right inverse for \(M^j\) of this entire j-array, as in Lemma4.102 and §4.92. It realizes exactly the genuine j-links on A and the e-links at C. Between j and e all physical-unit differences cost only fixed L powers. The original post-heal p-tests on this mixed A/top graph follow using the smooth trial as zero-defect reference. For \(v_A,v_C\) on a product ball \(\rho=c\mu_{\min}\) the chosen trial lies in the unmarked outer domain. In using the enlarged complex auxiliary disc the previously external \(\chi_j^c\) multiplier is *not differentiated*. The map being estimated is the unmarked extended determining field, defined on the source chart before that real multiplication. Evaluate the resulting bound at the actual strictly selected real \(v_A\) above and at the allowed C reinstatement. A is spatially separate from C's released rectangle; any variation of the corresponding released background occurring in (1.61) is kept in the total native map.

At the zero point of this analytic field family the compact C nodal baseline remains and the A data are identity. The mesh comparison (4.168)–(4.169) persists: A acquired at most the single new j-level layer, all earlier C rows before their healing are as in §4.92, and the two full prefix neighborhoods are separated. Thus (4.170) and the short-edge p-current comparison give the strict compatible old radius and physical reconstructed current on the actual A datum. The inequality is independent of whether the auxiliary origin obeys the previous external *real selector*. The actual evaluated A datum does obey it.

Now choose a bounded \(Y_4\) of diameter \(C_LMR_e\) incident to the corridor and the C return, with the closed earlier separation bands of §4.94. There is no need to differentiate a microscopic terminal gauge separately to bound the marked Hessian. The rank argument applies on the post-heal native graph; in A its mesh differs from e by just L, so its extra interpolation rows multiply (4.183) by at most \(C_L\). For one genuine fractional innermost wall, \(m_X\le C_L(1+MR_e)^4\) by binary interpolation, also when some other walls are zero. Combine (4.180) with Cauchy on the *completed normalized* scalar from (4.186). For every ordered pair of current indices in \(Y_4\) this yields the entrywise absolute sum after the signed normalization, both based-frame variations, the old \(P_{\rm old}\) current and the old contact. Coincident indices have their standard Cauchy factorial. With the original two separate radius costs left explicit one obtains
\[
 e^{\kappa d_j(X\setminus A)}
   \sum_{x,x'}h_xh_{x'}(1+\mathrm{off}_{x,x'})^\ell
               |D_xD_{x'}F_X|
 \le C_{\ell,L} K_A^m
       \exp[-\tfrac12\epsilon_{\rm sp}\kappa d_j(X\setminus A)].       \tag{4.187}
\]
The polynomial offsets here are in the original j/e units of this consecutive file. Their magnitude is \(O_L(MR_e)\). Their cost, \(\log m_X\), and any fixed powers of the inverse weak radius in converting the prescribed grades are \(O(\log R_j+\log x_j)\), strictly less than the corridor spare for all sufficiently weak profiles. No \(L^{4j}\) count and no outside \(T'_j(A)1\) mass enter. If a particular anchored norm has an additional exponential attachment \(a\,\tau\), its actual cost must satisfy \(a\sup\tau+\log m_X\le\frac12\epsilon_{\rm sp}\kappa d_j(X\setminus A)\). A fixed sufficiently small native attachment rate gives this on the stated short geometry. One cannot assign a larger arbitrary exponential at bare-site distances through A. The finite first source and second contact use their own physical source-radius factors on the same contours. A bounded number, or \(O(R_j)\) sufficiently sparse fractional innermost walls, can be allowed by the same argument retaining the exact \((\log2)N\) in the last comparison.

This controls an actual sector where (1.103) has removed the area of the still-live region from the tree metric and its rare mass from the normalized kernel. A densely fractional region with \(N\asymp R_j^4\) is not covered by spending its \(O(R_j)\) corridor; nor is a subsequent graph with many arbitrarily refined incident bodies or a file entirely within A with no connector. There is no disproof of the saved estimate from the overestimate (4.183).

### 4.101 A genuine logarithm in a detached current and its exact compensation

**Proposition 4.119 (P, an actual flat constrained operator test).** On a fixed periodic box in top units, with an unweakened homogeneous flat hierarchy and fine spacing \(\eta\to0\), the uniform bound
\(\|d^*d\,{\cal G} f\|_\infty\le C\|f\|_\infty\)
fails for arbitrary fine one-form forcing. Here \({\cal G}\) is the physical zero-target, compatible-gauge Green operator in the noncritical problem (116),(3.148). This statement is about the isolated operator, not the physical \(J_{1,2}\) of (1.55).

**Proof.** Work in one real Lie algebra generator at the flat orbit. Let \(a={\cal G}f\); it has centered \(Qa=0,\ RD^*a=0\). The flat Euler equation and Hodge projection give
\[
       d^*d a=P_{\rm T} f-P_{\rm T}Q^*\lambda,\qquad
       P_{\rm T}=I-d(d^*d)_{\rm scalar}^{-1}d^*,\qquad
       \lambda=H^* f .                                                \tag{4.188}
\]
Use the mean-zero inverse in the longitudinal term. Indeed the remaining gauge multiplier is a scalar gradient. Testing the Euler equation against \(Hc\) gives \(\lambda=H^*f\), since \(Hc\) is orthogonal to zero-target \(a\) in the flat Wilson quadratic form and is in the same Landau section. The native H estimate gives \(\|\lambda\|\le C\|f\|\) for the fixed unit spacing. At a fine bond in the interior of one coarse block, with a fixed positive distance to its faces, \(Q^*\lambda\) is bounded and Lipschitz on a fixed ball, uniformly in \(\eta\): the explicit centered stencil (1.18) is constant transversely and piecewise affine longitudinally there. Thus \(P_{\rm T}Q^*\lambda\) is bounded *at that bond*. To check this use the periodic scalar second-Green-difference bound \(C(\eta+|z|)^{-4}\) in density units, as in §4.25, and subtract the central value on the inner ball. The resulting radial integral has one positive power of distance; the outer integral is bounded on the fixed torus. This does not assert an analogous sup bound at a discontinuity of the coarse path transpose.

There is an explicit bounded test for \(P_{\rm T}f\). Center periodic angular coordinates at that 1-bond and sample fields at their appropriate bond midpoints. Let only component 2 of \(f\) be nonzero, with
\(f_2=S_m(\theta_1)S_m(\theta_2)\) constant in the other directions, where
\[
 S_m(t)=\frac4\pi\sum_{\substack{1\le k<m\\k\ {\rm odd}}}
             (1-k/m)\frac{\sin kt}{k}.
\]
This is a Fejér mean of \({\rm sign}(\sin t)\), so its sup norm is at most one. Take \(m\) of order \(\eta^{-1/2}\). In midpoint variables the off-diagonal multiplier of \(P_{\rm T}\) is
\(-\widehat k\,\widehat l/(\widehat k^2+\widehat l^2)\), with the usual lattice sines. At the chosen 1-bond, the four signs of each sine pair have the same sign after multiplication. For odd \(1\le k,l\le m/2\) their total contributes at least \(c/(k^2+l^2)\), since these modes are far below the Nyquist scale. Every included pair outside that square contributes with the same sign. Summing on the disjoint dyadic squares gives \(c\log m\). The bounded coarse correction in (4.188) cannot cancel it. This is an explicit lower bound for the absolute fine-force row at a legal flat base. It also illustrates why the covariant Laplacian exception after (3.138) cannot simply be removed by estimating every fine second derivative separately.

At coinciding hierarchies the *completed* readout does remove this particular term: \(Q{\cal G}f=0\), so \(D(P_{\rm old}q_{\rm old}){\cal G}f=0\) on that true common graph, and the physical re-readout current has zero first variation from this zero-target input. At a finer retained edge one instead re-averages before applying the old current response (4.164),(4.176). The first unmatched term is the boundary forcing in the exact (1.55) projection. The arbitrary square-wave \(f\) above has not been realized as that physical splice forcing; in particular it is not generally divergence-free. This proves a failure of a tempting *separated* operator estimate, not a legal counterexample to the completed H tensor.

**Current remaining operator.** The single-finest-edge result (4.187) is uniform through arbitrary microscopic depth but uses a short normalized still-live source and bounded postheal top/j geometry. Every later occurrence of a genuinely *fresh independent* current outside the critical-reference (175), such as an independent replacement for the affine forcing of (116), must supply its own summable column. The original (1.55) instead has the two physical forces of a splice; it cannot be differentiated as if both were freely chosen affine sources. On a long succession of overlapping still-live components a large family of binary current maps in (4.181) may also have true dimension exceeding the spare tree budget. The estimate \(\log m_X\le(\log2)N+O(\log|Y_4|)\) in that case is an upper bound, not an exhibited nonzero Hessian or lower bound for its absolute sum. A proof beyond it requires an absolute spatial estimate for the derivative of the *compensated* frame and split physical current with respect to each native marked endpoint in the weakened walk. The homogeneous compatible-potential estimate (4.178) and the exact lower-bound/compensation test (4.188) isolate the two sides of that step.

### 4.102 A spatial two-index sum at dense fractional walls without a body reserve

**Proposition 4.120 (P, with the explicit wall-radius comparison below).** On the actual normalized short file of §4.100 keep the earlier transmitting cuts and allow an arbitrary family of the original innermost wall cells, with independent intermediate values. The following estimate also applies at a general admitted background when (i) the external independent J enters the file through the same printed critical-reference edge, (ii) the true saved scalar cap \(A_X\) holds on the spare incoming current chart and on the indicated native wall contour, and (iii) the physical seed of that inner edge is supported in its bounded released rectangle \(\Lambda\). These hypotheses on this short family were verified in §§4.89–4.94,4.100. Write \(R_s=e^{\kappa_1}\) for an available original contour radius of CMP116 (1.7),(1.10)–(1.14). The constants in the scalar cap and input radius must be those valid on this contour. Let \(C_{\rm an}\) be a fixed connected-set counting constant of the native wall mesh and \(C_{\rm tr}\) the conversion from its path length to the required anchor weight \(a\). If
\[
               u=2C_{\rm an}e^{a C_{\rm tr}}
                         {r_s\over R_s-r_s}<1,                         \tag{4.189}
\]
then, uniformly in the *total* number of walls on \(\|s\|_\infty\le r_s\), the sum of the two fine-current entries with their actual source radii and absolute spatial weights is at most \(C A_X\). On the stipulated geometry there is an additional \(C e^{-cR_e}\) in entries that need the seed transmitted to the exposed synthetic skin. In the original interpolation \(0\le s_\Delta\le1\), (4.189) holds with the usual sufficiently large fixed \(\kappa_1\); the exact constant test is displayed to avoid changing the wall domain implicitly. The estimate requires no R-body rare factor, no full area of X, and no small rank of the *entire* matrix at the chosen dense s.

**Proof.** Expand the actual scalar, already contact- and denominator-combined, as \(F(s,J)=\sum_{\mathbf m}f_{\mathbf m}(J)s^{\mathbf m}\) on the original finite wall system. Set \(S=\mathrm{supp}(\mathbf m)\) and \(n=|S|\). Compute \(f_{\mathbf m}\) with all walls outside S closed, on the genuine source/current product chart. For each value of the walls on S, (4.181)–(4.182) give the *same list* of at most \(2^n\) binary compressions, although the coefficients of their combination change. Hence contour integration in s, or differentiation at zero, preserves this factorization. It is wrong here to add a fresh independent matrix rank for each point of a contour.

There is a further exact support restriction. In the localized \(H_e(s)\) equations, the forcing \(-\log V'_e/i\) is confined to \(\Lambda\). On each component closed off from \(\Lambda\), the zero-target small solution is zero, also for the independently extended current: (80),(175) have no affine linear forcing there. Every local nonlinear term in that component vanishes at the zero solution. This is precisely the component argument of CMP116 pp.3–4 preceding (1.10); the walk union of a term spans its connecting cells. Varying walls inside a component disjoint from the seed therefore has no effect on H, its block-local frame or the scalar in this test. Accordingly a nonzero \(f_{\mathbf m}\) requires S together with the protected seed cubes to form a connected set (using the fixed touching-neighbor convention). Only the compressed rows in that component and its fixed native halos are needed. On the e/post-reset-j graph at most \(C_L(1+n)\) native rows occur there, because \(\Lambda\) is contained in the fixed \(100M\) cube and the mesh ratio of this incident component is at most L. Each binary current map on such a component has its original microscopic terminal indices and their original measures. Moreover a J-dependent coefficient vanishes unless the component reaches an endpoint block of the synthetic skin: before that happens the (1.63) relative response and gauge are zero there, the (1.61),(1.62) transmitted rows are independent of J, and so is the old physical composite. Thus \(n\ge cR_e-C\) when the chosen true geometric separation from \(\Lambda\) is \(c MR_e-CM_1\).

Apply (4.180) to the Hessian of this single wall coefficient, not to an artificially factorized marked quotient. Two incoming current Cauchy contours and the \(S\) wall contours give
\[
  \sum_{x,x'}h_xh_{x'}|D_xD_{x'} f_{\mathbf m}|
     \le C_L(1+n)2^n A_X R_s^{-|\mathbf m|}.                         \tag{4.190}
\]
The same rank bound applies to a mixed source/current matrix with its true old source-radius normalization. The key here is that the rank depends on \(n\), not on the infinitely increasing microscopic count or on distant fractional walls. Outside the indicated component of S, microscopic J-entries vanish. Its anchored tree length in original e-units is at most \(C_{\rm tr}(1+n)\); including a home in X costs at most an additional \(C_LR_e\), which can be incorporated in \(C_{\rm tr}(1+n)\) for the nonzero entries just described. Polynomial moments are paid the same way. There are at most a fixed constant times \(C_{\rm an}^n\) such connected sets: a depth-first traversal of a spanning tree has at most \(2n\) nearest-neighbor steps with fixed finite degree, starting at a protected cube. For each S, summing positive multiplicities \(m_\Delta\ge1\) in \((r_s/R_s)^{|\mathbf m|}\) yields \([r_s/(R_s-r_s)]^n\). This proves the claimed absolute sum by a convergent series \(\sum_{n\ge cR_e-C}(1+n)u^n\). The order of the argument retains the original signed current/contact recombination before (4.190). It works for noncommuting source directions because it uses the actual scalar analytic cap and the exact current rank, not commutativity of differentiated site phases. It is uniform for arbitrary ultraviolet depth and for the number of eligible wall cells in the recorded finite region. ∎

**Outer radius test.** The same proof covers a previously prescribed complex wall disc of radius \(r_{\rm orig}\) in full if a *larger available unmarked estimate* verifies (4.189) with \(r_s=r_{\rm orig}\). In CMP116 (1.11), walks with more than \(2^4\) distinct cubes have decay \(\delta_1 m M\), and the short walks cost \(e^{16\kappa_1}\); the construction requires \(\delta_1 M\ge\kappa_1\) and the corresponding small field for this \(\kappa_1\). An auxiliary larger \(\kappa'_1>\kappa_1+\log(1+2C_{\rm an}e^{aC_{\rm tr}})\) is legitimate only if the *fixed* spacing, weak-field contraction and native member inclusion on that enlarged domain hold. Without it (4.189) proves the stated subdisc, and (4.183) still applies on the full native disc for a single or sufficiently sparse set of fractional walls. A loss when taking \(r_s\uparrow R_s\) is a failure of this Cauchy summation argument, not evidence for a divergent physical kernel. For general overlapping returns the first structural issue is whether a newly exposed affine current, or a normalized source carried in a new component of independently varying fine data, still factors through the incident compressed terminals and has the necessary connected support.

### 4.103 Local contacts combined with compressed genuine sources

**Lemma 4.121 (P, an absolute estimate on the already combined tensor).** Suppose the completed finite Hessian, in the literal two sets of source-radius units, is \(B=B_{\rm c}+B_{\rm loc}\) with \(\operatorname{rank} B_{\rm c}\le m\); for every row x, the possible support of \(B_{\rm loc}(x,\cdot)\) contains at most d actual fine neighbors. Its values and signs can be arbitrary. For \(d\ge1\),
\[
      \sum_{x,y}|B_{xy}|
          \le C(d m+\sqrt d)\,\|B\|_{\infty\to1}.                     \tag{4.191}
\]
The norm on the right belongs to the combined signed observable, including its true quotient subtraction. To prove this let \(E\) be the specified contact support. Select columns independently with probability \(p=1/(2d)\); keep only rows which have no contact-neighbor among the selected columns. The restricted matrix has rank at most m and its operator norm is bounded by the full signed norm. A noncontact pair is retained with probability at least \(p(1-p)^d\ge c/d\), so averaging (4.180) gives \(Cdm\|B\|_{\infty\to1}\) for all noncontacts. For the remaining pairs take independent signs on all columns. The expected absolute value of each row sum is at least a fixed multiple of its Euclidean norm by the elementary Khintchine inequality (or the second- and fourth-moment proof). Their sum is bounded by \(\|B\|_{\infty\to1}\). Cauchy–Schwarz on the at most d specified entries of each row now gives the remaining \(\sqrt d\) bound. This proof requires no separate estimate of the size of \(B_{\rm loc}\) before its signed recombination.

In particular, if an actual source expression has forms \(F(\lambda,J)\) with \(\lambda=PJ\) as above and the direct two-fine-slot derivative at fixed \(\lambda\) is a contact of fixed fine-stencil range, the other three chain-rule blocks have rank at most \(3\operatorname{rank}P\). Applying (4.191) keeps the true current/contact subtraction. A compact affine local source at a retained star, and its same-star bare product in Theorem3.11, have just these finite supports when differentiated in their *explicit* local slots. This does not make a general covariance between two new separated histories a local contact. The latter must be attached by its actual connected kernel. For the dense Taylor argument apply the lemma to each coefficient supported on S; the same fixed contact degree and the same compressed span apply before taking the absolute roots. This transfers the dense estimate whenever the inherited direct fine-slot row has the specified local range. A direct term whose fine support range instead grows like a whole old native block has no such automatic bound.

### 4.104 The exact flat first-jet cancellation

**Corollary 4.122 (P).** A compact flat baseline also has a useful cancellation which does not depend on a rank bound for the whole two-index readout. Let \(\Phi\) denote the actual complete old field/current map through the specified preparations, including its original frame variables, and let the saved invariant scalar \(R_j\) have zero first jet at the resulting flat orbit. This holds for the gauge-invariant scalar and fixed zero adjoint fluctuations at the compact flat orbit, by diagonal color invariance. Suppose the stored absolute second kernel of \(R_j\) is available on that record. Then
\[
  D_xD_{x'}(R_j\circ\Phi)=
             \sum_{a,b}D_aD_bR_j\,D_x\Phi_a\,D_{x'}\Phi_b             \tag{4.192}
\]
at the stated point. In particular the term \(DR_j\cdot D_xD_{x'}\Phi\), including the isolated mixed second variation of an axial frame, vanishes exactly. For each output slot a, phase duality on its original independent incoming disc gives an entrywise first-row bound for \(D_x\Phi_a\). Multiply the two such first-row sums *only after* the signed identity (4.192) and sum against the available old absolute Hessian. This gives the two-index bound with both first frame variations kept. With native attachment weights use (4.175), a verified first kernel, or the applicable short-file geometry for those weights; an unweighted directional Cauchy inequality alone does not give a growing exponential attachment. If the physical readout is identically constant on a coincident-target branch, its first and second derivatives vanish there. Vanishing of its first derivative at one point would be insufficient. At a nonflat baseline \(DR_j\) need not vanish and the omitted term reappears. The statement does not make a conditional covariance between distinct future histories a finite contact.

### 4.105 The full simultaneous wall contour on the earliest thin consecutive file

**Proposition 4.123 (P, including all fractional innermost walls at their prescribed complex radius).** Use the normalized A-still-live / C-heals record, the transmitted preparation bands and actual spare incoming chart of Proposition 4.118. Fix an early age bound \(e\le B R_e\), with B independent of the cutoff. In particular take the actual \(b_A=1,b_C=2,\ j=1+R_1,\ e=j+1\) equal-wait plateau. Choose the ordinary corridor from A to C a sufficiently large *fixed* multiple of \(LMR_e\), thin in its transverse directions, and keep the exposed \(Y_4\) incident to C of diameter \(C_L MR_e\) plus any requisite original halos. This is a legal choice in the fixed physical torus at all sufficiently fine early cutoffs: even the entire corridor has physical diameter \(O_B(a_e R_e)\), tending to zero there. The young C shell and old A reset chart, with all genuine readout stencils, have precisely the geometry verified in Proposition 4.118. On this class the weighted absolute current/contact estimate (4.187) holds at **every** allowed simultaneous value of the inner walls, including a full dense array on the largest contour used in the scalar cap. No second wall radius is needed here. For exponential attachment retain the small native rate and explicit placement comparison of (4.187).

Indeed every complex rectangular bilinear form on \(n_1,n_2\) coordinates satisfies
\[
       \sum_{x,y}|B_{xy}|\le C\sqrt{\min(n_1,n_2)}\,
                                \|B\|_{\infty\to1}.                    \tag{4.193}
\]
For independent real signs in the columns, the expected modulus of each row sum is at least a constant times its Euclidean norm by its second and fourth moments; the same bound holds for complex coefficients. Sum the rows and use \(\ell^1\le\sqrt{n_2}\,\ell^2\) in each row. The transpose gives the other dimension. Apply this to the signed Hessian of the *actual* marked scalar, after inserting its two original independent contour radii in the coordinates. Its operator norm on this product follows by Cauchy in two independent directional parameters from (4.186) and the spare member inclusion, uniformly in the walls. This Cauchy calculation keeps both based-frame variations, the physical reconstructed current, and the signed contact and normalizing denominator inside the scalar. It does not differentiate a real rough-set indicator.

For each independent fine-current index the determining region contains at most \(C_L(1+MR_e)^4 L^{4e}\) sites, counting complete stars and color. This count uses the original fine mesh, including the ultraviolet samples inside a collapsed native cell. If an index from the contacting file can range over the length of the corridor as well, a fixed corridor multiple changes only the constant in the bound. One fine-current side already suffices in (4.193) for a mixed rectangular matrix. The logarithmic expense of (4.193) is consequently at most \(2e\log L+O(\log(2+R_e))\). With fixed offset order \(\ell\), even measuring an offset in microscopic units adds at most \(\ell[e\log L+O(\log(2+R_e))]\); in the actual j/e units of this file it adds only \(O_\ell(\log(2+R_e))\). Write \(d=d_j(X\setminus A)\) in its printed \(M\)-cube units. A sufficient quantitative condition is
\[
 (2+\ell_{\rm bare})e\log L+
    C_m\log(2+R_e+g_j^{-1})+a\sup\tau
                   \le \tfrac12\epsilon_{\rm sp}\kappa d,             \tag{4.194}
\]
where \(\ell_{\rm bare}=0\) for the actual native offsets, and the original grade conversion, if required, must have the indicated fixed weak-coupling powers. In the original native weak profile \(\log(g_j^{-1})=O(\sqrt{R_j})\) on this plateau. For an attachment of rate \(a\) satisfying \(a\sup\tau\le\epsilon_{\rm sp}\kappa d/4\) on the chosen short geometry, the lower bound on d increases linearly with the chosen corridor multiple. Thus a fixed choice depending on B and the fixed orders makes (4.194) hold on the earliest cutoff sequence. Any unchanged mandatory grade or attachment outside this explicit comparison still requires its own estimate. The cost is paid entirely by the *ordinary connector* in (4.186); at no point is the external \(T'_j(A)1\) used. This gives a full-contour result with a thin genuinely finer boundary and no four-dimensional area reserve. At arbitrary later ages with \(e/R_e\to\infty\), (4.193) alone has excessive cost on a fixed-multiple corridor; it gives no lower bound on the true tensor. On that regime the compressed and connected estimates above remain applicable in their respective domains. \(\square\)

### 4.106 Current atoms in the actual innermost variational equation

**Lemma 4.124 (P/L, on the post-reset e/j mesh of Proposition 4.118).** Fix the original \(U\) of the incoming chart, the critical-reference determination (1.63), and any innermost walls on the full contour of CMP116 (1.11). For an independent fine current put \(\widehat J_x=\sigma_{n(x)}^3 J_x\), \(w_x=(\eta_e/\sigma_{n(x)})^4\) and use the following auxiliary norm solely to bound derivatives of the inner equation:
\[
        \|\widehat J\|_{\mathcal M}
          =\max_r\sum_{x\ {\rm assigned\ to}\ r}w_x|\widehat J_x|.
                                                                        \tag{4.195}
\]
Split the finitely many orientations and incident native cells by a fixed assignment, and retain all original cells. Here \(\sigma_{n(x)}\in\{1,L^{-1}\}\), with fixed halos. For sufficiently small \(\|\widehat J\|_{\mathcal M}\) the variational potential \(H_e(s)\) and its first physical difference in the compatible chart exist and are holomorphic in this norm with the same cutoff-independent weak bound \(C\|B'\|\) on the original wall contour. This says nothing about a single freely applied Green operator on the same current. The current in this lemma is the one entering (175).

**Proof.** The reference interpolation H in (3.126),(3.133) is independent of J. By CMP99 Theorems 3.10 and 3.12 the individual generalized H-walk has (3.108) also in the point evaluation and first-difference norms of (3.133). For its transpose terminal at native \(r\), with last fine position in a given native cell t, its integration against J costs at most the supremum of this kernel in t times \(\sum_{x\in t}w_x|\widehat J_x|\) after the native physical unit conversions (4.178). All other end scales here differ by at most L. The supremum is taken *after fixing the walk*; the native-distance bound changes by a constant on t. This proves the branch estimate from (3.136) with \(C_L\|\widehat J\|_{\mathcal M}\) in place of the original regularity factor. No derivative lands on J. In (3.135) the outer \(D,D^*\) act on neighboring propagators, exactly as in the proof of (3.138). For each occurrence of \(\Delta^{(2)}\) the same estimate can be applied successively to its own H branch. The remaining summation and geometry are those of the tree in CMP99 pp.423,428 and of CMP116 (1.6)–(1.11): after multiplying each complete tree by its union-wall monomial, the first summable factors in (1.7) pay its walks and the remaining length factor pays the wall weight. Only finitely many local operator types and fixed neighbor scale ratios occur; any fixed extra constant per marked-current insertion is absorbed in the small \(\mathcal M\)-radius of the Neumann series (3.138). This proves the same operator bounds there for \(H,H_0,G\) on this contour. In the explicit current-dependent vertex of (80) one uses the same H-transpose estimate. In particular the zero-seed stationary equation has zero solution. The original small-source contraction (1.2)–(1.5),(1.12)–(1.14) uses just these operator bounds and the same local nonlinear maps. Taking the auxiliary current radius and seed radius below fixed multiples of their original ceilings gives the stated holomorphic solution with the original finite difference norms. A fixed constant loss in this auxiliary radius is harmless at (4.170); no increase of the evaluation wall radius or of an original source radius is being assumed.

At an original input staying in a smaller weak current ball add, in the original normalized current coordinates, two single-site perturbations \(z\,\rho w_x^{-1}{\bf e}_x\), \(z'\rho w_{x'}^{-1}{\bf e}_{x'}\). Their \(\mathcal M\)-norms are at most \(\rho(|z|+|z'|)\), also within the same cell. Cauchy on fixed independent fractions of the auxiliary radius therefore gives
\[
 \rho^2 |\partial_{\widehat J_x}\partial_{\widehat J_{x'}}H_e(s)(y)|
       \le C\|B'\|\,w_xw_{x'},                                         \tag{4.196}
\]
with the analogous first-difference bound and its one-source variant. Summing over the fine bonds in native cells costs only a fixed factor per cell. A determining region of diameter \(C_LMR_e\) costs \(O((1+MR_e)^8)\) for both indices; its logarithm fits the thin connector spare in (4.186) for arbitrary e. The evaluation at nonzero seed and the whole original contour are included. This proves ultraviolet summability of the actual **inner compatible response** without the count in (4.194).

**Where this auxiliary continuation stops.** On the transmitting cuts (1.63) is still evaluated at the raw target
\(u_{k,\Lambda}^{-1}\exp(i\widetilde Q(\eta H_k))R(u_{k,\Lambda,+})M^\bullet U\)
on the synthetic skin. On two probes of (4.196), (1.65) for the total product has been proved for its native *pointwise* current domain. A single probe of magnitude \(\rho/w_x\) can exceed that bound by \(L^{4e}\). The compatible contraction just proved does not by itself place this particular framed raw target on its spare old domain. If it does there, (4.196)'s same two contours, applied directly to the signed scalar, give the complete weighted estimate with at most two native cell counts, its original radii and its full physical contact. This sufficiency is elementary; the strong conclusion that an arbitrary axial inverse sends all auxiliary atomic tests to the original range is not part of (1.65). In particular (4.171) cannot be applied on a larger input disc merely from the inverse's holomorphy at its center. The same distinction remains for the first newly incident affine current outside (175). \(\square\)

### 4.107 Exact charge selection in the normalized late scalar

**Proposition 4.125 (P).** Use the explicit logarithmic contour mean CMP109 (0.10)–(0.12), or (0.4) for its relative field average. Realize the A-still-live / C-heals record of Proposition 4.118 with its two retained A links, the small nonzero C reinstatement, reference trial, and other fixed external data in a common maximal torus. Allow all complex innermost walls simultaneously, the actual short file with arbitrary pre-entry e and gauge-invariant compact source tags. At a Cartan-valued independent current the completed marked scalar on this record obeys
\[
 D_{J_x^h}D_{J_{x'}^k}F_X=0,\qquad
 D_{J_x^\lambda}D_{J_{x'}^\nu}F_X=0
       \quad(\lambda+\nu\ne0).                                       \tag{4.197}
\]
Here h,k are any Cartan colors and \(\lambda,\nu\) are weights of the complexified adjoint representation, including zero. The first identity also holds for one Cartan current and differentiation of a gauge-invariant marking source. The only possible two-current entries remaining are the ordered opposite-root pairs for this explicit mean. The weight-selection identity, without the extra Cartan zero, uses only torus equivariance and also applies to the broader class (0.5)–(0.9). This includes SU(2), in which there is just one root pair. The actual marked coefficient remains the original signed normalized one and no estimate for a separated gauge was substituted.

Indeed in a common Cartan algebra, the original raw relative mean of link angles is affine, including the differences of its two endpoint contours. Every Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff remainder in its local expansion vanishes for commuting relative inputs, so the constraint remainder C and its linearized remainder D in CMP102 (70)–(73) vanish on the Cartan subspace. Their derivatives in Cartan directions along that space vanish. Conjugation by a constant group element of the maximal torus preserves all complete averages, the gauge conditions, and the primitive walk expansion term by term; the wall weights are scalars. Thus Cartan-input variations give Cartan outputs, and a derivative in a nonzero-weight direction has that same weight. A Cartan current paired with it is zero by invariance of the Lie algebra pairing. For (3.136) the J-dependent quadratic form consequently annihilates the Cartan sector when J is Cartan; so do all corresponding J-dependent propagator insertions on that sector. Binary and fractional wall weights preserve this algebraic zero term by term. In the nonlinear explicit (80) the current multiplies D, whose first variation against Cartan inputs likewise vanishes there. Uniqueness of the compatible small solution gives exactly the same \(H_e(s)\) for every Cartan J as for \(J=0\), even off the physical endpoint. The chosen earlier cuts make \(H_e(s)\) the sole dependence of the joined determining target on this independent J. The original restoring frames, old reconstruction and actual Wilson current are functions of those same targets. Consequently the *whole* prepared signed scalar, including its normalized A-operation and all retained invariant source parameters, is constant on Cartan current lines, which proves the first assertion. The entire computation is equivariant under constant torus conjugation, even when the normalized A-integral is over noncommuting internal links: change all these integration variables together. The Hessian at the torus-fixed external point therefore pairs only weights summing to zero, proving the second assertion on the entire original analytic chart. A non-Cartan current pair \(E_\lambda,E_{-\lambda}\) can enter the nonlinear Cartan equation at second order; the argument gives no identity for that pair.

There is a tensorial reduction even there. At the torus-fixed exterior, take the actual simple-root matrix basis \(E_{ab},E_{ba}\). First-order propagation of \(E_{ab}\) lies entirely in that one-dimensional color line, and the mixed \(E_{ab},E_{ba}\) second-order field lies in the Cartan sector. All other spectator colors remain diagonal. This follows directly by differentiating equivariance, or by keeping the \(2\times2\) active block and the other diagonal entries in each word. Thus the first missing noncommuting test on this late fixture is a charged linearized (106) phase followed by its neutral second-order target response; summing all arbitrary pairs of Lie algebra components is unnecessary. Its two microscopic positions are still independent. Nor does the \(\mathfrak{su}(2)\) subalgebra remove any part of the signed normalized partition. \(\square\)

### 4.108 Spare primitive walk length at the full contour

**Theorem 4.126 (P/L for the following explicit fixed-parameter range).** Take the normalized thin A–C file, actual current/mark radii, and exposed geometry of Proposition 4.118. The following test of its *already fixed* primitive scales is sufficient for (4.187) at **arbitrary age e and every simultaneous innermost wall on the original full complex contour**. It estimates the complete signed scalar with both frame variations, not only \(H_e\) in its compatible gauge. Let \(M_1\) be the primitive localization length in CMP116 (1.6)–(1.7), M the wall length, and \(\kappa_1\) the prescribed evaluation exponent. Set \(R_s=e^{\kappa_1}\). Write \(C_L\) for the fixed packing constant in the proof below and let \(\theta\) satisfy
\[
 e^\theta>1+2 C_{\rm an}e^{aC_{\rm tr}},\qquad
 C_L\theta\,M_1/M\le c_{\rm w}\log M_1.                              \tag{4.198}
\]
Here \(c_{\rm w}\) can be taken to be \(1/16\): retain this part of the separate walk-length factor of (3.108), or use the actual smaller leftover exponent if an intermediate version of the primitive estimate has spent more. We also keep the original spacing and weak-field tests of Proposition 4.118. The test holds for all sufficiently large initially fixed primitive lengths with \(M\ge M_1\), and also with fixed \(M_1>1\) and sufficiently large \(M/M_1\). All these choices are made before constructing histories. No numerical assertion for an unspecified small fixed primitive length is needed.

**Proof of the packing count.** A simple localization of (3.107) is a connected union of boundedly many primitive cubes, of diameter at most \(D_L M_1\) including its original fixed enlargements, in the current e/j geometry. Every neighboring pair in its walk meets. For the tree-like walks of CMP99 p.428, keep the main chain and all its attached chains. Join one center per localization along each of these edges and traverse the resulting tree, with multiplicities if needed. This gives a route of length at most \(D'_L M_1(|\omega|+1)\). Its fixed-\(D_L M_1\) neighborhood contains the localization to which the monomial of CMP116 (1.7) is assigned. With \(M\ge M_1\), cover the route by balls spaced by length M. Each enlarged ball has radius at most a fixed multiple of M and meets a bounded number of M-walls. The two post-reset native mesh sizes differ by only L, so the number of distinct factors obeys
\[
         m(\omega)\le C_L[1+(|\omega|+1)M_1/M].                       \tag{4.199}
\]
A zero-length parametrix term is included. This bound remains valid with fixed enlargements of its terminal set. For a tree counted by expanding its branches as in (3.138), \(|\omega|\) is the total count of primitive steps. If primitive chains of length zero meet at a current vertex, count one per junction; its contraction already carries the small current coefficient. With \(q\) junctions the right side increases by \(C_L q(1+M_1/M)\), to be charged below per junction, not to a zero-length walk.

**The unused factor.** Original CMP99 p.416 (3.108) has both the summable product \([O(M_1^{-1/2})]^{|\omega|}\) and a further \(M_1^{-|\omega|/2}\), besides the exponential in the native tree distance. CMP116 (1.7),(1.11) splits the corresponding walk and distance bounds to sum over walks and pay the original \(e^{\kappa_1m}\); the original supremum in (1.11) still contains a negative power of \(M_1^{|\omega|}\). In particular, leaving the original summability and the weakened distance exponent intact, one may retain \(M_1^{-|\omega|/16}\) when using the displayed (3.108) proof. Alternatively derive this directly there by using its *first* product to sum the bounded branching walks, reserving a fixed fraction of the second \(M_1^{-|\omega|/2}\) for the new purpose. The first product is summable for a sufficiently large fixed primitive length; it is the same primary small-parametrix hypothesis. By (4.198),(4.199),
\[
       e^{\theta m(\omega)}M_1^{-c_{\rm w}|\omega|}
               \le e^{C_L\theta(1+M_1/M)}                             \tag{4.200}
\]
for a single chain. The tree junction cost is at most another fixed \(e^{C_L\theta(1+M_1/M)}\) per junction, absorbed by taking the auxiliary weak input smaller by a fixed factor in the (3.138) contraction. For the original source an \(M_1^{-1/2}\) arising from the summable parametrix is not spent twice. In (1.11) keep the same tree exponent used for the original contour; short walks of at most sixteen wall cells also cost at most the fixed \(e^{16(\kappa_1+\theta)}\). No source attachment or old support weight was taken from (4.186).

It follows that the original primitive proof, for genuine ordinary small currents (no current atoms here), is bounded on the auxiliary contour \(R_s e^\theta\). The localized \(D,A_0\) and relative field in (1.2)–(1.5) are obtained by the same contraction with fixed enlarged constants. In particular, in the source's small-parameter test \(4B_0C_1 e^{16\kappa_1}\epsilon_1\le\alpha_2/2\) one can reduce the *auxiliary* input radius by a fixed factor depending on \(\theta\) and the constant of (4.200). In the p.374–375 construction of (1.65) no further wall weight is introduced into the actual based map: each subsequent determining problem is applied to the output of the preceding localized variational field with its same regularity and off-source estimates. With the transmitting outer cuts it is enough to rerun these same estimates with their larger fixed constants, including both actual frame factors. Equivalently, use the original fixed-contour member proof with \(\kappa_1+\theta\) in its smallness constants and (4.200) in the sole primitive contour test (1.11). This is a bound on the *usual* current product and the usual joined member, with all actual fine exterior values. Write the resulting auxiliary parameter disc \(c_\theta\mu_{\min}\), with \(c_\theta>0\) fixed. Then (4.168)–(4.170) retain their relative old margin for the **whole** chosen physical reinstatement because \(C_\theta\bar\alpha\, M_0\epsilon_e/\mu_{\min}^2\to0\) on the stated equal-exponent weak family. On a weak interval that is not asymptotic, use this explicit strict inequality rather than an asymptotic replacement. Thus the actual normalized coefficient with cap (4.186) is defined on the auxiliary wall contour needed for the Cauchy estimate. The evaluated reinstatement interval, current indices, original domain, and original saved metric are the same as before.

Finally put \(r_s=R_s\) and use the certified radius \(R_s e^\theta\) in (4.189)–(4.190). Its ratio is \(1/(e^\theta-1)\); (4.198) makes the series summable. The absolute moment and anchor costs are exactly those of Proposition 4.120 on the short normalized corridor. This proves (4.187) for every original wall at arbitrary e, including \(e/R_e\to\infty\), on the stated fixed native parameters. No microscopic cardinality or normalized-body mass is used. The sufficient primitive-parameter test has two distinct uses: it can be verified directly for a given fixed setup, or imposed when specifying its initially free sufficiently-large parameters. It cannot be imposed retroactively on a different fixed history. \(\square\)

### 4.109 An incident mixed determining frame at the next preparation stage

**Proposition 4.127 (P/L, one nonempty middle localization).** Keep the original normalized record of Theorem 4.126, including a closed separating band for the affine (1.55) forcing in the outermost (1.61) stage. In (1.62) now allow a nonempty \(Y'_3\) contained in \(Y_2\), meeting its true synthetic skin and containing the released C rectangle. For example take the rectangle with its fixed localization enlargement and an aligned M-cube chain through the interior to a face of \(Y_2\setminus Y_2^0\). Such a support is admitted by the p.374 component rule; its union with \(Y_2\) is \(Y_3=Y_2\). Choose its enclosing geometry as in Proposition 4.118, with the same positive distance of the *innermost* \(\Lambda\) to the synthetic skin of \(Y_3\), and complete original boundary halos. The independent middle walls may have all their original complex values. For the actual marked coefficient on this support, the two-current/source bound of Theorem 4.126 still holds, with the same thin outside connector, equal-exponent input family and (4.198). The proposition bounds every such filed coefficient; it does not require asserting a nonzero value on a selected middle wall.

Here the output is not obtained by setting \(H_{B_1}\) and its restoring gauge to zero on the skin. The actual (1.62) takes as seed
\[
