           -\,i^{-1}\log M^\bullet(U_{k,\Lambda})|_{Z''_k},
                                                                        \tag{4.201}
\]
and as background the very same prepared \(U_{k,\Lambda}\) from (1.63) (LFRII pp.374–375). Its other prescribed rows are the fixed retained data or the displayed functions of the independent U. At fixed U all new dependence on the incoming independent J therefore passes through \(U_{k,\Lambda}\), which already contains the first restoring \(u_{k,\Lambda}\). The second restoring gauge of (1.62) acts on the result. A physical current needed in a determining problem about this changing background is differentiated as a function of that background; it is not replaced by another freely varied affine J. The outer (1.61) stage is transmitted on its exposed skin by its specified cut, so has no additional free affine terminal there. Inner pinned \(M^\bullet U_0^{(AL)}\) values are kept; in particular the gauge change of that released field under a change of U is not replaced by zero.

For each innermost Taylor support S, close all other innermost cells. Then the full composed scalar at fixed remaining wall values still depends on J through (4.181), restricted to the component reachable from the physical seed in (1.64). The intermediate frame and the possibly finer middle determining rows assign no new independent index to J. If this component has not reached the synthetic skin of \(Y_3\), the entire (1.63) determination, including its interior imposed values, is independent of this J. Consequently (4.201), both frame factors and the old physical current are independent of it as well. Thus the lower bound \(n\ge cR_e-C\) of Proposition 4.120 persists. There are \(C_L(1+n)\) incident compressed rows per binary mask on the *innermost post-reset graph*; counting all finer sites of the middle chart instead would lose this bound.

For the analytic cap on the required contour, keep the middle wall exponent at its prescribed value, enlarge only the innermost contour by (4.198)–(4.200), and apply p.375's original successive member estimates. The compact base at zero has identity relative seeds at every stage, including (4.201); the retained pure nodal C representative is treated by the same single compact gauge as in §4.89. The chosen \(Y'_3\) leaves the determining geometry \(Y_3=Y_2\) unchanged. Hence the comparison (4.168)–(4.170), and the actual p-window and current estimates of Proposition 4.113, apply to the total product including the middle gauge; reduce the auxiliary constant by the fixed enlarged-contour factor as in Theorem 4.126. Multivariate Cauchy in the true incoming current probes differentiates the whole normalized scalar, so it includes the newly varying mixed frame, reconstruction and contact.

For clarity about the placement of absolute values in this extension, if \(B_s\) is the Hessian of the complete signed scalar on a wall coefficient contour and its rank at each point of that contour is at most m, the triangle inequality gives
\[
       \sum_{x,x'}|\partial_x\partial_{x'}[s^{\bf n}]F|
            \le R^{-|\bf n|}\,\sup_{|s|=R}\sum_{x,x'}|(B_s)_{xx'}|
            \le R^{-|\bf n|}\,m\,\sup_{|s|=R}\|B_s\|_{\infty\to1}.
                                                                        \tag{4.202}
\]
This requires no common rank-m range across contour points. Apply it to (4.190) with \(R=R_s e^\theta\), and retain the original anchor weights and genuine outside A mass convention. It gives the asserted absolute sum for the file at arbitrarily late e. Neither (4.201) nor its incident mixed gauge is an independent source. A history in which (1.55) itself exposes a new independent fine affine current on an old marked row is outside this proof. If one varies a new U slot rather than J, \(U_0^{(AL)}\) and unmarked exterior fields can vary independently of the compressed list. Those are different remaining cases. \(\square\)

Full saved-record coverage **O**. |
| [Boundary traces and reconstruction at a changing base](block_and_transfer/conditional_estimates/boundary_traces_and_reconstruction_at_a_changing_base.tex), §§1–9; [source estimates](source_estimates/README.md), LM42–LM52 pp.781–784, AE8 pp.932–935, NM12 pp.1215–1219 | Original readout, stored norm, scale conversion, unresolved curvature sum | Not a literature theorem. Definitions used; mathematical claims are proved above when labeled P. |

**Denominator (L/P).** LFR II (1.10) is

\[
D(U_0)^{-1}=\exp\{g_k^{-2}A(\zeta_0,U_0)+I(U_0)-E_k(\Lambda)\},
\quad |I(U_0)|<C\log M\,|\Lambda|.                              \tag{5.1}
\]

Thus the bounded analytic logarithm belongs to the **factored residual denominator**. The large Wilson action and the Gaussian/vacuum normalization must first cancel against their precise numerator partners. Dropping them and asserting a raw denominator ≥exp(−C|Z′|) is not what (5.1) proves. For the later same-operator T′ normalization, §3.1 gives cancellation even when its actual mass is arbitrarily small.

**Rare factor (L/ICR).** The statement of I-0c includes weights exp(−cg_k⁻²·size), whereas the regular R-origin activity estimate (1.100) contains exp[−p₀(g_k)]. These are not interchangeable; p₀(g) is polylogarithmic in g⁻² in the original construction. The assumed I-0c is retained as the interface, but no argument here cites (1.100) as proof of that stronger factor. All reserve statements above use a generic true reserve or (3.34), which already works for p₀(g) of order (log g⁻²)^p with p>1.

## 6. Interface Statement

**Assumed:** I-0c exactly as stated. Its unmarked representation is not silently strengthened. No I-1, I-2, SF package, Poincaré inequality, actual-law Gaussian comparison, or mass gap is assumed.

**Proved, with their exact scope:**

* **P/L:** same-operator marked normalization, first and second source rows, and a precise relative-mass criterion for a rare mismatched numerator (§§3.1–3.2,4.1).
* **P:** constructive old-minimum continuation criterion, complete changed-base/moving-row response hierarchy, physical gauge/frame derivatives, and unmatched-row residual formulas (§§3.3–3.5,4.2,4.6).
* **P/L:** native counting at δ₀/8 and δ₀/16 with the explicit original spacing test; relative-scale and native anchor moment sums; completion of the geometric assertion NM12.17 when that test holds (§§3.9,3.12).
* **P:** complete path occupation measures and their native-unit conversions; same-layer joined-interior row overlap; absolute-anchor composition (§§3.10,4.3,4.5,4.7).
* **P/L:** depth-uniform native multipliers and first derivatives on the original source hierarchy; full physical current at flat background via the original row adjoints (§4.10).
* **P/L:** exact original row enumeration for the first joined hierarchy, with a mixed-interior, top-boundary spacing estimate and depth-uniform application of the flat current theorem (§4.11).
* **P/L:** nonlinear physical-current bound in the common weak centered chart, including the exact cancellation (4.31)–(4.32) of the raw endpoint gauge contribution at a physical critical point (§4.12).
* **P/L:** original CMP102 real/complex admission test and first-join replay of old collar averages, preserving the complete descendant words (§4.13).
* **P/L:** localization of the actual zero-fluctuation changed-base physical forcing after its critical projection (4.34), and the AE8 estimate (4.37) on a refined collar under the displayed physical reference and target conditions (§§4.14–4.15).
* **P/L:** the matched p=k output physical-current row (4.38), an ordinary rough first return and a two-seed merger, full native-domain old readout on their protected seam-crossing boxes ((4.39),(4.41)), the fixed-spacing contraction check, and single-root scalar grade/age (4.43). The cubic and fourth-order seam sums also retain their scalar ages (§§4.16–4.18).

## 8. Summary

Full large-field admission for the prescribed susceptibility insertion remains open. On a fixed return record, one or two bounded insertions are controlled by the mass of the identical unmarked operator. Its localized complex logarithm retains the actual normalization. The original regular return estimate has an exponential in the logarithmic threshold.

An explicit fine harmonic wave with zero first averages produces an inverse-age response in a separately filed matching force. Its small-record exterior completion cancels that response. The prescribed physical coefficient includes both repairs, a common determining graph, and its lower, pointed and contact terms. For the globally clean first birth the actual connected quantum file uses the full conditional denominator. Independently normalized translated windows have a different Gaussian covariance.

On consecutive mixed returns, a matrix Schwarz estimate bounds the true spliced raw field and its reconstructed Wilson current. The native layer comparison retains a strict old margin through the entire reinstatement cutoff on the specified outer chart. The released axial–Landau representative remains part of the calculation. One separated intervening return preserves the inherited summand and its original clock.

A short normalized still-live file can meet a genuinely finer retained edge without containing a large ordinary subcube. Its critical-reference current dependence factors through native interpolation transposes. Binary wall interpolation and a finite-rank estimate bound both microscopic indices after signed contact and denominator recombination. Connected wall supports and spare primitive walk length control the full dense contour at arbitrary ages under the fixed packing test, also through one nonempty mixed determining frame. A detached transverse-current operator has a logarithm on explicit bounded fine inputs; the common constrained re-readout cancels that particular response. General nonflat overlapping records, independently exposed nonlinear gauges and the remaining all-history source and current estimates are unresolved. All test offsets retain their corresponding original birth units throughout this estimate.
